# AP-11 — Gallbladder Meridian (GB) — Foot Shaoyang (足少阳胆经)

> **Status:** Phase 2 **COMPLETE**. 44/44 points. Review done.
> 3 Tier A, 19 Tier B, 22 Tier C planned. 44 points total.
>
> **Meridian overview:** The Gallbladder channel (Foot Shaoyang)
> runs from the lateral canthus of the eye (GB1) → zigzag across
> the temporal and parietal skull (GB2–GB12) → forehead and occiput
> (GB13–GB20) → shoulder (GB21) → lateral trunk (GB22–GB28) →
> lateral hip (GB29–GB30) → lateral thigh and knee (GB31–GB34) →
> lateral leg (GB35–GB39) → ankle and foot (GB40–GB44). 44 points
> total — the SECOND LARGEST regular meridian after Bladder (67).
> Interior-exterior paired with Liver (Foot Jueyin, AP-12
> COMPLETE). Same-name Shaoyang pairing with Triple Burner (Hand
> Shaoyang, AP-10 COMPLETE). Peak time: 23:00–01:00 (Zi shi).
>
> **Element:** Wood (paired with LR). Yang manifestation of the
> Wood element. GB governs decision-making (中正之官, 决断出焉 —
> "official of central rightness, decisions and judgments stem
> from it," Su Wen Ch.8).
>
> **Unique features:**
> - **20 head points** (GB1–GB20) — most cranial points of ANY
>   channel. BL has ~10 (BL1–BL10), ST ~8 (ST1–ST8).
> - **Dai Mai (Belt Vessel) hub:** GB41 opens Dai Mai (Eight
>   Confluent), GB26–GB28 = GB-Dai Mai meeting points.
> - **Migraine dominance:** 8/11 top migraine points are Shaoyang
>   (GB+TE). GB20-GB8-TE5: 90.48% confidence (AP-10).
> - **Two Front-Mu points:** GB24 (own) + GB25 (KI) — only channel
>   hosting two Front-Mu points.
> - **Two Xi-Clefts at same level:** GB35 (Yang Wei Mai) + GB36
>   (GB proper), both at 7 cun above lateral malleolus.
>
> **First foot channel:** GB is the 9th Phase 2 meridian and the
> FIRST foot channel. All 6 hand channels (LU, LI, HT, SI, PC,
> TE) are COMPLETE.
>
> **Total points:** 44 standard | Phase 1 entries: None

---

## GB1 — Đồng Tử Liêu (瞳子髎, Tóng Zǐ Liáo)

**Evidence tier: B (Standard Entry)**
"Pupil's Bone-Hole" (瞳子髎) — 瞳子 (Tóngzǐ, pupil of the eye)
+ 髎 (Liáo, bone depression/crevice). Named for the bony
depression on the lateral orbital rim near the pupil. Unlike the
other two Yang same-name terminal-to-start transitions — SI19
"Palace of Hearing" (names auditory function) and LI20 "Welcome
Fragrance" (names olfactory function) — GB1's name references
vision. The Shaoyang transition (TE23→GB1) is the third and
final same-name facial handoff, at the LATERAL eye.

GB1 is the **STARTING POINT (起穴)** of the Gallbladder channel
— receiving the Shaoyang baton from TE23 (Sizhukong) at the
lateral eyebrow. TE23 sits on the lateral orbital rim at the
eyebrow tip; GB1 drops ~1 cm inferiorly to the lateral canthus.
Both points share CN V2 (zygomaticotemporal) and CN VII (temporal
branch) innervation, but their CN V1 branches diverge:
supraorbital nerve at TE23, lacrimal nerve at GB1. The same-name
transition crosses a V1 branch boundary while maintaining V2/VII
continuity — a neural handoff within a handoff.

GB1 is a **MEETING POINT of three channels**: Hand Taiyang (SI),
Hand Shaoyang (TE), and Foot Shaoyang (GB). This is the SAME
three-channel combination as SI19 — the only other triple-meeting
in the Atlas with this exact composition. SI19 meets these three
channels at the ear (pre-auricular), GB1 meets them at the eye
(lateral canthus). Two convergence nodes for the same three
channels, ~5 cm apart, at different sensory organs.

**Observation 1 — Same Three-Channel Meeting at Two Sensory
Sites:**

SI19 and GB1 share the same meeting-point composition (SI+TE+GB)
but serve different sensory organs. The SI channel runs from the
ear (SI19) through the cheek to the medial eye (BL1 transition).
The TE channel runs from near the ear (TE22) to the lateral
eyebrow (TE23) to GB1. The GB channel starts at GB1 and
immediately drops back toward the ear (GB2). These three channels
form a LOOP between the eye and ear — the two lateral sensory
organs — with SI19 and GB1 as anchoring nodes:

| Node | Location | Sensory Domain | Channels |
|------|----------|---------------|----------|
| SI19 | Pre-auricular | Hearing | SI, TE, GB |
| GB1 | Lateral canthus | Vision | SI, TE, GB |

Whether this triple-channel convergence at both lateral sensory
organs reflects a systematic design principle or trajectory
coincidence is unstudied. 🟡

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): in the depression on the
lateral side of the orbit, lateral to the outer canthus of
the eye.

Anatomical landmarks: 0.5 cun lateral to the outer canthus,
in the bony depression on the lateral margin of the orbital
rim (frontal process of the zygomatic bone). The depression
is palpable with gentle pressure against the orbital rim.

Spatial relationships:
- TE23 (Sizhukong) is directly superior — lateral end of the
  eyebrow, ~1 cm above. The PRECEDING channel's terminal point.
- BL2 (Zanzhu) is at the MEDIAL end of the eyebrow — TE23 and
  BL2 bracket the eyebrow, while GB1 sits below TE23.
- ST1 (Chengqi) is the infraorbital point at the LOWER orbital
  rim midline — GB1 and ST1 represent lateral vs inferior
  orbital approaches.
- GB2 (Tinghui) is at the pre-auricular intertragic notch —
  a large jump anteriorly and inferiorly (~4 cm). The gap
  between GB1 and GB2 is the largest between any consecutive
  GB head points.

Needle depth: 3–5 mm subcutaneous, directed laterally along the
orbital rim. Transverse insertion (subcutaneous) is standard.
NEVER directed medially or posteriorly into the orbit.

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Zygomaticotemporal nerve (CN V2):** branch of the zygomatic
  nerve from the maxillary division of CN V. Emerges through the
  zygomaticotemporal foramen on the temporal surface of the
  zygomatic bone. Sensory to the lateral temple and lateral
  orbital region. SHARED with TE23 — both points access this
  nerve, providing V2 continuity across the Shaoyang transition.
- **Zygomaticofacial nerve (CN V2):** emerges through the
  zygomaticofacial foramen on the anterior surface of the
  zygomatic bone. Sensory to the skin over the zygomatic
  prominence. At GB1's depth, this branch contributes to the
  periorbital sensory field.
- **Lacrimal nerve (CN V1):** lateral-most branch of the
  ophthalmic division. Runs along the lateral wall of the orbit,
  supplies the lacrimal gland and lateral upper eyelid. Terminal
  branches emerge near the lateral canthus. This is the V1
  branch that DIFFERS from TE23 (which accesses the supraorbital
  nerve, also V1 but from the frontal nerve).
- **Temporal branch of facial nerve (CN VII):** motor to
  frontalis and orbicularis oculi. Crosses superficially over
  the zygomatic arch at or near GB1's level.
- Spinal segments: CN V1 + CN V2 (trigeminal) + CN VII (motor)
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1

**Neural Zone 1 (lateral orbital):** GB1 occupies the first
neural zone of the GB channel — CN V1/V2 territory (ophthalmic
and maxillary divisions of the trigeminal nerve). The
zygomaticotemporal (V2) and lacrimal (V1) nerves converge at
the lateral orbital rim. GB2 transitions to CN V3
(auriculotemporal), establishing the first zone boundary within
the GB head sequence.

| Neural comparison: TE23 → GB1 transition |
|------------------------------------------|
| Shared: CN V2 (zygomaticotemporal), CN VII (temporal branch) |
| TE23 only: CN V1 supraorbital (frontal nerve branch) |
| GB1 only: CN V1 lacrimal (direct V1 branch), CN V2 zygomaticofacial |

The same-name transition maintains V2/VII continuity but shifts
the V1 component from supraorbital to lacrimal — a branch-level
change within the same trigeminal division.

Fascial plane: orbicularis oculi (lateral orbital fibers) →
lateral palpebral raphe → periosteum of the lateral orbital rim.
→ Cross-ref: A03 S3.4

Vascular:
- Zygomatico-orbital artery (branch of the superficial temporal
  artery) — the primary vessel of the lateral orbital region.
- Lateral palpebral arteries (from lacrimal artery, branch of
  the ophthalmic artery) — supply the lateral eyelids. Small-
  caliber but risk periorbital hematoma.
- Superficial temporal artery terminal branches in the lateral
  temple, superior to GB1.

Muscle layers (superficial → deep):
- Skin (thin, periorbital)
- Subcutaneous tissue (minimal fat)
- Orbicularis oculi, lateral orbital portion
- Periosteum of the lateral orbital rim (frontal process of
  zygomatic bone)
- Orbit (deep — NO ENTRY)

### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

No Five Shu, Yuan-Source, Luo-Connecting, Xi-Cleft, Back-Shu,
Front-Mu, Eight Confluent, or Hui-Meeting designation.
→ Cross-ref: B01 S3.1

**Channel starting point (起穴):** GB1 is the first point on
the GB channel. It receives qi flow from **TE23 (Sizhukong)**
at the lateral eyebrow — the **Hand Shaoyang (TE) → Foot
Shaoyang (GB)** same-name transition. This completes the
documentation of the Shaoyang same-name transition from BOTH
sides (sender: TE23 in AP-10 Obs. 13, receiver: GB1 here).

All three Yang same-name transitions at the face, now
documented from both sender and receiver:

| Same-name Pair | Sender (止穴) | Receiver (起穴) | Location |
|---------------|-------------|----------------|----------|
| Yangming | LI20 (AP-02) | ST1 (future) | Nose |
| Taiyang | SI19 (AP-06) | BL1 (future) | Medial eye |
| **Shaoyang** | **TE23 (AP-10)** | **GB1 (here)** | **Lateral eye** |

**Meeting point (交会穴):** meeting of Hand Taiyang (SI), Hand
Shaoyang (TE), and Foot Shaoyang (GB) — same three-channel
composition as SI19. See Observation 1.

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM acupoint:** GB1 Tongziliao (瞳子髎). Functions: clears
heat, brightens the eyes (明目), dispels wind from the eye,
expels lateral headache. Primary eye point on the GB channel —
the starting point of the "eye → ear → head" trajectory.
Indications: eye redness, eye pain, lacrimation (tearing),
blurred vision, photophobia, early-stage cataracts, lateral
headache, facial paralysis.

**2. Trigger point:** orbicularis oculi trigger points in the
lateral orbital portion can refer pain around the eye and to the
ipsilateral temple. GB1 overlies the lateral attachment of
orbicularis oculi. Moderate correspondence. 🟡
→ Cross-ref: B11

**3. Ashi correspondence:** tender in periorbital headache,
eye strain (digital eye strain, prolonged near work), temporal
headache, and acute conjunctivitis. Lateral orbital tenderness
is a common clinical finding in migraine patients — consistent
with Shaoyang migraine territory. 🟡

**4. Marma point:** **Apanga Marma (अपांग)** — one of the 107
Marma points in the Sushruta system. Located at the outer corner
of the eye, at the lateral margin of the orbit. Classified as
**Sira Marma** (vessel type), size 1/2 angula (~1 cm). Injury
is said to cause andhatva (blindness) or impaired vision
(drishti-hani). STRONG spatial correspondence — Apanga Marma and
GB1 occupy the same anatomical location. Functional
correspondence also aligns: both are primary treatment sites for
eye disorders. This is one of the strongest Marma-acupoint
correspondences documented in the Atlas. 🟡
→ Cross-ref: C04

**5. Head zone:** CN V1/V2 dermatome — ophthalmic and maxillary
trigeminal territories. The eye (cornea, conjunctiva) receives
sensory supply from V1 (ophthalmic) — GB1 is in V1/V2 territory
treating a V1-innervated organ. Direct segmental correspondence
for eye conditions, following the same principle as SI19 (V3
territory treating V3-innervated ear) and LI20 (V2 territory
treating V2-innervated nose).
→ Cross-ref: A07 S2.2

**6. Anatomy Trains:** no major myofascial meridian terminates
specifically at the lateral canthus. The Lateral Line (LL)
ascends the lateral body to the mastoid/occipital region but
does not extend to the orbital rim. The Superficial Front Line
(SFL) connects through the SCM to the mastoid, not the orbit.
Weak anatomical trains correspondence. 🔴

**7. Neurogenic inflammatory spot:** no specific data for the
lateral orbital region. Kim 2017 focused on trunk and limb
points.

**8. Cupping point:** NOT applicable — periorbital face.

**Convergence score: 3/8** (TCM, TrP/Ashi, Marma). The Apanga
Marma correspondence elevates GB1 above typical facial points.
Head Zone provides dermatomal logic but is a different mapping
system than spatial convergence.

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Nguồn cổ:
- Ling Shu Ch.1 (Ben Shu): starting point of Foot Shaoyang.
- Jiayi Jing: headache (偏头痛, lateral predominant), eye
  redness and pain (目赤痛), excessive tearing (目泪出),
  visual blurring (目茫茫), nearsightedness (青盲, literally
  "green blindness"), lateral canthus pain. Moxibustion: 3
  cones permitted.
- Zhenjiu Dacheng: wind-tearing of the eyes, eye screen
  (翳膜, corneal opacity), blurred vision, lateral headache,
  deviation of the mouth.

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- Eye disorders: conjunctivitis, keratitis, early cataract,
  optic atrophy, glaucoma (traditional, not evidence-based)
- Headache: lateral/temporal headache, migraine
- Facial: facial paralysis, trigeminal neuralgia (V1/V2
  distribution)
- All indications cluster around two domains: EYE and LATERAL
  HEAD — consistent with GB1's V1/V2 innervation territory.

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

🔴 Unvalidated for all indications. No RCT or systematic review
isolating GB1. GB1 appears as a component of multi-point
protocols for eye disorders (commonly combined with BL1, BL2,
ST1, GB20, LR3) and facial paralysis (with ST4, ST6, LI4, TE17),
but its individual contribution is unstudied.

The absence of GB1-specific evidence parallels the broader
pattern: facial acupoints are almost exclusively studied in
combination. The same evidence gap exists at TE23, SI19, and
LI20 — all sensory-region terminal/starting points.

### Dynamic acupoint properties

No specific sensitization data for GB1. The lateral canthus
region has not been studied for disease-state threshold changes
(Kim 2017 protocol focused on trunk dermatomes). Whether orbital
inflammation (conjunctivitis, keratitis) produces measurable
sensitization at GB1 is unstudied.

Clinically, the lateral orbital area often becomes tender during
migraine attacks — suggesting potential dynamic sensitization in
the Shaoyang headache pattern, but no quantified data exist.
→ Cross-ref: C04 S4

### Cơ chế giải thích (BSA framework attribution)

**Segmental mechanism:** GB1 accesses CN V1/V2 territory.
The ophthalmic division (V1) provides sensory innervation to
the cornea, conjunctiva, lacrimal apparatus, and upper eyelid.
Needling at GB1 stimulates V1/V2 afferents → trigeminal nucleus
caudalis → modulation of trigeminal-autonomic reflexes:
- Lacrimal reflex: V1 afferent → superior salivatory nucleus
  → greater petrosal nerve → pterygopalatine ganglion →
  lacrimal gland secretion. GB1 stimulation could modulate
  tear production (both excess tearing and dry eye).
- Corneal reflex: V1 afferent → brainstem → CN VII efferent
  → orbicularis oculi contraction (blink). GB1 at the lateral
  orbicularis directly overlies this motor endpoint.
- Trigeminal-vascular reflex: V1/V2 stimulation modulates
  meningeal blood flow via the trigeminovascular system —
  the basis for migraine pathophysiology.

**Gap:** Traditional indications include early cataracts, optic
atrophy, and glaucoma — conditions involving intraocular
structures far deeper than needle access at GB1. The segmental
V1 pathway provides a theoretical link (V1 afferent →
parasympathetic modulation of ciliary body, iris), but no
mechanism study has tested whether GB1 needling affects
intraocular pressure, lens transparency, or optic nerve
function. This is a large claim-to-mechanism gap. 🔴

### Kỹ thuật kích thích + dose-response

Châm (B01): 3–5 mm subcutaneous (transverse insertion), directed
laterally along the orbital rim or slightly inferiorly. De Qi:
distension around the eye, sometimes radiating to the temple.
Deep insertion or perpendicular angle CONTRAINDICATED — orbital
penetration risk.

EA: no point-specific frequency data for GB1. In eye disorder
protocols, GB1 is typically included without electrodes (too
close to the eye for safe EA application). Manual needle
stimulation preferred.

Bấm huyệt (B02): gentle circular pressure on the lateral
orbital rim, 2–3 minutes. Standard component of eye exercise
routines (眼保健操) widely practiced in East Asian schools.
→ Cross-ref: B02

Cứu (B08): Jiayi Jing permits 3 moxa cones. Modern practice
avoids direct moxa due to orbital proximity, though there is NO
classical moxa prohibition (禁灸) at GB1 — unlike TE23 which
carries explicit 禁灸. Indirect moxa (bird-pecking technique)
used with caution, maintaining distance from the eye.

### An toàn

**ORBITAL PENETRATION RISK:** The orbital cavity is immediately
medial and posterior to GB1. Needle MUST be directed laterally
(subcutaneous along the orbital rim), NEVER medially or
posteriorly. Orbital penetration → retrobulbar hemorrhage,
optic nerve damage, globe perforation.

Periorbital hematoma: lateral palpebral arteries and
zygomatico-orbital artery branches create hematoma risk. The
periorbital tissue has minimal connective tissue support —
bruising spreads easily. Apply firm pressure post-withdrawal.

No pregnancy contraindication.
No classical moxa prohibition (distinguished from TE23).
Caution with EA near the eye (electrical stimulation of
orbicularis oculi → involuntary blink/spasm).

## GB2 — Thính Hội (听会, Tīng Huì)

**Evidence tier: B (Standard Entry)**
"Auditory Convergence" (听会) — 听 (Tīng, hear/listen) + 会
(Huì, converge/meet/gather). The name declares GB2's role as
where hearing qi gathers — parallel to SI19 "Palace of Hearing"
(hearing's headquarters) and TE21 "Ear Gate" (hearing's entry
point). GB2 is the INFERIOR point in the **Three Pre-auricular
Points** (耳前三穴): TE21 (superior, supratragic), SI19 (middle,
tragus), GB2 (inferior, intertragic). Three separate channels,
three names, ~2 cm vertical distance, all accessing the SAME
nerve (auriculotemporal, CN V3).

GB2 completes the documentation of this triad from all three
channel perspectives: SI19 (AP-06 Obs. 34), TE21 (AP-10), and
now GB2. The functional differentiation in TCM theory —
SI19 = "central, most comprehensive," TE21 = "opens the ear
orifice," GB2 = "converges auditory qi" — maps to a vertical
sequence around the tragus with no clear anatomical basis for
the claimed functional distinctions.

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): anterior to the intertragic
notch, in the depression at the posterior border of the
condyloid process of the mandible, formed when the mouth is
opened.

Anatomical landmarks: with the mouth OPEN, palpate below the
tragus at the intertragic notch (the notch between the tragus
and antitragus). The mandibular condyle translates anteriorly
when the mouth opens, creating the palpable depression. GB2 is
in this depression, inferior to SI19 (~0.5 cun below, at
intertragic level vs tragus level).

Spatial relationships:
- SI19 (Tinggong) is directly superior (~0.5 cun), at tragus
  level
- TE21 (Ermen) is further superior (~1 cun above SI19), at
  supratragic notch
- GB1 (Tongziliao) is anterior and superior (~4 cm), at the
  lateral canthus — the largest gap between consecutive GB
  head points
- GB3 (Shangguan) is superior, on the zygomatic arch

Needle depth: 10–15 mm perpendicular, with mouth OPEN (same
requirement as SI19 and TE21 — condylar translation creates the
treatment space). De Qi: fullness or aching radiating into the
ear canal.

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Auriculotemporal nerve (CN V3):** PRIMARY nerve at GB2 —
  the SAME primary nerve as SI19 and TE21. Sensory to the
  tragus, anterior ear, external auditory canal, outer surface
  of the tympanic membrane, and TMJ capsule. At GB2's level
  (intertragic notch), the auriculotemporal nerve is slightly
  more inferior than at SI19 (tragus) and TE21 (supratragic),
  but it is the same nerve trunk.
- **Great auricular nerve (C2-C3):** sensory to the ear lobule
  and lower auricle. MORE prominent at GB2 than at SI19 or
  TE21 — GB2 is closest to the lobule of the three pre-
  auricular points, placing it in the overlap zone between V3
  and C2-C3 territories.
- **Facial nerve (CN VII):** main trunk exits the stylomastoid
  foramen posterior to the ear. At GB2's level, the mandibular
  branch (marginal mandibular) and cervical branch pass
  inferiorly. Risk is lower here than at TE17 (direct CN VII
  trunk access).
- Spinal segments: CN V3 (primary) + C2-C3 (great auricular
  contribution)
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1

**Neural Zone 2 (pre-auricular):** GB2 is the first point in
Zone 2 — CN V3 (auriculotemporal) territory. The transition
from Zone 1 (GB1, CN V1/V2 orbital) to Zone 2 represents a
trigeminal DIVISION change: V1/V2 → V3. This is the first
neural zone boundary in the GB channel and it occurs between
two consecutive points (GB1→GB2) separated by ~4 cm.

Zone boundary comparison (orbital → pre-auricular):
- TE channel: Zone 7 to Zone 8 transition at TE20 was V3
  throughout — no division change.
- GB channel: Zone 1 to Zone 2 transition at GB1→GB2 crosses
  V1/V2 → V3 — a division change within the first 2 points.

Fascial plane: parotid fascia (tail of parotid gland) → TMJ
capsule (at depth). Same tissue plane as SI19 and TE21.
→ Cross-ref: A03 S3.4

Vascular:
- **Superficial temporal artery:** ascending anterior to the
  ear. Palpable at the root of the zygomatic arch. At GB2's
  level, the artery is ~1 cm anterior. Palpate before needling.
- Superficial temporal vein: accompanies the artery.
- Transverse facial artery: branch of the superficial temporal,
  running anteriorly along the zygomatic arch. More superior
  than GB2.
- Deep: maxillary artery in the infratemporal fossa.

Muscle layers (superficial → deep):
- Skin → subcutaneous tissue
- Parotid gland (tail or accessory lobe, variable)
- TMJ capsule + mandibular condyle (mouth closed) or articular
  disc space (mouth open)

### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

No Five Shu, Yuan-Source, Luo-Connecting, Xi-Cleft, Back-Shu,
Front-Mu, Eight Confluent, or Hui-Meeting designation.
→ Cross-ref: B01 S3.1

**Meeting point (交会穴):** some classical sources list GB2 as
a meeting point of Foot Shaoyang (GB) and Hand Shaoyang (TE).
The Jiayi Jing specifically notes this convergence.

**Three Pre-auricular Points (耳前三穴):**
GB2 is the INFERIOR point in the standard auditory grouping.
Documentation from all three channel perspectives is now
complete:

| Point | Channel | Position | AP File | Landmark |
|-------|---------|----------|---------|----------|
| TE21 | Hand Shaoyang | Superior | AP-10 | Supratragic notch |
| SI19 | Hand Taiyang | Middle | AP-06 | Tragus level |
| **GB2** | **Foot Shaoyang** | **Inferior** | **AP-11** | **Intertragic notch** |

**Observation 2 — Three Pre-auricular Points: Complete Triad
Documentation:**

All three pre-auricular auditory points are now documented from
their respective channel perspectives. The key unresolved
question from AP-10 (Obs. 12, "three names for one neural
target?") can now be refined:

The three points access the SAME nerve (auriculotemporal, V3)
at slightly different vertical positions (~0.5 cun apart). The
anatomical distinction is minimal. However, GB2 adds one
variable that SI19 and TE21 lack: the great auricular nerve
(C2-C3) contribution is more prominent at the inferior
(intertragic) position, closer to the ear lobule. If any
functional differentiation exists, it may emerge from this
C2-C3 overlap at GB2 — mixing trigeminal (ear canal, tympanic
membrane) with cervical plexus (external ear, lobule) afferents.

The three names for three nerves hypothesis:
- TE21: pure V3 auriculotemporal (superior, toward temporal
  region)
- SI19: V3 auriculotemporal (middle, toward ear canal)
- **GB2: V3 + C2-C3 overlap (inferior, toward lobule +
  external ear)**

Whether this C2-C3 enrichment at GB2 produces measurably
different clinical effects is untested. 🟡

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM acupoint:** GB2 Tinghui (听会). Functions: benefits
the ears (利耳), opens ear orifices (通耳窍), dispels Wind,
activates the channel. Primary indications: tinnitus, deafness
(all types), otitis media, ear discharge, toothache (lower jaw),
TMJ dysfunction, facial paralysis.

**2. Trigger point:** lateral pterygoid TrP — referred pain to
the TMJ and pre-auricular area. Same TrP correspondence as SI19
and TE21. Masseter TrP1 refers to the ear region. Moderate
correspondence. 🟡
→ Cross-ref: B11

**3. Ashi correspondence:** tender in TMJ dysfunction, acute
tinnitus, pre-auricular lymphadenitis. Same Ashi profile as
SI19 and TE21 — the three pre-auricular points share
indistinguishable Ashi findings. 🟡

**4. Marma point:** no specific pre-auricular Marma. Vidhura
Marma is behind the ear (corresponding more closely to TE17).
Same negative finding as at TE21. 🔴
→ Cross-ref: C04

**5. Head zone:** CN V3 (mandibular) dermatome —
auriculotemporal territory. Same segmental correspondence as
SI19 and TE21. V3 supplies the ear canal and tympanic membrane;
GB2 is in V3 territory treating ear conditions. Direct
dermatomal logic.
→ Cross-ref: A07 S2.2

**6. Anatomy Trains:** no significant fascial line involvement
at the pre-auricular face. Same as SI19 and TE21.

**7. Neurogenic inflammatory spot:** no data for the pre-
auricular region.

**8. Cupping point:** NOT applicable — pre-auricular face.

**Convergence score: 3/8** (TCM, TrP, Ashi). Same score as SI19
and TE21 — the three pre-auricular points have identical cross-
tradition profiles. The pre-auricular area is a universal ear
treatment site but lacks distinctive non-TCM correspondence
beyond trigger points and ashi.

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Nguồn cổ:
- Ling Shu Ch.1 (Ben Shu): second point of Foot Shaoyang.
- Jiayi Jing: deafness (耳聋), tinnitus (耳鸣), tooth pain
  (齿痛, especially lower jaw), lockjaw/TMJ (牙关拘急),
  deviation of the mouth (口歪), facial edema.
  Moxibustion: 3 cones.
- Zhenjiu Dacheng: deafness, tinnitus, ear discharge (耳中脓),
  toothache, headache, deviation of the mouth.
- Su Wen Ch.17 (Meridian Pathways): notes the GB channel passing
  "in front of the ear" (耳前) — GB2 is the anatomical marker
  for this trajectory description.

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- Ear: deafness, tinnitus, otitis media, otitis externa
- TMJ: temporomandibular joint disorders, trismus
- Dental: toothache (especially lower molar region)
- Facial: facial paralysis, trigeminal neuralgia (V3)
- All indications overlap heavily with SI19 and TE21 — the
  three pre-auricular points are clinically near-interchangeable.

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

🔴 Unvalidated for all indications. No RCT or systematic review
isolating GB2. Used in combination with SI19 and TE21 for
tinnitus and hearing loss protocols, but GB2's individual
contribution has not been tested.

A systematic review of acupuncture for TMJ disorders (La Touche
et al. 2010) includes protocols using pre-auricular points but
does not isolate any single point. The three pre-auricular
points are used interchangeably or in combination.

Same evidence gap as SI19 and TE21 — the three pre-auricular
points share an identical evidence desert. 🔴

### Dynamic acupoint properties

No specific sensitization data for GB2. The pre-auricular area
has not been studied for disease-state threshold changes.
Clinically, the region becomes tender during acute otitis and
TMJ flare-ups, suggesting potential dynamic behavior, but no
quantified data exist (same gap as SI19 and TE21).
→ Cross-ref: C04 S4

### Cơ chế giải thích (BSA framework attribution)

**Segmental mechanism:** identical to SI19 and TE21 —
auriculotemporal nerve (V3) stimulation provides direct access
to: (1) the external auditory canal and tympanic membrane
(sensory V3), (2) the TMJ capsule (mechanoreceptors and
nociceptors via V3), and (3) the parotid gland secretomotor
pathway (parasympathetic fibers traveling with the
auriculotemporal nerve from the otic ganglion).

Needling at GB2 stimulates V3 afferents → trigeminal nucleus
caudalis → modulation of: auricular pain perception, TMJ
proprioception, and potentially middle ear muscle tension (tensor
tympani via V3 motor branch — the "acoustic reflex" modulation
hypothesis, 🟡).

**Gap:** the three pre-auricular points (SI19, TE21, GB2) share
the same nerve, same tissue depth, same treatment targets. If
mechanism is identical, the TCM claim of functional
differentiation between them has no mechanistic support. The
question "three names for one neural target?" (AP-10 Obs. 12)
remains unanswered from a mechanism perspective. The C2-C3
enrichment at GB2 (Observation 2) is the only anatomical
candidate for differentiation. 🟡

### Kỹ thuật kích thích + dose-response

Châm (B01): 10–15 mm perpendicular, with mouth OPEN. Same
technique as SI19 and TE21. De Qi: fullness or aching radiating
into the ear canal. Some patients report transient tinnitus
alteration during needling — consistent with auriculotemporal
nerve stimulation affecting tympanic membrane tension.

EA: 2 Hz low-frequency stimulation used in tinnitus protocols
(typically SI19-GB2 or SI19-TE21 electrode pair). No GB2-
specific frequency data. Han's frequency model: 2 Hz →
enkephalin, potentially relevant for chronic ear pain.
→ Cross-ref: B01 S5.2

Bấm huyệt (B02): firm pressure anterior to the intertragic
notch with mouth open. Self-acupressure for TMJ tension and
earache — accessible for patient self-care.
→ Cross-ref: B02

Cứu (B08): Jiayi Jing permits 3 cones. Modern practice:
indirect moxa with caution (ear canal proximity). Direct moxa
not recommended near the ear.

### An toàn

MODERATE risk. TMJ capsule at depth — avoid excessive depth
(>20 mm) to prevent TMJ capsule penetration. Superficial
temporal artery palpable ~1 cm anterior — palpate before
needling.

Facial nerve (CN VII): mandibular branch passes inferior to
GB2. Risk of nerve stimulation with deep or inferiorly-directed
needling — can cause transient facial muscle twitch. No
permanent injury risk at standard depths.

Parotid gland: variable extension of parotid tail tissue at
GB2's level. Needling through parotid tissue is not dangerous
but may produce discomfort.

No pregnancy contraindication.

---

## GB3 — Thượng Quan (上关, Shàng Guān)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable)**
"Upper Gate" (上关) — 上 (Shàng, upper) + 关 (Guān, gate/pass).
The "gate" is the zygomatic arch (关骨, guāngǔ), also called
the "cheekbone gate." GB3 sits on the UPPER border of this
gate. Its counterpart is **ST7 (Xiaguan 下关, "Lower Gate")**
on the lower border of the same arch. Together, GB3 and ST7
bracket the zygomatic arch from above and below — an upper/lower
gate pair spanning TWO CHANNELS (GB and ST). This cross-channel
gate pairing is named for the same anatomical structure but
approached from opposite sides.

Meeting of Foot Shaoyang (GB), Foot Yangming (ST), and Hand
Shaoyang (TE). Three channels converge at the zygomatic arch
region — a TMJ-oriented meeting that continues the dense
multi-channel convergence pattern of the GB head trajectory.

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): in front of the ear, in the
depression above the zygomatic arch, at the upper border of the
zygomatic arch, on the anterior border of the temporalis muscle.

Anatomical landmarks: palpate the zygomatic arch. GB3 is on the
UPPER border, directly above SI19 (and the TMJ). ST7 (Xiaguan)
is on the lower border of the same arch, in the depression
formed when the mouth is opened. GB3 is ~0.5 cun superior to
ST7, separated by the thickness of the zygomatic arch.

Spatial relationships:
- ST7 (Xiaguan) — directly inferior, on the lower zygomatic
  arch border. The upper/lower gate pair.
- GB2 (Tinghui) — inferior and slightly posterior, at the
  intertragic notch.
- GB4 (Hanyan) — superior and anterior, within the hairline.
- SI19 — at the same level but more posterior (in front of
  tragus).

Needle depth: 5–10 mm perpendicular or oblique. The zygomatic
arch provides a bony floor. Needling is INTO the temporalis
muscle, against the temporal fossa bone.

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Zygomaticotemporal nerve (CN V2):** passes through or near
  the zygomatic arch, providing sensory innervation to the
  temporal region above the arch.
- **Auriculotemporal nerve (CN V3):** courses posterior to the
  superficial temporal artery and anterior to the ear. At GB3's
  level, the auriculotemporal nerve is slightly posterior.
- **Deep temporal nerves (CN V3, motor):** branches of the
  mandibular division supplying the temporalis muscle. GB3 sits
  on the anterior border of temporalis, where the anterior deep
  temporal nerve enters the muscle.
- **Temporal branch of facial nerve (CN VII):** crosses over the
  zygomatic arch superficially — at risk during needling.
- Spinal segments: CN V2 (sensory) + CN V3 (sensory and motor)
  + CN VII (motor)
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1

**Neural zone:** GB3 occupies a transitional position between
Zone 1 (V1/V2 orbital at GB1) and Zone 2 (V3 auriculotemporal
at GB2). The zygomatic arch is the boundary between V2 and V3
dermatomes — GB3 sits precisely on this boundary, receiving both
V2 (from above) and V3 (from below). This V2/V3 overlap zone is
a consistent finding at zygomatic-level points across multiple
channels (ST7, SI18).

Vascular: superficial temporal artery (crosses the zygomatic arch
~1 cm posterior to GB3), middle temporal artery (branch of
superficial temporal, penetrates the temporalis fascia at the
arch level). Zygomatico-orbital artery.

Muscle layers (superficial → deep):
- Skin → subcutaneous tissue
- Temporalis fascia (superficial layer)
- Temporalis muscle (anterior fibers)
- Periosteum of the temporal fossa / zygomatic arch

### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

No Five Shu, Yuan-Source, Luo, Xi-Cleft, Eight Confluent, or
Hui-Meeting designation.

Meeting point (交会穴): GB, ST, TE. The zygomatic arch is a
crossroads where all three channels' trajectories converge.
→ Cross-ref: B01 S3.1

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM acupoint:** GB3 Shangguan (上关). Functions: dispels
Wind, benefits the ears, opens the jaw, alleviates pain. Primary
for TMJ disorders (lockjaw, jaw pain), ear conditions (tinnitus,
deafness), facial paralysis.

**2. Trigger point:** temporalis anterior TrP — referred pain to
the temple and upper teeth. The temporalis muscle's anterior
portion directly underlies GB3. Strong spatial correspondence.
🟡
→ Cross-ref: B11

**3. Ashi correspondence:** tender in TMJ dysfunction, temporal
headache, bruxism-related pain. The zygomatic arch is a common
palpation site for jaw-related pain. 🟡

**4. Marma point:** no zygomatic-arch Marma. Shankha Marma is at
the temple (more posterosuperior). Partial correspondence at
best. 🔴

**5. Head zone:** CN V2/V3 overlap zone at the zygomatic arch.
Direct dermatomal correspondence for TMJ (V3) and upper dental
(V2) conditions.
→ Cross-ref: A07 S2.2

**6. Anatomy Trains:** temporalis fascia connects to the Deep
Front Line via the lateral pterygoid. Weak correspondence.

**7. Neurogenic spot:** no data.

**8. Cupping:** not applicable — face.

**Convergence score: 3/8** (TCM, TrP, Ashi).

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Nguồn cổ:
- Jiayi Jing: deviation of the mouth, lockjaw (牙关拘急),
  deafness, tinnitus, toothache (upper teeth), headache.
- Su Wen Ch.3 (Sheng Qi Tong Tian Lun): "If needling Shangguan,
  the mouth cannot be opened" (刺上关不能欠) — the ONLY
  acupoint with a specific classical needling-consequence
  warning about jaw movement. This likely describes the effect
  of stimulating the temporalis motor nerve (deep temporal, V3):
  muscle contraction → jaw closure.

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- TMJ: temporomandibular disorders, trismus, lockjaw
- Ear: tinnitus, deafness
- Dental: upper toothache
- Facial: facial paralysis, trigeminal neuralgia

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

🔴 Unvalidated. No RCT or SR isolating GB3. Used in TMJ and
facial paralysis protocols in combination with ST7 (the lower
gate), LI4, and ST6. The GB3-ST7 upper/lower gate combination
is a standard clinical pairing but untested as an isolated pair.

### An toàn

LOW-MODERATE risk. Temporal branch of CN VII crosses the
zygomatic arch superficially — deep or aggressive needling may
stimulate this motor nerve, causing transient frontalis or
orbicularis oculi twitch. Superficial temporal artery ~1 cm
posterior — palpate before needling.

No pneumothorax risk. No pregnancy contraindication.

---

## GB4 — Hàm Yểm (颔厌, Hàn Yàn)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable)**
"Jaw Serenity" (颔厌) — 颔 (Hàn, jaw/chin) + 厌 (Yàn,
satiated/suppressed — also read as "press down"). Interpreted
as the point that suppresses or calms jaw-related disorders.
Located within the hairline in the temporal region. Meeting
point of GB, ST, and TE.

GB4 is the first of FOUR EVENLY-SPACED temporal points (GB4,
GB5, GB6, GB7) positioned along a curved line connecting ST8
(Touwei, corner of the forehead) and GB7 (Qubin, above the ear
apex). GB4 sits at the **1/4 mark** from ST8.

**Observation 3 — Temporal Arc: Geometric Subdivision, Not
Independent Landmarks:**

GB4, GB5, and GB6 are defined by their fractional position on
the ST8-to-GB7 curve (1/4, 1/2, 3/4 respectively). They are
the ONLY consecutive acupoints in the Atlas whose locations are
determined by geometric subdivision of a line between two
independently-defined anchor points, rather than by individual
anatomical landmarks (tendons, bony prominences, muscle borders).

This raises a question: were GB4-GB6 individually discovered as
clinically significant sites, or were they derived by geometric
interpolation to fill the temporal region between ST8 and GB7?
The temporal fossa is anatomically homogeneous (temporalis muscle
under temporal fascia, with uniform V3 innervation), offering
no obvious anatomical basis for distinguishing three
equidistant points.

Parallels: the abdominal SP points (SP13-SP16) showed similar
segmental homogeneity but were spaced by SPINAL SEGMENTS
(T8-T12), not geometric fractions. The temporal arc points
(GB4-GB6) lack even segmental differentiation — they are on the
same cranial bone, same muscle, same nerve territory. 🟡

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): within the hairline, at the
junction of the upper 1/4 and lower 3/4 of the curved line
connecting ST8 and GB7.

Needle depth: 5–15 mm transverse (subcutaneous), directed
posteriorly along the scalp. Transverse insertion is standard
for all temporal scalp points.

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Deep temporal nerves (CN V3, motor):** branches innervating
  the temporalis muscle. The anterior and posterior deep temporal
  nerves enter the muscle from its deep surface.
- **Auriculotemporal nerve (CN V3, sensory):** terminal branches
  over the temporal region.
- **Zygomaticotemporal nerve (CN V2, sensory):** contributes to
  the anterior-inferior temporal region (diminishing posteriorly).
- Spinal segments: CN V3 (primary) + CN V2 (minor anterior)
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1

Vascular: superficial temporal artery (parietal branch ascending
through the temporal region), middle temporal artery (deep to
temporalis fascia).

Muscle layers: skin → subcutaneous tissue → temporalis fascia
(superficial and deep layers with intervening fat pad) →
temporalis muscle → periosteum of the temporal bone.

### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

No special category. Meeting of GB, ST, TE.
→ Cross-ref: B01 S3.1

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM acupoint:** GB4 Hanyan (颔厌). Functions: dispels Wind,
alleviates pain. Indications: lateral headache, migraine,
dizziness, tinnitus, toothache.

**2. Trigger point:** temporalis TrP — referred pain from the
temporalis muscle includes the temple, forehead, and upper
teeth. Moderate correspondence. 🟡

**3. Ashi:** tender in temporal headache and migraine. 🟡

**4. Marma:** Shankha Marma (temple region) — general
correspondence to the temporal area. 🟡

**5. Head zone:** CN V3 dermatome. Consistent with headache and
jaw-related indications.

**6-8.** No significant Anatomy Trains, neurogenic spot, or
cupping correspondence.

**Convergence score: 3/8** (TCM, TrP/Ashi, Marma — general
temporal).

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Nguồn cổ:
- Jiayi Jing: lateral headache (偏头痛), dizziness (头眩),
  tinnitus, epilepsy-like symptoms (惊痫).
- Deadman/WHO: migraine, temporal headache, dizziness, tinnitus,
  toothache.

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

🔴 Unvalidated. No point-specific study. Used as a component
of temporal headache protocols. Indistinguishable from GB5 and
GB6 in clinical application.

### An toàn

LOW risk. Scalp point — subcutaneous needling over the temporal
bone. Superficial temporal artery branches in the area; bruising
possible. No deep structures at risk with transverse insertion.
No pregnancy contraindication.

---

## GB5 — Huyền Lô (悬颅, Xuán Lú)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable)**
"Suspended Skull" (悬颅) — 悬 (Xuán, suspended/hanging) + 颅
(Lú, skull/cranium). Evokes the temporal bone region as the
"hanging" part of the skull — the temporal fossa is the thinnest
area of the cranial vault. Located at the **midpoint** of the
ST8-to-GB7 curve. Meeting of GB, ST, BL, and TE (some sources
omit TE or BL — channel meeting lists vary for GB5-GB6).

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): within the hairline, at the
midpoint of the curved line connecting ST8 and GB7.

Needle depth: 5–15 mm transverse (subcutaneous), directed
posteriorly or anteriorly along the scalp.

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Auriculotemporal nerve (CN V3, sensory):** terminal branches
  in the temporal region. More posterior distribution than GB4.
- **Deep temporal nerves (CN V3, motor):** temporalis muscle
  innervation.
- Spinal segments: CN V3 (primary)
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1

Vascular: superficial temporal artery (parietal branch). Middle
temporal artery.

Muscle layers: skin → subcutaneous tissue → temporalis fascia →
temporalis muscle → periosteum of the temporal bone (pterion
region — thinnest cranial vault area).

### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

No special category. Meeting of GB, ST, BL (± TE).
→ Cross-ref: B01 S3.1

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM acupoint:** GB5 Xuanlu (悬颅). Functions: dispels Wind,
clears Heat, alleviates pain. Indications: lateral headache,
migraine, eye pain, facial edema.

**2. Trigger point:** temporalis TrP — same correspondence as
GB4. 🟡

**3. Ashi:** tender in temporal headache. 🟡

**4. Marma:** Shankha Marma (general temple). 🟡

**5. Head zone:** CN V3 dermatome.

**6-8.** No significant correspondence.

**Convergence score: 3/8** (TCM, TrP/Ashi, Marma).

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Nguồn cổ:
- Jiayi Jing: lateral headache, dizziness (头眩), eye redness
  and pain, rhinorrhea, facial edema.
- Deadman/WHO: migraine, temporal headache, toothache, facial
  swelling.

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

🔴 Unvalidated. No point-specific study. Same evidence desert as
all temporal arc points.

### An toàn

LOW risk. Scalp point with transverse insertion. Note: GB5 is
near the **pterion** — the thinnest region of the skull where
frontal, parietal, temporal, and sphenoid bones meet. The middle
meningeal artery runs on the internal surface of the skull at
this level. PERPENDICULAR deep needling is contraindicated due
to the risk of skull penetration and epidural hematoma at the
pterion. Transverse (subcutaneous) insertion avoids this risk
entirely.

No pregnancy contraindication.

---

## GB6 — Huyền Ly (悬厘, Xuán Lí)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable)**
"Suspended Tuft" (悬厘) — 悬 (Xuán, suspended) + 厘 (Lí, a
fine hair/thread, also: regulate). Located at the **3/4 mark**
from ST8 on the ST8-to-GB7 curve. Meeting of GB, ST, BL
(± TE). The third geometric subdivision point on the temporal
arc (see Observation 3 at GB4).

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): within the hairline, at the
junction of the lower 1/4 and upper 3/4 of the curved line
connecting ST8 and GB7.

Needle depth: 5–15 mm transverse (subcutaneous).

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Auriculotemporal nerve (CN V3):** more prominent here than
  at GB4-GB5 (closer to the nerve's ascending course anterior
  to the ear).
- **Deep temporal nerves (CN V3, motor):** temporalis.
- Spinal segments: CN V3
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1

Vascular: superficial temporal artery branches. Middle temporal
artery.

Muscle layers: same as GB4-GB5 — skin → temporalis fascia →
temporalis muscle → temporal bone periosteum.

### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

No special category. Meeting of GB, ST, BL (± TE).
→ Cross-ref: B01 S3.1

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM acupoint:** GB6 Xuanli (悬厘). Functions: dispels Wind,
clears Heat. Indications: lateral headache, eye pain, tinnitus.

**2. Trigger point:** temporalis TrP. 🟡

**3. Ashi:** temporal headache. 🟡

**4. Marma:** Shankha Marma (general temple). 🟡

**5. Head zone:** CN V3.

**6-8.** No significant correspondence.

**Convergence score: 3/8** (TCM, TrP/Ashi, Marma).

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Nguồn cổ:
- Jiayi Jing: lateral headache, facial heat, tinnitus, eye pain
  (目锐眦赤痛 — lateral canthus redness and pain, same
  territory as GB1 but accessed from the temple).
- Deadman/WHO: migraine, temporal headache, facial swelling.

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

🔴 Unvalidated. No point-specific study.

### An toàn

LOW risk. Same safety profile as GB4-GB5. Near the pterion but
slightly more posterior — same precaution against perpendicular
deep needling applies. Transverse insertion is safe.

No pregnancy contraindication.

---

## GB7 — Khúc Tấn (曲鬓, Qū Bìn)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable)**
"Curved Temple-Hair" (曲鬓) — 曲 (Qū, curved/bent) + 鬓 (Bìn,
temple hair/sideburn). Named for the point's location where
the hairline curves above the ear. GB7 is the ANCHOR POINT that
terminates the ST8-to-GB7 temporal arc — an independently-
defined landmark (ear apex + hairline intersection) that anchors
the geometric positions of GB4, GB5, and GB6. Meeting of Foot
Shaoyang (GB) and Foot Taiyang (BL).

GB7 marks a trajectory inflection: the GB channel arrives from
the temporal arc (GB4-GB7, anterior-to-posterior along the
curved hairline) and transitions to the post-auricular path
(GB8-GB12, curving behind and below the ear). This is the
second major directional change in the GB head trajectory
(after the GB1→GB2 periorbital-to-periauricular drop).

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): within the hairline, at the
crossing point of the vertical line from the ear apex with the
anterior border of the hairline above the ear.

Anatomical landmarks: find the apex (highest point) of the ear.
Draw a vertical line upward from the ear apex. GB7 is where
this line intersects the temporal hairline, directly above the
ear apex. GB8 (Shuaigu) is 1.5 cun directly superior to the
ear apex — posterior-superior to GB7.

Needle depth: 5–15 mm transverse (subcutaneous).

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Auriculotemporal nerve (CN V3):** dominant sensory nerve.
  GB7 is near the nerve's ascending course, placing it squarely
  in V3 auriculotemporal territory.
- **Lesser occipital nerve (C2-C3):** begins to contribute at
  this posterior-temporal position. GB7 is near the boundary
  where auriculotemporal (V3) territory transitions to cervical
  plexus (C2-C3) territory behind the ear.
- **Temporal branch of facial nerve (CN VII):** motor to the
  auricularis superior and anterior muscles, frontalis.
- Spinal segments: CN V3 (primary) + C2-C3 (emerging)
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1

**Neural zone transition note:** GB7 is the last point in Zone
2 (CN V3 auriculotemporal territory). Starting at GB8 or GB9
(posterior to the ear), the lesser occipital and greater
auricular nerves (C2-C3) become dominant, marking the transition
to Zone 3 (cervical plexus territory). The V3→C2-C3 transition
at the posterior ear mirrors the same boundary seen in the TE
channel at TE19→TE16 (but TE approached from below the ear, GB
from above).

Vascular: superficial temporal artery (parietal branch),
posterior auricular artery (ascending behind the ear — begins
to appear at this level).

Muscle layers: skin → subcutaneous tissue → temporalis fascia →
temporalis muscle (posterior fibers) → temporal bone periosteum.

### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

No special category. Meeting of GB and BL (Foot Taiyang).

The GB-BL meeting pattern begins here (GB7) and continues
through GB8, GB9, GB10, GB11, GB12 — a long sequence of GB-BL
meeting points around the ear and occiput. The Foot Taiyang (BL)
and Foot Shaoyang (GB) channels run parallel across the head's
lateral-posterior region, and their meeting points reflect this
shared trajectory.
→ Cross-ref: B01 S3.1

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM acupoint:** GB7 Qubin (曲鬓). Functions: dispels Wind,
alleviates pain, benefits the jaw. Indications: lateral
headache, temporal pain, lockjaw (牙车急痛), jaw pain, neck
stiffness.

**2. Trigger point:** temporalis posterior TrP — referred pain
to the temple and behind the ear. GB7 overlies the posterior
fibers of temporalis. 🟡

**3. Ashi:** tender in temporal headache, post-auricular tension.
🟡

**4. Marma:** Shankha Marma (temple). The posterior extent of
Shankha Marma reaches this area. 🟡

**5. Head zone:** CN V3 → C2-C3 transition zone.

**6-8.** No significant correspondence.

**Convergence score: 3/8** (TCM, TrP/Ashi, Marma).

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Nguồn cổ:
- Jiayi Jing: jaw pain and lockjaw (颔痛, 口噤不能言 — cannot
  speak from jaw rigidity), swelling of neck and cheek, lateral
  headache.
- Deadman/WHO: temporal headache, TMJ disorders, trismus,
  swelling of cheek.

GB7's jaw-related indications are more prominent than GB4-GB6,
despite being FURTHER from the TMJ. This may reflect the
posterior temporalis muscle fibers' role in jaw
retraction/closure — the posterior fibers (underlying GB7) are
the primary retractors, while the anterior fibers (underlying
GB4) are elevators. Different functions of the same muscle,
accessed at different points along the temporal arc.

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

🔴 Unvalidated. No point-specific study. Used in temporal
headache and TMJ protocols.

### An toàn

LOW risk. Scalp point, transverse insertion. Post-auricular
artery branches in the area. Less pterion risk than GB5 (more
posterior, temporal bone thicker here).

No pregnancy contraindication.

---

## GB8 — Suất Cốc (率谷, Shuài Gǔ)

**Evidence tier: B (Standard Entry)**
"Leading Valley" (率谷) — 率 (Shuài, lead/command) + 谷 (Gǔ,
valley/depression). Named for the temporal depression above
the ear that "leads" (guides) toward the occiput. Located 1.5
cun directly above the ear apex, within the hairline. Meeting
of Foot Shaoyang (GB) and Foot Taiyang (BL).

GB8 is the **LOCAL ANCHOR** of the most quantified acupuncture
prescription in migraine literature: **GB20–GB8–TE5** (support
15.65%, confidence 90.48% — He et al. 2025, 911 trials). In
this triad, GB20 is the suboccipital hub, TE5 is the distal
forearm point, and **GB8 is the temporal-local point** — sitting
directly over the temporal region where migraine pain
concentrates. The three points span the full Shaoyang migraine
architecture: local (GB8, temporal), regional (GB20, occipital),
and distal (TE5, forearm + Yang Wei Mai).

GB8 is also the traditional point for alcohol-related headache
(酒后头痛) — the only acupoint with a specific alcohol-headache
indication in classical texts. The temporal location
(superficial temporal artery dilation is a hallmark of alcohol-
induced vascular headache) provides anatomical coherence.

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): superior to the ear apex,
1.5 cun directly above, within the hairline.

Anatomical landmarks: find the ear apex (highest point of the
auricle). Measure 1.5 cun (~3 cm) directly upward into the
temporal scalp. GB8 is within the hairline over the temporal-
parietal junction. Palpable as a shallow depression or tender
spot in migraine-prone individuals.

Spatial relationships:
- GB7 (Qubin) is anteroinferior — at the hairline directly
  above the ear apex. GB8 is 1.5 cun above where GB7 contacts
  the hairline.
- TE20 (Jiaosun) is at the ear apex level — GB8 is directly
  above TE20. The TE channel (TE20) and GB channel (GB8) stack
  vertically at the ear apex line, ~1.5 cun apart.
- GB9 (Tianchong) is posteroinferior — 0.5 cun posterior to
  GB8, behind the ear root.
- GV20 (Baihui) is on the vertex — GB8 is approximately 4 cun
  lateral and inferior.

Needle depth: 5–15 mm transverse (subcutaneous), directed
posteriorly or anteriorly along the scalp. Same technique as
all temporal scalp points.

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Auriculotemporal nerve (CN V3):** terminal ascending
  branches in the temporal scalp. At GB8's level (1.5 cun above
  the ear), the auriculotemporal nerve's territory is at its
  uppermost extent — transitioning toward the territory of the
  greater and lesser occipital nerves posteriorly.
- **Lesser occipital nerve (C2-C3):** ascending from the
  posterior auricular region. At GB8's posterior edge, C2-C3
  sensory branches begin to contribute. GB8 sits at the
  boundary zone between V3 and C2-C3 territories.
- **Posterior auricular nerve (CN VII branch):** motor to the
  occipitalis and posterior auricular muscles. At GB8's level,
  this branch is ascending from behind the ear.
- Spinal segments: CN V3 (primary) + C2-C3 (emerging) + CN VII
  (minor motor)
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1

**Neural Zone 2→3 transition:** GB8 is at the boundary between
Zone 2 (CN V3 auriculotemporal, dominant through GB2-GB7) and
Zone 3 (C2-C3 occipital nerves, which dominate GB9-GB12). The
ear apex represents the V3/C2-C3 sensory watershed — anterior
= V3, posterior = C2-C3. GB8, sitting 1.5 cun above the ear
apex, straddles this boundary.

This transition is clinically relevant: migraine pain often
radiates from temporal (V3) to occipital (C2-C3) territory
along exactly this pathway. GB8's position at the V3/C2-C3
watershed may explain why it is the preferred LOCAL point for
migraine — it spans both trigeminal and cervical sensory
territories.

Fascial plane: temporoparietal fascia (galea aponeurotica
lateral extension) → temporal fascia → temporalis muscle
(superior fibers thinning toward the parietal bone).
→ Cross-ref: A03 S3.4

Vascular:
- **Superficial temporal artery (parietal branch):** the major
  scalp vessel. Ascending from in front of the ear, the
  parietal branch courses superiorly through the temporal
  scalp. At GB8's level, this artery is palpable in many
  patients. MIGRAINE RELEVANCE: vasodilation of the superficial
  temporal artery is associated with migraine attacks.
  Classical GB8 indications for "alcohol headache" align with
  alcohol-induced superficial temporal artery dilation.
- Posterior auricular artery: ascending behind the ear, with
  branches reaching the posterior aspect of GB8's region.

Muscle layers (superficial → deep):
- Skin → subcutaneous tissue (scalp, richly vascularized)
- Temporoparietal fascia (thin aponeurotic sheet)
- Loose areolar tissue (subaponeurotic space — allows scalp
  mobility)
- Temporal fascia (deep) / pericranium
- Temporal bone (temporal-parietal junction)

### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

No Five Shu, Yuan-Source, Luo, Xi-Cleft, Eight Confluent, or
Hui-Meeting designation.

Meeting point (交会穴): GB + BL (Foot Taiyang). The GB-BL
meeting series continues from GB7 through the periauricular
and occipital region (GB7-GB12 = 6 consecutive GB-BL meetings).
→ Cross-ref: B01 S3.1

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM acupoint:** GB8 Shuaigu (率谷). Functions: dispels
Wind-Damp, alleviates pain, benefits the head. PRIMARY
indications: lateral/temporal headache, migraine, dizziness,
vomiting (especially alcohol-related). The alcohol-headache
specificity is unique among scalp points.

**2. Trigger point:** temporalis superior TrP — referred pain
to the temple, vertex, and behind the ear. The superior fibers
of temporalis underlie GB8 (thinning but present). Also:
occipitofrontalis (occipital belly) — referred pain to the
lateral scalp. Moderate correspondence. 🟡
→ Cross-ref: B11

**3. Ashi correspondence:** strongly tender in migraine patients
(temporal area), alcohol headache, and tension-type headache.
The temporal scalp above the ear is one of the most consistently
tender areas in headache disorders. 🟡

**4. Marma point:** Shankha Marma (शंख, temple) — the superior
extent of Shankha Marma reaches GB8's region. Classified as
Asthi Marma (bone type). Size: 2 angula (~4 cm). Injury to
Shankha can cause loss of consciousness (sanyasa). Moderate
spatial correspondence. 🟡
→ Cross-ref: C04

**5. Head zone:** V3/C2-C3 transition zone. The temporal-
occipital sensory watershed. Migraine pathophysiology involves
both trigeminal (V1/V2/V3) and cervical (C2-C3) afferents
converging at the trigeminocervical complex (TCC) in the
brainstem. GB8's position at this transition has direct
relevance to migraine neuroanatomy.
→ Cross-ref: A07 S2.2

**6. Anatomy Trains:** galea aponeurotica — the epicranial
aponeurosis connecting the occipitalis and frontalis muscles.
GB8 sits on the lateral edge of this fascial sheet. Tension
in the galea (from occipitalis or frontalis contraction)
transmits to GB8's location. Moderate relevance for tension-
type headache. 🟡

**7. Neurogenic spot:** no specific data for the temporal scalp.

**8. Cupping point:** NOT applicable — scalp.

**Convergence score: 4/8** (TCM, TrP, Ashi/Marma, Head Zone).
Higher than GB1-GB7, reflecting GB8's clinical significance
as a migraine point. The Head Zone convergence (V3/C2-C3
transition = TCC afferent territory) is particularly strong.

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Nguồn cổ:
- Ling Shu Ch.21 (Han Re Bing): one of the points for cold-
  induced headache.
- Jiayi Jing: lateral headache (偏头痛), dizziness (头眩),
  vomiting (呕吐), child's acute convulsions.
- Zhenjiu Dacheng: **alcohol headache (酒后头痛)** — the most
  distinctive classical indication. Also: migraine, vomiting
  after drinking, phlegm-damp headache.
- Qianjin Yaofang (Sun Simiao): includes GB8 for Wind-type
  headache with vomiting — the alcohol + vomiting combination
  is consistent across multiple sources.

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- Migraine and temporal headache — PRIMARY modern indication
- Dizziness and vertigo
- Vomiting (especially alcohol-related)
- Infantile convulsions (historically; not modern practice)

Kinh nghiệm lâm sàng:
- GB8 is the LOCAL POINT in the core migraine prescription
  **GB20-GB8-TE5**. While GB20 and TE5 are used for many
  conditions, GB8's selection is specifically migraine-driven.
- The alcohol headache indication is unique to GB8 and
  anatomically coherent: the superficial temporal artery
  (directly underlying GB8) is the vessel most affected by
  alcohol-induced vasodilation.
- Standard Shaoyang headache protocol: TE5+GB41 (Eight
  Confluent pair for Yang Wei/Dai Mai) + GB20+GB8 (local),
  ± Taiyang EX-HN5.
→ Cross-ref: AP-10 TE5 (migraine evidence)

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

**Migraine — 🟡 Plausible (strong indirect)**

GB8 is a component of the most-quantified migraine prescription
in acupuncture literature:

- **He et al. 2025** (Journal of Pain Research): data mining of
  911 clinical trials, 1,610 prescriptions, 181 points. Core
  association rule: **GB20-GB8-TE5** (support 15.65%, confidence
  90.48%). GB8 is the LOCAL component — the point that localizes
  the prescription to the temporal region.
- **Zhao et al. 2017** (JAMA Internal Medicine): 249 patients,
  3-arm RCT. For Shaoyang-type headache, **GB8 was included in
  the obligatory point set** alongside GB20. The highest-quality
  migraine acupuncture RCT (JAMA-level) explicitly includes
  GB8.
- **Linde et al. 2016** (Cochrane): 22 RCTs. Acupuncture ≥
  prophylactic drugs. GB8 listed among commonly used temporal
  points across protocols.

Rating: 🟡 Plausible — GB8 is consistently included in the
strongest migraine evidence but never isolated. Its contribution
cannot be separated from the multi-point protocols. However,
the 90.48% confidence in the GB20-GB8-TE5 association rule
across 911 trials provides the strongest INDIRECT evidence of
any temporal scalp point.

**Observation 4 — Migraine Core Triad Architecture:**

The GB20-GB8-TE5 migraine prescription spans three anatomical
tiers and two Shaoyang channels:

| Point | Channel | Level | Neural Access | Role |
|-------|---------|-------|---------------|------|
| GB20 | Foot Shaoyang | Occipital | C2-C3 + CN XI | Regional hub |
| **GB8** | **Foot Shaoyang** | **Temporal** | **V3 + C2-C3** | **Local target** |
| TE5 | Hand Shaoyang | Forearm | Radial (C5-C8) | Distal modulator |

The triad targets both afferent inputs to the trigeminocervical
complex (TCC): GB8 covers trigeminal (V3) and GB20 covers
cervical (C2-C3). TE5 adds systemic descending modulation via
insular cortex activation (AP-10 TE5 neuroimaging data). This
is an architecturally complete migraine intervention — local,
regional, and systemic — achieved with only 3 points from 2
channels of the same Shaoyang family.

Whether this architecture was designed by traditional
practitioners with proto-neuroanatomical understanding or
emerged from empirical optimization over centuries is an open
question. The convergence of traditional selection and modern
neuroanatomy is striking either way. 🟡

### Dynamic acupoint properties

No formal sensitization study (Kim 2017 protocol). However,
clinical observation strongly suggests dynamic behavior at GB8:
- Migraine patients frequently report spontaneous tenderness
  at the temporal scalp (GB8 area) during attacks
- The tenderness resolves between attacks
- This pattern (disease-state sensitization → resolution) is
  the hallmark of dynamic acupoint behavior (C04 S4)

Whether GB8 specifically (vs the general temporal scalp)
sensitizes is not quantified. The superficial temporal artery
dilation during migraine may contribute to local tenderness
through perivascular inflammation — a vascular mechanism
distinct from the neurogenic inflammation described at trunk
acupoints. 🟡
→ Cross-ref: C04 S4

### Cơ chế giải thích (BSA framework attribution)

**Migraine mechanism (LOCAL-SEGMENTAL):**
GB8 accesses the V3/C2-C3 sensory watershed. Both trigeminal
(V1-V3) and upper cervical (C1-C3) afferents converge at the
**trigeminocervical complex (TCC)** in the brainstem — the
primary relay for migraine nociception. GB8 stimulation at the
V3/C2-C3 boundary directly engages both afferent arms of the
TCC:

```
GB8 needling → V3 afferent (auriculotemporal)
            → C2-C3 afferent (lesser occipital)
            → BOTH converge at TCC (trigeminal nucleus caudalis
              extending to C3 dorsal horn)
            → modulation of trigeminovascular nociception
```

This DUAL-AFFERENT access is the most parsimonious explanation
for why GB8 outperforms other temporal scalp points in migraine
protocols — it is the one point that bridges both trigeminal
and cervical migraine pathways.

**Alcohol headache mechanism (LOCAL-VASCULAR):**
Alcohol → vasodilation of the superficial temporal artery
(directly underlying GB8) → perivascular distension →
trigeminal nociceptor activation → headache. GB8 needling may
modulate local vascular tone through axon reflex (CGRP release
modulation) and sympathetic vasoconstriction. The mechanism is
directly local — no segmental or supraspinal pathway required.

**Gap:** no neuroimaging study specific to GB8 stimulation. The
TCC engagement hypothesis is anatomically sound but not
confirmed by functional imaging. Whether GB8 activates the TCC
differently from other temporal points (GB4-GB7) is unstudied.
🟡

### Kỹ thuật kích thích + dose-response

Châm (B01): 5–15 mm transverse (subcutaneous), directed
posteriorly toward GB9 or anteriorly toward GB7. De Qi:
distension or heaviness radiating across the temporal scalp.
In migraine patients, De Qi may be more intense due to local
sensitization.

EA: commonly used in migraine protocols. Typical electrode
pairing: GB8-GB20 (bridging temporal and occipital regions) or
GB8-Taiyang EX-HN5 (spanning the temporal headache zone).
Frequency: 2 Hz or 2/100 Hz alternating (dense-disperse).
→ Cross-ref: B01 S5.2

Bấm huyệt (B02): circular pressure above the ear apex, 1.5
cun into the temporal scalp. Self-massage for headache relief —
widely practiced. Accessible for patient self-care.

Cứu (B08): indirect moxa applied for Wind-Cold headache
(traditional). Less common in modern migraine treatment where
EA is preferred.

### An toàn

LOW risk. Scalp point over the temporal-parietal junction.
Temporal bone is reasonably thick at this level (thicker than
at GB5/pterion). Transverse insertion avoids any deep structure
risk.

Superficial temporal artery (parietal branch): directly
underlying GB8. Minor hematoma risk. Palpate before needling
if the artery is prominent.

No pregnancy contraindication.

---

## GB9 — Thiên Xung (天冲, Tiān Chōng)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable)**
"Heavenly Rushing" (天冲) — 天 (Tiān, heaven/celestial) + 冲
(Chōng, rush/surge). The 天 prefix indicates a superior/head
position (consistent with the "Heavenly" naming convention for
head points: TE16 天牖, TE15 天髎, SI16 天窗, etc.). Located
posterior to the ear root, 2 cun above the hairline (or 0.5 cun
posterior to GB8). Meeting of Foot Shaoyang (GB) and Foot
Taiyang (BL).

GB9 is the first point clearly BEHIND the ear — the trajectory
transitions from the temporal arc (GB4-GB8, above and in front
of the ear) to the post-auricular descent (GB9-GB12, behind and
below the ear toward the mastoid). GB9 serves as the UPPER
ANCHOR for a second geometric arc: GB10 and GB11 are defined as
fractional positions on the curved line from GB9 to GB12.

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): posterior to the ear, 0.5
cun posterior to GB8, within the hairline.

Anatomical landmarks: from GB8 (1.5 cun above ear apex), move
0.5 cun (~1 cm) posteriorly. GB9 is behind the root of the
ear, at the posterior border of the auricular attachment. The
temporal-parietal bone underlies this point.

Spatial relationships:
- GB8 (Shuaigu) is 0.5 cun anterior — above the ear apex
- GB10 (Fubai) is posteroinferior — on the curve toward the
  mastoid
- TE20 (Jiaosun) is inferior — at the ear apex level

Needle depth: 5–15 mm transverse (subcutaneous).

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Lesser occipital nerve (C2-C3):** ascending posterior to the
  ear. NOW the primary sensory nerve — marking the transition
  from V3 (dominant at GB8) to C2-C3 territory.
- **Greater auricular nerve (C2-C3):** terminal branches to the
  posterior ear and mastoid region.
- **Posterior auricular nerve (CN VII):** motor branch ascending
  behind the ear to the occipitalis and posterior auricular
  muscles.
- Spinal segments: C2-C3 (primary) + CN V3 (diminishing) +
  CN VII (motor)
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1

**Neural Zone 3 (post-auricular/occipital):** GB9 enters Zone
3 — C2-C3 cervical plexus territory (lesser occipital + greater
auricular nerves). The transition from Zone 2 (V3) occurred at
GB8 (where V3 and C2-C3 overlapped). From GB9 onward through
GB12, C2-C3 dominates.

Vascular: posterior auricular artery (branch of external carotid,
ascending behind the ear). Occipital artery branches beginning
to appear.

Muscle layers: skin → subcutaneous tissue → occipitalis muscle
(lateral fibers beginning) → periosteum of temporal/parietal
bone.

### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

No special category. Meeting of GB and BL.
→ Cross-ref: B01 S3.1

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM acupoint:** GB9 Tianchong (天冲). Functions: calms the
spirit (安神), dispels Wind, benefits the ears. Indications:
fright, palpitations, epilepsy-like conditions, headache,
gingival swelling.

**2. Trigger point:** occipitalis TrP — referred pain to the
lateral-posterior scalp. GB9 overlies the lateral border of the
occipitalis muscle. 🟡

**3. Ashi:** tender in post-auricular headache, mastoid
tenderness. 🟡

**4. Marma:** no specific Marma at the post-auricular scalp.
Krikatika (base of skull) is more inferior. 🔴

**5. Head zone:** C2-C3 dermatome. Post-auricular territory.

**6-8.** No significant correspondence.

**Convergence score: 2/8** (TCM, TrP/Ashi).

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Nguồn cổ:
- Jiayi Jing: fright and palpitations (惊恐善惊), epilepsy
  (痫), headache, gingival swelling and pain (牙龈肿痛).
- Deadman/WHO: headache, epilepsy, gingival swelling, anxiety.

The SPIRIT indications (fright, palpitations, epilepsy) are
notable for a scalp point. The 天 prefix and "rushing" imagery
may reflect classical association with sudden upward movement
of pathological Qi (逆气上冲).

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

🔴 Unvalidated. No point-specific study.

### An toàn

LOW risk. Scalp point, transverse insertion. Posterior auricular
artery in the vicinity — minor hematoma risk.
No pregnancy contraindication.

---

## GB10 — Phù Bạch (浮白, Fú Bái)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable)**
"Floating White" (浮白) — 浮 (Fú, float/superficial) + 白
(Bái, white). Possibly refers to the whitish appearance of the
mastoid region or the superficial (floating) position on the
temporal bone. Located posterior and superior to the mastoid
process. Meeting of Foot Shaoyang (GB) and Foot Taiyang (BL).

GB10 is the first GEOMETRIC INTERPOLATION point on the second
post-auricular arc — defined by its fractional position on the
curved line from GB9 (upper anchor, behind ear root) to GB12
(lower anchor, behind mastoid tip).

**Observation 5 — Second Geometric Arc (Post-auricular):**

GB10 and GB11 are defined by their position on the GB9-to-GB12
curve, exactly as GB4-GB5-GB6 were defined on the ST8-to-GB7
curve (Observation 3). The GB head trajectory contains TWO
geometric arcs:

| Arc | Anchors | Interpolated Points | Region |
|-----|---------|-------------------|--------|
| 1 (Temporal) | ST8 → GB7 | GB4 (1/4), GB5 (1/2), GB6 (3/4) | Above ear |
| **2 (Post-auricular)** | **GB9 → GB12** | **GB10, GB11** | **Behind ear** |

Both arcs fill anatomically homogeneous regions (temporal fossa
and post-auricular scalp respectively) with geometrically
spaced points between independently-defined endpoints. The GB
channel's 20 head points may contain 5 geometrically derived
points (GB4-GB6, GB10-GB11) out of 20 — a 25% interpolation
rate. Whether this reflects geometric convenience in textbook
standardization (post-classical systematization) or original
point discovery methodology is an open question. 🟡

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): posterior and superior to
the mastoid process, at the junction of the upper 1/3 and
middle 1/3 of the curved line connecting GB9 and GB12.

Needle depth: 5–15 mm transverse (subcutaneous).

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Lesser occipital nerve (C2-C3):** primary sensory nerve.
  Ascending posterior to the ear, distributing to the scalp
  behind the ear and above the mastoid.
- **Greater auricular nerve (C2-C3):** terminal branches to the
  mastoid region.
- Spinal segments: C2-C3
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1

Vascular: posterior auricular artery, occipital artery (branches
appearing at this level).

Muscle layers: skin → subcutaneous tissue → occipitalis muscle
(or galea) → periosteum of temporal bone (mastoid region).

### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

No special category. Meeting of GB and BL.
→ Cross-ref: B01 S3.1

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM acupoint:** GB10 Fubai (浮白). Functions: dispels Wind,
resolves Phlegm, benefits the ears. Indications: headache,
tinnitus, deafness, toothache, goiter/scrofula, leg weakness.

**2. Trigger point:** occipitalis TrP or SCM posterior TrP —
referred pain to the post-auricular and mastoid area. 🟡

**3. Ashi:** tender in mastoid headache, post-auricular
lymphadenopathy. 🟡

**4. Marma:** no specific post-auricular Marma. 🔴

**5. Head zone:** C2-C3 dermatome.

**6-8.** No significant correspondence.

**Convergence score: 2/8** (TCM, TrP/Ashi).

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Nguồn cổ:
- Jiayi Jing: tinnitus, deafness (耳鸣耳聋), toothache,
  throat obstruction, goiter/scrofula (瘿气), leg weakness
  (足不能行).
- Deadman/WHO: tinnitus, deafness, headache, toothache, goiter.

The leg weakness indication is unexpected for a head point. The
BL channel meeting at GB10 provides a theoretical (if tenuous)
link to BL's lower extremity trajectory. 🟡

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

🔴 Unvalidated. No point-specific study.

### An toàn

LOW risk. Scalp point over temporal/mastoid bone. Transverse
insertion. Posterior auricular artery branches — minor hematoma.
No pregnancy contraindication.

---

## GB11 — Đầu Khiếu Âm (头窍阴, Tóu Qiào Yīn)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable)**
"Head's Portal of Yin" (头窍阴) — 头 (Tóu, head) + 窍阴
(Qiàoyīn, portal/orifice of Yin). The 头 prefix distinguishes
this point from its foot counterpart **GB44 (足窍阴, Zú Qiào
Yīn, "Foot's Portal of Yin")**. Both share the 窍阴 name —
one of only a few name-sharing pairs where HEAD and FOOT
variants exist on the SAME channel.

The 窍阴 naming: 窍 (Qiào) = orifice/portal, 阴 (Yīn) =
Yin. Interpreted as the Yin aspect of sensory orifices — both
GB11 (near the ear, a Yin-associated sensory organ in Five
Element theory) and GB44 (the distal Well point, Metal=Ke
Controller of Wood). Meeting of Foot Shaoyang (GB) and Foot
Taiyang (BL).

GB11 is the second geometric interpolation point on the post-
auricular arc (GB9→GB12), positioned at the junction of the
middle 1/3 and lower 1/3 of the curve. Near GB12 (behind the
mastoid tip).

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): posterior and inferior to
GB10, at the junction of the middle 1/3 and lower 1/3 of the
curved line connecting GB9 and GB12.

Anatomical landmarks: between GB10 (posterosuperior to mastoid)
and GB12 (behind mastoid tip). The mastoid process is the
primary palpable landmark.

Needle depth: 5–15 mm transverse (subcutaneous).

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Greater auricular nerve (C2-C3):** primary sensory nerve at
  this mastoid level. Ascending over the SCM to the mastoid and
  ear lobule.
- **Lesser occipital nerve (C2-C3):** posterior branches.
- **Greater occipital nerve (C2, dorsal ramus):** beginning to
  appear at this more medial-posterior position.
- Spinal segments: C2-C3
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1

Vascular: posterior auricular artery, mastoid emissary vein
(connects the sigmoid sinus intracranially to the posterior
auricular venous system — deep, at the mastoid foramen).

Muscle layers: skin → subcutaneous tissue → insertions of SCM
(mastoid attachment) and splenius capitis → periosteum of the
mastoid portion of the temporal bone.

### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

No special category. Meeting of GB and BL.
→ Cross-ref: B01 S3.1

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM acupoint:** GB11 Touqiaoyin (头窍阴). Functions:
dispels Wind, clears Heat, benefits the head and ears.
Indications: headache, dizziness, tinnitus, deafness, stiff
neck, eye pain.

**2. Trigger point:** SCM mastoid attachment TrP — deep
tenderness at the mastoid, referred pain to the ear and
temporal area. Moderate correspondence. 🟡

**3. Ashi:** tender in mastoiditis, post-auricular headache. 🟡

**4. Marma:** Vidhura Marma (behind the ear) — partial overlap.
Vidhura is more specifically behind the ear lobule (TE17 area),
but GB11's proximity places it in the extended Vidhura zone. 🟡

**5. Head zone:** C2-C3 dermatome.

**6-8.** No significant correspondence.

**Convergence score: 3/8** (TCM, TrP/Ashi, Marma partial).

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Nguồn cổ:
- Jiayi Jing: headache, dizziness (头晕), tinnitus, deafness,
  lateral headache, tongue root stiffness (舌本出血 — also
  tongue bleeding).
- Deadman/WHO: headache, tinnitus, deafness, pain of the outer
  canthus (connecting back to the GB1 eye territory).

The eye pain indication at a post-auricular point mirrors GB1's
ear-related context at an orbital point — the GB channel
cross-references its starting organ (eye) even at points far
from the orbit.

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

🔴 Unvalidated. No point-specific study.

### An toàn

LOW risk. Scalp point over the mastoid process (thick cortical
bone). Transverse insertion.

Mastoid emissary vein: passes through the mastoid foramen at
depth. Transverse subcutaneous technique avoids any vascular
complication. Deep perpendicular needling at the mastoid
foramen (rare variant position) could theoretically access
the emissary venous system — but standard technique makes this
a negligible risk.

No pregnancy contraindication.

---

## GB12 — Hoàn Cốt (完骨, Wán Gǔ)

**Evidence tier: B (Standard Entry)**
"Mastoid Bone" (完骨) — 完 (Wán, complete/whole) + 骨 (Gǔ,
bone). In classical Chinese anatomy, 完骨 IS the name for the
mastoid process — the prominent bony projection behind and
below the ear. GB12 is located in the depression directly
behind the mastoid process, making it a purely anatomical name:
the point IS the bone it is named after. This is the most
direct bone-naming in the Atlas (compared to GB5 "Suspended
Skull" which metaphorically references the temporal bone).

GB12 sits at the inferior terminus of the 6-point GB-BL meeting
sequence (GB7-GB12) and the lower anchor of the second post-
auricular arc (GB9→GB12). From GB12, the channel makes a major
directional shift — it reverses course, ascending FORWARD to
GB13 (forehead), then climbing to the vertex area (GB14-GB19),
and descending to GB20 (suboccipital fossa). The GB head
trajectory thus forms a figure-eight or pretzel-like path: eye
(GB1) → ear (GB2-GB3) → temporal arc (GB4-GB7) → post-auricular
descent (GB8-GB12) → REVERSAL → forehead (GB13-GB14) →
vertex (GB15-GB19) → occiput (GB20).

**Wind pathway significance:** GB12 is a Wind-treatment point
in the suboccipital/mastoid cluster alongside **GB20 (Fengchi,
"Wind Pool")** and **TE17 (Yifeng, "Wind Screen")**. These
three points form a triangle behind the ear: TE17 (behind the
earlobe), GB12 (behind the mastoid tip), and GB20 (between SCM
and trapezius at the occiput). All share C2-C3 innervation and
all treat Wind conditions (facial paralysis, headache, stiff
neck, dizziness).

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): in the depression
posteroinferior to the mastoid process.

Anatomical landmarks: palpate the mastoid process (the bony
prominence behind and below the ear). GB12 is in the depression
BEHIND the mastoid tip, at its posteroinferior aspect. The
depression is bounded by the mastoid (anterior), the superior
nuchal line (superior), and the trapezius/SCM insertions.

Spatial relationships:
- TE17 (Yifeng) is anterosuperior — behind the earlobe, between
  mastoid and mandibular angle. ~2-3 cm from GB12.
- GB11 (Touqiaoyin) is superior — on the post-auricular arc.
- GB20 (Fengchi) is medial and slightly inferior — in the
  suboccipital fossa between SCM and trapezius. ~3-4 cm from
  GB12.
- BL10 (Tianzhu) is medial — on the lateral border of the
  trapezius at the occiput.
- GB13 (Benshen) is the NEXT GB point but located on the
  FOREHEAD — a dramatic trajectory reversal of ~15 cm.

Needle depth: 10–15 mm oblique, directed toward the opposite
mastoid or slightly inferiorly. Perpendicular insertion is safe
(mastoid bone provides a solid floor) but oblique allows
greater needle manipulation.

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Greater occipital nerve (C2, dorsal ramus):** the major
  sensory nerve of the posterior scalp. At GB12's level, the
  greater occipital nerve is ascending from its exit between
  the atlas (C1) and axis (C2) through the semispinalis and
  trapezius. GB12 is at the lateral extent of the greater
  occipital nerve's territory — the nerve's main trunk is more
  medial (closer to GB20), but lateral branches reach the
  mastoid region.
- **Lesser occipital nerve (C2-C3, ventral rami):** ascending
  along the posterior border of SCM. Provides sensory supply to
  the lateral occipital scalp and mastoid region. At GB12, this
