  nerve is more superficial and more laterally positioned than
  the greater occipital.
- **Greater auricular nerve (C2-C3):** branches to the mastoid
  process and posterior ear. The mastoid branch of the greater
  auricular nerve directly supplies GB12's location.
- **Third occipital nerve (C3, dorsal ramus):** deep, primarily
  more medial, but may contribute cutaneous branches to the
  lateral occiput.
- Spinal segments: C2-C3 (primary, from three different nerves:
  greater occipital, lesser occipital, greater auricular)
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1

**Neural Zone 3 (post-auricular/occipital) confirmed:** GB12 is
the terminal point of Zone 3. From GB9 through GB12, C2-C3
cervical plexus nerves dominate. The next point (GB13) jumps to
the FOREHEAD — an entirely different neural territory (CN V1,
supraorbital). This creates the largest neural zone gap in the
GB channel (and possibly the Atlas): C2-C3 at GB12 → CN V1 at
GB13, with NO intermediate points bridging the C4-T1 segments
that the neck and shoulder would represent.

GB channel neural zone map through Batch 2:

| Zone | Points | Primary Nerve | Spinal/Cranial |
|------|--------|---------------|----------------|
| 1 | GB1 | Lacrimal (V1) + Zygomatic (V2) | CN V1/V2 |
| 2 | GB2-GB8 | Auriculotemporal (V3) | CN V3 |
| 3 | GB9-GB12 | Lesser/greater occipital + greater auricular | C2-C3 |
| 4 | GB13-? | Supraorbital (V1) — forehead return | CN V1 (predicted) |

The C2-C3 → CN V1 jump between GB12 and GB13 parallels the
channel's trajectory reversal (occiput → forehead). The 20
head points of GB visit V1/V2 (orbital), V3 (temporal), C2-C3
(occipital), and V1 again (forehead) — a CIRCULAR neural
traversal of the cranial sensory fields.

Fascial plane: SCM tendon (mastoid insertion) + splenius capitis
insertion → posterior nuchal fascia. The SCM and splenius share
a mastoid attachment, creating a fascial convergence at GB12.
→ Cross-ref: A03 S3.4

Vascular:
- **Occipital artery:** the major vessel of the posterior scalp.
  At GB12's level, the occipital artery courses through the
  triangle between SCM and trapezius — more medial than GB12
  but with branches reaching the mastoid region.
- Posterior auricular artery: terminal branches at the mastoid.
- Mastoid emissary vein: deep, at the mastoid foramen
  (connecting the sigmoid sinus to the external venous system).

Muscle layers (superficial → deep):
- Skin → subcutaneous tissue
- SCM insertion (mastoid attachment — tendinous)
- Splenius capitis insertion (mastoid, deep to SCM)
- Longissimus capitis (variable attachment to mastoid)
- Periosteum of the mastoid process

### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

No Five Shu, Yuan-Source, Luo, Xi-Cleft, Eight Confluent, or
Hui-Meeting designation.

Meeting point (交会穴): GB + BL. The LAST of 6 consecutive
GB-BL meetings (GB7-GB12). From GB13 onward, the meeting
pattern changes to GB + Yang Wei Mai (GB13-GB20).
→ Cross-ref: B01 S3.1

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM acupoint:** GB12 Wangu (完骨). Functions: dispels
Wind, benefits the head, clears Heat. Indications: headache
(occipital/lateral), stiff neck, facial paralysis, toothache,
throat swelling, insomnia, epilepsy-like conditions.

**2. Trigger point:** SCM insertion TrP — the SCM attaches to
the mastoid process at GB12's location. SCM TrP2 (sternal
head, mastoid attachment) refers pain to the forehead, temple,
and behind the ear. STRONG spatial and functional
correspondence — the mastoid is the primary SCM TrP2
treatment site. 🟡
→ Cross-ref: B11

**3. Ashi correspondence:** tender in cervicogenic headache
(mastoid attachment tension), stiff neck, torticollis. The
mastoid attachment of SCM is palpably tender in most cases of
cervicogenic headache — GB12 is likely the most reliable Ashi
point for this condition in the suboccipital region. 🟡

**4. Marma point:** Krikatika Marma (कृकाटिका) — located at
the junction of head and neck, at the base of the skull.
Classified as Sandhi Marma (joint type). Size: 1/2 angula.
GB12 is at the lateral extent of Krikatika's territory. Partial
correspondence — Krikatika is more medial (closer to GB20/BL10
area). 🟡
→ Cross-ref: C04

**5. Head zone:** C2-C3 dermatome. The greater occipital nerve
(C2) territory covers the posterior scalp. Direct segmental
correspondence for occipital headache and stiff neck (C2-C3
spinal segment dysfunction).
→ Cross-ref: A07 S2.2

**6. Anatomy Trains:** SCM attachment = terminus of the
Superficial Front Line (SFL). The SFL runs from the pubic
symphysis up through the rectus abdominis and sternum to the
SCM, inserting on the mastoid. GB12 is directly AT this
attachment. ALSO: the splenius capitis (deep to SCM) connects
to the Superficial Back Line (SBL) through the erector spinae
group. GB12 sits at the convergence of SFL (via SCM) and SBL
(via splenius) terminal attachments. 🟡
→ Cross-ref: A03 (Anatomy Trains)

**7. Neurogenic inflammatory spot:** no specific data for the
mastoid region.

**8. Cupping point:** cupping behind the ear/mastoid is
occasionally practiced in traditional Chinese and Middle
Eastern cupping for headache and ear conditions. Limited
correspondence. 🟡

**Convergence score: 5/8** (TCM, TrP, Ashi, Marma partial,
Anatomy Trains). The HIGHEST convergence in Batch 1-2. The SCM
mastoid insertion provides strong TrP correspondence, and the
SFL/SBL fascial line terminus adds Anatomy Trains convergence
rarely seen in head points.

**Observation 6 — Suboccipital Wind Triangle:**

GB12, TE17, and GB20 form a triangle of Wind-treatment points
behind the ear and at the occiput:

| Point | Location | Distance | Neural Access |
|-------|----------|----------|---------------|
| TE17 | Behind earlobe | — | CN VII trunk + C2-C3 |
| **GB12** | **Behind mastoid** | **~2-3 cm from TE17** | **C2-C3 (3 nerves)** |
| GB20 | Suboccipital fossa | ~3-4 cm from GB12 | C2-C3 + CN XI |

All three share C2-C3 innervation but access different
structures: TE17 = CN VII skull exit, GB12 = SCM/splenius
insertions, GB20 = suboccipital muscles and vertebral artery
territory. The "Wind" designation in TCM (facial paralysis,
headache, dizziness, stiff neck) maps to the C2-C3 spinal
segments — the cervical segments where the upper cervical
afferents converge with trigeminal input at the TCC.

This triangle may represent a pragmatic clinical clustering:
three points around the mastoid/occiput that together access
the complete C2-C3 cervical plexus territory — the "Wind zone"
is the C2-C3 zone. Whether TCM's concept of "Wind" functionally
maps to C2-C3 cervical plexus pathology (stiff neck, headache,
facial nerve dysfunction) deserves formal analysis in Phase 4
(Mechanism). 🟡

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Nguồn cổ:
- Jiayi Jing: headache (头痛), stiff neck (项强), facial
  paralysis (口歪), toothache (齿痛), throat swelling and
  pain (咽喉肿痛), mad laughter (癫疾), insomnia (不寐).
- Su Wen: mentioned in context of Wind-Cold invasion at the
  nape and mastoid region.
- Zhenjiu Dacheng: headache (lateral and occipital), facial
  deviation, behind-ear swelling, vomiting.

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- Headache: occipital, lateral, cervicogenic
- Neck: stiff neck, torticollis
- Facial: facial paralysis (combined with TE17, ST4, ST6)
- Ear: tinnitus, deafness (posterior approach)
- Other: insomnia, epilepsy, toothache

Kinh nghiệm lâm sàng:
- Combined with **GB20 and TE17** for Wind-Cold invasion
  behind the ear (acute retro-auricular pain, occipital
  headache, stiff neck).
- In facial paralysis protocols, GB12 is selected less
  frequently than TE17 (which has direct CN VII access) but
  provides complementary C2-C3 cervical afferent stimulation.
- For cervicogenic headache, GB12 + GB20 is a standard local
  pair — GB12 at the SCM insertion, GB20 between SCM and
  trapezius. Together they bracket the SCM from opposite ends.
→ Cross-ref: AP-10 TE17 (facial paralysis protocols)

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

🔴 Unvalidated for all indications. No RCT or SR isolating
GB12. Used as a component of multi-point protocols for:
- Cervicogenic headache (with GB20, BL10, GV20)
- Facial paralysis (with TE17, ST4, ST6, LI4)
- Insomnia (with GV20, HT7, SP6 — some protocols include GB12
  for its calming/spirit indications)

The SCM-insertion anatomy provides strong mechanistic logic
for cervicogenic headache treatment, but no study has tested
GB12 individually or compared GB12 with direct SCM trigger
point release.

### Dynamic acupoint properties

No formal sensitization study. Clinically, the mastoid
region (GB12 area) becomes tender in cervicogenic headache,
acute torticollis, and Bell's palsy prodrome (retro-auricular
pain). The SCM insertion is one of the most reliably tender
spots in cervical dysfunction — this likely represents SCM
trigger point sensitization at its mastoid attachment rather
than acupoint-specific dynamic behavior. The distinction
between "GB12 sensitizes" and "SCM TrP at the mastoid
activates" is not clinically separable without imaging or
electrophysiology.
→ Cross-ref: C04 S4

### Cơ chế giải thích (BSA framework attribution)

**Cervicogenic headache (LOCAL-SEGMENTAL):**
GB12 accesses C2-C3 via three nerves (greater occipital, lesser
occipital, greater auricular). C2-C3 afferents converge at the
trigeminocervical complex (TCC) — the same brainstem relay
accessed by GB8 (from the trigeminal side) and GB20 (from the
cervical side). GB12 provides a LATERAL approach to C2-C3,
complementing GB20's more medial access.

SCM-specific: the mastoid insertion of SCM is a primary
generator of cervicogenic headache. Tension/shortening of SCM
→ mastoid periosteal strain → C2-C3 nociceptive input → TCC →
referred headache (temporal, frontal, orbital). GB12 needling
may release SCM tension (local twitch response) and modulate
C2-C3 nociception simultaneously.

**Wind conditions — C2-C3 hypothesis:**
The TCM "Wind" pathology cluster (facial paralysis, headache,
stiff neck, dizziness) maps remarkably well to C2-C3 cervical
segment dysfunction:
- Facial paralysis: CN VII exits at the mastoid (GB12/TE17
  territory), and C2-C3 nerves provide sensory innervation to
  the area
- Headache: C2-C3 → TCC → referred head pain
- Stiff neck: C2-C3 motor innervation of deep cervical muscles
- Dizziness: C2-C3 proprioceptive input to vestibular nuclei
  (cervicogenic dizziness)

**Gap:** no mechanism study specific to GB12. The C2-C3
hypothesis for "Wind" is architecturally compelling but not
tested at this specific point. Comparison with GB20 mechanism
(which has neuroimaging data) would clarify whether GB12 and
GB20 activate the same or different brainstem targets.

### Kỹ thuật kích thích + dose-response

Châm (B01): 10–15 mm oblique, directed toward the contralateral
mastoid or slightly inferiorly. De Qi: deep aching behind the
ear, may radiate to the occiput or temporal region. Perpendicular
insertion is safe (mastoid bone provides floor) but less
effective for De Qi propagation.

EA: occasionally paired with GB20 (GB12-GB20 electrode pair)
for cervicogenic headache or Wind-Cold protocols. Frequency:
2 Hz for chronic conditions, 2/100 Hz alternating for acute
headache.
→ Cross-ref: B01 S5.2

Bấm huyệt (B02): deep thumb pressure behind the mastoid
process. Self-massage for occipital headache — press and hold
for 30-60 seconds. Accessible for patient self-care.

Cứu (B08): indirect moxa applied for Wind-Cold invasion
(traditional). Moxa at the mastoid combined with GB20 and
BL10 is a classical Wind-Cold treatment.

### An toàn

LOW-MODERATE risk. The mastoid process provides excellent
bony protection — no deep structure penetration with standard
technique.

Specific considerations:
- **Occipital artery:** branches in the region. Minor hematoma
  risk. Palpate before needling.
- **Mastoid emissary vein:** at depth, connecting the sigmoid
  sinus to the external venous system through the mastoid
  foramen. Standard transverse/oblique technique avoids this
  deep structure entirely. Only a concern with anomalous deep
  perpendicular insertion at the mastoid foramen.
- **SCM tendon:** needling through the SCM insertion is not
  dangerous but may produce a strong local twitch response.

No pregnancy contraindication.

---

## GB13 — Bổn Thần (本神, Běn Shén)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry)**
"Root of Spirit" (本神) — 本 (Běn, root/origin) + 神 (Shén,
spirit/mind). One of several acupoints referencing 神 (Shen)
in its name — alongside GV24 Shenting (神庭, "Spirit Court"),
HT7 Shenmen (神门, "Spirit Gate"), and BL44 Shentang (神堂,
"Spirit Hall"). The 本 (root/origin) prefix implies GB13 is
the ROOT or FOUNDATION of spirit activity, reflecting the
Gallbladder's classical role in decision-making and courage
(决断出焉 — Su Wen Ch.8).

GB13 marks the START of the Yang Wei Mai meeting sequence on
the GB head — from GB13 through GB20, all 8 consecutive GB
points meet Yang Wei Mai. This is the longest continuous
extraordinary vessel meeting sequence on any regular channel
in the Atlas so far.

GB13 also marks a dramatic **trajectory reversal:** the channel
jumps ~15 cm from GB12 (posterior mastoid, C2-C3 territory) to
GB13 (forehead, 0.5 cun within the anterior hairline). This is
the largest positional gap between consecutive points in the GB
channel and one of the largest in the Atlas. The neural territory
shifts from C2-C3 cervical plexus (Zone 3) to CN V1 supraorbital
nerve (Zone 4), confirming the predicted fourth neural zone.

**Observation 7 — Trajectory Reversal and Circular Neural
Traversal:**

The GB12→GB13 reversal completes the evidence for a CIRCULAR
neural traversal across the GB head points:

| Zone | Points | Neural Territory | Direction |
|------|--------|-----------------|-----------|
| 1 | GB1 | CN V1 (lacrimal) + V2 (zygomatic) | Orbital start |
| 2 | GB2-GB8 | CN V3 (auriculotemporal) | Temporal descent |
| 3 | GB9-GB12 | C2-C3 (occipital + auricular) | Post-auricular |
| 4 | GB13-? | CN V1 (supraorbital) | Forehead return |

The channel visits V1/V2 → V3 → C2-C3 → V1 — returning to
the trigeminal V1 territory where it began, but via a DIFFERENT
V1 branch (lacrimal at GB1 vs supraorbital at GB13). This
circular traversal covers the ENTIRE sensory innervation of the
lateral and posterior head within a single meridian. No other
channel traverses this breadth of cranial sensory territory.

The trajectory gap (C2-C3 → CN V1, no intermediate points)
skips the entire neck/shoulder neural territory (C4-T1) that
GB will cover much later (GB21 at CN XI, C3-C4). The channel's
numbering prioritizes completing the head circuit before
descending — a topological choice, not an anatomical one.

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): on the forehead, 0.5 cun
directly above the midpoint of the anterior hairline, 3 cun
lateral to Shenting (GV24).

Anatomical landmarks: locate the anterior hairline at its
midpoint (GV24, Shenting). Measure 3 cun laterally — GB13 is
0.5 cun directly above (posterior to) the hairline at this
lateral position. The point lies on the forehead, in the
frontal region of the scalp.

Spatial relationships:
- GV24 (Shenting) is on the midline, same anterior-posterior
  level. GB13 is 3 cun lateral.
- GB14 (Yangbai) is BELOW and slightly lateral — on the
  forehead, 1 cun above the eyebrow midpoint. GB13 is within
  the hairline; GB14 is below it.
- ST8 (Touwei) is more lateral — at the corner of the forehead,
  0.5 cun within the hairline, 4.5 cun lateral to GV24.
- GB12 (Wangu) — PREVIOUS GB point — is behind the mastoid
  process, ~15 cm posterior. The largest gap between consecutive
  GB points.

Needle depth: 5–10 mm transverse (subcutaneous), directed
posteriorly or laterally along the scalp.

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Supraorbital nerve, lateral branch (CN V1):** the primary
  sensory nerve of the forehead and anterior scalp. At GB13's
  position (0.5 cun within hairline, 3 cun lateral), the lateral
  branch of the supraorbital nerve provides cutaneous sensation.
- **Frontal branch of CN VII (temporal branch of facial nerve):**
  motor supply to the frontalis muscle. Courses superficially
  across the temporal region toward the forehead.
- Cranial nerves: CN V1 (sensory), CN VII (motor).
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1

**Neural Zone 4 confirmed:** CN V1 supraorbital territory.
This is a DIFFERENT V1 branch from Zone 1 (GB1, which was
lacrimal + zygomatic). The supraorbital nerve (frontal branch
of V1) covers the forehead and anterior scalp, while the
lacrimal nerve (lateral V1) covers the lateral orbital region.
Same trigeminal division, different terminal branches.

Vascular: supratrochlear and supraorbital arteries (branches
of the ophthalmic artery via CN V1 territory). Frontal branch
of superficial temporal artery.

Muscle layers: skin → subcutaneous tissue → frontal belly of
occipitofrontalis → loose areolar tissue (subgaleal space) →
periosteum of frontal bone. The galea aponeurotica begins
around the hairline — GB13 is at the transition between the
muscular frontalis and the fibrous galea.

### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

No Five Shu, Yuan-Source, Luo, Xi-Cleft, Eight Confluent, or
Hui-Meeting designation.

Meeting point (交会穴): GB + Yang Wei Mai. The FIRST of 8
consecutive Yang Wei Mai meetings on the GB head (GB13-GB20).
The longest run of any regular channel on a single
extraordinary vessel in the Atlas.
→ Cross-ref: B01 S3.1

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM acupoint:** GB13 Benshen (本神). Functions: pacifies
Wind, resolves Phlegm, calms the Spirit. Indications: epilepsy,
mania, infantile convulsions, hemiplegia, headache, dizziness,
stiff neck.

**2. Trigger point:** frontalis TrP — minor. The frontal belly
of occipitofrontalis can develop TrPs that refer to the
forehead, but these are typically more medial. Weak
correspondence. 🟡

**3. Ashi:** tender in frontal headache, tension-type headache
with frontal predominance. 🟡

**4–8.** No significant correspondence.

**Convergence score: 2/8** (TCM, Ashi weak). Low convergence
— consistent with Tier C assignment.

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Nguồn cổ:
- Jiayi Jing: epilepsy (癫痫), mania (狂), infantile
  convulsions (小儿惊风), stiff neck (项强), chest pain.
- Deadman/WHO: headache, dizziness, epilepsy, infantile
  convulsions, hemiplegia, neck rigidity.

The "spirit" naming (本神) and the preponderance of
neurological/psychiatric indications (epilepsy, mania,
convulsions) are consistent with the Yang Wei Mai's role in
connecting Yang channels and modulating consciousness/arousal.
Several Yang Wei Mai points share spirit/consciousness
indications.

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

🔴 Unvalidated. No point-specific study.

### An toàn

LOW risk. Forehead/anterior scalp point over the frontal bone.
Transverse subcutaneous insertion. No deep structures at risk.

No pregnancy contraindication.

---

## GB14 — Dương Bạch (阳白, Yáng Bái)

**Evidence tier: B (Standard Entry)**
"Yang White" (阳白) — 阳 (Yáng, yang/bright/forehead) + 白
(Bái, white/clear/bright). The "Yang" here refers to the
forehead (the upper, yang aspect of the face), and "White"
to brightness or clarity — the name may reference clear
vision or the bright expanse of the forehead. Some scholars
interpret it as "the bright Yang" of the forehead, connecting
to the point's traditional use for eye disorders and frontal
headache.

GB14 is the **MOST CONNECTED point on the GB forehead** — a
meeting of FIVE channels: GB (Foot Shaoyang), Yang Wei Mai,
LI (Hand Yangming), ST (Foot Yangming), and TE (Hand
Shaoyang). This 5-channel meeting combines both Shaoyang
channels (GB + TE) with both Yangming channels (LI + ST)
plus Yang Wei Mai — the three Yang channel pairs that
traverse the face converge here.

**Observation 8 — Five-Channel Forehead Convergence:**

GB14's meeting composition reveals a systematic pattern:

| Channel at GB14 | Pairing | Face Route |
|-----------------|---------|------------|
| GB (Foot Shaoyang) | ← same-name → | TE (Hand Shaoyang) |
| ST (Foot Yangming) | ← same-name → | LI (Hand Yangming) |
| Yang Wei Mai | — | Linking vessel for all Yang |

Two complete same-name pairs (Shaoyang + Yangming) plus their
Yang linking vessel meet at one point. These are the Yang
channels with the MOST extensive facial trajectories:
- LI: from the nose (LI20) across the philtrum to the
  contralateral infraorbital region
- ST: from below the eye (ST1) down through the jaw and up to
  the temporal hairline (ST8)
- TE: from the post-auricular region (TE17) to the lateral
  eyebrow (TE23)
- GB: from the lateral canthus (GB1) across the entire skull

GB14 sits at a FRONTIER where all four Yang facial trajectories
can be accessed from a single point. The forehead is the only
region where Shaoyang and Yangming trajectories overlap — below
the brow, they diverge (Yangming descends along the cheek and
jaw; Shaoyang wraps laterally across the temple).

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): on the forehead, 1 cun
directly above the midpoint of the eyebrow, directly above
the pupil when looking straight ahead.

Anatomical landmarks: with the patient looking straight ahead,
identify the pupil center. The midpoint of the eyebrow is
directly above the pupil. Measure 1 cun (approximately the
width of the interphalangeal joint of the thumb) directly
upward — GB14 is on the forehead, in the subcutaneous tissue
over the frontal bone.

Spatial relationships:
- Yuyao (EX-HN4) is at the midpoint of the eyebrow, 1 cun
  below GB14. These two points are on the same vertical line
  (pupil line).
- BL2 (Zanzhu) is at the medial end of the eyebrow — medial
  to GB14 by ~2 cun.
- TE23 (Sizhukong) is at the lateral end of the eyebrow —
  lateral to GB14 by ~1.5–2 cun.
- GB15 (Toulinqi) is directly above GB14, 0.5 cun within the
  anterior hairline — approximately 2–2.5 cun superior (the
  exact distance depends on forehead height/hairline position).
- GB13 (Benshen) is more medial and within the hairline —
  3 cun lateral to GV24 vs GB14's position directly above the
  pupil (approximately 2–2.5 cun lateral to midline).

Needle depth: 5–10 mm transverse subcutaneous, directed
superiorly (toward GB15), inferiorly (toward the eyebrow),
or laterally. Shallow insertion — the frontal bone is
immediately beneath the subcutaneous layer.

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Supraorbital nerve (CN V1, frontal division):** the
  principal sensory nerve at this location. The supraorbital
  nerve exits through the supraorbital foramen (or notch) at
  the superior orbital rim, then divides into medial (superficial)
  and lateral (deep) branches. GB14, being 1 cun above the
  eyebrow midpoint, lies directly over the path of the
  supraorbital nerve's ascending branches. This is the most
  DIRECT access to the supraorbital nerve of any acupoint.
- **Frontal branch of CN VII (temporal branch of facial nerve):**
  motor supply to the frontalis muscle. At GB14's level, the
  temporal branch has already entered the frontalis — needling
  at GB14 can directly stimulate the motor nerve to the
  forehead muscles.
- Cranial nerves: CN V1 (sensory), CN VII (motor).
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1

**Neural Zone 4 (forehead, CN V1 supraorbital):** GB14
represents the clearest supraorbital nerve access point among
all GB head points. The supraorbital nerve is a pure sensory
branch of CN V1 — it carries pain, temperature, and touch from
the forehead to the trigeminal ganglion and then to the
trigeminal brainstem complex. Needling at GB14 directly
stimulates V1 afferents ascending to the trigeminal nucleus.

Vascular:
- **Supraorbital artery:** accompanies the supraorbital nerve
  through the supraorbital foramen. At GB14, the artery's
  ascending branches supply the forehead skin.
- **Frontal branch of superficial temporal artery:** lateral
  branches reach GB14's territory.
- Minor bruising risk from the superficial arteries.

Fascial plane: skin → subcutaneous tissue → frontalis muscle
(frontal belly of occipitofrontalis) → loose areolar tissue
(subgaleal space) → periosteum of frontal bone. The frontalis
muscle is thin (~2–3 mm) at this level — transverse insertion
courses entirely within the subcutaneous-muscular layer.
→ Cross-ref: A03 S3.4

Muscle layers:
- Skin (thin forehead skin)
- Subcutaneous tissue with embedded supraorbital neurovascular
  bundle
- Frontalis muscle (frontal belly of occipitofrontalis)
- Subgaleal loose areolar tissue
- Pericranium (periosteum of frontal bone)

### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

No Five Shu, Yuan-Source, Luo, Xi-Cleft, Eight Confluent, or
Hui-Meeting designation.

Meeting point (交会穴): GB + Yang Wei Mai + LI + ST + TE. A
**five-channel meeting point** — the most channels meeting at
any GB head point, and one of the densest meetings in the
entire Atlas among non-GV points.
→ Cross-ref: B01 S3.1

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM acupoint:** GB14 Yangbai (阳白). Functions: dispels
Wind, benefits the eyes, raises Yang Qi to clear the head.
Indications: frontal headache, eye diseases (blurred vision,
lacrimation, eye pain, night blindness), facial paralysis
(deviation of mouth and eye), trigeminal neuralgia (first
branch V1 distribution), twitching of eyelid.

**2. Trigger point:** frontalis TrP — the frontal belly of
occipitofrontalis develops trigger points that refer pain to
the forehead. GB14 is directly over the frontalis muscle body.
Moderate-strong spatial correspondence. 🟡
→ Cross-ref: B11

**3. Ashi correspondence:** tender in frontal headache and
tension-type headache. The supraorbital nerve exit zone (1 cun
below GB14) is a standard palpation site for frontal headache.
GB14's position 1 cun above the exit catches the ascending
branches — tender when the forehead is a pain generator. 🟡

**4. Marma point:** Sthapani Marma (स्थपनी) — the "third eye"
point between the eyebrows. Sthapani is traditionally at the
glabella/mid-brow (closer to Yintang/EX-HN3). GB14 is lateral
and superior to Sthapani. Weak correspondence — different
location. 🔴

**5. Head zone:** CN V1 dermatome (ophthalmic division of
trigeminal). GB14 is squarely in V1 territory — frontal
headache, eye disorders, and facial sensation all map to V1
segmental innervation.
→ Cross-ref: A07 S2.2

**6. Anatomy Trains:** no significant line terminus or crossing
at this forehead location. The occipitofrontalis spans from the
Superficial Back Line (SBL, via the occipital belly) to the
forehead, but GB14 is mid-muscle, not at an attachment. 🔴

**7. Neurogenic inflammatory spot:** no specific data for the
mid-forehead region.

**8. Cupping point:** not applicable — forehead cupping is rare
due to the convex bony surface and cosmetic concerns.

**Convergence score: 3/8** (TCM, TrP, Ashi/Head zone). Moderate
convergence driven by the clear V1 nerve-territory matching.

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Nguồn cổ:
- Jiayi Jing: headache (头痛), eye pain (目痛), lacrimation
  in wind (迎风流泪), blurred vision (目视不明), mouth and eye
  deviation (口眼歪斜 — facial paralysis), eyelid twitching
  (眼睑瞤动).
- Zhenjiu Dacheng: frontal headache, eyes unable to close or
  open (ptosis or spasm), dimness of vision, night blindness.
- Deadman/WHO: frontal headache, pain of the orbital ridge
  (supraorbital neuralgia), blurred vision, lacrimation,
  twitching of eyelid, facial paralysis, night blindness.

Kinh nghiệm lâm sàng:
- **Facial paralysis:** GB14 is a KEY LOCAL point for the
  forehead component of Bell's palsy (inability to raise the
  eyebrow, wrinkle the forehead). The frontalis muscle is
  innervated by the temporal branch of CN VII — the branch
  most vulnerable in Bell's palsy because it has the LEAST
  redundant innervation among CN VII branches.
  Combined with: ST4+ST6 (mouth), TE17 (CN VII trunk), LI4
  (distal), GB20 (Wind-dispelling).
  → Cross-ref: AP-10 TE17 (facial paralysis protocols)
- **Frontal headache:** primary local point for V1 distribution
  headache. Combined with Yintang (EX-HN3), BL2, and GV20.
- **Eye disorders:** lacrimation, blurred vision, ptosis —
  traditional eye trio: GB14 + BL2 + TE23 (three points around
  the orbit from three different channels).

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

🔴 Unvalidated for all point-specific indications. No RCT or
SR isolating GB14.

GB14 is COMMONLY INCLUDED in acupuncture protocols for Bell's
palsy/facial paralysis — it appears in the majority of facial
paralysis RCTs as part of multi-point prescriptions. However,
it is never tested as an independent variable:

- Facial paralysis SR/MA data: systematic reviews of
  acupuncture for Bell's palsy consistently include GB14 in
  the majority of tested protocols alongside ST4, ST6, TE17,
  LI4, and other local points. Positive overall conclusion for
  acupuncture vs no treatment, but individual point
  contributions are not isolatable.

Protocol inclusion without isolation: GB14 joins LI15 (AP-02)
and GB20 in the pattern of high-frequency inclusion in
protocols but zero individual testing. This is a SYSTEMATIC
gap in acupuncture research — the most commonly used points
are never tested alone because they are always part of
prescriptions.
→ Cross-ref: AP-02 Obs. 25 (protocol inclusion without
  isolation)

### Dynamic acupoint properties

No formal sensitization study specific to GB14.

Clinically, the forehead region becomes tender in:
- Frontal headache (tension-type or sinusitis-related)
- Bell's palsy prodrome (forehead heaviness or numbness
  preceding motor loss)
- Frontal sinusitis (referred pain through V1)

The supraorbital nerve exit zone (1 cun below GB14) is a
recognized trigger zone for migraine and cluster headache.
GB14 may sensitize as a secondary zone when the primary
supraorbital zone is active — though this is not
experimentally confirmed.
→ Cross-ref: C04 S4

### Cơ chế giải thích (BSA framework attribution)

**Frontal headache (LOCAL-SEGMENTAL):**
GB14 directly accesses the supraorbital nerve (CN V1). V1
afferents project to the trigeminal nucleus caudalis (TNC) in
the brainstem. Needling at GB14 modulates V1 input at the
peripheral level — reducing nociceptive signaling before it
reaches the TNC. For tension-type frontal headache, the
additional effect on frontalis muscle tension (mechanical
release via needle insertion through the muscle) provides a
dual mechanism: peripheral nerve modulation + local muscle
relaxation.

**Facial paralysis — frontalis component (LOCAL-MOTOR):**
The temporal branch of CN VII passes through the subcutaneous
tissue at GB14's level to innervate the frontalis muscle.
Electroacupuncture at GB14 provides DIRECT electrical
stimulation to the motor nerve and muscle — equivalent to
functional electrical stimulation (FES) of the frontalis. In
Bell's palsy, the temporal branch is often the last to recover
(and sometimes does not recover fully) because it is the only
CN VII branch with no cross-innervation from the contralateral
side above the forehead midline.

**Eye disorders (REFERRED-SEGMENTAL):**
V1 connects the forehead (skin) and the orbit (cornea, upper
eyelid, conjunctiva) through the same trigeminal division.
Stimulating V1 cutaneous afferents at GB14 may modulate
orbital pain processing via shared brainstem pathways (V1 →
TNC → thalamic relay). This is segmental neuromodulation
within the same trigeminal division.

**Gap:** no neuroimaging or electrophysiology study specific to
GB14. The CN V1 access mechanism is anatomically
straightforward but not experimentally validated at this
specific point. Comparison with Yintang (EX-HN3, V1 midline
access) would clarify whether lateral V1 and medial V1
stimulation produce different central effects.

### Kỹ thuật kích thích + dose-response

Châm (B01): 5–10 mm transverse subcutaneous insertion.
Directed superiorly toward GB15 (for treating frontal headache
with radiation to the vertex) or inferiorly toward the eyebrow
(for eye disorders and facial paralysis — bringing the needle
tip closer to the supraorbital nerve exit). De Qi: distending
sensation on the forehead, may radiate to the orbital ridge or
the anterior scalp.
→ Cross-ref: B01 S5.2

EA: commonly paired with:
- Taiyang (EX-HN5) for temporal + frontal headache
- ST6 (Jiache) for facial paralysis — GB14-ST6 electrode pair
  spans the temporal branch (forehead) to the masseter
  (buccal/marginal mandibular branch), providing
  cross-territory CN VII stimulation
- Contralateral GB14 for bilateral frontalis stimulation
Frequency: 2 Hz for chronic conditions, 2/100 Hz alternating
for Bell's palsy (neural recovery protocol).

Bấm huyệt (B02): gentle finger pressure or circular massage
on the forehead, 1 cun above the eyebrow midpoint. Self-care
for frontal headache. Duration: 1–2 minutes per side.
→ Cross-ref: B02

Cứu (B08): rarely used on the forehead due to proximity to
the eyebrow and hair. Indirect moxa (held above, not in
contact) occasionally applied for chronic frontal heaviness
or numbness.

### An toàn

LOW risk. Forehead point over the frontal bone. Transverse
subcutaneous insertion — the frontal bone provides an
excellent solid floor. No deep structures at risk.

Specific considerations:
- **Supraorbital vessels:** minor branches may be punctured,
  causing local bruising (cosmetically visible on the forehead).
  Ice compression post-needling if bruise occurs.
- **Frontalis nerve (CN VII temporal branch):** intentional
  stimulation target in facial paralysis treatment. No adverse
  effect from needling — the motor nerve is superficial and
  small-caliber.

No pregnancy contraindication.

---

## GB15 — Đầu Lâm Khấp (头临泣, Tóu Lín Qì)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry)**
"Head Overlooking Tears" (头临泣) — 头 (Tóu, head) + 临 (Lín,
to approach/overlook) + 泣 (Qì, tears/weeping). The name
references the point's traditional connection to lacrimation
and eye disorders, specifically the sensation of tears welling
up. 头 (head) prefix distinguishes this from GB41 足临泣
(Zulinqi, "Foot Overlooking Tears") — making this the SECOND
head-foot naming pair on the GB channel, after GB11/GB44
(窍阴 Qiaoyin).

**Observation 9 — Second Head-Foot Naming Pair:**

GB now has TWO head-foot naming pairs — more than any other
channel in the Atlas:

| Head Point | Foot Point | Shared Name | Meaning |
|-----------|------------|-------------|---------|
| GB11 头窍阴 | GB44 足窍阴 | 窍阴 (Qiaoyin) | "Portal of Yin" |
| **GB15 头临泣** | **GB41 足临泣** | **临泣 (Linqi)** | **"Overlooking Tears"** |

Both foot counterparts carry special designations: GB44 is
Well/Metal (Ke Controller), GB41 is Stream/Wood (Horary) +
Eight Confluent (Dai Mai). The head points are undistinguished
(no special category). This asymmetry — named foot points are
"special," named head points are not — may reflect the Five
Shu system's exclusive location on the extremities. The naming
pairs link head and foot functionally (both Linqi points treat
eye disorders) while the categorical system remains
anatomically grounded below the elbow/knee.

GB15 sits at the anterior hairline directly above the pupil —
on the same vertical line as GB14 (1 cun above eyebrow) but
~2–2.5 cun superior, just within the hairline. From GB15, the
channel continues posteriorly along the parietal scalp in a
remarkably regular sequence: GB15 → GB16 → GB17 → GB18, each
separated by exactly 1.5 cun.

GB15 meets GB + BL + Yang Wei Mai — BL reappears here after
being absent at GB13 and GB14. This may reflect BL's own
nearby head points (BL4 Quchai is ~0.75 cun medial to GB15,
at the same anterior-posterior level).

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): on the head, directly
above the pupil when looking straight ahead, 0.5 cun within
the anterior hairline, midway between Shenting (GV24) and
Touwei (ST8).

Anatomical landmarks: with the patient looking straight ahead,
draw a vertical line through the pupil center. GB15 is on this
line, 0.5 cun posterior to the anterior hairline. The point is
at the transition from the forehead (frontal region) to the
parietal scalp.

Spatial relationships:
- GB14 (Yangbai) is directly below, on the same pupil line,
  1 cun above the eyebrow (~2–2.5 cun inferior).
- GB16 (Muchuang) is 1.5 cun directly posterior.
- BL4 (Quchai) is ~0.75 cun medial, at the same
  anterior-posterior level (on the BL head line, 1.5 cun
  lateral to midline).
- GV24 (Shenting) is on the midline, at the same
  anterior-posterior level.
- ST8 (Touwei) is ~2 cun lateral, at the corner of the
  forehead.

Needle depth: 5–10 mm transverse subcutaneous, directed
posteriorly along the scalp.

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Supraorbital nerve, lateral branch (CN V1):** primary
  sensory nerve. At 0.5 cun within the hairline, the lateral
  (deep) branch of the supraorbital nerve provides cutaneous
  sensation. This branch extends further posteriorly than the
  medial branch.
- **Frontal branch of CN VII:** motor to the frontalis, though
  at this level the frontalis is transitioning to the galea
  aponeurotica.
- Cranial nerves: CN V1 (sensory), CN VII (motor — diminishing).
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1

**Neural Zone 4 continued:** still CN V1 supraorbital
territory. GB15 is at the posterior extent of reliable
supraorbital nerve coverage — beyond this point (GB16-GB17),
the nerve territory thins and begins to overlap with the
parietal territory that may receive branches from V3
(auriculotemporal, from the lateral side) or C2 (greater
occipital, from the posterior side).

Vascular: superficial temporal artery (frontal branch), with
supraorbital artery branches.

Muscle layers: skin → subcutaneous tissue → galea aponeurotica
(the frontalis muscle has transitioned to the fibrous galea at
this level) → subgaleal loose areolar tissue → periosteum.

### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

No Five Shu, Yuan-Source, Luo, Xi-Cleft, Eight Confluent, or
Hui-Meeting designation.

Meeting point (交会穴): GB + BL + Yang Wei Mai. BL reappears
in the meeting composition after being absent from GB13-GB14.
The BL head line (at 1.5 cun lateral to midline) and GB head
line (at ~2.25 cun lateral) run parallel; their meeting at
GB15 reflects anatomical proximity at the anterior hairline.
→ Cross-ref: B01 S3.1

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM acupoint:** GB15 Toulinqi (头临泣). Functions:
dispels Wind, benefits the eyes, clears the head. Indications:
headache, lacrimation, eye pain, nasal obstruction, dizziness,
epilepsy, infantile convulsions.

**2. Trigger point:** no specific TrP at this scalp location.
The galea aponeurotica does not develop classical trigger
points. 🔴

**3. Ashi:** tender in frontal-vertex headache. Localized
tenderness can occur at the hairline in tension-type headache
with frontal-parietal distribution. 🟡

**4–8.** No significant correspondence.

**Convergence score: 2/8** (TCM, Ashi weak).

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Nguồn cổ:
- Jiayi Jing: headache (头痛), lacrimation in wind (迎风流泪),
  nasal obstruction (鼻塞), blurred vision (目视不明),
  epilepsy (癫痫), infantile convulsions (小儿惊风), body
  heaviness and cold (身重恶寒).
- Deadman/WHO: headache, lacrimation, nasal congestion,
  eye pain, dizziness, epilepsy.

The lacrimation indication connects to the point's name
(临泣, "overlooking tears") and its functional pairing with
GB41 (足临泣, which also treats eye disorders).

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

🔴 Unvalidated. No point-specific study.

### An toàn

LOW risk. Scalp point at the anterior hairline, over the
frontal bone (transitioning to the coronal suture area).
Transverse subcutaneous insertion.

No pregnancy contraindication.

---

## GB16 — Mục Song (目窗, Mù Chuāng)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry)**
"Eye Window" (目窗) — 目 (Mù, eye) + 窗 (Chuāng, window).
The name implies this parietal point is a "window" through
which the eyes can be treated — an eye-related indication
at a location ~6–7 cm from the orbit. This is consistent with
the traditional Chinese medicine observation that scalp points
can influence distant organs, but the modern interpretation
maps to shared CN V1 innervation: both the orbit (via the
ophthalmic nerve's main trunk) and the anterior parietal scalp
(via the supraorbital nerve's posterior extensions) belong to
the same V1 division.

GB16 is the first of three equally-spaced parietal points
(GB16-GB17-GB18) at 1.5-cun intervals posterior to GB15.
These three points, together with GB15, form a 4-point linear
sequence spanning 4.5 cun along a parasagittal line on the
parietal scalp.

**Observation 10 — Third Geometric Sequence and Cumulative
Head Point Pattern:**

GB15-GB18 (GB18 in Batch 4) form a LINEAR sequence at 1.5-cun
intervals — the third geometric pattern in the GB head after
the temporal arc (GB4-GB6 on the ST8→GB7 curve) and the
post-auricular arc (GB10-GB11 on the GB9→GB12 curve):

| Sequence | Points | Type | Interval | Count |
|----------|--------|------|----------|-------|
| 1 (Temporal) | GB4-GB6 | Arc | ~equal divisions | 3 |
| 2 (Post-auricular) | GB10-GB11 | Arc | ~equal divisions | 2 |
| 3 (Parietal) | GB15-GB18 | Line | 1.5 cun | 4* |

*GB18 to be documented in Batch 4.

Combined: 9 of 20 head points (45%) appear to be geometrically
placed at equal intervals between functionally distinctive
anchor points. The anchors — GB1 (eye), GB2 (ear), GB3 (TMJ),
GB7 (temporal), GB8 (migraine), GB9 (post-auricular), GB12
(mastoid), GB14 (forehead), GB19 (occiput), GB20 (Fengchi) —
each has unique anatomical landmarks, distinctive innervation,
or special clinical significance. The interpolated points share
the anchor's general neural territory and similar indications,
distinguished primarily by spinal segment or dermatome position.

This 45% figure suggests that nearly half the GB head density
(20 points — more than any channel) is achieved through
systematic spatial subdivision rather than independent clinical
discovery. The anchors represent empirically significant sites;
the interpolations achieve coverage density. This partially
answers Key Question #1 from the child plan: the 20 head
points combine ~11 anatomically distinctive points with ~9
systematically interpolated ones.

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): on the head, 1.5 cun
directly posterior to Toulinqi (GB15), on the line connecting
GB15 to Fengchi (GB20).

Anatomical landmarks: from GB15 (0.5 cun within the hairline,
directly above the pupil), measure 1.5 cun directly
posteriorly along the scalp. GB16 is on the parietal region,
on the same parasagittal line as GB15.

Spatial relationships:
- GB15 (Toulinqi) is 1.5 cun anterior.
- GB17 (Zhengying) is 1.5 cun posterior.
- The midline (GV channel) is ~2.25 cun medial.
- BL6 (Chengguang) is approximately medial on the BL head
  line (~1.5 cun lateral to midline).

Needle depth: 5–10 mm transverse subcutaneous.

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Supraorbital nerve, lateral branch (CN V1):** at this
  parietal position (~2 cun posterior to the hairline), the
  supraorbital nerve's lateral branch is at its posterior
  extent. Coverage may be thinning.
- **Auriculotemporal nerve (CN V3):** lateral branches may
  reach this parasagittal line from the temporal side —
  creating a potential V1/V3 overlap zone.
- Cranial nerves: CN V1 (primary, weakening) ± CN V3 (lateral
  contribution possible).
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1

**Neural Zone 4–5 transition?** GB16 may sit at the posterior
boundary of reliable V1 supraorbital coverage. Whether it
represents the beginning of a V1→V3 or V1→C2 transition zone
cannot be determined without detailed nerve mapping data at
this specific parasagittal line.

Vascular: parietal branch of superficial temporal artery.

Muscle layers: skin → subcutaneous tissue → galea aponeurotica
→ subgaleal loose areolar tissue → periosteum of parietal
bone. No muscle at this level — the galea is a pure fibrous
aponeurosis between the frontal and occipital bellies of
occipitofrontalis.

### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

No special designation.

Meeting point (交会穴): GB + Yang Wei Mai.
→ Cross-ref: B01 S3.1

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM acupoint:** GB16 Muchuang (目窗). Functions: benefits
the eyes, dispels Wind, clears Heat. Indications: eye pain,
blurred vision, lacrimation, swelling of the face, headache
(vertex), dizziness, infantile convulsions.

**2. Trigger point:** no TrP — galea aponeurotica region. 🔴

**3. Ashi:** parietal tenderness in vertex headache. 🟡

**4–8.** No significant correspondence.

**Convergence score: 2/8** (TCM, Ashi weak).

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Nguồn cổ:
- Jiayi Jing: eye pain (目痛), blurred vision (目视不明),
  facial edema (面目浮肿), headache (头痛), dizziness (头晕),
  infantile convulsions (小儿惊风), cold shivering with
  counterflow Qi (寒热呕逆 — alternating cold/heat with
  vomiting, classic Shaoyang presentation).
- Deadman/WHO: headache, blurred vision, lacrimation, nasal
  obstruction, facial edema.

The persistent eye/vision indications (目窗, 目痛, 目视不明)
at a parietal point 6+ cm from the orbit are consistent across
classical and modern sources, suggesting stable traditional
attribution rather than random assignment.

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

🔴 Unvalidated. No point-specific study.

### An toàn

LOW risk. Parietal scalp point over the parietal bone.
Transverse subcutaneous insertion. The parietal bone is thick
cortical bone — no risk of penetration with standard technique.

No pregnancy contraindication.

---

## GB17 — Chính Doanh (正营, Zhèng Yíng)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry)**
"Upright Camp" (正营) — 正 (Zhèng, upright/correct/proper) +
营 (Yíng, camp/encampment/nourish). The meaning is debated:
some interpret 营 as Ying-Qi (营气, nutritive Qi), making this
the "correct/proper Ying" — a point where nutritive Qi is
properly positioned. Others connect it to the military metaphor
(营 = camp/garrison), implying a strategic position where the
channel "camps" on the parietal crown. The name does not
reference a specific anatomical feature or therapeutic function
— one of the more abstract names in the GB head sequence.

GB17 is the third point in the 1.5-cun parietal sequence
(GB15→GB16→GB17→GB18). At 3 cun posterior to GB15 (and ~3.5
cun posterior to the anterior hairline), GB17 is approaching
the vertex of the skull — the transitional zone between the
parietal and occipital regions.

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): on the head, 1.5 cun
directly posterior to Muchuang (GB16), on the line connecting
GB15 to Fengchi (GB20).

Anatomical landmarks: from GB16, measure 1.5 cun directly
posteriorly. GB17 is on the mid-parietal scalp, approaching
the vertex.

Spatial relationships:
- GB16 (Muchuang) is 1.5 cun anterior.
- GB18 (Chengling) is 1.5 cun posterior (Batch 4).
- GV21 (Qianding) is approximately at the same
  anterior-posterior level on the midline.
- The point is ~3.5 cun posterior to the anterior hairline
  (0.5 cun of GB15 + 1.5 cun to GB16 + 1.5 cun to GB17 =
  3.5 cun from hairline).

Needle depth: 5–10 mm transverse subcutaneous.

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- At this mid-parietal position, the nerve supply is
  transitional:
- **Supraorbital nerve, lateral branch (CN V1):** posterior
  terminal branches — at the limit of V1 coverage. Some
  anatomical studies show the lateral supraorbital branch
  reaching 5–6 cm behind the hairline, which would include
  GB17.
- **Greater occipital nerve (C2, dorsal ramus):** anterior
  extent. The greater occipital nerve covers the posterior
  scalp and can extend forward to the vertex in many
  individuals.
- GB17 likely sits in a **V1/C2 overlap zone** — where the
  supraorbital nerve's posterior reach meets the greater
  occipital nerve's anterior reach. This is analogous to the
  V3/C2-C3 overlap identified at GB7-GB8 (Zone 2–3
  transition), but on the medial rather than lateral aspect
  of the skull.
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1

Vascular: parietal branch of superficial temporal artery,
with possible branches of the occipital artery reaching
forward.

Muscle layers: skin → subcutaneous tissue → galea
aponeurotica → subgaleal loose areolar tissue → periosteum
of parietal bone. Identical layering to GB16. No muscle
fibers — pure galea territory.

### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

No special designation.

Meeting point (交会穴): GB + Yang Wei Mai.
→ Cross-ref: B01 S3.1

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM acupoint:** GB17 Zhengying (正营). Functions: dispels
Wind, benefits the eyes, relieves pain. Indications: headache
(vertex, lateral), toothache (upper jaw), dizziness, vomiting,
lip twitching.

**2. Trigger point:** no TrP — galea aponeurotica. 🔴

**3. Ashi:** parietal tenderness in vertex headache. 🟡

**4–8.** No significant correspondence.

**Convergence score: 2/8** (TCM, Ashi weak).

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Nguồn cổ:
- Jiayi Jing: headache (头痛), dizziness (头晕), toothache
  (齿痛, upper jaw), lip twitching (唇吻强), vomiting (呕吐).
- Deadman/WHO: headache (lateral, vertex), dizziness,
  toothache, vomiting.

The toothache indication at a parietal point is notable —
upper jaw pain (maxillary teeth) maps to CN V2, not V1 or C2.
This could represent: (1) a referred pain phenomenon via
intratrigeminal convergence (V1/V2 interaction at the
trigeminal ganglion), (2) inaccurate classical attribution,
or (3) scalp needling accessing broader trigeminal modulation
beyond the local dermatome.

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

🔴 Unvalidated. No point-specific study.

### An toàn

LOW risk. Parietal scalp point over parietal bone. Transverse
subcutaneous insertion. Same safety profile as GB16.

No pregnancy contraindication.

---

## GB18 — Thừa Linh (承灵, Chéng Líng)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry)**
"Receiving Spirit" (承灵) — 承 (Chéng, to receive/bear/carry)
+ 灵 (Líng, spirit/efficacy/nimbleness). Like GB13 (本神,
"Root of Spirit"), GB18's name invokes the spirit-mind domain.
承灵 suggests a point that "receives" or "catches" spiritual
influence — possibly related to its position on the posterior
parietal scalp approaching the vertex area associated with
GV20 (Baihui, "Hundred Meetings") and its consciousness-
related functions.

GB18 is the FOURTH and LAST point of the 1.5-cun parietal
linear sequence (GB15→GB16→GB17→GB18), completing the third
geometric sequence identified in Obs. 10. At 4.5 cun posterior
to GB15, GB18 sits at the posterior parietal region,
transitioning toward the occipital scalp. From here, GB19
drops to the level of the external occipital protuberance —
the final descent before GB20 (Fengchi) in the suboccipital
fossa.

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): on the head, 1.5 cun
directly posterior to Zhengying (GB17), on the line connecting
GB15 to Fengchi (GB20).

Anatomical landmarks: from GB17, measure 1.5 cun directly
posteriorly. GB18 is on the posterior parietal scalp, ~5 cun
posterior to the anterior hairline.

Spatial relationships:
- GB17 (Zhengying) is 1.5 cun anterior.
- GB19 (Naokong) is further posterior, at the level of GV17
  (Naohu) — the occipital protuberance level.
- GV20 (Baihui) is approximately medial and slightly anterior
  on the midline vertex.

Needle depth: 5–10 mm transverse subcutaneous.

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Greater occipital nerve (C2, dorsal ramus):** at this
  posterior parietal position, the greater occipital nerve is
  the PRIMARY sensory nerve. Its territory extends forward to
  the vertex from the occipital protuberance.
- **Supraorbital nerve (CN V1):** may provide minor lateral
  branch coverage at this level, but C2 dominance is
  established.
- GB18 is likely within C2 territory (greater occipital nerve
  primary), representing the return to cervical innervation
  from the ANTERIOR direction — completing the neural circle:
  V1 (Zone 1, orbital) → V3 (Zone 2, temporal) → C2-C3
  (Zone 3, post-auricular) → V1 (Zone 4, forehead) → C2
  (posterior parietal, returning).
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1

Vascular: parietal branches of the occipital artery, with
diminishing contributions from the superficial temporal artery.

Muscle layers: skin → subcutaneous tissue → galea aponeurotica
→ subgaleal loose areolar tissue → periosteum of parietal bone.
Same layering as GB16-GB17.

### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

No special designation.

Meeting point (交会穴): GB + Yang Wei Mai. Sixth of 8
consecutive Yang Wei Mai meetings (GB13-GB20).
→ Cross-ref: B01 S3.1

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM acupoint:** GB18 Chengling (承灵). Functions: dispels
Wind, clears the head, benefits the nose. Indications:
headache, dizziness, nasal obstruction, nosebleed (epistaxis),
rhinorrhea.

**2. Trigger point:** no TrP — galea aponeurotica. 🔴

**3. Ashi:** parietal-occipital tenderness in vertex/occipital
headache. 🟡

**4–8.** No significant correspondence.

**Convergence score: 2/8** (TCM, Ashi weak).

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Nguồn cổ:
- Jiayi Jing: headache (头痛), dizziness (头晕), nasal
  obstruction (鼻塞), nosebleed (鼻衄), rhinorrhea with clear
  discharge (鼻流清涕), loss of smell (不闻香臭).
- Deadman/WHO: headache, dizziness, nasal obstruction,
  epistaxis, rhinitis.

The nasal indications at a posterior parietal point are
notable. Classical theory attributes this to the GB channel's
internal pathway passing through the nasal region, but
segmental analysis offers no direct link (C2 does not
innervate the nasal mucosa, which is CN V2 + olfactory CN I
territory). This may represent a traditional association
without clear modern anatomical basis.

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

🔴 Unvalidated. No point-specific study.

### An toàn

LOW risk. Posterior parietal scalp point over parietal bone.
Transverse subcutaneous insertion. Same safety profile as
GB16-GB17.

No pregnancy contraindication.

---

## GB19 — Não Không (脑空, Nǎo Kōng)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry)**
"Brain Hollow" (脑空) — 脑 (Nǎo, brain) + 空 (Kōng,
hollow/empty/space). The name directly references the brain
— one of the few acupoint names to do so. 空 suggests a
depression or hollow space, referring to the natural concavity
of the occipital bone at this level. The brain reference is
architecturally interesting: GB19 is the last point before
GB20 (Fengchi), which itself sits over the suboccipital fossa
leading to the foramen magnum and brainstem.

GB19 sits at the occipital level, roughly at the height of
the external occipital protuberance (level with GV17 Naohu,
"Brain's Door"). This is the final descent from the parietal
sequence (GB15-GB18) to the suboccipital region (GB20). The
channel has now completed its passage through forehead
(GB13-GB14) → parietal (GB15-GB18) → occiput (GB19) → and
will terminate the head portion at the suboccipital fossa
(GB20).

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): on the head, directly
above Fengchi (GB20), level with the upper border of the
external occipital protuberance, level with Naohu (GV17).

Anatomical landmarks: find the external occipital protuberance
(the bony bump at the back of the skull). GV17 is at its upper
border on the midline. GB19 is on the same horizontal level,
~2.25 cun lateral to GV17, directly above GB20.

Spatial relationships:
- GB18 (Chengling) is anterior, on the parietal scalp.
- GB20 (Fengchi) is BELOW — in the suboccipital fossa between
  SCM and trapezius, ~1.5 cun inferior.
- GV17 (Naohu) is on the midline, same level.
- BL9 (Yuzhen) is ~1.3 cun lateral to GV17 — medial to GB19.

Needle depth: 5–10 mm transverse subcutaneous.

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Greater occipital nerve (C2, dorsal ramus):** the PRIMARY
  sensory nerve. At the occipital level, this nerve is in its
  main trunk territory — the densest C2 coverage on the
  posterior scalp.
- **Third occipital nerve (C3, dorsal ramus):** may contribute
  at this lateral occipital position.
- Spinal segments: C2-C3 (primarily C2).
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1

GB19 sits in clear C2 occipital territory — the greater
occipital nerve's primary distribution zone. The neural map
from the parietal side shows: Zone 4 (V1 forehead at GB13-
GB15) → V1/C2 transition (GB16-GB17) → C2 dominant (GB18-
GB19). This return to C2 from the ANTERIOR direction mirrors
Zone 3's C2-C3 from the LATERAL direction (GB9-GB12).

Vascular: occipital artery (major posterior scalp vessel),
with terminal branches of the posterior auricular artery.

Muscle layers: skin → subcutaneous tissue → occipitalis muscle
(occipital belly of occipitofrontalis) → subgaleal tissue →
periosteum of occipital bone.

### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

No special designation.

Meeting point (交会穴): GB + Yang Wei Mai. Seventh of 8
consecutive Yang Wei Mai meetings (GB13-GB20).
→ Cross-ref: B01 S3.1

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM acupoint:** GB19 Naokong (脑空). Functions: dispels
Wind, clears Heat from the head, pacifies the Liver.
Indications: headache (occipital), dizziness, stiff neck,
epilepsy, palpitations, tinnitus.

**2. Trigger point:** occipitalis TrP — the occipital belly
of occipitofrontalis develops trigger points that refer pain
forward along the scalp toward the vertex and behind the eye.
Moderate correspondence. 🟡
→ Cross-ref: B11

**3. Ashi:** tender in occipital headache. The occipital
region above the nuchal line is a standard palpation site for
cervicogenic and tension-type headache. 🟡

**4. Marma point:** Krikatika Marma zone — the base-of-skull
area. GB19 is at the upper margin of the Krikatika territory.
Partial. 🟡

**5. Head zone:** C2 dermatome.
→ Cross-ref: A07 S2.2

**6–8.** No significant correspondence.

**Convergence score: 3/8** (TCM, TrP/Ashi, Marma partial,
Head zone).

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Nguồn cổ:
- Jiayi Jing: headache (头痛), stiff neck (项强), dizziness
  (头晕), epilepsy (癫痫), palpitations (心悸), blurred
  vision (目视不明).
- Deadman/WHO: headache (occipital), dizziness, neck rigidity,
  epilepsy, tinnitus, palpitations.

GB19's indications closely match GB20's traditional functions
(minus GB20's Wind-specific emphasis). GB19 may function as
GB20's "preparation zone" — anatomically above the
suboccipital fossa, accessing the same C2 territory from the
scalp rather than the deep suboccipital musculature.

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

🔴 Unvalidated. No point-specific study.

### An toàn

LOW risk. Occipital scalp point over the occipital bone.
Transverse subcutaneous insertion. The occipital bone is thick
at this level (above the nuchal line), providing solid
protection.

No pregnancy contraindication.

---

## GB20 — Phong Trì (风池, Fēng Chí)

**Evidence tier: A (Deep Analysis)**
"Wind Pool" (风池) — 风 (Fēng, wind) + 池 (Chí, pool/pond).
The suboccipital fossa between the SCM and trapezius forms a
natural depression — a "pool" where Wind pathology "collects."
GB20 is the most significant Wind-related point in the entire
acupuncture system, and the MOST REFERENCED acupoint across
the Atlas: 14 cross-references from 8 completed meridians,
mentioned in migraine, headache, hypertension, cervical
conditions, facial paralysis, dizziness, and common cold
protocols across LU, SI, LR, and TE entries.

GB20 holds the densest EXTRAORDINARY VESSEL convergence among
GB head points — meeting of four pathways: GB (Foot Shaoyang),
TE (Hand Shaoyang), Yang Wei Mai, and Yang Qiao Mai. It is the
ONLY point in the Atlas where Yang Wei Mai and Yang Qiao Mai
meet simultaneously — connecting the Yang Linking vessel
(temporal-parietal trajectory) with the Yang Stepping vessel
(lateral body trajectory) at the suboccipital gateway.

GB20 is the TERMINAL point of the 8-point Yang Wei Mai
sequence on the GB head (GB13-GB20). It is also the final
head point of the GB channel — GB21 descends to the shoulder.
These 20 head points (GB1-GB20) form the densest cranial
sequence in the Atlas, and GB20 is their crowning destination:
the deepest, most anatomically complex, and most evidence-
supported of them all.

**Observation 11 — Trigeminocervical Complex Dual-Access
Architecture:**

The trigeminocervical complex (TCC) is the brainstem relay
where trigeminal (V1-V3) and upper cervical (C1-C3) afferents
converge — the key neural substrate for migraine and
cervicogenic headache. The GB head points access the TCC from
TWO opposite directions:

| Access Point | Nerve | TCC Entry Side | Mechanism |
|-------------|-------|---------------|-----------|
| GB8 (temporal) | Auriculotemporal V3 | Trigeminal | V3 afferents → trigeminal nucleus caudalis |
| **GB20 (suboccipital)** | **Greater occipital C2** | **Cervical** | **C2 afferents → upper cervical dorsal horn → TCC** |
| TE5 (forearm) | Radial nerve | Supraspinal (indirect) | Cortical/insula modulation → descending inhibition |

The migraine core triad (GB20-GB8-TE5) provides a THREE-LEVEL
attack on the TCC:
1. **GB20:** cervical C2 input — modulates the BOTTOM of the
   TCC from the spinal side
2. **GB8:** trigeminal V3 input — modulates the TOP of the TCC
   from the cranial side
3. **TE5:** supraspinal modulation — descending inhibition from
   insula/cortex (Tong et al. 2025 fMRI data)

This tri-level architecture explains the 90.48% confidence of
the GB20-GB8-TE5 association rule (He et al. 2025, 911 trials):
the combination attacks the migraine generator from three
different neural pathways simultaneously. Neither two-point
pair achieves the same effect because each misses one access
level. The triad's efficacy is NOT additive but CONVERGENT —
all three inputs must arrive at the TCC/brainstem processing
centers for maximum modulation.

This is the strongest mechanistic explanation for a specific
acupoint combination in the Atlas. 🟡

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): on the nape of the neck,
below the occipital bone, in the depression between the
origins of the sternocleidomastoid and trapezius muscles.

Anatomical landmarks: the suboccipital fossa — a natural
depression palpable when the neck is flexed, located at the
base of the skull between the two prominent muscle masses (SCM
laterally, trapezius medially). The external occipital
protuberance is on the midline above; the mastoid process is
lateral. GB20 is approximately at the level of the superior
nuchal line, or slightly below it.

Spatial relationships:
- GV16 (Fengfu, "Wind Palace") is on the midline, in the
  depression directly below the external occipital
  protuberance, between the two trapezius muscles. GB20 is
  ~2.5–3 cm lateral.
- BL10 (Tianzhu) is ~1.3 cun lateral to GV15, medial to GB20
  and on the lateral border of the trapezius. GB20 is more
  lateral and in the SCM-trapezius interval.
- GB12 (Wangu) is superior and lateral — behind the mastoid
  process. Part of the Wind Triangle (GB12+TE17+GB20, Obs. 6).
- GB19 (Naokong) is directly above on the occipital scalp.
- TE17 (Yifeng) is anterolateral — behind the earlobe. Part
  of the Wind Triangle.
  → Cross-ref: AP-10 TE17
- GB21 (Jianjing) is the next GB point — midpoint of C7 to
  acromion, descending to the shoulder.

Needle depth: 15–30 mm oblique, directed toward the TIP OF
THE NOSE (contralateral nasal ala) or the contralateral
orbital ridge. This standard direction angles the needle
anteroinferiorly, staying ABOVE the vertebral artery and AWAY
from the foramen magnum. Deep insertions (30–50 mm) are used
by experienced practitioners but carry significantly higher
risk.

⚠ **CRITICAL SAFETY NOTE:** see An toàn section. Direction
and depth control at GB20 are LIFE-CRITICAL. Incorrect
angulation can reach the vertebral artery or brainstem.

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply — GB20 has the richest neural access of any
single point in the GB channel:

- **Greater occipital nerve (C2, dorsal ramus):** the PRINCIPAL
  sensory nerve. Ascends from between the atlas (C1) and axis
  (C2) through the semispinalis capitis and trapezius. At GB20,
  the greater occipital nerve is either in its main trunk or
  its early branches — this is the most PROXIMAL access point
  for this nerve in the Atlas. Needling at GB20 reaches the
  nerve before it fans out across the posterior scalp.
- **Suboccipital nerve (C1, dorsal ramus):** the MOTOR nerve
  to the suboccipital triangle muscles (rectus capitis
  posterior major/minor, obliquus capitis superior/inferior).
  Deep to the greater occipital nerve. Needling through the
  suboccipital musculature likely stimulates C1 motor fibers.
- **Lesser occipital nerve (C2-C3, ventral rami):** ascending
  along the posterior border of SCM. At GB20's level, this
  nerve is more lateral.
- **Third occipital nerve (C3, dorsal ramus):** deep, more
  medial. Courses over the C2-C3 facet joint — a common source
  of cervicogenic headache.
- **Spinal accessory nerve (CN XI):** the motor nerve to the
  trapezius and SCM. In the posterior triangle of the neck,
  CN XI courses superficially between SCM and trapezius — the
  SAME interval where GB20 is located. Needling at GB20 can
  directly stimulate CN XI motor fibers.
- Spinal segments: C1 (motor), C2 (primary sensory), C3
  (secondary). Plus CN XI (motor).
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1

**Neural architecture:** GB20 accesses the C1-C3 spinal
segments from BELOW the skull, in contrast to GB9-GB12 which
accessed C2-C3 from the scalp ABOVE. This deep suboccipital
approach reaches the nerve trunks before they branch — the
most concentrated neural input from a single needling site
among all GB points.

The C2 dorsal ramus (greater occipital nerve) → converges at
the trigeminocervical complex (TCC) with V1/V2/V3 trigeminal
afferents. This C2→TCC pathway is the anatomical basis for
GB20's headache efficacy: modulating C2 input at the TCC
modulates the shared processing center for ALL head pain.

Fascial plane:
- **Suboccipital fascia:** continuous with the deep cervical
  fascia. Connects the suboccipital muscles to the spinal dura
  mater via the **myodural bridge** (Hack et al. 1995, Zheng
  et al. 2014). The rectus capitis posterior minor (RCPmi) has
  a direct connective tissue attachment to the posterior spinal
  dura — meaning that suboccipital muscle tension can directly
  affect dural tension. Needling at GB20 through the
  suboccipital musculature may release dural tension via this
  myodural bridge — a biomechanical pathway for headache relief
  that supplements the neural mechanism.
→ Cross-ref: A03 S3.4

Vascular:
- **Vertebral artery:** at GB20's depth (~30–50 mm), the
  vertebral artery courses through the transverse foramen of C1
  and then loops posteriorly before entering the foramen magnum.
  This artery is the CRITICAL vascular structure at risk. See
  An toàn.
- **Occipital artery:** courses through the SCM-trapezius
  interval at approximately GB20's level. More superficial than
  the vertebral artery. Minor hematoma risk.

Muscle layers (superficial → deep):
- Skin → subcutaneous tissue
- Trapezius (medial edge) and/or SCM (posterior edge) — GB20 is
  in the interval between them
- Splenius capitis
- Semispinalis capitis
- Suboccipital muscles: obliquus capitis superior, rectus
  capitis posterior major (the suboccipital triangle)
- Posterior atlanto-occipital membrane (at depth)
- Vertebral artery (deep, at C1 transverse foramen level)

### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

No Five Shu, Yuan-Source, Luo, Xi-Cleft, Eight Confluent, or
Hui-Meeting designation.

Meeting point (交会穴): GB + TE + Yang Wei Mai + Yang Qiao
Mai. FOUR-pathway meeting — the most complex meeting in the
GB head region. GB20 is the ONLY point where Yang Wei Mai and
Yang Qiao Mai converge — linking the Yang connecting vessel
(temporal-parietal trajectory) with the Yang stepping vessel
(lateral body trajectory) at the base of the skull.

This is also the 8th and LAST of the consecutive Yang Wei Mai
meetings on the GB head (GB13-GB20). The Yang Wei Mai
trajectory through the GB head: forehead (GB13-GB14) → hairline
(GB15) → parietal (GB16-GB18) → occiput (GB19) → suboccipital
(GB20).
→ Cross-ref: B01 S3.1

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM acupoint:** GB20 Fengchi (风池). THE principal
Wind-dispelling point in TCM. Functions: dispels Wind (both
external Wind-Cold/Wind-Heat and internal Liver Wind), subdues
Liver Yang, clears the head, benefits the eyes and ears, opens
the sensory orifices.

Indications (comprehensive): headache (ALL types — frontal,
temporal, occipital, vertex, migraine), dizziness, vertigo,
tinnitus, blurred vision, eye pain, nasal obstruction, common
cold (Wind-Cold invasion), stiff neck, torticollis, facial
paralysis, hemiplegia, hypertension, epilepsy, insomnia,
memory impairment.

**2. Trigger point:** suboccipital TrPs — the suboccipital
muscles (rectus capitis posterior major/minor, obliquus capitis
superior/inferior) are among the most common trigger point
generators in the body. They refer pain to the suboccipital
region, behind the eye, the temporal area, and diffusely
across the half-skull. GB20 sits directly over this muscle
group. STRONG spatial AND functional correspondence — one of
the strongest TrP-acupoint matches in the Atlas. 🟡
→ Cross-ref: B11

**3. Ashi correspondence:** universally tender in cervicogenic
headache, tension-type headache, whiplash, and upper cervical
dysfunction. The suboccipital fossa is one of the most
reliably tender points in clinical practice — palpated as a
standard part of headache evaluation across Western and
Eastern traditions. 🟢

**4. Marma point:** Krikatika Marma (कृकाटिका) — the junction
of head and neck at the base of the skull. One of the 107
classical Marma points, classified as Sandhi Marma (joint
type), size 1/2 angula. GB20's position directly at the skull-
neck junction makes this one of the STRONGEST Marma-acupoint
correspondences in the Atlas. 🟡
→ Cross-ref: C04

**5. Head zone:** C2 dermatome (greater occipital nerve
territory). Direct segmental match for occipital headache,
cervicogenic headache, and referred head pain from C2
dysfunction.
→ Cross-ref: A07 S2.2

**6. Anatomy Trains:** the suboccipital muscles connect to
the Superficial Back Line (SBL) through the erector spinae
group. GB20 sits at the TERMINUS of the SBL's posterior
chain (scalp → occipitalis → suboccipital muscles → erector
spinae → hamstrings → plantar fascia). The myodural bridge
(Hack et al. 1995) connects the suboccipital fascia directly
to the spinal dura mater — a fascial-dural connection
architecturally significant beyond standard Anatomy Trains
classification. 🟡
→ Cross-ref: A03

**7. Neurogenic inflammatory spot:** the suboccipital region
shows elevated neurogenic inflammation markers during migraine
episodes. The C2 territory (including GB20's location) is part
of the trigeminocervical sensitization zone — active migraine
episodes may produce neurogenic spots at this location. 🟡

**8. Cupping point:** occipital cupping (behind the neck at
the base of the skull) is practiced in traditional Chinese
medicine for Wind-Cold invasion (common cold, stiff neck,
occipital headache). Also practiced in Arabic/Sunnah cupping
(Al-Hijama) for headache — the suboccipital region
(Al-Akhda'ain) is a classical cupping site. 🟡

**Convergence score: 7/8** — all 8 traditions show some
correspondence at this site. This is likely the HIGHEST
convergence score in the entire Atlas.

**Observation 12 — Highest Cross-Tradition Convergence:**

GB20's 7/8 convergence score is the highest recorded in the
Atlas. The suboccipital fossa appears to be a universal
therapeutic target: independently identified by TCM (Wind
treatment), trigger point therapy (suboccipital TrPs),
Ayurveda (Krikatika Marma), Head's reflex zones (C2
dermatome), Anatomy Trains (SBL terminus + myodural bridge),
neurogenic inflammation research, and traditional cupping
across at least two cultural traditions (Chinese and Arabic).

This level of cross-cultural convergence at a single
anatomical site strongly supports the multi-system convergence
zone model (A09 S2.2): GB20 is not merely an acupoint — it is
a site where neural, fascial, vascular, and muscular systems
overlap so densely that EVERY systematic approach to body
therapy independently identified it as clinically significant.

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Nguồn cổ:
- **Su Wen Ch.60:** 风池 among the key points for "Wind-Bi"
  (风痹) — painful obstruction from Wind pathogen.
- **Ling Shu Ch.21:** needling method for eye disorders — GB20
  among the points for treating vision problems.
- **Jiayi Jing:** headache (头痛), dizziness (头晕目眩),
  Wind-Cold invasion (风寒), stiff neck (项强), nasal
  obstruction (鼻塞), eye redness and pain (目赤痛),
  lacrimation (流泪), epistaxis (鼻衄), deafness (耳聋),
  facial paralysis (口眼歪斜), epilepsy (癫痫), mania (癫狂),
  malaria (疟疾), febrile disease (热病).
- **Zhenjiu Dacheng:** "Wind Pool — treats all diseases caused
  by Wind, whether external or internal."

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- Headache: ALL types (occipital, temporal, frontal, vertex,
  migraine)
- Neck: stiff neck, neck pain, torticollis, cervical
  spondylosis
- Eyes: blurred vision, eye pain, night blindness, glaucoma
- Ears: tinnitus, deafness
- Nose: nasal obstruction, rhinitis, epistaxis
- Neurological: dizziness, vertigo, epilepsy, hemiplegia,
  insomnia, memory impairment
- General: common cold, fever, hypertension, facial paralysis

Kinh nghiệm lâm sàng:
- **Migraine protocol:** GB20 + GB8 + TE5 = core triad
  (He et al. 2025, 90.48% confidence). GB20 is the OBLIGATORY
  point in every migraine acupuncture protocol.
  → Cross-ref: AP-10 TE5
- **Hypertension:** GB20 + LR3 (Taichong) + LI11 (Quchi) =
  standard prescription. GB20 among top 4 points across 23
  RCTs (overview of 15 SRs, referenced in AP-12 LR3).
  → Cross-ref: AP-12 LR3
- **Cervicogenic headache:** GB20 + GB12 (brackets the SCM) +
  BL10 + GV20.
- **Wind-Cold invasion:** GB20 + TE5 + LI4 + GV14 — the
  standard exterior-releasing prescription.
- **Facial paralysis:** GB20 + TE17 + ST4 + ST6 + LI4 + GB14
  — GB20 provides the Wind-dispelling/cervical component.
  → Cross-ref: AP-10 TE17
- **SI3+BL62 Du Mai pair:** GB20 added as local point for
  cervical/spinal conditions.
  → Cross-ref: AP-06 SI3

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

**1. MIGRAINE:**

🟢 **Confirmed** — the strongest evidence for any specific
acupoint-indication pair in the GB channel.

- **Linde et al. 2016 (Cochrane Database of Systematic
  Reviews):** 22 RCTs, 4,985 patients. Acupuncture superior
  to sham for migraine prophylaxis (≥50% reduction in headache
  days). GB20 included in the majority of protocols.

- **Zhao et al. 2017 (JAMA Internal Medicine):** 249 patients,
  3-arm RCT (true acupuncture vs sham vs waiting list). GB20
  was OBLIGATORY in the true acupuncture arm for ALL migraine
  subtypes. Significant reduction in migraine days at weeks
  13-20 vs sham.

- **He et al. 2025 (Journal of Pain Research):** data mining
  of 911 clinical trials, 1,610 prescriptions, 181 acupoints.
  GB20-GB8-TE5 association rule: support 15.65%, confidence
  90.48%. GB20 appeared in >80% of all migraine prescriptions.

- **Tong et al. 2025 (Frontiers in Neurology):** systematic
  review of 40 fMRI studies of acupuncture for migraine. GB20
  tied with TE5 as the MOST FREQUENTLY STUDIED point in
  migraine neuroimaging (21/40 studies). 8/11 most frequently
  used points were Shaoyang (GB+TE).

Neuroimaging findings at GB20 for migraine (synthesized from
Tong et al. 2025 and related studies):
- Activation: periaqueductal gray (PAG), anterior cingulate
  cortex (ACC), insula, prefrontal cortex
- Deactivation: thalamic pain-processing regions (during
  migraine episode)
- Connectivity: increased PAG-cortex connectivity after
  acupuncture treatment course — suggesting reorganization of
  the descending pain modulatory system

**Note on evidence scope:** GB20 is never tested as an
ISOLATED point in these studies — it is always part of
multi-point protocols. However, GB20 is the ONE point that
appears in virtually ALL migraine protocols (>80%, He et al.
2025) and is the only OBLIGATORY point (Zhao et al. 2017).
This makes GB20 the least substitutable element. The 🟢
rating applies to protocols centered on GB20.

**2. TENSION-TYPE HEADACHE:**

🟡 **Plausible** — Linde et al. 2009 (Cochrane) for tension-
type headache found acupuncture effective, with GB20 in the
majority of tested protocols. Not point-specifically isolated.

**3. CERVICOGENIC HEADACHE:**

🟡 **Plausible** — strong anatomical logic (C2 afferent access
at the TCC). GB20 is the primary local point for cervicogenic
headache in clinical practice, but no RCT isolates GB20 from
multi-point protocols.

**4. HYPERTENSION:**

🟡 **Plausible** — overview of 15 SRs (referenced at AP-12
LR3) places GB20 among the top 4 most frequently selected
points across 23 RCTs. Positive overall conclusion for
acupuncture + medication > medication alone.

**5. DIZZINESS/VERTIGO:**

🟡 **Plausible** — C1-C2 proprioceptive input to vestibular
nuclei provides anatomical basis for cervicogenic dizziness
treatment. No point-specific RCT.

**6. COMMON COLD, FACIAL PARALYSIS, STROKE:**

🔴 **Unvalidated** — included in multi-point protocols for all
three conditions but never isolated. GB20's contribution to
these protocols is not individually validated.

### Dynamic acupoint properties

The suboccipital fossa (GB20 region) sensitizes in multiple
pathological states:

- **Migraine:** suboccipital tenderness is a DIAGNOSTIC
  CRITERION for cervicogenic headache (Sjaastad et al. 1998,
  International Headache Society). The greater occipital nerve
  at GB20 becomes hyperalgesic during migraine episodes —
  pressure pain thresholds drop by ~30-40% compared to
  headache-free intervals.
- **Upper cervical dysfunction:** C1-C2 segmental dysfunction
  produces predictable suboccipital tenderness. GB20 is one of
  the first points to become tender in acute neck strain.
- **Hypertension:** some studies report increased suboccipital
  muscle tension in hypertensive patients, but this is not
  specific to GB20.

The suboccipital TrPs (rectus capitis posterior major/minor)
have well-documented sensitization patterns — they activate
during any condition that increases upper cervical muscle tone
(stress, forward head posture, cervical dysfunction,
post-concussion). GB20's dynamic behavior is likely driven by
TrP sensitization rather than acupoint-specific mechanisms.
→ Cross-ref: C04 S4

### Cơ chế giải thích (BSA framework attribution)

**Migraine (CONVERGENT — TCC tri-level access):**
GB20's migraine mechanism operates through the
trigeminocervical complex (TCC):
1. **Peripheral:** needling stimulates C2 afferents (greater
   occipital nerve) → C2 dorsal horn → TCC convergence zone.
   C2 input modulates the shared processing center where
   trigeminal and cervical pain signals converge.
2. **Segmental:** suboccipital muscle relaxation (local twitch
   response, myodural bridge release) → reduced dural tension
   → reduced meningeal nociceptive input.
3. **Supraspinal:** neuroimaging shows GB20 needling activates
   PAG (periaqueductal gray) → descending inhibition of pain
   processing at the TCC level. This PAG activation is NOT
   unique to GB20 (other strong acupoints show it too) but the
   COMBINATION of peripheral C2 input + central PAG activation
   makes GB20's TCC modulation bidirectional.

The myodural bridge (Hack et al. 1995): the rectus capitis
posterior minor (RCPmi) has a connective tissue bridge to the
posterior spinal dura. Suboccipital muscle tension → dural
tension → headache. Needling through the suboccipital muscles
at GB20 can release this tension mechanically, providing a
FASCIAL-DURAL pathway for headache relief that supplements the
purely neural TCC mechanism.

**Hypertension (PROPOSED — C2 → NTS/RVLM):**
C2 afferent stimulation may modulate cardiovascular centers in
the brainstem. The nucleus tractus solitarius (NTS) and rostral
ventrolateral medulla (RVLM) — both critical for blood
pressure regulation — receive convergent input from cervical
afferents. GB20's C2 access may provide a peripheral entry to
these cardiovascular brainstem nuclei. This mechanism is
PROPOSED but not validated for GB20 specifically.
Compare: LI11 accesses RVLM through a different pathway
(radial nerve → cortical relay, AP-02).

**Cervicogenic dizziness (LOCAL-SEGMENTAL + VESTIBULAR):**
C1-C2 proprioceptors in the suboccipital muscles project to
