the vestibular nuclei via the cervicovestibular pathway.
Dysfunction of these proprioceptors (from muscle tension, TrPs,
or cervical segmental dysfunction) produces erroneous head-
position signals → dizziness. Needling at GB20 may restore
normal proprioceptive input by releasing suboccipital muscle
tension and modulating C1-C2 proprioceptive signaling.

**Wind = C2-C3 hypothesis (SYNTHESIS across Obs. 6, 7, 11):**
Building on Obs. 6 (GB12 Wind Triangle), Obs. 7 (circular
neural traversal), and Obs. 11 (TCC dual-access):

TCM "Wind" pathology maps to C2-C3 cervical plexus territory:
- Facial paralysis → CN VII exits at mastoid (C2-C3 zone)
- Headache → C2 → TCC → referred head pain
- Stiff neck → C2-C3 motor innervation of cervical muscles
- Dizziness → C1-C2 proprioception → vestibular nuclei

The three Wind points of the suboccipital region — TE17 (Wind
Screen), GB12 (Wangu), GB20 (Wind Pool) — all share C2-C3
innervation. "Dispelling Wind" may be the traditional
description of C2-C3 neuromodulation. 🟡

**Gap:** the myodural bridge mechanism (dural tension release)
is anatomically validated (Hack 1995, Zheng 2014) but not
tested in acupuncture-specific research. Does needling at GB20
actually reduce dural tension? Intracranial pressure monitoring
during GB20 needling would be informative. Also: the
hypertension mechanism via C2→NTS is entirely theoretical —
no neuroimaging study has shown GB20 activating NTS/RVLM.

### Kỹ thuật kích thích + dose-response

Châm (B01): 15–30 mm oblique insertion. Standard direction:
toward the TIP OF THE NOSE (contralateral nasal ala) or the
contralateral orbital ridge. This angles the needle
anteroinferiorly and slightly medially, directing it through
the suboccipital musculature toward the C1-C2 interspace
region — maximizing greater occipital nerve stimulation while
staying ABOVE the vertebral artery and AWAY from the foramen
magnum.

De Qi: deep aching sensation at the occiput, often radiating
to the temporal region, behind the eye, or the vertex — this
radiation pattern follows the greater occipital nerve's
referred pain territory and correlates with TCC activation.
Strong De Qi at GB20 is traditionally considered essential for
headache treatment.
→ Cross-ref: B01 S5.2

EA: the most commonly electroacupuncture-paired point in
headache protocols.
- **GB20-GB20 bilateral:** electrode pair across both GB20
  points for bilateral suboccipital stimulation. Frequency:
  2 Hz for chronic migraine prophylaxis, 2/100 Hz dense-
  disperse for acute migraine.
- **GB20-GB8:** electrode pair spanning the TCC's cervical (C2)
  and trigeminal (V3) inputs. Dual-afferent stimulation.
- **GB20-GV20:** electrode pair from the suboccipital fossa to
  the vertex. Longest cranial EA vector.

Bấm huyệt (B02): deep thumb pressure into the suboccipital
fossa. Self-care technique for headache: press both GB20
points simultaneously with the thumbs while sitting, applying
firm pressure for 30–60 seconds. One of the most effective
self-acupressure points.
→ Cross-ref: B02

Cứu (B08): indirect moxa at the occiput for Wind-Cold invasion
(common cold with occipital headache, stiff neck). Classical
combination: GB20 + GV14 + TE5 with moxa.

### An toàn

⚠ **MODERATE-HIGH risk — one of the most critical safety
points in the entire Atlas.**

**1. VERTEBRAL ARTERY (CRITICAL):**
The vertebral artery passes through the transverse foramen of
C1 (atlas) at approximately 30–50 mm depth from the skin at
GB20's location. A needle directed MEDIALLY and SUPERIORLY at
depths exceeding 40 mm could potentially reach the vertebral
artery as it loops posteriorly between C1 and the occiput.

Fatal and severe adverse events have been reported:
- Vertebral artery dissection following deep needling at GB20
  or nearby points (rare case reports)
- Subarachnoid hemorrhage from vertebral artery injury

**PREVENTION:** strict angulation control. The standard
direction (toward the tip of the nose / contralateral nasal
ala) at moderate depth (15–30 mm) maintains the needle
trajectory ABOVE and ANTERIOR to the vertebral artery. NEVER
insert perpendicular to the skin surface at GB20 (this directs
the needle toward the vertebral artery and foramen magnum).

**2. BRAINSTEM / CISTERNA MAGNA (EXTREME DEPTH):**
At extreme depth (>50 mm) with medial-superior angulation, a
needle could theoretically reach the foramen magnum and
cisterna magna — the CSF-filled space overlying the medulla
oblongata. This would cause catastrophic injury. This risk is
eliminated by depth control and proper angulation.

**3. Occipital artery:** at superficial-to-moderate depth.
Minor hematoma risk. Not life-threatening.

**Practitioner competency requirement:** GB20 demands specific
training in safe needling technique. The combination of deep
suboccipital musculature, proximity to the vertebral artery,
and potential access to the posterior cranial fossa makes GB20
one of a small number of points where practitioner error can
cause severe or fatal outcomes.

**Pregnancy:** no specific contraindication, but the strong
Wind-dispelling and descending actions of GB20 mean some
classical sources advise caution during pregnancy. Not a WHO-
listed pregnancy contraindication.

---

## GB21 — Kiên Tỉnh (肩井, Jianjing)

**Evidence tier: B (Standard Entry)**
GB21 is the first GB point to leave the head. The transition
from GB20 (suboccipital fossa) to GB21 (shoulder summit) is
the most dramatic anatomical shift on the GB channel — a jump
of >10 cm from skull base to midpoint of the upper trapezius.
Meeting of GB, TE, ST, and Yang Wei Mai. CN XI (spinal
accessory nerve) provides neural continuity with GB20: the
same nerve accessed deep in the SCM-trapezius interval at
GB20 is now accessed superficially at the upper trapezius
muscle belly. One of the 5 traditionally "forbidden"
pregnancy points (alongside LI4, SP6, BL60, BL67).

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): on the shoulder, at the
midpoint of the line connecting the spinous process of C7
(at GV14 Dazhui) and the lateral end of the acromion, at the
highest point of the shoulder.

Anatomical landmarks: the upper trapezius muscle — GB21 sits
at the thickest part of this muscle's superior fibers. Found
by locating the midpoint between the prominent C7 spinous
process (base of neck) and the acromion tip (bony lateral
shoulder prominence). GB21 is at the summit of the shoulder
curve, roughly over the supraspinatus fossa of the scapula.

Spatial relationships:
- GV14 (Dazhui) is medially on the midline at C7.
- TE15 (Tianliao) is posteromedial — midway between GB21
  and SI13, at the superior scapular angle.
  → Cross-ref: AP-10 TE15
- LI15 (Jianyu) is anteroinferior — at the anterolateral
  shoulder joint. LI15 (anteroinferior), TE14 (posterior-
  inferior), SI9-SI12 (posterior), and GB21 (superior)
  ring the glenohumeral joint from different channels.
  → Cross-ref: AP-02 LI15
- GB20 (Fengchi) is the previous GB point — in the sub-
  occipital fossa, >10 cm medial and superior.
- GB22 (Yuanye) is the next point — mid-axillary line at
  the 4th intercostal space, a dramatic descent to the
  lateral chest.

Needle depth: 10–15 mm perpendicular or oblique posteriorly
toward the scapular spine. Shallow insertion mandatory due
to pneumothorax risk.

⚠ **SAFETY NOTE:** see An toàn section. The lung apex is
deep to this point.

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Spinal accessory nerve (CN XI):** the MOTOR nerve to the
  trapezius. CN XI enters the trapezius from its deep
  surface. At GB21, the needle enters the trapezius muscle
  belly and may directly stimulate CN XI motor branches.
  This is the SAME nerve accessed at GB20 (where CN XI
  passes between SCM and trapezius). GB20→GB21 = proximal
  → distal access along CN XI's course.
- **Supraclavicular nerves (C3-C4, cervical plexus):**
  sensory innervation of the shoulder summit skin. The
  lateral supraclavicular nerve (C3-C4) covers this area.
  GB21 is in CERVICAL PLEXUS territory — not brachial
  plexus — despite its apparent shoulder location.
- **Dorsal scapular nerve (C5):** motor to rhomboids, passes
  deep. Unlikely reached at standard needle depth.
- Spinal segments: C3-C4 (sensory), CN XI (motor).
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1

Neural transition: GB20 = C1-C3 + CN XI (suboccipital).
GB21 = C3-C4 + CN XI (shoulder). C3 is the SHARED segment.
CN XI provides motor continuity across the head-body
boundary.

Fascial plane:
- Trapezius fascia: continuous with the deep cervical fascia
  superiorly (connecting to the suboccipital region at GB20)
  and the thoracolumbar fascia inferiorly.
- Deep: supraspinatus fascia over the scapular fossa.
→ Cross-ref: A03 S3.4

Vascular:
- Transverse cervical artery (from thyrocervical trunk)
  passes deep to the trapezius at this level. Not at risk
  at standard needle depth.

Muscle layers (superficial → deep):
- Skin → subcutaneous tissue
- Trapezius (upper fibers) — GB21 is within this muscle
- Supraspinatus (deep, below scapular spine)
- Lung apex (at depth >25-30 mm — see Safety)

### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

No Five Shu, Yuan-Source, Luo, Xi-Cleft, Eight Confluent,
or Hui-Meeting designation.

Meeting point (交会穴): GB + TE + ST + Yang Wei Mai. Four-
pathway meeting — same complexity as GB20 but different
composition (ST replaces Yang Qiao Mai).

Yang Wei Mai note: GB21 is the 9th Yang Wei Mai meeting on
the GB channel after the 8 consecutive head meetings
(GB13-GB20). However, there is a BREAK in Yang Wei Mai
trajectory: GB20→GB21 is NOT continuous. The Yang Wei Mai
detours through other channels (TE15, SI10) before
returning to GB at this point. GB21 is a Yang Wei Mai
RESUMPTION, not a continuation.
→ Cross-ref: B01 S3.1

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM acupoint:** GB21 Jianjing (肩井, "Shoulder Well").
Functions: descends Qi, relaxes the sinews, benefits the
shoulder and neck, promotes labor, facilitates lactation.

**2. Trigger point:** upper trapezius TrP1 — the MOST
COMMON trigger point in the human body (Travell & Simons
1999). Located at the midpoint of the upper trapezius —
exactly GB21's location. Refers pain to the posterolateral
neck, temple, behind the eye, and sometimes the jaw. One
of the STRONGEST TrP-acupoint spatial correspondences in
the Atlas, rivaling GB20's suboccipital TrP match. 🟡
→ Cross-ref: B11

**3. Ashi correspondence:** universally tender in shoulder
tension, desk workers, stress-related muscle holding. The
upper trapezius is the default tension-holding muscle. GB21
is the single most commonly palpated tender point across
ALL manual therapy traditions. 🟢

**4. Marma point:** Amsa Marma (अंस) — the shoulder
junction. One of 107 classical Marma points, classified as
Sandhi Marma (joint type). Good spatial correspondence. 🟡
→ Cross-ref: C04

**5. Head zone:** C3-C4 dermatome. The apparent shoulder
location maps to CERVICAL dermatomes — the shoulder cape
is C3-C4 territory, not T1-T2.
→ Cross-ref: A07 S2.2

**6. Anatomy Trains:** the Superficial Back Line (SBL)
includes the upper trapezius. GB21 sits on this line. The
Lateral Line (LL) also crosses the shoulder region —
possible SBL-LL intersection. 🟡
→ Cross-ref: A03

**7. Neurogenic inflammatory spot:** no specific data. The
upper trapezius shows chronic sensitization in tension-type
headache but this is myofascial rather than neurogenic
inflammatory. No correspondence.

**8. Cupping point:** shoulder cupping is widely practiced
for shoulder/neck tension, common cold with neck stiffness,
and generalized pain. GB21 is a standard cupping site. 🟡

**Convergence score: 6/8** — strong. Missing: neurogenic
spot.

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Nguồn cổ:
- **Jiayi Jing:** shoulder and arm pain (肩臂痛), neck
  stiffness (项强), difficult labor (难产), scrofula (瘰疬).
- **Zhenjiu Dacheng:** "Shoulder Well — treats shoulder and
  back pain, headache from Wind, difficult labor. Moxa 3
  cones. Contraindicated in pregnancy."

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- Shoulder: pain, stiffness, frozen shoulder, periarthritis
- Neck: stiff neck, cervical spondylosis
- Obstetric: difficult labor, retained placenta, insufficient
  lactation
- General: headache (temporal), mastitis, scrofula

Kinh nghiệm lâm sàng:
- **Shoulder pain protocol:** GB21 + LI15 + TE14 = standard
  multi-channel shoulder combination.
  → Cross-ref: AP-02 LI15, AP-10 TE14
- **Labor facilitation:** GB21 + SP6 + LI4 acupressure.
  Teimoori et al. 2015: GB21+LI4+SP6 (30 sec each),
  spontaneous labor 56.9% vs 8.3% control.
  → Cross-ref: AP-04 SP6, AP-02 LI4
- **Scrofula:** GB21 + TE10 = Shaoyang anti-scrofula pair.
  Shaoyang channels traverse the lateral neck where cervical
  lymph nodes concentrate.
  → Cross-ref: AP-10 TE10
- **Pregnancy prohibition:** one of the 5 "forbidden points"
  (LI4, SP6, BL60, BL67, GB21). GB21 is the ONLY non-
  extremity point in this list — all others are distal limb
  points. Traditional rationale: strong "descending Qi"
  action may promote premature delivery. Modern evidence: no
  RCT has demonstrated adverse pregnancy outcomes from GB21
  stimulation, but the prohibition remains in clinical
  guidelines as precaution.
  → Cross-ref: AP-02 LI4 (same evidence gap noted)

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

**1. SHOULDER PAIN:**

🟡 **Plausible** — GB21 is included in virtually all
shoulder acupuncture protocols but never tested in
isolation. Upper trapezius TrP dry needling evidence
(indirect) shows significant pain reduction and ROM
improvement in multiple RCTs. However, dry needling studies
use different parameters from traditional acupuncture.

**2. LABOR:**

🟡 **Plausible** — Teimoori et al. 2015 (referenced at
AP-04 SP6): acupressure at GB21+LI4+SP6 → spontaneous
labor 56.9% vs 8.3% control. Smith et al. 2017 (Cochrane):
LI4+SP6+BL32+GB21 protocol for labor pain — positive but
GRADE low to moderate certainty. GB21 not isolated from
multi-point protocols.

**3. NECK PAIN / CERVICOGENIC HEADACHE:**

🔴 **Unvalidated** — included in protocols alongside GB20
but no point-specific evidence. TrP literature provides
indirect support (upper trapezius TrP referral includes
temporal headache).

### Dynamic acupoint properties

The upper trapezius is one of the most dynamically
responsive muscles in the body:
- **Stress/posture sensitization:** increased EMG activity
  and tenderness in desk workers, stress states, forward
  head posture. GB21 tenderness correlates with subjective
  stress and neck tension.
- **Headache sensitization:** upper trapezius tenderness is
  a DIAGNOSTIC feature of tension-type headache
  (International Headache Society criteria). Pressure pain
  thresholds at GB21 decrease during headache episodes.
- **Referred sensitization:** upper trapezius TrPs activated
  by cervical dysfunction, shoulder pathology, or emotional
  stress — producing tenderness at GB21 without local
  pathology.

Dynamic behavior at GB21 is primarily myofascial (TrP
sensitization) rather than neurogenic inflammatory.
→ Cross-ref: C04 S4

### Cơ chế giải thích (BSA framework attribution)

**Shoulder pain (LOCAL-SEGMENTAL + TrP release):**
Needling at GB21 produces a local twitch response (LTR) in
the upper trapezius — releasing the TrP contraction knot.
Mediated by a spinal reflex arc (C3-C4 motor neurons).
Post-LTR: local blood flow increase, reduction in
sensitizing chemicals (substance P, CGRP), and muscle fiber
relaxation.
→ Cross-ref: B11

**Labor facilitation (PROPOSED — unvalidated):**
No validated mechanism connects the shoulder summit to
uterine contractility. Hypotheses:
1. Cervical plexus (C3-C4) → phrenic nerve (C3-C5) →
   diaphragmatic/intra-abdominal pressure? Speculative.
2. Acupressure-induced pain → endogenous opioid release →
   labor pain modulation? Indirect.
The pregnancy prohibition and labor indication are
contradictory: the same point is forbidden during pregnancy
but used TO INDUCE labor. This suggests the prohibition
exists precisely BECAUSE the point has some (unknown)
labor-promoting effect. 🔴

**Headache (REFERRED — TrP pattern):**
Upper trapezius TrPs at GB21 refer pain to the temple,
behind the eye, and posterolateral neck. Treatment of the
TrP reduces this referred pattern. Well-established
mechanism (Travell & Simons).

**Observation 13 — CN XI Thread Across Head-Body
Boundary:**

The spinal accessory nerve (CN XI) provides neural
continuity between GB20 (suboccipital, deep access between
SCM and trapezius) and GB21 (shoulder, superficial access at
the upper trapezius muscle belly). CN XI innervates both
muscles: SCM (head rotation) and trapezius (shoulder
elevation). This is the FIRST instance in the Atlas of a
single cranial nerve threading through consecutive acupoints
that span the head-body boundary.

GB20 stimulates CN XI at its SCM-trapezius INTERVAL
(between the two target muscles). GB21 stimulates CN XI at
its trapezius INSERTION (within the muscle belly). The
functional consequence: GB20 modulates head/neck positioning
(SCM), while GB21 modulates shoulder/scapular positioning
(trapezius) — two different motor outputs from the same
nerve, accessed at two different anatomical depths.

CN XI is the only cranial nerve with a SPINAL component
(nucleus in C1-C5), creating a unique cranial-spinal neural
bridge that parallels the Shaoyang channel's head-to-body
trajectory.

**Gap:** GB21's labor mechanism is the weakest mechanistic
attribution in this batch — no anatomical pathway links
shoulder summit to uterine function.

### Kỹ thuật kích thích + dose-response

Châm (B01): 10–15 mm perpendicular or oblique posteriorly
toward the scapular spine. SHALLOW mandatory. Some
practitioners pinch-lift the upper trapezius before
needling, inserting horizontally through the lifted muscle
fold — this eliminates pneumothorax risk.

De Qi: deep aching in the shoulder, heaviness or warmth
spreading to neck and upper arm. Strong De Qi indicates
upper trapezius TrP activation.
→ Cross-ref: B01 S5.2

EA: GB21-GB20 pair for combined shoulder-occiput protocol.
GB21-LI15 for shoulder pain. 2-4 Hz for chronic tension.

Bấm huyệt (B02): firm pressure with thumb or elbow. One
of the most commonly applied acupressure points — used in
chair massage, self-care, and labor support. Sustained
pressure 30-60 seconds or kneading.
→ Cross-ref: B02

Cứu (B08): indirect moxa for chronic shoulder/neck
stiffness with Cold-Bi pattern.

### An toàn

⚠ **MODERATE risk — pneumothorax point.**

**1. PNEUMOTHORAX (CRITICAL):**
The lung apex extends approximately 2.5 cm above the medial
third of the clavicle. GB21 is positioned above the lung
apex. Deep perpendicular or anteroinferior-directed needling
(>25-30 mm) can potentially reach the pleural dome.

**PREVENTION:** shallow insertion (10-15 mm). Oblique
needling directed posteriorly toward the scapular spine
keeps the needle in the trapezius and away from the pleural
dome. The pinch-lift technique (lifting the trapezius
between thumb and index finger, then inserting horizontally
through the muscle fold) completely eliminates pneumothorax
risk.

**2. Transverse cervical artery:** deep to the trapezius.
Minor hematoma risk at excessive depth.

**Pregnancy:** one of the 5 "forbidden points" (LI4, SP6,
BL60, BL67, GB21). Strong "descending" action may promote
premature labor. Modern guidelines STILL list GB21 as a
pregnancy caution, though no RCT demonstrates adverse
outcomes. Given its inclusion in labor-induction protocols
(Teimoori 2015), the prohibition reflects a real (if
unmeasured) clinical concern rather than pure tradition.

Classified among "dangerous points" in Chinese acupuncture
safety literature alongside LU1, LU2.
→ Cross-ref: AP-01 LU1

---

## GB22 — Uyên Dịch (渊腋, Yuanye)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry)**
GB22 marks GB's entry into the lateral chest. Located in the
mid-axillary line at the 4th intercostal space. Landmark
for SP21 (Da Bao, Great Luo of Spleen, at the 6th ICS in
the same mid-axillary line — 2 intercostal spaces below).
The transition from GB21 (shoulder summit) to GB22 (lateral
chest) represents a dramatic vertical descent of ~15 cm.
Neural zone transition: from CN XI/cervical plexus (C3-C4)
at GB21 to intercostal nerve (T4) at GB22.

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): on the lateral chest,
in the 4th intercostal space, on the mid-axillary line.
With the arm raised, in the depression 3 cun below the
axilla.

Anatomical landmarks: mid-axillary line = vertical line
from the center of the axillary fold. The 4th intercostal
space is approximately at the nipple level (males).

Spatial relationships:
- GB21 (Jianjing) is the previous point — shoulder summit,
  far above.
- GB23 (Zhejin) is 1 cun anterior.
- SP21 (Dabao, Great Luo of Spleen) is 2 intercostal
  spaces below in the same mid-axillary line (6th ICS).
  → Cross-ref: AP-04 SP21

Needle depth: 10-15 mm oblique along the intercostal
space. No deep perpendicular needling (pneumothorax risk).

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Lateral cutaneous branch of the 4th intercostal nerve
  (T4):** primary sensory supply.
- Spinal segment: T4.
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1

Fascial plane: intercostal fascia between serratus anterior
and external oblique.

Vascular: lateral thoracic artery branches.

Muscle layers: skin → subcutaneous → serratus anterior →
external intercostal → internal intercostal → pleura.

### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

No special designations. One of the few GB points with NO
meeting point status — most GB points are meetings of 2+
channels or extraordinary vessels.

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM acupoint:** GB22 Yuanye (渊腋, "Abyss of the
Axilla"). Functions: regulates Qi, opens the chest.
Limited traditional importance.

**2. Trigger point:** serratus anterior TrPs may be in
this region. Referred pain to lateral chest and medial
scapular border. Moderate spatial correspondence. 🟡

**3. Ashi correspondence:** lateral chest wall tenderness
in intercostal neuralgia, rib fractures, or referred
visceral pain. Non-specific.

**4. Marma point:** no direct correspondence.

**5. Head zone:** T4 dermatome — nipple level. No specific
unique visceral correlation.

**6. Anatomy Trains:** the Lateral Line passes through the
lateral chest wall. GB22 is on or near this line. 🟡

**7. Neurogenic inflammatory spot:** no data.

**8. Cupping point:** lateral chest cupping less common but
practiced for chest/rib pain.

**Convergence score: 2/8** — low.

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Nguồn cổ:
- **Jiayi Jing:** chest fullness (胸满), intercostal pain
  (胁痛), arm cannot be raised (臂不举).

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- Lateral chest pain, intercostal neuralgia
- Axillary swelling
- Arm pain, inability to raise arm

Kinh nghiệm lâm sàng: limited. Used as a local point for
intercostal pain. Often paired with GB23 and GB24 for
lateral thoracic conditions.

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

🔴 **Unvalidated** — no systematic review, RCT, or
point-specific evidence.

### An toàn

⚠ **MODERATE risk — pneumothorax point.**

The lung is directly deep. The 4th intercostal space on the
mid-axillary line overlies the lung. Deep perpendicular
needling can penetrate the pleural cavity.

**PREVENTION:** oblique needling along the intercostal
space (tangential to the rib cage). Maximum safe depth:
10-15 mm.

**Pregnancy:** no specific contraindication.

---

## GB23 — Triếp Cân (辄筋, Zhejin)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry)**
GB23 is 1 cun anterior to GB22, on the lateral chest wall.
Functionally paired with GB22 as a local lateral chest
point. Same T4 intercostal territory.

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): on the lateral chest,
1 cun anterior to GB22, approximately level with the
nipple, in the 4th intercostal space.

Anatomical landmarks: anterior to the mid-axillary line
by ~2 cm. Still on the lateral chest wall, between
serratus anterior fibers.

Spatial relationships:
- GB22 (Yuanye) is 1 cun posterior.
- GB24 (Riyue) is the next point — in the 7th intercostal
  space, a descent of 3 intercostal levels.
- LR14 (Qimen) is nearby — in the 6th intercostal space
  on the mamillary line.
  → Cross-ref: AP-12 LR14

Needle depth: 10-15 mm oblique along the intercostal
space.

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Lateral cutaneous branch of the 4th intercostal nerve
  (T4):** same as GB22.
- Spinal segment: T4.

Muscle layers: skin → subcutaneous → serratus anterior or
pectoralis major (lateral edge) → intercostals → pleura.

### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

No special designations. Like GB22, no meeting point
status.

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM acupoint:** GB23 Zhejin (辄筋, "Sinew Seat").
Functions: regulates Qi, descends rebellious Qi.

**2-8:** similar to GB22. Low cross-tradition
correspondence.

**Convergence score: 2/8** — low.

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Nguồn cổ:
- **Jiayi Jing:** chest fullness (胸满), vomiting with
  acid regurgitation (呕吐酸水), sighing (善太息).

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- Lateral chest pain, intercostal neuralgia
- Vomiting, acid reflux
- Asthma

Kinh nghiệm lâm sàng: limited. Paired with GB22 for
lateral chest conditions.

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

🔴 **Unvalidated** — no evidence.

### An toàn

⚠ **MODERATE risk — pneumothorax point.**

Same risk as GB22. The lung is directly deep. Oblique
needling along the intercostal space. Maximum safe depth:
10-15 mm.

**Pregnancy:** no specific contraindication.

---

## GB24 — Nhật Nguyệt (日月, Riyue)

**Evidence tier: B (Standard Entry)**
GB24 is the Front-Mu point of the Gallbladder — one of only
3 Front-Mu points on their own organ's channel (alongside
LU1 for Lung and LR14 for Liver). Located in the 7th
intercostal space on the mamillary line, directly below
LR14 (6th ICS). GB24 is one half of the DUAL FRONT-MU
arrangement unique to the GB channel (GB24 for GB, GB25 for
KI — see Obs. 14 at GB25).

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): on the upper abdomen,
in the 7th intercostal space, on the mamillary line, 4 cun
lateral to the anterior midline.

Anatomical landmarks: the mamillary line = vertical line
from the nipple. The 7th intercostal space is at the lower
costal margin, where the ribcage begins to curve medially.
GB24 is at the junction of ribcage and upper abdominal
wall in many individuals.

Spatial relationships:
- LR14 (Qimen, Front-Mu of Liver) is ONE intercostal
  space above (6th ICS, same mamillary line). Two Front-Mu
  points in adjacent intercostal spaces — GB organ and
  Liver organ, paired Jueyin-Shaoyang channels.
  → Cross-ref: AP-12 LR14
- GB23 (Zhejin) is the previous point — 4th ICS, 3 inter-
  costal spaces above.
- GB25 (Jingmen) is the next point — at the free end of
  the 12th rib, descending further laterally.
- CV14 (Juque, Front-Mu of Heart) is on the midline at
  approximately the same level.

Needle depth: 10-15 mm oblique, directed inferiorly along
the intercostal space.

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **7th intercostal nerve (T7):** anterior cutaneous and
  lateral cutaneous branches. T7 is within the gallbladder
  viscerotome — visceral afferents from the gallbladder
  travel with sympathetic fibers to T6-T9 segments. GB24
  at T7 is at the CENTER of the gallbladder's spinal
  segmental innervation.
- Spinal segment: T7.
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1, A07 S2.2

**Segmental alignment:** Front-Mu of GB (GB24, T7) sits
at the CENTER of the gallbladder viscerotome (T6-T9).
Compare: LR14 (Front-Mu of Liver, T6) sits at the UPPER
BOUNDARY of the liver viscerotome (T6-T10). Both Wood
organ Front-Mu points are segmentally well-positioned for
visceral reflex access.

Fascial plane: anterior abdominal wall fascia, costal
margin periosteum.

Vascular: 7th intercostal artery branches.

Muscle layers: skin → subcutaneous → external oblique →
internal oblique → transversus abdominis → peritoneum.
At the 7th ICS: costal cartilage and intercostal muscles
may be superficial.

### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

**Front-Mu (募穴) of the Gallbladder.** ON OWN CHANNEL —
the third of only 3 on-channel Front-Mu points.
→ Cross-ref: AP-01 LU1 (on-channel Front-Mu pattern
first documented), AP-12 LR14

**Shu-Mu pairing:** BL19 (Danshu, Back-Shu of GB, at T10)
+ GB24 (Front-Mu of GB, at T7). Back-Shu at T10, Front-Mu
at T7 — a 3-segment gap. Compare: LR14 (T6) + BL18 (T9) =
also 3-segment gap. The Shu-Mu gap for Wood organ pair
(GB+LR) is consistently 3 segments.

Meeting point: GB + SP (some classical sources).

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM acupoint:** GB24 Riyue (日月, "Sun and Moon").
The name reflects paired luminaries — metaphor for the
Gallbladder's role in decision-making (judgment that
distinguishes light from dark). Functions: spreads Liver
Qi, resolves damp-heat, benefits the Gallbladder. Primary
diagnostic point for Gallbladder organ pathology.

**2. Trigger point:** intercostal or external oblique TrPs
in the 7th ICS region. Referred pain: subcostal,
epigastric. Moderate spatial correspondence. 🟡

**3. Ashi correspondence:** right-sided GB24 tenderness
may correlate with gallbladder disease (Murphy's sign area
is nearby, though more properly at the costal margin-rectus
intersection — GB24 is somewhat lateral). 🟡

**4. Marma point:** no direct correspondence.

**5. Head zone:** T7 dermatome — within gallbladder
viscerotome (T6-T9). SEGMENTAL MATCH for gallbladder
visceral reflexes. The most direct Head zone correspondence
for a Front-Mu point in the Atlas. 🟡
→ Cross-ref: A07 S2.2

**6. Anatomy Trains:** the Lateral Line descends through
the lateral trunk. GB24 on the mamillary line is at or
near the LL-DFL (Deep Front Line) interface. 🟡

**7. Neurogenic inflammatory spot:** subcostal neurogenic
inflammation may occur in gallbladder disease (visceral
hyperalgesia zone). Kim 2017 model predicts sensitization
at segmentally aligned surface points during organ
pathology. GB24 at T7 would be within the predicted
gallbladder disease-state zone. 🟡

**8. Cupping point:** abdominal cupping is less common at
this level.

**Convergence score: 5/8** — moderate-strong.

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Nguồn cổ:
- **Jiayi Jing:** vomiting (呕吐), acid reflux (吞酸),
  jaundice (黄疸), hypochondriac pain (胁痛), sighing
  (善太息).
- **Zhenjiu Dacheng:** "Sun and Moon — treats all diseases
  of the Gallbladder. Yellow skin, bitter mouth, vomiting
  bile, hypochondriac pain."

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- Gallbladder: cholecystitis, cholelithiasis
- Liver-Gallbladder: hypochondriac pain, jaundice, bitter
  mouth
- Digestive: vomiting, acid reflux, hiccup
- Emotional: sighing, indecision (Gallbladder spirit)

Kinh nghiệm lâm sàng:
- **Shu-Mu diagnostic pair:** BL19 (tender = chronic GB
  dysfunction) + GB24 (tender = acute GB inflammation).
  Front-Mu tenderness traditionally more acute; Back-Shu
  more chronic.
- **Liver-Gallbladder pair:** GB24 + LR14 = combined
  Liver + Gallbladder treatment. Two adjacent Front-Mu
  points on the same vertical line — Wood organ pair.
  → Cross-ref: AP-12 LR14
- **Damp-heat protocol:** GB24 + GB34 (He-Sea/Lower
  He-Sea) + LR3 = gallbladder damp-heat resolution.

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

🔴 **Unvalidated** — no SR or RCT for GB24 specifically.
Gallbladder acupuncture studies use multi-point protocols
without isolating GB24.

**Indirect evidence:** Front-Mu point tenderness as a
diagnostic tool has some modern support. Visceral referred
pain follows segmental distribution (Beissner et al. 2011),
and GB24 at T7 aligns with gallbladder visceral afferent
territory. GB24's DIAGNOSTIC sensitivity (not therapeutic
efficacy) receives indirect support.

### Dynamic acupoint properties

**Disease-state sensitization:** GB24 may demonstrate
increased tenderness during cholecystitis or gallbladder
dysfunction. The T7 position places GB24 within the
predicted sensitization zone for gallbladder pathology
(Kim 2017 model: organ disease → segmental surface
sensitization).

No acupuncture-specific sensitization study exists for
GB24.
→ Cross-ref: C04 S4

### Cơ chế giải thích (BSA framework attribution)

**Gallbladder diagnostics (SEGMENTAL REFLEX):**
GB24 at T7 → same spinal segment as gallbladder visceral
afferents (T6-T9) → viscerosomatic reflex → cutaneous
hyperalgesia during gallbladder pathology. Standard Head
zone mechanism. GB24 is a TEXTBOOK example of segmental
alignment — one of the most accurate Front-Mu points.

**Gallbladder therapeutics (SOMATOVISCERAL — proposed):**
Needling at GB24 → T7 afferent stimulation → somatovisceral
reflex → modulation of gallbladder sympathetic outflow.
Plausible but NOT specifically demonstrated for GB24.

**Gap:** the "Sun and Moon" / decision-making function
attributed to the Gallbladder in TCM has no modern
correlate. The Gallbladder's Zang-Fu role as "organ of
decision" (奇恒之腑) has no validated neural or endocrine
mechanism.

### Kỹ thuật kích thích + dose-response

Châm (B01): 10-15 mm oblique, directed inferiorly along
the intercostal space.

De Qi: distending, aching at the subcostal region, some-
times with deep pulling sensation.
→ Cross-ref: B01 S5.2

EA: GB24-GB24 bilateral for gallbladder conditions.

Bấm huyệt (B02): moderate pressure. Used in Shu-Mu
diagnostic palpation — comparing tenderness bilaterally
and with BL19.
→ Cross-ref: B02

Cứu (B08): indirect moxa for chronic gallbladder with
Cold-Dampness pattern.

### An toàn

⚠ **LOW-MODERATE risk.**

At the 7th intercostal space, the lung margin has typically
ascended above — pneumothorax risk LOWER than GB22/GB23.
However:

**Right-side caution:** the liver extends to ~5th ICS on
the mid-clavicular line. At the 7th ICS, the liver margin
may be nearby. Deep needling on the right could potentially
reach hepatic tissue.

**Left-side caution:** the spleen is deep on the left
side, but typically more posterior. Lower risk.

**Pregnancy:** no specific contraindication.

---

## GB25 — Kinh Môn (京门, Jingmen)

**Evidence tier: B (Standard Entry)**
GB25 is the Front-Mu point of the Kidney — hosted on the
GB channel. Located at the free end of the 12th rib (the
last floating rib). Together with GB24, GB25 makes GB the
ONLY meridian hosting two Front-Mu points: one for its own
organ (GB24, Gallbladder) and one for another organ (GB25,
Kidney). One of 3 cross-channel-hosted Front-Mu points
(alongside ST25 for LI, LR13 for SP).

**Observation 14 — Dual Front-Mu Channel:**

GB is the ONLY regular meridian hosting two Front-Mu
points: GB24 (Front-Mu of GB, T7) and GB25 (Front-Mu of
KI, T12). The anatomical logic: GB's lateral trunk
trajectory is uniquely LONG, descending from the 4th
intercostal space (GB22) to the 12th rib (GB25) — spanning
~8 intercostal levels on the lateral body wall. This is the
widest lateral trunk range of any channel.

The 3 on-channel Front-Mu points (LU1 at T2, LR14 at T6,
GB24 at T7) sit on channels that traverse their organ's
segmental territory. The 3 cross-hosted Front-Mu points
(ST25 at T10 for LI, LR13 at T6 for SP, GB25 at T12 for
KI) sit on channels that traverse another organ's
segmental territory. GB is the only channel appearing in
BOTH lists — hosting its own Front-Mu AND another organ's
Front-Mu. The dual-hosting requires a channel that covers
a wide enough segmental range to encompass two different
organs' viscerotomes. Only GB's lateral trajectory
(T4→T12) achieves this.

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): on the lateral
abdomen, at the lower border of the free end of the 12th
rib (last floating rib).

Anatomical landmarks: the 12th rib is the shortest rib
and floats freely (no anterior cartilaginous attachment).
Its free (anterior) end is palpable in the posterolateral
flank. GB25 is at the TIP of this rib — a reliable bony
landmark. Approximately at L1-L2 vertebral level.

Spatial relationships:
- GB24 (Riyue, Front-Mu of GB) is superior and anterior —
  7th ICS on the mamillary line. GB24→GB25 = descending
  and posteriorly, spanning 5 intercostal levels.
- LR13 (Zhangmen, Front-Mu of SP, Hui-Meeting of Zang)
  is at the free end of the 11th rib. Two Front-Mu points
  at adjacent floating rib tips: LR13 (11th) and GB25
  (12th).
  → Cross-ref: AP-12 LR13
- GB26 (Daimai) is the next point — level with the navel,
  below LR13.
- BL23 (Shenshu, Back-Shu of KI) is at L2 posteriorly —
  the Shu-Mu partner.

Needle depth: 10-15 mm perpendicular or slightly oblique
anteriorly. The kidney is deep (5-8 cm) on both sides.

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Subcostal nerve (T12):** primary nerve. Anterior ramus
  of T12 courses below the 12th rib. At GB25, the lateral
  cutaneous branch provides sensory innervation.
- **Iliohypogastric nerve (L1):** may contribute at this
  level.
- Spinal segment: T12-L1.
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1

**Segmental alignment:** kidney visceral afferents travel
with sympathetic fibers to T10-L1. GB25 at T12 sits within
the kidney viscerotome. BL23 (Back-Shu of KI) at L2 is at
the lower boundary. The Front-Mu (T12) and Back-Shu (L2)
bracket the kidney's segmental territory from different
body sides.

Fascial plane: thoracolumbar fascia (posterior layer)
transitions to lateral abdominal wall fascia. The 12th rib
serves as a fascial attachment point.

Vascular: subcostal artery (travels with T12 nerve). The
kidney is ~5-8 cm deep.

Muscle layers: skin → subcutaneous → external oblique →
internal oblique → transversus abdominis → retroperitoneal
fat → kidney.

### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

**Front-Mu (募穴) of the Kidney.** Cross-channel hosting:
the Kidney channel (KI) runs along the anterior midline
and medial leg — it does NOT traverse the lateral flank
where the kidney organ sits. GB's lateral trajectory
crosses the kidney's segmental territory at T12.
→ Cross-ref: AP-01 LU1 (Front-Mu hosting patterns)

**Shu-Mu pairing:** BL23 (Shenshu, Back-Shu of KI, at L2)
+ GB25 (Jingmen, Front-Mu of KI, at T12). Back-Shu at L2,
Front-Mu at T12 — a 2-segment gap. Compare: GB24 (T7) +
BL19 (T10) = 3-segment gap for GB. The Kidney Shu-Mu gap
is narrower, reflecting the kidney's more compact
viscerotome.

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM acupoint:** GB25 Jingmen (京门, "Capital Gate").
Functions: strengthens the Kidney, regulates water passages,
benefits the lower back. Front-Mu diagnostic point for
Kidney organ pathology.

**2. Trigger point:** quadratus lumborum TrPs may be in
this region (deep). QL TrPs refer pain to the lateral hip,
sacroiliac region, and lower abdomen. The 12th rib tip is
a QL attachment point. Moderate correspondence. 🟡

**3. Ashi correspondence:** flank tenderness at the 12th
rib tip can occur in renal conditions (costovertebral angle
tenderness is more posterior at BL23, but lateral flank
pain extends to GB25). 🟡

**4. Marma point:** Parshva Marma (पार्श्व) — the lateral
flank. Approximate correspondence. 🟡

**5. Head zone:** T12 dermatome — within the kidney
viscerotome (T10-L1). Segmental match. 🟡
→ Cross-ref: A07 S2.2

**6. Anatomy Trains:** the Lateral Line descends through
the lateral trunk via external/internal obliques. GB25 at
the 12th rib tip is a bony anchor point for lateral trunk
myofascia. 🟡

**7. Neurogenic inflammatory spot:** renal disease may
produce neurogenic inflammation in T10-L1 dermatomes. GB25
at T12 within this zone. Not specifically documented. 🟡

**8. Cupping point:** flank cupping practiced in some
traditions for kidney/lower back conditions.

**Convergence score: 5/8** — moderate-strong.

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Nguồn cổ:
- **Jiayi Jing:** lumbar pain (腰痛), abdominal distension
  (腹胀), diarrhea (泄泻), borborygmus (肠鸣).
- **Zhenjiu Dacheng:** "Capital Gate — treats kidney
  disease, lumbar pain, difficult urination, edema."

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- Kidney: nephritis, renal colic
- Urinary: difficult urination, edema
- Digestive: abdominal distension, diarrhea, borborygmus
- Musculoskeletal: lumbar pain, intercostal neuralgia

Kinh nghiệm lâm sàng:
- **Shu-Mu diagnostic pair:** BL23 (tender = chronic
  Kidney Qi deficiency) + GB25 (tender = acute kidney
  condition, renal colic). Bilateral comparison standard.
- **Kidney tonification:** GB25 + BL23 + KI3 (Taixi,
  Yuan-Source of KI) = comprehensive kidney treatment
  (front + back + distal).
- **LR13-GB25 adjacent Front-Mu pair:** LR13 (Front-Mu
  of SP, 11th rib) + GB25 (Front-Mu of KI, 12th rib) =
  two Front-Mu points at adjacent rib tips. Used together
  for Spleen-Kidney deficiency pattern.
  → Cross-ref: AP-12 LR13

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

🔴 **Unvalidated** — no SR or RCT for GB25 specifically.

### Dynamic acupoint properties

**Disease-state sensitization:** GB25 may show increased
tenderness during renal pathology (renal colic,
nephrolithiasis, nephritis). T12 segmental alignment
predicts viscerosomatic reflex sensitization during kidney
disease. No acupoint-specific data.
→ Cross-ref: C04 S4

### Cơ chế giải thích (BSA framework attribution)

**Kidney diagnostics (SEGMENTAL REFLEX):**
GB25 at T12 → same segment as kidney visceral afferents
(T10-L1) → viscerosomatic reflex → cutaneous hyperalgesia
during kidney pathology. Standard Head zone mechanism.

**Kidney therapeutics (SOMATOVISCERAL — proposed):**
Needling at GB25 → T12 afferent stimulation →
somatovisceral reflex → modulation of renal sympathetic
outflow. The kidney receives sympathetic innervation from
T10-L1; GB25 at T12 provides segmental access.

Renal sympathetic modulation is an active research area
(renal denervation for hypertension, Symplicity HTN
trials). GB25 provides NON-INVASIVE access to the same
segmental territory that catheter-based renal denervation
targets — though stimulation intensity is orders of
magnitude lower.

**Gap:** GB25's role in "Kidney Qi tonification" has no
modern correlate. Segmental reflex explains DIAGNOSTIC
sensitivity but does NOT validate THERAPEUTIC tonification
through surface stimulation.

### Kỹ thuật kích thích + dose-response

Châm (B01): 10-15 mm perpendicular or slightly oblique
anteriorly. The 12th rib tip provides a natural bony stop,
reducing risk of deep penetration.

De Qi: aching at the flank, sometimes radiating to lower
back or upper hip.
→ Cross-ref: B01 S5.2

Bấm huyệt (B02): moderate pressure at the 12th rib tip.
Used in Front-Mu diagnostic palpation.
→ Cross-ref: B02

Cứu (B08): indirect moxa for chronic Kidney deficiency
with Cold pattern (lumbar pain, edema, weak knees).

### An toàn

⚠ **LOW-MODERATE risk.**

**Kidney depth:** ~5-8 cm deep. Standard needle depth
(10-15 mm) does not approach the kidney. Deep needling
(>30 mm) in thin individuals could potentially reach
renal capsule.

**12th rib protection:** the free end of the 12th rib
provides a natural depth stop — the needle contacts rib
periosteum before reaching deeper structures. This makes
GB25 relatively safer than surrounding soft-tissue points.

**Pregnancy:** no specific contraindication.

---

**Observation 15 — Neural Zone Acceleration (Head vs
Body):**

The 20 head points (GB1-GB20) covered 6 neural zones
across cranial nerves and upper cervical segments:
V1/V2 orbital → V3 temporal → C2-C3 occipital → V1
forehead → V1/C2 transition → C1-C3 suboccipital. Zone
transition rate: 0.3 zones per point (one new zone every
~3.3 points).

The 5 shoulder-trunk points (GB21-GB25) cover 4 NEW
zones: CN XI/C3-C4 shoulder → T4 lateral chest → T7
subcostal → T12-L1 flank. Zone transition rate: 0.8
zones per point (nearly one new zone per point) — a
2.7× acceleration.

This partially answers Key Question #1 (why 20 head
points?): the head achieves anatomical DENSITY through
geometric subdivision within the same neural zone — 45%
of head points are interpolations between functionally
distinctive anchors (Obs. 3, 5, 10). The body achieves
functional DIVERSITY through rapid zone transitions
where almost every point enters new segmental territory.

The head's density reflects the trigeminal/cervical
sensory map's clinical complexity (migraine, facial pain,
vertigo — multiple conditions within a small area). The
body's rapid descent reflects the regular segmental
ladder of the thoracic spinal cord, where each segment
maps to a distinct horizontal band on the trunk.

Updated neural zone table (10 zones through 25 points):

| Zone | Points | Primary Nerve | Segment |
|------|--------|---------------|---------|
| 1 | GB1 | Lacrimal/Zygomatic | CN V1/V2 |
| 2 | GB2-GB8 | Auriculotemporal | CN V3 |
| 3 | GB9-GB12 | Occipital nerves | C2-C3 |
| 4 | GB13-GB15 | Supraorbital | CN V1 |
| 5 | GB16-GB19 | V1→C2 transition | V1/C2 |
| 6 | GB20 | Deep suboccipital | C1-C3+XI |
| 7 | GB21 | Supraclavicular+XI | C3-C4+XI |
| 8 | GB22-GB23 | Intercostal | T4 |
| 9 | GB24 | Intercostal | T7 |
| 10 | GB25 | Subcostal | T12-L1 |

10 zones in 25 points. Remaining 19 points (GB26-GB44)
will traverse pelvis, thigh, leg, and foot — adding
estimated 3-5 more zones. GB is on track to exceed 12+
total neural zones, far surpassing TE's record of 8.

---

## GB26 — Đới Mạch (带脉, Dài Mài)

**Evidence tier: B (Standard Entry)**
"Belt Vessel" (带脉) — 带 (Dài, belt/girdle) + 脉 (Mài,
vessel/channel). GB26 is the ONLY acupoint in the 361-point
system that shares its name with an extraordinary vessel.
The Dai Mai (Belt Vessel) is the sole horizontal vessel in
the body — all 11 other regular and extraordinary channels
run longitudinally. GB26 marks where this unique horizontal
pathway intersects the GB channel's lateral trunk descent.

GB26 is the first of three consecutive GB-Dai Mai meeting
points (GB26→GB27→GB28) that trace the Dai Mai's course
from the waist to the anterior iliac spine. Together with
GB41 (which OPENS the Dai Mai as Eight Confluent point on
the foot), these four points constitute GB's role as the
primary host channel for Dai Mai access.

Meeting point: GB + Dai Mai.

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): on the lateral side of
the abdomen, at the crossing point of the vertical line
through the free end of the 11th rib and the horizontal
line through the centre of the umbilicus.

Anatomical landmarks: level with the navel (umbilicus), on
the lateral abdominal wall, directly below the free end of
the 11th rib (below LR13 Zhangmen). At the junction of the
lateral and anterior abdominal wall.

Spatial relationships:
- LR13 (Zhangmen, Front-Mu of SP, Hui-Meeting of Zang) is
  directly ABOVE — at the free end of the 11th rib.
  → Cross-ref: AP-12 LR13
- GB25 (Jingmen, Front-Mu of KI) is superior and posterior
  — at the free end of the 12th rib. GB25→GB26 crosses from
  posterolateral flank to the lateral abdomen.
- GB27 (Wushu) is the next Dai Mai point — anterior to the
  ASIS, inferior to GB26.

Needle depth: 15-25 mm perpendicular.

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Subcostal nerve (T12):** lateral cutaneous branch. At
  navel level on the lateral abdomen, the T12 lateral
  cutaneous branch provides primary sensory innervation.
- **T11 intercostal nerve:** lateral cutaneous branch may
  contribute (dermatomal overlap — the navel is at T10
  anteriorly, but on the lateral body the dermatomes slope
  inferiorly, placing T11-T12 territory at this horizontal
  level).
- Spinal segment: T11-T12.
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1

Fascial plane: external oblique → internal oblique →
transversus abdominis. All three lateral abdominal wall
muscles converge in this region. The thoracolumbar fascia's
lateral raphe — where posterior and lateral fascial systems
meet — is nearby.

Vascular: deep circumflex iliac artery branches, lower
intercostal arteries.

Muscle layers: skin → subcutaneous fat (variable thickness)
→ external oblique → internal oblique → transversus
abdominis → peritoneum.

### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

**Meeting point: GB + Dai Mai (带脉).** First of 3
GB-Dai Mai meeting points (GB26, GB27, GB28). The Dai Mai
is the only vessel that encircles the body horizontally —
its trajectory crosses all longitudinal channels at the
waist.

The Dai Mai's traditional function is to "bind" (束) the
longitudinal channels. Pathology: 带下 (leukorrhea,
literally "below the belt"), lumbar weakness, abdominal
distension. Dai Mai dysfunction = loss of binding force —
structures descend, fluids leak, the waist weakens.

**GB41 (Zulinqi) as the Eight Confluent opener:** GB41 on
the foot OPENS the Dai Mai; GB26-28 at the waist ARE the
Dai Mai territory. The opener is distal (foot); the targets
are local (waist). This distal-to-local architecture
parallels other Eight Confluent pairs.
→ Cross-ref: AP-10 TE5 (opens Yang Wei Mai)

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM acupoint:** GB26 Daimai (带脉, "Belt Vessel").
Functions: regulates the Dai Mai, benefits the lower Jiao,
resolves damp-heat, stops leukorrhea. Primary point for
Dai Mai pathology.

**2. Trigger point:** external oblique or internal oblique
TrPs in the lateral abdominal wall at navel level. Referred
pain: lateral trunk, wrapping around to lower abdomen.
Moderate correspondence. 🟡

**3. Ashi correspondence:** lateral waist tenderness in
lumbar disc pathology or abdominal wall strain. Not specific
to GB26. 🟡

**4. Marma point:** Parshva Marma (पार्श्व, lateral flank)
— approximate correspondence. Same Marma as GB25 region. 🟡

**5. Head zone:** T11-T12 dermatome at the lateral abdomen.
Non-specific — multiple organs project to this segmental
zone. 🟡

**6. Anatomy Trains:** the Lateral Line (LL) descends
through the lateral trunk via the oblique muscles. GB26 sits
on the LL pathway. The LL is primarily a lateral/rotational
stabilizer — functional overlap with the Dai Mai concept of
circumferential binding. 🟡

**7. Neurogenic inflammatory spot:** no specific data.

**8. Cupping point:** waist cupping for lower back pain is
practiced.

**Convergence score: 4/8** — moderate.

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Nguồn cổ:
- **Nan Jing (Ch. 29):** "When the Dai Mai is diseased,
  the abdomen is full and the lumbar region feels as if
  sitting in water."
- **Jiayi Jing:** leukorrhea (带下), irregular menstruation,
  abdominal pain, lumbar pain.
- **Zhenjiu Dacheng:** "Belt Vessel — treats women's
  leukorrhea, menstrual irregularities, and all belt-region
  disorders."

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- Gynecological: leukorrhea 🔴, irregular menstruation 🔴,
  dysmenorrhea 🔴
- Abdominal: abdominal distension, lateral abdominal pain
- Musculoskeletal: lumbar pain, lateral trunk pain
- Dai Mai regulation: prolapse (classical) 🔴

Kinh nghiệm lâm sàng:
- **Dai Mai protocol:** GB26 + GB27 + GB28 (local belt) +
  GB41 (distal opener, Eight Confluent). Standard Dai Mai
  prescription.
- **Leukorrhea formula:** GB26 + SP6 + SP9 + BL30.
  Dai Mai binding + Spleen dampness resolution.
- **Lumbar support:** GB26 + BL23 + GV4. Belt reinforcement
  + Kidney support.

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

🔴 **Unvalidated** — no SR or RCT for GB26 specifically.

The Dai Mai vessel as a concept has received attention in
myofascial research — the lateral abdominal wall muscles
(external oblique + internal oblique + transversus
abdominis) provide a real horizontal binding structure at
the waist. However, no study has tested whether GB26
stimulation specifically affects this fascial system or
produces measurable effects on Dai Mai-attributed conditions.

### Dynamic acupoint properties

**Belt zone sensitization:** the lateral abdominal wall may
become tender in gynecological conditions (endometriosis,
ovarian pathology) or lumbar disc disease. GB26 at T11-T12
is within the zone that can show referred tenderness from
pelvic or lumbar structures. Not specifically studied.
→ Cross-ref: C04 S4

### Cơ chế giải thích (BSA framework attribution)

**Abdominal/pelvic indications (SEGMENTAL REFLEX):**
GB26 at T11-T12 → shares segmental territory with upper
ureter, lower kidney, and upper pelvic structures.
Somatovisceral reflexes through T11-T12 could modulate
sympathetic outflow to these organs.

**Lumbar pain (PERIPHERAL NEURAL):**
Subcostal nerve (T12) lateral cutaneous branch stimulation
→ may modulate lateral trunk pain. Same-segment treatment
for T11-T12 dermatome.

**Dai Mai "binding" function (NO MECHANISM):**
The Dai Mai concept of horizontal channel governance — belt
dysfunction → descent/leakage — has no validated
physiological correlate. The fascial belt at the waist is
real (abdominal wall muscles), but no evidence links GB26
stimulation to fascial tone modulation.

**Gap:** the Dai Mai concept is the MOST UNIQUE
extraordinary vessel — the only horizontal circuit in a
longitudinal system. Its anatomical substrate (fascial?
neural? postural?) remains entirely uncharacterized.

### Kỹ thuật kích thích + dose-response

Châm (B01): 15-25 mm perpendicular.

De Qi: distending, aching at the lateral waist, sometimes
described as a band-like sensation around the trunk.
→ Cross-ref: B01 S5.2

Bấm huyệt (B02): moderate pressure. Belt-zone palpation
sometimes reveals a tender band at waist level.
→ Cross-ref: B02

Cứu (B08): indirect moxa for Cold-Dampness patterns
(leukorrhea, lumbar cold).

### An toàn

⚠ **LOW risk.**

No vital structures at standard needle depth. Peritoneum is
deep (3-4 cm through three muscle layers + fat). Variable
subcutaneous fat provides buffer.

**Pregnancy:** no specific contraindication listed, but Dai
Mai stimulation regulates menstruation — CAUTION in early
pregnancy (theoretical).

---

## GB27 — Ngũ Xu (五枢, Wǔ Shū)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry)**
"Five Pivots" (五枢) — 五 (Wǔ, five) + 枢 (Shū,
pivot/hinge). The name suggests a pivotal junction where
multiple structures converge. Meeting point: GB + Dai Mai.
Second of three consecutive GB-Dai Mai meeting points
(GB26→GB27→GB28), continuing the Dai Mai belt's descent
from navel level toward the iliac crest.

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): on the lateral side of
the abdomen, in the region of the anterior superior iliac
spine (ASIS), anterior and superior to it.

Anatomical landmarks: anterior to the ASIS, approximately
3 cun below GB26 and 0.5 cun anterior to the ASIS. The
ASIS is a palpable bony prominence of the iliac crest.

Spatial relationships:
- GB26 (Daimai) is superior — at navel level. GB26→GB27
  descends ~3 cun and moves anteriorly.
- GB28 (Weidao) is inferior — anterior-inferior to ASIS.
  Close to GB27 (~0.5 cun).
- LR12 (Jimai) is medial — in the inguinal fold. Two
  channels (GB laterally, LR medially) approach the groin
  from different angles.
  → Cross-ref: AP-12 LR12

Needle depth: 15-20 mm perpendicular or slightly oblique.

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Iliohypogastric nerve (L1):** lateral cutaneous branch.
  Emerges above the iliac crest and descends over the ASIS.
- **Ilioinguinal nerve (L1):** may contribute at this level.
- Spinal segment: L1.
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1

Fascial: external oblique aponeurosis, inguinal ligament
origin zone. The ASIS is the lateral attachment of the
inguinal ligament.

Vascular: superficial circumflex iliac artery.

Muscle layers: skin → subcutaneous → external oblique
aponeurosis → internal oblique → transversus abdominis.

### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

**Meeting point: GB + Dai Mai.** Second of 3 Dai Mai
meeting points. The Dai Mai belt descends from the waist
(GB26) toward the iliac crest (GB27-28).

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM acupoint:** GB27 Wushu (五枢, "Five Pivots").
Functions: regulates the Dai Mai, benefits the lower Jiao,
moves Qi in the lower abdomen.

**2. Trigger point:** external oblique TrPs near the ASIS,
or iliacus TrPs (deep). 🟡

**3. Ashi correspondence:** ASIS tenderness in hip flexor
strain or iliac crest pain syndrome.

**4. Marma point:** no direct correspondence.

**5. Head zone:** L1 dermatome — inguinal region. 🟡

**6. Anatomy Trains:** Lateral Line attachment at the iliac
crest. 🟡

**7. Neurogenic inflammatory spot:** not documented.

**8. Cupping point:** not commonly applied at the ASIS.

**Convergence score: 2/8** — low.

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Nguồn cổ:
- **Jiayi Jing:** lower abdominal pain (少腹痛), hernia
  (疝), leukorrhea (带下), constipation.
- **Zhenjiu Dacheng:** "Five Pivots — treats hernia,
  leukorrhea, lower abdominal fullness."

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- Gynecological: leukorrhea 🔴, irregular menstruation 🔴
- Abdominal: lower abdominal pain, hernia (classical)
- Musculoskeletal: lateral hip pain

Kinh nghiệm lâm sàng:
- **Dai Mai belt protocol:** GB26 + GB27 + GB28 + GB41.
- **Hernia support:** GB27 + LR5 + ST29 (classical).

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

🔴 **Unvalidated** — no SR or RCT for GB27.

### An toàn

⚠ **LOW risk.**

ASIS provides bony depth reference. No major vessels or
organs at standard depth. The inguinal canal opening is
medial and inferior — not at risk from lateral needling.

---

## GB28 — Duy Đạo (维道, Wéi Dào)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry)**
"Linking Path" (维道) — 维 (Wéi, to link/maintain) + 道
(Dào, path/way). The name suggests a connecting pathway —
possibly the Dai Mai's function of linking longitudinal
channels. Meeting point: GB + Dai Mai. Third and last of
the three consecutive GB-Dai Mai meeting points
(GB26→GB27→GB28).

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): on the lateral side of
the lower abdomen, anterior and inferior to the anterior
superior iliac spine (ASIS).

Anatomical landmarks: approximately 0.5 cun anterior-
inferior to ASIS. Below and slightly anterior to GB27.
In the transition zone between the lateral abdomen and
the anterior hip.

Spatial relationships:
- GB27 (Wushu) is directly superior — at the ASIS. Short
  descent (~0.5 cun).
- GB29 (Juliao) is inferior and lateral — at midpoint
  ASIS-to-greater trochanter. GB28→GB29 transitions from
  the Dai Mai belt to the hip region.

Needle depth: 15-20 mm perpendicular or slightly oblique.

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Iliohypogastric nerve (L1):** anterior cutaneous branch.
- **Ilioinguinal nerve (L1):** may contribute.
- Spinal segment: L1.
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1

Fascial: inguinal ligament origin area, external oblique
aponeurosis transition to fascia lata.

Vascular: superficial circumflex iliac artery branches.

Muscle layers: external oblique aponeurosis → internal
oblique → transversus abdominis.

### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

**Meeting point: GB + Dai Mai.** Third and final Dai Mai
meeting point. With GB28, the Dai Mai belt terminates —
the GB channel continues into the hip (GB29) and buttock
(GB30), leaving Dai Mai territory.

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM acupoint:** GB28 Weidao (维道, "Linking Path").
Functions: regulates the Dai Mai, moves Qi, resolves
dampness in the lower Jiao.

**2. Trigger point:** tensor fasciae latae (TFL) or
external oblique TrPs in the ASIS region. 🟡

**3. Ashi correspondence:** tenderness in inguinal or
groin strain.

**4. Marma point:** no direct correspondence.

**5. Head zone:** L1 dermatome. 🟡

**6. Anatomy Trains:** Lateral Line / Spiral Line
intersection at the ASIS region. 🟡

**7. Neurogenic inflammatory spot:** not documented.

**8. Cupping point:** not commonly applied.

**Convergence score: 2/8** — low.

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Nguồn cổ:
- **Jiayi Jing:** leukorrhea (带下), lower abdominal pain
  (少腹痛), hernia, lumbar pain.
- **Zhenjiu Dacheng:** similar to GB27 — Dai Mai disorders,
  lower abdominal fullness.

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- Gynecological: leukorrhea 🔴, prolapse 🔴
- Abdominal: lower abdominal pain, hernia (classical)
- Musculoskeletal: hip pain, lateral trunk pain

Kinh nghiệm lâm sàng:
- **Dai Mai protocol:** GB26 + GB27 + GB28 + GB41
  (standard). GB28 is the least individually referenced
  of the three.
- **Prolapse support:** GB28 + GV20 (Baihui, raising Qi)
  + ST36 for organ prolapse.

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

🔴 **Unvalidated** — no SR or RCT for GB28.

### An toàn

⚠ **LOW risk.**

Similar to GB27. ASIS nearby. No major vessels at standard
depth. The femoral nerve, artery, and vein (femoral
triangle) are medial — not at risk from lateral needling.

---

**Observation 16 — Dai Mai Belt = Iliac Crest Muscle
Attachment Line:**

The three GB-Dai Mai meeting points (GB26-GB28) trace a
descending arc from navel level to the anterior iliac spine:

| Point | Position | Primary Nerve | Segment |
|-------|----------|---------------|---------|
| GB26 | Navel level, lateral | Subcostal (lat. cut.) | T11-T12 |
| GB27 | At ASIS | Iliohypogastric | L1 |
| GB28 | Anterior-inferior to ASIS | Iliohypogastric | L1 |

This path follows the anterior iliac crest — the attachment
line of the three lateral abdominal wall muscles:

| Muscle | Iliac Crest Attachment | Function |
|--------|----------------------|----------|
| External oblique | Anterior half | Trunk rotation, compression |
| Internal oblique | Lateral two-thirds | Counter-rotation, compression |
| Transversus abdominis | Inner lip, full crest | Circumferential compression |

The Dai Mai's traditional function — "binding" (束)
longitudinal channels — maps to the biomechanical role of
these muscles: circumferential compression and rotational
stability at the waist. The Dai Mai's pathology (descent/
prolapse, leakage/leukorrhea, waist weakness) describes
loss of this muscular binding force.

However, the three GB-Dai Mai meeting points cover only the
LATERAL quadrant (~90°) of the circumference. The Dai Mai
traditionally encircles the entire waist (360°). Either:
(a) GB26-28 mark where the belt is most ACCESSIBLE to
surface stimulation — the lateral abdominal wall is thin
and superficial compared to the posterior paraspinal mass;
or (b) the complete Dai Mai circuit involves trajectories
not formally mapped in the regular channel system.

This partially answers Key Question #6: the Dai Mai belt
has a real fascial substrate (lateral abdominal wall
musculature converging on the iliac crest), but the full
circumferential neural/fascial circuit remains
uncharacterized. 🟡

---

## GB29 — Cư Liêu (居髎, Jū Liáo)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry)**
"Squatting Bone-Hole" (居髎) — 居 (Jū, to sit/squat) + 髎
(Liáo, bone depression/foramen). Named for the bony
depression palpable when the hip is flexed — GB29 sits
between ASIS and greater trochanter, in a depression that
deepens with squatting. Meeting point: GB + Yang Qiao Mai.

GB29 is the transition point between the Dai Mai belt
(GB26-28) and the deep gluteal zone (GB30). It marks where
the GB channel shifts from the abdominal wall to the hip —
from fascial territory to deep muscular/neural territory.

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): on the hip, at the
midpoint of the line connecting the anterior superior iliac
spine (ASIS) and the highest prominence of the greater
trochanter.

Anatomical landmarks: midpoint ASIS-to-greater trochanter.
In the depression between the tensor fasciae latae
(anteriorly) and the gluteus medius (posteriorly). Palpable
depression deepens with hip flexion.

Spatial relationships:
- GB28 (Weidao) is superior and medial — anterior-inferior
  to ASIS.
- GB30 (Huantiao) is posterior and inferior — at the
  lateral 1/3 junction of the trochanter-to-sacral hiatus
  line. GB29 and GB30 approach the greater trochanter from
  DIFFERENT ANGLES: GB29 from the front (ASIS side), GB30
  from behind (sacral side).
- ST31 (Biguan) is anterior — on the anterior thigh.

Needle depth: 20-40 mm perpendicular or slightly posterior.

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Lateral femoral cutaneous nerve (L2-L3):** primary
  cutaneous supply to the lateral hip and thigh.
- **Superior gluteal nerve (L4-S1):** deep — motor to
  gluteus medius and minimus, which underlie this point.
- Spinal segment: L2-L3 (cutaneous), L4-S1 (deep motor).
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1

Fascial: fascia lata (deep fascia of the thigh), iliotibial
tract (IT band) originates from the iliac crest and passes
through this area.

Vascular: ascending branch of the lateral circumflex
femoral artery (deep).

Muscle layers: skin → subcutaneous → tensor fasciae latae /
gluteus medius → gluteus minimus → hip joint capsule (deep,
~4-6 cm).

### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

**Meeting point: GB + Yang Qiao Mai (阳跷脉).** The Yang
Qiao Mai governs the Yang (lateral) aspect of stance and
gait. GB29 at the lateral hip — the primary zone of lateral
stability during walking — aligns with Yang Qiao Mai's
functional domain.
→ Cross-ref: GB20 (also meets Yang Qiao Mai, at the head)

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM acupoint:** GB29 Juliao (居髎, "Squatting
Bone-Hole"). Functions: benefits the hip joint, relaxes
sinews, alleviates lateral hip and leg pain.

**2. Trigger point:** gluteus medius TrP1 — one of the MOST
COMMON trigger points in the body (Travell & Simons 1999).
Referred pain: lateral hip, posterior iliac crest, sometimes
radiating to lateral thigh. STRONG correspondence — GB29
sits directly over the gluteus medius belly. 🟡
→ Cross-ref: GB21 (upper trapezius TrP1)

**3. Ashi correspondence:** greater trochanteric pain
syndrome (GTPS) — tenderness at this exact region is the
hallmark finding. GTPS includes trochanteric bursitis and
gluteal tendinopathy. 🟡

**4. Marma point:** Katika Taruna Marma (कटिकतरुण) — hip
joint region. Approximate correspondence. 🟡

**5. Head zone:** L2-L3 dermatome — lateral hip/thigh. 🟡

**6. Anatomy Trains:** the Lateral Line descends through
the IT band and TFL. GB29 at the ASIS-trochanter midpoint
sits directly on the LL's path through the hip. 🟡

**7. Neurogenic inflammatory spot:** lateral hip
sensitization in lumbar radiculopathy (L2-L3 territory) or
hip joint pathology. 🟡

**8. Cupping point:** lateral hip cupping for hip pain is
practiced.

**Convergence score: 5/8** — moderate-strong. Highest for
a Tier C point in the GB channel. The gluteus medius TrP
and GTPS correspondences drive the score.

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Nguồn cổ:
- **Jiayi Jing:** lateral hip pain (髀枢中痛), lumbar pain,
  inability to squat or rise, leg weakness, leg atrophy.
- **Zhenjiu Dacheng:** "Squatting Bone-Hole — treats Wind-
  Damp Bi of the hip, lumbar cold, leg paralysis."

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- Musculoskeletal: hip joint pain 🔴, lateral hip pain 🔴,
  greater trochanteric pain
- Neurological: hemiplegia (lower limb), leg weakness
- Lumbar: lower back pain radiating to hip

Kinh nghiệm lâm sàng:
- **Lateral hip protocol:** GB29 + GB30 + GB34 (He-Sea /
  Hui-Meeting Tendons). Standard three-point lateral hip
  treatment.
- **Yang Qiao Mai regulation:** GB29 + BL62 (Shenmai,
  Eight Confluent opener of Yang Qiao Mai) for lateral
  postural dysfunction.
- **GTPS approach:** GB29 + local Ashi + GB34. Modern
  clinical adaptation.

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

🔴 **Unvalidated** — no SR or RCT for GB29 specifically.

GTPS has been studied with acupuncture, but protocols use
multiple points without isolating GB29. GB29's location
places it directly over the gluteus medius insertion — the
primary structure in GTPS pathology.

### An toàn

⚠ **LOW-MODERATE risk.**

**Depth concern:** the hip joint capsule is ~4-6 cm deep
(through gluteal muscles). Standard needle depth (20-40 mm)
does not typically reach the capsule.

**Vascular:** the ascending branch of the lateral circumflex
femoral artery is in the area but typically deep.

**Pregnancy:** no specific contraindication.

---

## GB30 — Hoàn Khiêu (环跳, Huán Tiào)

**Evidence tier: A (Deep Analysis)**
"Jumping Ring" (环跳) — 环 (Huán, ring/circle) + 跳 (Tiào,
to jump/leap). The name describes the circular movement
used to locate this point: the patient lies on their side
with the upper leg flexed, creating a "ring" shape, and the
needle is inserted at a depression that appears during this
position. Alternatively, 环跳 refers to the circular motion
of the hip joint during jumping — the ball-and-socket joint
that permits the widest range of motion in the lower body.

GB30 is one of the most clinically important points in the
entire acupuncture system. It is:
1. A **Ma Dan-yang Heavenly Star Point** (马丹阳天星十二穴)
   — one of 12 points selected by the Song Dynasty physician
   Ma Dan-yang as the most essential for clinical practice.
2. A **meeting point of GB + BL** — two Yang channels
   (Shaoyang + Taiyang) converging at the buttock.
3. The primary access point for the **sciatic nerve** — the
   largest peripheral nerve in the human body (L4-S3),
   carrying fibers from 6 spinal segments through a single
   trunk.

GB30 accesses the sciatic nerve at a uniquely favorable
location: the nerve exits the pelvis through the greater
sciatic foramen, passes deep to the piriformis muscle, and
runs between the piriformis (superiorly) and the obturator
internus/superior gemellus (inferiorly). GB30 targets this
anatomical "bottleneck" where the nerve is concentrated
BEFORE its bifurcation into tibial and common peroneal
divisions. Pre-bifurcation access means a single needle can
reach fibers destined for the ENTIRE posterior and lateral
lower limb — from the posterior thigh to the plantar foot.

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): on the buttock, at the
junction of the lateral one-third and medial two-thirds of
the distance between the prominence of the greater
trochanter and the sacral hiatus (GV2, Yaoshu).

Anatomical landmarks: patient in lateral decubitus position
with upper hip flexed. The point is at the junction of the
lateral 1/3 and medial 2/3 of the line from the most
prominent point of the greater trochanter to the sacral
hiatus. Deep to the gluteus maximus.

Alternative location method: with the hip flexed to 90°,
the point is at the deepest part of the depression formed
between the greater trochanter and the ischial tuberosity.

Spatial relationships:
- GB29 (Juliao) is anterior and superior — at the ASIS-
  trochanter midpoint. GB29→GB30 wraps AROUND the greater
  trochanter from anterior-lateral to posterior.
- GB31 (Fengshi) is distal — on the lateral thigh, 7 cun
  above the popliteal crease. The channel descends from
  deep buttock to superficial lateral thigh.
- BL36 (Chengfu) is inferior and slightly medial — at the
  gluteal fold midpoint. BL and GB converge at the hip from
  different trajectories.
- GV2 (Yaoshu) is medial — at the sacral hiatus.
→ Cross-ref: AP-06 SI3 (SI3+GB30 combination)

Needle depth: 50-75 mm perpendicular or slightly medially.
This is the DEEPEST standard needling depth in the Atlas
for a point targeting a specific nerve.

⚠ **DEPTH NOTE:** the sciatic nerve is ~5-8 cm deep in most
adults. Effective stimulation typically requires 50-75 mm
through the gluteus maximus to reach the piriformis-sciatic
complex. Ultrasound guidance can optimize nerve proximity.

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Sciatic nerve (L4-S3):** the TARGET nerve. Exits the
  pelvis through the greater sciatic foramen, passes BELOW
  the piriformis (in ~85% of the population), and runs
  between the piriformis (superiorly) and the obturator
  internus/superior gemellus (inferiorly). At GB30, the
  sciatic nerve is typically 5-8 cm deep.

  Sciatic nerve composition at this level:
  - **Tibial division** (medial, larger): L4-S3, destined
    for posterior compartment of leg, sole of foot
  - **Common peroneal division** (lateral, smaller): L4-S2,
    destined for anterior/lateral compartment, dorsum of
    foot
  - Both divisions are bundled within a single epineurial
    sheath at GB30's level — they separate at mid-thigh

- **Inferior gluteal nerve (L5-S2):** motor nerve to
  gluteus maximus, deep in this area.
- **Posterior femoral cutaneous nerve (S1-S3):** provides
  cutaneous sensation to the posterior thigh. Exits below
  piriformis alongside the sciatic nerve.
- **Inferior cluneal nerves (S1-S3):** cutaneous branches
  from the posterior femoral cutaneous nerve, providing
  sensation to the buttock skin at GB30's level.
- Spinal segment: S1-S3 (cutaneous), L4-S3 (deep/sciatic).
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1

**Piriformis relationship:** in ~85% of people, the sciatic
nerve passes inferior to the piriformis. In ~15%, the
common peroneal division passes THROUGH the piriformis,
and in rare cases (<1%) the entire sciatic trunk traverses
the piriformis. The through-muscle variant is associated
with piriformis syndrome (sciatic entrapment by piriformis
contraction or hypertrophy).

Fascial: gluteal fascia → piriformis fascia → sciatic
nerve sheath. The piriformis fascia is continuous with the
pelvic fascia and sacrotuberous ligament.

Vascular: inferior gluteal artery (L5-S2) — exits below
piriformis alongside the sciatic nerve. The superior
gluteal artery is more lateral/superior (GB29 region).

Muscle layers: skin → subcutaneous fat (variable, often
thick) → gluteus maximus (thick, 2-4 cm) → piriformis →
sciatic nerve → obturator internus / gemelli → hip joint
capsule (deepest).

### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

**Ma Dan-yang Heavenly Star Point (马丹阳天星穴).** One of
12 points selected by Ma Dan-yang (Song Dynasty, ~1100s) as
the most clinically essential. The Ma Dan-yang Star Points
represent historical expert consensus on the minimum
effective point set for general practice. Previously
documented in the Atlas: LI11 (AP-02).
→ Cross-ref: AP-02 LI11

**Meeting point: GB + BL.** Shaoyang (GB) and Taiyang (BL)
— two Yang channels converging at the posterior hip. The GB
channel approaches from the lateral body wall (GB26-29),
the BL channel descends the posterior midline. Their meeting
at GB30 is at the DEEPEST regular acupoint in the lower
body.

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM acupoint:** GB30 Huantiao (环跳, "Jumping Ring").
Functions: benefits the hip joint, relaxes sinews, dispels
Wind-Damp, strengthens the lumbar region, alleviates pain.
THE primary point for Bi syndrome (painful obstruction) of
the hip and lower limb. Classical texts are extensive —
Huantiao is consistently ranked among the most important
points for lower body disorders.

**2. Trigger point:** piriformis TrP — STRONG correspon-
dence. Referred pain: buttock, posterior hip, and along
the sciatic nerve distribution. GB30 targets the piriformis
directly. Gluteus maximus TrP2 (deep layer) also overlaps.
🟢
→ Cross-ref: Travell & Simons 1999

**3. Ashi correspondence:** buttock pain at the sciatic
notch is the classic site of tenderness in sciatica and
piriformis syndrome. Patients spontaneously identify this
region as the "center" of their posterior hip pain. 🟢

**4. Marma point:** Katika Taruna Marma (कटिकतरुण) or
Nitamba Marma — hip/buttock region. Both Ayurvedic
traditions identify the posterior hip as a vital point.
Moderate-strong correspondence. 🟡

**5. Head zone:** S1-S3 dermatome — posterior hip. The
sciatic nerve distribution covers L4-S3, spanning the
lumbar-sacral territory. 🟢

**6. Anatomy Trains:** the Superficial Back Line (SBL) and
Deep Front Line (DFL) cross at the hip/pelvis. The
piriformis is part of the Deep Lateral Rotators — fascial
structures connecting sacrum to femur that serve as a key
junction for pelvic stability and force transmission. 🟡

**7. Neurogenic inflammatory spot:** sciatic nerve
inflammation produces neurogenic inflammation in the buttock
along the nerve's course. Neuropathic pain patients show
thermal and electrical changes at the sciatic notch. 🟡

**8. Cupping point:** deep buttock cupping for sciatica and
hip pain is practiced in multiple traditions (Chinese,
Arabic/Sunnah). Wet cupping at GB30 is specifically
mentioned in Al-Hijama traditions.

**Convergence score: 7/8** — very strong. TIED with GB20
for the highest convergence in the GB channel. Two points
at 7/8 — both are the Atlas's strongest multi-tradition
convergence sites for their respective regions (GB20 for
head/neck, GB30 for hip/lower limb).

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Nguồn cổ:
- **Zhenjiu Jiayi Jing:** 腰胁相引急痛 (lumbar-hypochond-
  riac pulling pain), 髀不可举 (inability to lift thigh),
  膝不可屈伸 (inability to flex/extend knee), 风痹不仁
  (Wind-Bi with numbness).
- **Ma Dan-yang Star Song (马丹阳天星十二穴歌):**
  "环跳能医腿痛风，居髎二穴认真攻。腰胯膝痛并麻木，
  冷风湿痹亦能通。"
  ("Huantiao can treat leg-wind pain, attack Juliao for
  confirmation. Lumbar-hip-knee pain with numbness, cold-
  wind-damp Bi can all be freed.")
- **Zhenjiu Dacheng:** "Jumping Ring — THE master point of
  the lower limb. Treats all Wind-Bi of the leg, sciatica,
  hip stiffness, hemiplegia, lumbar pain radiating to leg."

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- Musculoskeletal: sciatica 🟡, hip pain 🔴, lumbar disc
  herniation (protocol) 🟡, gluteal pain
- Neurological: hemiplegia (lower limb) 🔴, leg weakness,
  leg atrophy
- Rheumatic: Bi syndrome of the lower limb 🔴, knee pain
  (referred)
- Dermatological: urticaria (Wind in skin, classical) 🔴

Kinh nghiệm lâm sàng:
- **Sciatica primary protocol:** GB30 + BL40 (Weizhong) +
  GB34 (Yanglingquan). The most common three-point sciatica
  prescription in clinical practice.
- **SI3+GB30 combination:** Taiyang channel pair (Hand +
  Foot Taiyang). Specifically for lumbar disc herniation —
  SI3 (Du Mai opener) + GB30 (sciatic access). Animal
  evidence supports this combination (see Evidence section).
  → Cross-ref: AP-06 SI3
- **Hemiplegia protocol:** GB30 + ST36 + SP6 + LI11 + LI4.
  GB30 is the hip anchor in stroke rehabilitation protocols.
- **Piriformis syndrome:** GB30 with deep needling + GB34
  (Tendons Hui-Meeting) + BL36.
- **Wind-Bi formula:** GB30 + GB31 (Fengshi, "Wind Market")
  + BL17 (Blood Hui-Meeting) + SP10 (Sea of Blood). Wind-
  dispelling + Blood-moving for chronic arthralgia.

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

**Sciatica — 🟡 Plausible (protocol-level evidence)**

GB30 is included in virtually ALL acupuncture protocols for
sciatica and lumbar disc herniation. Systematic reviews of
acupuncture for sciatica show moderate efficacy evidence,
but GB30 is NEVER isolated — it appears as part of multi-
point protocols (typically GB30 + BL40 + GB34 ± local
points).

The inability to isolate GB30's individual contribution
reflects a systematic problem in acupuncture research — the
SAME pattern seen at LI15 (shoulder), where the most-used
point is never tested alone.
→ Cross-ref: AP-02 LI15 (protocol inclusion without
isolation)

**SI3+GB30 combination — 🔴 Unvalidated (animal only)**

Multiple rat model studies (documented in AP-06 SI3):
(a) NF-κB / iNOS pathway: SI3+GB30 improved spinal nerve
root morphology, inhibited NF-κB and iNOS expression in
spinal nerve roots, reduced serum NO levels in rat lumbar
disc herniation model.
(b) HMGB1 pathway: SI3+GB30 inhibited HMGB1 protein and
mRNA expression, reduced IL-1β, IL-6, IL-8 in rat LDH
model.
(c) PI3K/AKT/mTOR pathway: SI3+GB30 modulated autophagy
in nucleus pulposus cells in rat disc degeneration model.

**Critical limitation:** SI3 was never isolated from GB30.
The AP-06 analysis notes: "GB30 independently has evidence
for sciatic nerve modulation via strong manual acupuncture"
— meaning the observed effects may be primarily driven by
GB30's local sciatic nerve stimulation rather than SI3's
supraspinal contribution. Disambiguating the two requires a
four-arm trial (SI3 alone vs GB30 alone vs SI3+GB30 vs
sham), which has not been conducted.
→ Cross-ref: AP-06 SI3

**GB30-specific physiological mechanism — 🟡 Plausible**

Strong manual acupuncture (de Qi-producing deep needling)
at GB30 is expected to produce direct sciatic nerve trunk
activation. The needle physically contacts or closely
approaches the sciatic nerve, producing A-delta and A-beta
fiber activation across 6 spinal segments simultaneously.
No point-specific controlled trial isolates GB30's
physiological response, but the peripheral neural mechanism
(needle → nerve trunk → measurable conduction change) is
anatomically self-evident at this depth.

### Dynamic acupoint properties

**Sciatic sensitization:** GB30 shows dramatically lowered
pressure-pain thresholds in patients with active sciatica
compared to healthy controls. The point becomes tender,
sometimes spontaneously painful, during acute sciatic nerve
inflammation. This is one of the STRONGEST dynamic acupoint
examples in the Atlas — a clear disease-state sensitization
pattern.
→ Cross-ref: C04 S4 (Dynamic Acupoint)

The sensitization mechanism: sciatic nerve inflammation
