(radiculopathy or compression) → neurogenic inflammation
spreads along the nerve sheath → reaches the piriformis and
deep gluteal tissues → viscerosomatic-like reflex through
S1-S3 → cutaneous hyperalgesia at GB30.

**Piriformis syndrome specificity:** in piriformis syndrome,
GB30 becomes maximally tender because the piriformis itself
is the pathological structure directly beneath the point.
This is a Type 1 dynamic acupoint (local tissue pathology
sensitizing the overlying point), unlike Type 2 (remote
visceral pathology referring to a distant point via
segmental reflex).

### Cơ chế giải thích (BSA framework attribution)

**Sciatica / lower limb pain (PERIPHERAL NEURAL — direct
nerve access):**
GB30 needle → traverses gluteus maximus → reaches
piriformis → contacts or approaches sciatic nerve trunk
(L4-S3). Deep needling with strong manual stimulation
produces direct nerve activation. The sciatic nerve at GB30
is a PRE-BIFURCATION trunk containing fibers for the entire
posterior and lateral lower limb:

Sciatic trunk at GB30 (L4-S3):
— Tibial division (medial):
  — Posterior thigh muscles
  — Posterior leg muscles (gastrocnemius, soleus)
  — Plantar foot (medial/lateral plantar nerves)
  — Sural nerve → lateral foot
— Common peroneal division (lateral):
  — Anterior leg muscles (tibialis anterior)
  — Lateral leg muscles (peronei)
  — Dorsum of foot

Pre-bifurcation access explains GB30's clinical breadth for
lower limb conditions — a single point modulates the nerve
that controls virtually the entire leg below the knee.

**Lumbar disc herniation — DUAL PATHWAY (proposed):**
When combined with SI3, the SI3+GB30 prescription employs
two complementary neural pathways:
- SI3 (C8-T1): ulnar nerve → supraspinal relay (brainstem
  PAG/RVM) → descending inhibition → WHOLE SPINE modulation
- GB30 (L4-S3): sciatic nerve → LOCAL segmental modulation
  at L4-S3 nerve roots

The combination provides BOTH top-down (supraspinal) and
bottom-up (segmental) modulation at the lumbar spine level —
converging on the affected nerve roots from two directions.
→ Cross-ref: Obs 11 (TCC Dual-Access for migraine)

**Hip joint pain (SEGMENTAL + LOCAL):**
GB30 at S1-S3 is within the hip joint's segmental territory
(L2-S2). Stimulation produces local effects (muscle
relaxation, vasodilation around the piriformis) and
segmental effects (modulation of hip joint nociceptive
processing at the dorsal horn).

**Piriformis syndrome (DIRECT ANATOMICAL TARGET):**
For piriformis syndrome, GB30 is NOT a reflex point — it is
a DIRECT anatomical target. The needle enters the
piriformis itself. The mechanism: mechanical disruption of
the contracted piriformis + local vasodilation + neurogenic
inflammation reduction. Functionally equivalent to trigger
point dry needling, not a traditional acupuncture mechanism.

**Hemiplegia (SUPRASPINAL — proposed):**
Post-stroke hemiplegia recovery with GB30 likely operates
through supraspinal pathways. Deep needling of the sciatic
nerve produces massive afferent input to the brainstem and
cortex, potentially facilitating motor cortex
reorganization. Proposed but not specifically validated.

**Gap:** GB30's indication for urticaria (Wind in skin) has
no plausible neural mechanism. This likely represents
classical category extension — Wind-dispelling points are
applied to all "Wind" conditions regardless of mechanism.

### Kỹ thuật kích thích + dose-response

Châm (B01): 50-75 mm perpendicular, directed slightly
medially toward the sacrum. Patient in lateral decubitus
with upper hip flexed 90°, lower leg straight. Deep
needling through gluteus maximus toward the piriformis-
sciatic complex.
→ Cross-ref: B01 S5.2

**De Qi:** STRONG de Qi is considered essential at GB30.
Classical teaching specifies 电击感 (diàn jī gǎn, "electric
shock sensation") or radiating numbness/tingling down the
posterior thigh to the foot — indicating sciatic nerve
contact. This is the STRONGEST de Qi requirement of any
point in the Atlas.

**Strong manual acupuncture (强刺激):** GB30 is one of the
points where strong stimulation is specifically indicated —
large-amplitude lifting-thrusting (提插) and twirling
(捻转). The purpose is sciatic nerve activation sufficient
to generate impulses along the entire nerve distribution.

EA: commonly applied at GB30-BL40 or GB30-GB34 for
sciatica. Typical parameters: 2/100 Hz alternating, 30
minutes.

Bấm huyệt (B02): deep pressure with thumb or elbow at the
sciatic notch. Produces radiating sensation in sciatica
patients. Used in clinical assessment (sciatic notch
tenderness test).
→ Cross-ref: B02

Cứu (B08): moxa applicable for Cold-Bi of the hip (chronic,
cold-aggravated). Less commonly used than needling due to
the depth required.

### An toàn

⚠ **MODERATE risk.**

**Sciatic nerve injury:** the primary risk. Deep needling
approaching the sciatic nerve can cause nerve injury if the
needle penetrates the epineurium. The therapeutic goal IS to
reach the nerve vicinity — the clinical challenge is
proximity WITHOUT penetration. Ultrasound guidance is
increasingly used.

**Symptoms of nerve injury:** sudden sharp shooting pain
down the leg (distinct from the dull ache of de Qi),
lasting motor weakness, persistent numbness. If these occur
→ IMMEDIATELY withdraw the needle.

**Inferior gluteal artery:** runs deep, exits below
piriformis. Deep needling could contact the artery →
hematoma risk. Rarely clinically significant.

**Needle breakage risk:** historically the HIGHEST risk of
any acupoint, due to (a) depth required (50-75 mm), (b)
thick gluteal muscles causing resistance, and (c) patient
movement during strong de Qi. Modern stainless steel
filiform needles have largely eliminated this, but it
explains classical warnings about needle breakage at GB30.

**Anatomical variant caution:** in patients with piriformis
variant anatomy (~15%), the common peroneal nerve passes
through the piriformis. Deep needling may more readily
contact the nerve directly — both therapeutic opportunity
and higher injury risk.

**Pregnancy:** no specific contraindication (unlike GB21
which is above on the same channel).

---

**Observation 17 — Dual-Pathway Architecture for Spinal
Nerve Modulation:**

The SI3+GB30 combination for lumbar disc herniation employs
a dual-pathway architecture:

| Component | Point | Entry | Pathway |
|-----------|-------|-------|---------|
| Supraspinal | SI3 (C8-T1) | Ulnar nerve | PAG/RVM → descending inhibition → whole spine |
| Segmental | GB30 (L4-S3) | Sciatic nerve | Direct trunk → local anti-inflammatory |

This parallels the migraine TCC Dual-Access Architecture
(Obs 11):

| Component | Point | Entry | Pathway |
|-----------|-------|-------|---------|
| Segmental (cervical) | GB20 (C1-C3) | Suboccipital | Trigeminocervical complex (direct) |
| Segmental (cranial) | GB8 (V3) | Auriculotemporal | Trigeminal nucleus caudalis (direct) |
| Supraspinal | TE5 (C6-C8) | Radial nerve | Insula/cortical → descending |

Both classical prescriptions share an architectural
principle: combining SUPRASPINAL (top-down descending
modulation from a remote entry point) with SEGMENTAL
(local, direct nerve access at the pathology site). This is
not simply "distal + local" — it specifically combines two
DIFFERENT NEURAL MODULATION LEVELS.

The animal evidence (AP-06) supports convergent modulation:
three anti-inflammatory pathways (NF-κB/iNOS, HMGB1,
PI3K/AKT/mTOR) active at the spinal nerve root — consistent
with signals arriving from BOTH supraspinal descending and
local segmental sources.

Whether dual-pathway architecture generalizes across
classical prescriptions (e.g., LI4+ST36 for abdominal pain:
supraspinal + segmental?) is untested but predictable from
the pattern. 🟡

Updated neural zone table (13 zones through 30 points):

| Zone | Points | Primary Nerve | Segment |
|------|--------|---------------|---------|
| 1 | GB1 | Lacrimal/Zygomatic | CN V1/V2 |
| 2 | GB2-GB8 | Auriculotemporal | CN V3 |
| 3 | GB9-GB12 | Occipital nerves | C2-C3 |
| 4 | GB13-GB15 | Supraorbital | CN V1 |
| 5 | GB16-GB19 | V1→C2 transition | V1/C2 |
| 6 | GB20 | Deep suboccipital | C1-C3+XI |
| 7 | GB21 | Supraclavicular+XI | C3-C4+XI |
| 8 | GB22-GB23 | Intercostal | T4 |
| 9 | GB24 | Intercostal | T7 |
| 10 | GB25-GB26 | Subcostal/lower intercostal | T11-L1 |
| 11 | GB27-GB28 | Iliohypogastric/ilioinguinal | L1 |
| 12 | GB29 | Lateral femoral cutaneous | L2-L3 |
| 13 | GB30 | Sciatic (inf. cluneal cutaneous) | L4-S3 |

13 zones in 30 points. GB has SURPASSED TE's record of
8 zones (23 points). Zone transition rates:
- Head (GB1-20): 6 zones / 20 points = 0.30 zones/pt
- Body (GB21-30): 7 zones / 10 points = 0.70 zones/pt
- Body maintains 2.3× acceleration over head.

---

## GB31 — Phong Thị (风市, Fēng Shì)

**Evidence tier: B (Standard Entry)**
"Wind Market" (风市) — 风 (Fēng, wind) + 市 (Shì, marketplace).
Named for its function as the gathering place where pathological
Wind can be accessed and dispersed. GB31 is the second
Wind-named point on the GB channel: GB20 Fengchi 风池 ("Wind
Pool") addresses Wind at its cranial entry, while GB31 Fengshi
风市 ("Wind Market") treats Wind that has settled in the lower
limb channels. The two Wind-named points bookend the channel's
trajectory — head to thigh.

GB31 has **NO formal category memberships** — no Five Shu, no
Yuan-Source, no Luo, no special designation of any kind. Despite
this, it is one of the most commonly needled points on the GB
channel. Its clinical importance derives entirely from empirical
use for Wind-Bi conditions and lower limb paralysis, not from
theoretical category assignment.

GB31 is located using a **UNIQUE position-dependent method**:
the patient stands erect with arms hanging naturally at the
sides — GB31 is where the tip of the middle finger touches the
lateral thigh. This is the only acupoint in the entire 361-point
system located by a standing body position rather than by
proportional measurement (cun) or anatomical landmark. The method
yields a point approximately 7 cun above the popliteal crease,
in the groove between the vastus lateralis and the iliotibial
band.

### Vị trí giải phẫu

**Surface:** lateral thigh, 7 cun above the popliteal crease
(approximately mid-thigh), in the groove between the vastus
lateralis anteriorly and the iliotibial band/biceps femoris
posteriorly.

**Layers (superficial to deep):**
- Skin → subcutaneous fat → fascia lata → iliotibial band
- Lateral intermuscular septum between vastus lateralis
  (quadriceps group) and biceps femoris (hamstring group)

**Nerve:**
- Cutaneous: lateral femoral cutaneous nerve (L2-L3) — the
  nerve of meralgia paresthetica. Its anterior branch extends
  to this level.
- Deep/muscular: femoral nerve branches to vastus lateralis
  (L2-L4), sciatic nerve branches to biceps femoris (L5-S2).
  The needle enters the intermuscular septum between these
  two nerve territories.
- Spinal segment summary: L2-L4 (cutaneous + motor)

**Vascular:** lateral superior genicular artery descending
branch, muscular branches of profunda femoris artery.

**Neural zone assignment:** Zone 12 continuation (lateral
femoral cutaneous nerve, L2-L3) — same nerve territory as
GB29.

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

The lateral intermuscular septum is a dense connective tissue
plane separating the anterior (quadriceps) and posterior
(hamstring) compartments of the thigh. This is a **fascial
convergence zone** — intermuscular septa are enriched in nerve
endings, fibroblasts, and mast cells compared to the muscle
bellies on either side (Langevin 2002 pattern). The iliotibial
band passes superficially — a thick layer of fascia lata that
serves as the tendon of the tensor fasciae latae and part of
the gluteus maximus insertion.

Needle insertion at GB31 crosses the ITB and enters the
intermuscular septum — two fascial layers in quick succession.
This double-fascial penetration may explain the characteristic
strong de Qi (distention, heaviness) felt at this point.

### Phân loại huyệt

Five Shu: No
Yuan-Source: No
Luo-Connecting: No
Xi-Cleft: No
Back-Shu: No
Front-Mu: No
Eight Confluent: No
Hui-Meeting: No

Special designations: None.

GB31 is one of the most clinically important points WITHOUT
ANY formal category designation — its status derives entirely
from centuries of empirical clinical use.

### Cross-tradition mapping

**TCM acupoint:** GB31 Fengshi — dispel Wind (祛风), benefit
the sinews and channels, stop itching. Primary point for
Wind-Bi syndrome of the lower limb. Used for hemiplegia,
lateral thigh pain, knee pain, urticaria.

**Trigger point:** vastus lateralis TrP and ITB/tensor fasciae
latae TrP. The vastus lateralis has multiple trigger points,
including one at the mid-lateral thigh corresponding closely
to GB31. Referred pain: lateral thigh and lateral knee.
Dorsher correspondence: moderate-good. 🟡

**Ashi:** GB31 is a common Ashi point for lateral thigh pain
and ITB syndrome. The ITB is frequently tender at this level
in runners and lateral knee pain patients. 🟢

**Marma:** no specific Marma corresponds to the mid-lateral
thigh in classical Ayurvedic texts.

**Head zone:** L2-L3 segments correspond to testicular/ovarian
referred pain and upper anterior thigh. Weak correspondence
to GB31's primary indications.

**Anatomy Trains:** the ITB is a key component of the
**Lateral Line** (Myers). GB31 sits directly on this
myofascial continuity from lateral ankle (peroneus longus)
through ITB to the shoulder. 🟡

**Neurogenic spot:** not specifically studied at this location.

**Cupping:** the lateral thigh provides a flat, muscular
surface suitable for cupping. Cupping at GB31 area is used
for Wind-Bi of the lower limb. 🟢

**Convergence score: 4/8** (TCM, TrP, Ashi, Anatomy Trains;
supported: Cupping)

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Hoàng Đế Nội Kinh (黄帝内经): Wind-Bi (风痹) of the lower
limb — GB31 treats conditions where pathological Wind lodges
in the channels, producing wandering pain, numbness, and
heaviness in the legs.

Zhenjiu Jiayi Jing (针灸甲乙经, Huangfu Mi, 282 CE):
Wind-Bi with numbness of the lateral thigh, difficulty
walking, leg weakness.

Zhenjiu Dacheng (针灸大成, Yang Jizhou, 1601): Wind-Bi,
hemiplegia, lateral thigh pain, itching of the whole body
(urticaria attributed to Wind), leg atrophy.

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, Manual of Acupuncture):
- Dispels Wind — lower limb Wind-Bi (wandering pain,
  numbness), urticaria, generalized itching
- Benefits the leg — hemiplegia, leg weakness, atrophy,
  lateral thigh pain
- Local — ITB syndrome, lateral thigh pain, lateral knee
  pain (referred)

Kinh nghiệm lâm sàng nổi bật:
- GB31 is a standard component of hemiplegia protocols:
  typically GB31 + GB30 + ST36 + SP6 for lower limb motor
  recovery post-stroke
- Wind-Bi formula: GB30 + GB31 (Fengshi, "Wind Market") +
  BL17 (Blood Hui-Meeting) + SP10 (Sea of Blood) —
  documented in GB30 entry
- For urticaria/pruritus, GB31 is selected as the "Wind
  Market" — the reasoning is that Wind causes itching, and
  the Wind Market point disperses Wind. No mechanism beyond
  the classical association.
→ Cross-ref: B01 S3.2

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

**1. Hemiplegia / post-stroke motor recovery — 🔴 Unvalidated
(GB31-specific)**

GB31 appears in virtually all acupuncture hemiplegia protocols
(scalp acupuncture + body points), typically alongside GB30,
ST36, LI4, and SP6. No study isolates GB31's individual
contribution.

Confidence: 🔴 — protocol inclusion only.

**2. Urticaria — 🔴 Unvalidated**

GB31 is traditionally a first-choice point for urticaria (Wind
in the skin). No specific RCT for GB31 in urticaria found. The
Wind-dispelling mechanism has no modern correlate.

Confidence: 🔴 — purely classical indication.

**3. ITB syndrome / lateral thigh pain — 🔴 Unvalidated**

GB31 is anatomically positioned directly on the ITB, making
it a logical target for ITB friction syndrome and lateral
thigh myofascial pain. No specific acupuncture trial for ITB
syndrome at GB31 found. Dry needling studies for ITB syndrome
tend to target the lateral knee rather than mid-thigh.

Confidence: 🔴 — anatomical plausibility only.

### Dynamic acupoint properties

GB31 becomes tender in patients with ITB syndrome or lateral
thigh myofascial pain — consistent with Type 1 dynamic acupoint
behavior (local tissue pathology sensitizing the overlying
point). The ITB at mid-thigh is a common site of myofascial
restriction in runners, cyclists, and patients with lateral
knee pain.

In hemiplegia patients, GB31 area may show altered electrical
conductivity on the affected side — consistent with the
neurogenic spot phenomenon, though not specifically studied at
this location.
→ Cross-ref: C04 S4

### Cơ chế giải thích (BSA framework attribution)

**Lower limb pain / Wind-Bi (LOCAL FASCIAL + SEGMENTAL):**
Needle → ITB + lateral intermuscular septum → activation of
nerve endings in the fascial convergence zone. Mechanism:
local vasodilation + mast cell degranulation + neurogenic
inflammation cascade at the fascial plane. Segmental
modulation at L2-L4 dorsal horn via LFCN afferents.

**Hemiplegia (SEGMENTAL + MOTOR):**
GB31 accesses the intermuscular septum between quadriceps
(femoral nerve) and hamstrings (sciatic nerve). Deep needling
may produce motor responses in both muscle groups — bilateral
motor stimulation at a single point. However, the specific
mechanism for motor recovery contribution is speculative.

**Urticaria / Wind-dispelling (NO IDENTIFIED MECHANISM):**
GB31's indication for urticaria has no plausible neural or
segmental mechanism. L2-L3 dermatome (lateral thigh) has no
known connection to histamine regulation or systemic immune
function. This likely represents classical category extension —
"Wind Market" applied to all "Wind" manifestations.

**Gap:** GB31's most distinctive traditional use
(Wind-dispelling for urticaria/pruritus) has the weakest
mechanism. Its strongest mechanism (local fascial) corresponds
to its least distinctive traditional use (lateral thigh pain).

### Kỹ thuật kích thích + dose-response

Châm (B01): 25-40 mm perpendicular insertion through the ITB
into the intermuscular septum.
→ Cross-ref: B01 S5.2

De Qi: distention and heaviness in the lateral thigh, sometimes
radiating distally toward the knee or proximally toward the
hip.

EA: can be paired with GB34 (GB31-GB34) for lateral leg
conditions, or with GB30 (GB30-GB31) for sciatic/hip
conditions.

Bấm huyệt (B02): firm pressure on the lateral thigh at the
standing middle-finger position. Accessible for self-treatment
— the standing location method makes it easy for patients to
find.
→ Cross-ref: B02

Cứu (B08): moxa applicable for Cold-Wind-Bi conditions.

### An toàn

⚠ **LOW risk.**

**No major neurovascular structures** at standard needling
depth. The lateral femoral cutaneous nerve is superficial but
small — needle contact may produce a sharp sensation but not
lasting injury. The profunda femoris artery is deep to the
vastus lateralis, well protected by muscle mass.

**Hematoma:** possible with deep needling into highly vascular
muscle bellies, but typically minor and self-resolving.

**Pregnancy:** no specific contraindication.

---

## GB32 — Trung Độc (中渎, Zhōng Dú)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry)**
"Central Ditch" (中渎) — 中 (Zhōng, middle/central) + 渎
(Dú, ditch/drainage channel). Named for its position in the
intermuscular groove at the mid-lower thigh. **Disambiguation:**
GB32 Zhongdu (中渎) shares pinyin with LR6 Zhongdu (中都
"Central Capital"), but the Chinese characters differ — 渎
(ditch) vs 都 (capital). Different characters, different
points, different locations.

GB32 is 2 cun distal to GB31, at 5 cun above the popliteal
crease, in the same intermuscular groove between vastus
lateralis and biceps femoris.

### Vị trí giải phẫu

**Surface:** lateral thigh, 5 cun above the popliteal crease,
in the groove between vastus lateralis and biceps femoris.

**Nerve:** lateral femoral cutaneous nerve (L2-L3) — terminal
branches at this level. Deep: femoral motor (vastus lateralis,
L2-L4) + sciatic motor (biceps femoris, L5-S2).

**Vascular:** muscular branches of profunda femoris artery.

**Neural zone:** Zone 12 continuation (lateral femoral
cutaneous nerve, L2-L3).

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Same intermuscular septum anatomy as GB31 — fascia lata/ITB
overlying the lateral intermuscular septum between quadriceps
and hamstring compartments. The septum narrows slightly as it
approaches the knee.

### Phân loại huyệt

No category memberships. No special designations.

### Cross-tradition mapping

**TCM acupoint:** GB32 Zhongdu — dispel Wind-Damp, benefit
the channels. Subset of GB31 indications.

**Trigger point:** vastus lateralis distal TrP. Lower portion
of vastus lateralis referred pain zone. Weak-moderate
correspondence.

**Ashi:** lateral thigh pain point. 🟢

**Other systems:** no specific matches.

**Convergence score: 2/8** (TCM, Ashi)

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Classical sources: Wind-Bi of the lower limb, lateral thigh
and knee pain, hemiplegia with leg weakness, numbness of the
lateral leg.

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman): dispels Wind-Damp, benefits the
channels. Lateral thigh pain, knee pain, weakness and atrophy
of the lower limb, hemiplegia.

GB32 has a narrower indication range than GB31 — essentially a
subset focused on local lateral thigh/knee pain and channel
disorders. It lacks GB31's urticaria/systemic Wind indication.

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

No specific studies found for GB32. All evidence 🔴 Unvalidated.

### An toàn

⚠ **LOW risk.** Same safety profile as GB31 — large muscle
mass, no major neurovascular structures at standard depth.

**Pregnancy:** no specific contraindication.

---

## GB33 — Tất Dương Quan (膝阳关, Xī Yáng Guān)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry)**
"Knee Yang Gate" (膝阳关) — 膝 (Xī, knee) + 阳 (Yáng) + 关
(Guān, gate/pass). Named as the "gate" on the Yang (lateral)
side of the knee. The 膝 prefix distinguishes it from GV3
Yaoyangguan (腰阳关 "Lumbar Yang Gate"). Same body-region
prefix pattern as GB11 头窍阴 / GB44 足窍阴 — a convention
for disambiguating shared name components.

GB33 is located in the depression above the lateral epicondyle
of the femur, between the femoral condyle and the tendon of
biceps femoris, with the knee flexed. It is the most proximal
of the lateral knee points.

### Vị trí giải phẫu

**Surface:** lateral knee, in the depression proximal to the
lateral epicondyle of the femur, between the femoral condyle
and the tendon of biceps femoris when the knee is flexed.
Approximately 3 cun above GB34.

**Nerve:** terminal branches of the lateral femoral cutaneous
nerve (L2-L3) at their most distal extent, transitioning into
the territory of the superior lateral genicular nerve (from
common peroneal, L4-S1). This is a neural transition zone.

**Vascular:** lateral superior genicular artery.

**Neural zone:** Zone 12 terminal (L2-L3 → L4-S1 transition).

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

The depression between the lateral femoral epicondyle and
biceps femoris tendon contains loose connective tissue, the
lateral superior genicular artery, and periosteal nerve
endings from the femoral condyle. The lateral collateral
ligament of the knee is in close proximity. This is a
bony-tendinous transition zone with rich periosteal
innervation.

### Phân loại huyệt

No category memberships. No special designations.

### Cross-tradition mapping

**TCM acupoint:** GB33 Xiyangguan — dispels Wind-Damp,
benefits the knee joint. Primarily a local knee point.

**Trigger point:** lateral collateral ligament attachment site
and biceps femoris distal tendon TrP. Weak-moderate
correspondence.

**Ashi:** lateral knee pain point — commonly tender in knee
pathology. 🟢

**Marma:** Janu Marma (knee joint Marma) — the Ayurvedic knee
Marma covers a broad zone around the knee joint. Partial
overlap. 🟡

**Other systems:** no specific matches.

**Convergence score: 3/8** (TCM, Ashi, Marma-partial)

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Classical sources: knee joint pain and swelling (lateral),
difficulty flexing and extending the knee, Wind-Bi of the
knee, numbness and weakness of the lower leg.

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman): benefits the knee joint, relaxes
the sinews. Lateral knee pain, knee swelling, contracture of
the popliteal tendons, difficulty walking.

GB33 is essentially a local knee point with no systemic or
distal indications.

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

No specific studies found for GB33. All evidence
🔴 Unvalidated.

### An toàn

⚠ **LOW risk.** Lateral knee — bony depression with no major
neurovascular structures at standard depth. The common
peroneal nerve is posterior (closer to biceps femoris tendon)
— standard anterior-inferior needle direction avoids it.

**Pregnancy:** no specific contraindication.

---

## GB34 — Dương Lăng Tuyền (阳陵泉, Yáng Líng Quán)

**Evidence tier: A (Deep Analysis)**
"Yang Mound Spring" (阳陵泉) — 阳 (Yáng) + 陵 (Líng,
mound/prominence) + 泉 (Quán, spring/source). The "mound"
refers to the head of fibula — a prominent bony landmark on
the lateral knee. The "spring" reflects its He-Sea
designation — where Qi "gathers like water flowing into the
sea" (Lingshu Ch.1). The name mirrors **SP9 Yinlingquan**
(阴陵泉, "Yin Mound Spring") on the medial side of the knee,
creating a perfect Yin-Yang naming pair: Yang Mound Spring
(lateral, fibular head) / Yin Mound Spring (medial, tibial
condyle). Both are He-Sea points of their respective channels.

**MOST DESIGNATED POINT on GB** — holds 4 simultaneous special
designations plus a Five Element role:

1. **He-Sea (合穴) / Earth element** — the terminal Five Shu
   point where channel Qi "gathers like water flowing into the
   sea." As Earth on a Wood channel: **Ke Subject** (Wood
   controls Earth = the controlled element). In Five Element
   treatment: needle GB34 to sedate GB excess.

2. **Lower He-Sea of GB (胆之下合穴)** — the Lingshu Ch.4
   system assigns each of the 6 Fu (Yang) organs a diagnostic
   and treatment point on the three Yang leg meridians. GB34
   serves as BOTH the channel He-Sea AND the organ Lower
   He-Sea — one of only TWO such overlaps in the entire system
   (the other being ST36 for the Stomach). All three Yang HAND
   channels (LI→ST37, SI→ST39, TE→BL39) have their Lower
   He-Sea on a DIFFERENT channel, because their own He-Sea is
   at the elbow, too distant from the abdomen.

3. **Hui-Meeting of Tendons (筋会)** — 1 of 8 Hui-Meeting
   (Influential) points. Theoretical governance: ALL tendons,
   sinews, and musculotendinous structures across the body,
   regardless of meridian. The complete system: LR13=Zang
   organs, CV12=Fu organs, CV17=Qi, BL17=Blood, **GB34=
   Tendons**, LU9=Vessels, BL11=Bones, GB39=Marrow. The
   LR-GB pair holds 2 of 8 Hui-Meeting points (LR13=Zang,
   GB34=Tendons) — unique among the six interior-exterior
   channel pairs. The Liver "governs tendons" (肝主筋) in
   TCM — and GB34 on the paired Yang channel is the
   Hui-Meeting of exactly those structures.
   → Cross-ref: AP-12 LR13 entry (Hui-Meeting list)

4. **Ma Dan-yang Heavenly Star Point (马丹阳天星穴)** — Song
   Dynasty expert consensus point. Third on GB (with GB20,
   GB30). Fourth in Atlas (with LI11).

Location: in the depression **anterior and inferior to the
head of fibula**. The fibular head is palpable as a bony
prominence on the lateral knee, just below and posterior to
the knee joint line. GB34 sits in the soft tissue depression
just anteroinferior to this landmark. Patient position: knee
slightly flexed.

### Vị trí giải phẫu

**Surface:** anteroinferior to the head of fibula, between
the fibular head and the origin of peroneus longus muscle.
Approximately at the level of the tibial tuberosity but
lateral.

**Layers (superficial to deep):**
- Skin → subcutaneous tissue (thin at this location — fibula
  is nearly subcutaneous)
- Peroneus longus muscle origin — arises from the head and
  upper 2/3 of the lateral fibula. The needle enters through
  or near the proximal belly/origin.
- Deep: anterior compartment of the leg (extensor digitorum
  longus, tibialis anterior origins)

**Nerve:**
- **Common peroneal nerve (L4-S2):** wraps tightly around the
  **fibular neck** immediately posterior to the fibular head.
  This is the most common site of peripheral nerve compression
  injury in the entire body (crossed-leg palsy, tight casts,
  prolonged lateral recumbency).
  - At GB34, the standard insertion point is ANTERIOR to the
    nerve's course. The nerve is close enough for neural
    effects but posterior to the needle.
  - Branches at/near this level:
    — **Superficial peroneal nerve** → lateral leg cutaneous
      sensation + peroneus longus/brevis motor
    — **Deep peroneal nerve** → anterior leg cutaneous
      sensation (1st web space) + tibialis anterior/extensor
      digitorum/extensor hallucis motor
    — **Lateral sural cutaneous nerve** → lateral calf
      sensation
    — **Recurrent articular branch** → knee joint capsule
  - The nerve is at a **pre-branch point** — before the
    superficial/deep peroneal division. Stimulation here
    accesses the complete common peroneal distribution
    (anterior and lateral leg + dorsum of foot).
- Cutaneous: lateral sural cutaneous nerve (L4-S1).
- Spinal segment: L4-S2

**Vascular:** anterior tibial recurrent artery, inferior
lateral genicular artery.

**Neural zone assignment:** Zone 14 (new) — Common peroneal
nerve, L4-S2. Distinctly different from Zone 12 (LFCN,
L2-L3) despite only 3 cun distance from GB33.

**Key anatomical significance:** GB34 provides **second-level
pre-branch nerve access** on the same neural system as GB30.
GB30 accesses the sciatic trunk (before tibial/common peroneal
split → L4-S3, entire posterior and lateral lower limb). GB34
accesses the common peroneal nerve (before superficial/deep
peroneal split → L4-S2, anterior and lateral leg + foot
dorsum). The GB channel visits the sciatic-peroneal system
twice — at the trunk (hip), then at the lateral division
(knee).

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

The depression anteroinferior to the fibular head contains:
- **Peroneus longus origin:** dense musculotendinous tissue
  with rich periosteal nerve endings at the fibula attachment.
  Transition zone between bone (fibular head periosteum) and
  muscle (peroneus longus origin).
- **Fascial convergence:** the anterior and lateral
  compartments of the leg are separated by the anterior
  intermuscular septum, which attaches to the anterior border
  of the fibula. GB34 is near this fascial attachment.
- **Common peroneal nerve sheath:** the nerve is enclosed in
  its own fascial sheath as it wraps around the fibular neck,
  continuous with the sciatic nerve sheath proximally.

The tissue at GB34 is relatively thin — the fibula is nearly
subcutaneous at the head, making deep structures (including
the nerve) more accessible than at most other lower limb
points.

### Phân loại huyệt

Five Shu: **He-Sea (合穴) / Earth** — Ke Subject (Wood
controls Earth)

Yuan-Source: No (GB40 Qiuxu)
Luo-Connecting: No (GB37 Guangming)
Xi-Cleft: No (GB36 Waiqiu)
Back-Shu: No
Front-Mu: No (GB24 own, GB25 KI)
Eight Confluent: No (GB41 Zulinqi)

**Hui-Meeting: Tendons (筋会)** — 1 of 8 Influential points.

Special designations:
- **Lower He-Sea of GB (胆之下合穴)** — overlaps with He-Sea.
  One of only 2 such overlaps (with ST36/ST organ).
- **Ma Dan-yang Heavenly Star Point**
- **Ke Subject** (Five Element: Earth controlled by Wood)

**Total designations: 5** (He-Sea/Earth, Lower He-Sea,
Hui-Meeting Tendons, Ma Dan-yang, Ke Subject). Among the
highest for any single point in the Atlas. The GB plan's Key
Question #4 asked whether this is the most — comparable to
ST36 (He-Sea/Earth, Lower He-Sea, Command Point of Abdomen,
Ma Dan-yang, Horary).
→ Cross-ref: B01 S3.1

### Cross-tradition mapping

**TCM acupoint:** GB34 Yanglingquan — smooth Liver Qi
(疏肝理气), benefit tendons and sinews (舒筋活络), clear
Gallbladder damp-heat (清利胆腑湿热). The primary point for
tendon disorders body-wide (Hui-Meeting), Liver-Gallbladder
conditions (Lower He-Sea), and lateral knee/leg disorders
(local).

**Trigger point:** **peroneus longus TrP** — proximal origin
near the fibular head, referred pain to the lateral ankle and
lateral calf. Dorsher correspondence: anatomically precise.
GB34 needle enters through the peroneus longus origin. Also:
**tibialis anterior TrP** — deep to peroneus longus, partially
accessible from GB34. 🟢

**Ashi:** frequently tender point in patients with lateral
knee pain, peroneal tendinopathy, ITB syndrome, and knee
osteoarthritis. The fibular head is a common site of lateral
knee tenderness. 🟢

**Marma:** **Janu Marma** (जानु मर्म, knee Marma) —
classified as a Sandhi (joint) Marma in Sushruta Samhita.
Located at the knee joint, injury produces pangu (lameness).
GB34 at the fibular head falls within the Janu Marma zone.
Moderate correspondence. 🟡

**Head zone:** L4-S2 dermatome → lower limb, partial perineal.
GB34's territory does NOT overlap with gallbladder viscerotome
(T7-T9). This segmental mismatch means Lower He-Sea organ
effects CANNOT be explained by simple segmental reflex. 🟡

**Anatomy Trains:** GB34 at the fibular head is a key node on
the **Lateral Line** (Myers). The fibular head is a bony relay
station where tension transfers from the ITB above to the
peroneal muscles below. Also relevant to the **Spiral Line**
which crosses the lateral knee. 🟢

**Neurogenic spot:** not specifically studied. The common
peroneal nerve's vulnerability to compression at the fibular
neck suggests neurogenic potential.

**Cupping:** the lateral knee/fibular head area has limited
surface for cupping. Not a typical cupping site.

**Convergence score: 6/8** (TCM, Trigger Point 🟢, Ashi 🟢,
Marma 🟡, Head zone 🟡, Anatomy Trains 🟢). Multi-system
convergence at a critical bony landmark where nerve, fascial
line, trigger point, and traditional point align.

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Hoàng Đế Nội Kinh (黄帝内经):
- Lingshu Ch.1 (九针十二原): GB34 as He-Sea — "When the Fu
  organs are diseased, treat the He-Sea."
- Lingshu Ch.4 (邪气脏腑病形): GB34 as Lower He-Sea of GB —
  "When there is bitter taste and frequent sighing, the
  disease is in the Gallbladder; needle GB34."
- Nanjing Ch.45 (难经): GB34 as Hui-Meeting of Tendons —
  "for all tendon diseases, use GB34."

Zhenjiu Jiayi Jing (针灸甲乙经, Huangfu Mi, 282 CE): bitter
taste, sighing, vomiting bile, lateral knee pain, numbness of
the shin, difficulty walking.

Ma Dan-yang Heavenly Star Songs (马丹阳天星十二穴歌, Song
Dynasty): GB34 for cold-Bi of the knee, lateral leg numbness,
hemiplegia, muscle cramping.

Zhenjiu Dacheng (针灸大成, Yang Jizhou, 1601): jaundice,
bitter taste, vomiting, hypochondriac pain, knee pain,
swelling, tendon contracture, hemiplegia, childhood
convulsions.

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, Manual of Acupuncture):
- **Tendon disorders (Hui-Meeting):** tendinitis, muscle
  cramps, contractures, spasms, weakness, atrophy — at ANY
  body region, not just local. This is the defining
  Hui-Meeting function: GB34 treats ALL tendon/sinew
  pathology regardless of location.
- **Liver-Gallbladder conditions:** Liver Qi Stagnation
  (hypochondriac pain, depression, irritability, PMS),
  jaundice, cholecystitis, cholecystolithiasis, bitter taste,
  nausea
- **Lateral knee/leg:** knee joint pain, lateral knee pain,
  peroneal nerve disorders
- **Hemiplegia:** both upper and lower limb (Hui-Meeting
  extends influence beyond local territory)
- **Childhood convulsions:** traditional indication — sinew
  disorder in TCM framework

Kinh nghiệm lâm sàng nổi bật:
- **Liver Qi Stagnation formula: LR3 + LR14 + PC6 + GB34** —
  the most frequently prescribed 4-point combination for Liver
  Qi Stagnation across all documented meridians. GB34 serves
  as the Yang channel complement to three Yin channel points.
  → Cross-ref: AP-12 LR3, LR14 entries
- **Sciatica primary protocol: GB30 + BL40 + GB34** — two
  levels of the sciatic-peroneal nerve system (GB30 trunk,
  GB34 common peroneal) plus popliteal fossa (BL40 tibial).
  Covers the sciatic nerve at three anatomical levels.
- **Knee OA protocol: SP9 (medial) + GB34 (lateral)** —
  medial and lateral knee covering both compartments. Often
  supplemented with ST35/EX-LE2 Xiyan (knee eyes).
  → Cross-ref: AP-04 SP9 entry
- **GB24 + GB34 + LR3: Gallbladder damp-heat** — Front-Mu
  (GB24) + Lower He-Sea (GB34) + Yuan-Source (LR3) for
  cholecystitis/jaundice. Documented in GB24 entry.
- **Shaoyang migraine: GB20 + GB8 + TE5 + GB34** — Zhao
  et al. 2017 (JAMA Internal Medicine) specified GB34 as
  additional point for Shaoyang-type headache.
  → Cross-ref: AP-10 TE5 entry
- **Perioperative protocol: LI4 + PC6 + ST36 + GB34** —
  standard 4-point combination for postoperative nausea, pain,
  and recovery. → Cross-ref: AP-02 LI4 entry
- **LR4 + GB34 + LR3 + ST44: jaundice** — classical
  combination from Zhen Jiu Zi Sheng Jing (1220).
  → Cross-ref: AP-12 LR4 entry
- **LR7 + GB34 + ST35 + ST36: knee disorders** — classical
  combination from Zhen Jiu Zi Sheng Jing (1220).
  → Cross-ref: AP-12 LR7 entry
→ Cross-ref: B01 S3.1, S3.2

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

**1. Gallbladder motility / organ-specific effects —
🟡 Plausible**

Multiple studies have examined GB34's effect on gallbladder
function using real-time ultrasound. Acupuncture at GB34 has
been shown to modulate gallbladder volume and ejection
fraction in healthy subjects. This is consistent with the
Lower He-Sea traditional function — the point designated
for Gallbladder organ treatment produces measurable
gallbladder physiological changes.

This parallels the SP9-LR8 organ-specificity finding (Kim
2012, documented in AP-04): SP9 → splenic blood flow, LR8 →
hepatic blood flow. GB34 → gallbladder motility. Three He-Sea
points on three different channels, each affecting its own
traditional organ. This emerging pattern of He-Sea
organ-specificity is one of the most compelling matches
between traditional point categories and modern physiological
outcomes.

The segmental mismatch (GB34 at L4-S2 vs gallbladder at
T7-T9) means the mechanism CANNOT be simple segmental reflex —
it would require a supraspinal relay pathway, which has not
been mapped.

Confidence: 🟡 — multiple supporting studies but limited
sample sizes. No systematic review. Mechanism unknown.

**2. Brain activation patterns — 🟡 Plausible**

2025 (Frontiers in Neuroscience): ST36 vs GB34 direct
comparison. Despite anatomical proximity (both near the knee,
overlapping L4-S2 dermatomes, DIFFERENT meridians):
- ST36 → visual network activation, correlated with
  noradrenaline/acetylcholine receptor maps
- GB34 → **subcortical/basal ganglia activation**, correlated
  with **dopamine/serotonin** receptor maps

This is significant for two reasons:
(a) It demonstrates point-specificity BEYOND simple somatotopy
— two nearby points producing distinct brain activation
patterns.
(b) The basal ganglia are the brain's primary motor control
centers — movement planning, muscle tone regulation, motor
coordination. GB34's traditional role as Hui-Meeting of
Tendons (governing musculotendinous function body-wide) would
require a CENTRAL motor system pathway — and the basal ganglia
are exactly that system. The fMRI data provides a candidate
neural substrate for the "tendon governance" claim. 🟡
→ Cross-ref: AP-03 ST36 entry, A09 S3.4

SP9+GB34 for knee osteoarthritis: fMRI studies (Frontiers in
Integrative Neuroscience 2019) detected limbic network
connectivity changes during acupuncture at SP9+GB34 for KOA.
The limbic component (emotional/pain processing) is distinct
from the basal ganglia component (motor) — suggesting GB34
may produce different central effects depending on clinical
condition or combination context.
→ Cross-ref: AP-04 SP9 entry

Confidence: 🟡 — single fMRI comparison study for the ST36
vs GB34 finding. The basal ganglia-tendon governance
connection is a hypothesis, not confirmed mechanism.

**3. Knee osteoarthritis — 🔴 Unvalidated (GB34-specific)**

GB34 appears in most multi-point knee acupuncture protocols.
As the primary lateral knee point, it complements SP9
(medial), with ST35/EX-LE2 (anterior) providing comprehensive
knee coverage. Multiple positive RCTs exist for multi-point
knee protocols, but GB34's individual contribution has never
been isolated.

Confidence: 🔴 — combination-only evidence.

**4. Liver Qi Stagnation formula — 🔴 Unvalidated
(formula-specific)**

The LR3+LR14+PC6+GB34 combination is one of the most
frequently prescribed formulas in clinical practice. Despite
ubiquity, no RCT has tested this specific 4-point combination
against its subsets or against a control condition.

Confidence: 🔴 — centuries of empirical use but no controlled
evidence.

**5. Perioperative protocol — 🟡 Plausible (protocol-level)**

The LI4+PC6+ST36+GB34 protocol has positive evidence for
postoperative nausea and pain. GB34's specific contribution
cannot be separated from the combination.
→ Cross-ref: AP-02 LI4 entry
Confidence: 🟡 at protocol level, 🔴 for GB34-specific.

**6. Migraine (Shaoyang protocol) — 🟡 Plausible
(protocol-level)**

Zhao et al. 2017 (JAMA Internal Medicine): 249 patients,
Shaoyang-type temporal headache. GB34 was a specified
additional point alongside GB20+GB8+TE5. Positive result
(true acupuncture > sham > usual care). GB34's individual
contribution not isolatable.
→ Cross-ref: AP-10 TE5 entry
Confidence: 🟡 — positive JAMA-level RCT, but GB34 is one of
multiple points.

**7. Spasticity / motor recovery — 🔴 Unvalidated
(GB34-specific)**

GB34 is commonly included in post-stroke spasticity protocols.
As Hui-Meeting of Tendons, it is conceptually relevant. Some
preclinical studies suggest EA at GB34 may reduce muscle
spasticity markers, but controlled clinical evidence for
GB34's specific antispasticity effect is lacking.

Confidence: 🔴 — preclinical and protocol-level only.

**Sham comparison note:** GB34 has been used as both an ACTIVE
POINT (in treatment protocols) and a CONTROL POINT (as an
"irrelevant" point in studies of other conditions). The ST36
vs GB34 fMRI study (2025) explicitly chose GB34 as a contrast
point because they share dermatomes — yet found distinct brain
activation patterns, demonstrating that GB34 is NOT a neutral
control but an active neural site with its own specificity.

### Dynamic acupoint properties

**Lateral knee sensitization:** GB34 shows increased
tenderness in patients with knee osteoarthritis, lateral knee
pain, and peroneal tendinopathy. The fibular head area becomes
pressure-sensitive — Type 1 dynamic acupoint (local
musculoskeletal pathology sensitizing the overlying point).

**Gallbladder disease sensitization:** classical teaching
describes GB34 as becoming tender in cholecystitis and
cholelithiasis — consistent with Type 2 dynamic acupoint
(remote visceral pathology referring to a distant point). If
validated, this would be significant because the segmental
mismatch (GB34 at L4-S2 vs gallbladder at T7-T9) means the
sensitization pathway requires a NON-SEGMENTAL mechanism
(supraspinal or extra-segmental). No modern study has tested
GB34 pressure-pain thresholds in acute cholecystitis patients.

**Tendon pathology sensitization (untested prediction):**
whether GB34 becomes tender in tendon disorders at DISTANT
body sites (e.g., rotator cuff tendinitis, Achilles
tendinopathy) has not been studied. If confirmed, this would
be the strongest possible evidence for the Hui-Meeting of
Tendons concept — a point sensitizing in response to ANY
tendon pathology regardless of location. This is an untested
but testable prediction of the traditional system.
→ Cross-ref: C04 S4

### Cơ chế giải thích (BSA framework attribution)

**Tendon disorders — Hui-Meeting (CENTRAL MOTOR — proposed):**
The Hui-Meeting of Tendons claim requires a SYSTEMIC mechanism
— affecting tendons anywhere in the body. No peripheral
pathway explains whole-body tendon governance from a single
knee-level point. The 2025 fMRI finding (GB34 → subcortical/
basal ganglia → dopamine/serotonin) provides a candidate:
basal ganglia modulate muscle tone and motor coordination
throughout the body via the extrapyramidal system. If GB34
stimulation reliably activates basal ganglia circuits, this
COULD explain how a single lateral knee point affects
musculotendinous function systemically. 🟡

Proposed pathway:
Needle at GB34 → common peroneal nerve (L4-S2) → dorsal horn
→ spinocerebellar/spinothalamic tracts → basal ganglia/motor
cortex → descending modulation of muscle tone body-wide.

This is speculative but anatomically plausible. The common
peroneal nerve carries both sensory and motor fibers; strong
stimulation at a pre-branch point produces a robust afferent
signal reaching supraspinal motor centers.

**Gallbladder function — Lower He-Sea (SUPRASPINAL):**
GB34's effect on gallbladder motility cannot operate through
segmental reflex (L4-S2 ≠ T7-T9). Proposed supraspinal
pathway:
GB34 → common peroneal afferents → brainstem (NTS?) → vagal
efferents → gallbladder smooth muscle modulation.

This parallels SP9 → splenic blood flow (Kim 2012): He-Sea
points affecting their traditional organs via ascending
pathways that relay through the brainstem and descend via
autonomic efferents. If confirmed across multiple He-Sea
points, this would establish a common Lower He-Sea mechanism:
leg point → brainstem relay → vagal → specific Fu organ.

**Lateral knee/leg pain (PERIPHERAL + SEGMENTAL):**
Local: needle enters peroneus longus origin near the fibular
head → local vasodilation, mast cell degranulation, fascia
needle grasp (Langevin 2006) at the musculotendinous junction
+ periosteum. Segmental: common peroneal afferents (L4-S2) →
dorsal horn → segmental pain modulation for lateral leg and
knee.

**Liver Qi Stagnation formula (COMBINED — untested):**
In the LR3+LR14+PC6+GB34 formula, GB34's contribution is
unclear. Possible roles: (a) Yang channel complement providing
Lateral Line balance, (b) motor system modulation via basal
ganglia for physical manifestations (sighing, chest tightness),
(c) common peroneal → brainstem → descending modulation
parallel to other formula points' pathways.

**Gap:** GB34's Hui-Meeting of Tendons claim (body-wide tendon
governance) is the boldest traditional assertion for any point
in the Atlas. The fMRI basal ganglia finding is suggestive but
far from confirmatory. A definitive test would require:
(a) demonstrating that GB34 stimulation modulates tendon
reflex parameters (H-reflex amplitude, tendon jerk) at
DISTANT body sites, and (b) showing this modulation is
abolished by blocking the common peroneal nerve or the basal
ganglia circuit.

### Kỹ thuật kích thích + dose-response

Châm (B01): 25-40 mm perpendicular or slightly anteriorly
directed. The needle enters through the peroneus longus
origin. Do NOT direct posteriorly — the common peroneal nerve
wraps around the fibular neck posteriorly.
→ Cross-ref: B01 S5.2

**De Qi:** distention, heaviness, and numbness at the lateral
knee, sometimes radiating down the lateral leg following the
superficial peroneal nerve distribution.

**EA:** extensively used.
- **GB30-GB34:** standard sciatica protocol. Connects two
  levels of the sciatic-peroneal nerve system. 2/100 Hz
  alternating, 30 min.
- **SP9-GB34:** bilateral knee protocol for knee OA. Medial +
  lateral knee EA. 2 Hz continuous, 20-30 min. fMRI-studied.
- **GB34-GB39:** lateral leg protocol for lower limb tendon
  and muscle disorders.

Bấm huyệt (B02): firm pressure in the depression
anteroinferior to the fibular head. The bony landmark makes
it easy to locate by palpation.
→ Cross-ref: B02

Cứu (B08): moxa applicable for Cold-Bi of the knee and
cold-type tendon contracture.

### An toàn

⚠ **MODERATE risk.**

**Common peroneal nerve injury:** the primary safety concern.
The nerve wraps around the fibular neck immediately posterior
to the standard insertion point. Deep or posteriorly-directed
needling can contact the nerve.
- Symptoms: foot drop (inability to dorsiflex foot), lateral
  leg numbness (superficial peroneal territory), anterior leg
  numbness (deep peroneal territory)
- Peroneal nerve palsy from acupuncture at GB34 has been
  reported in case reports — typically from posteriorly-
  directed deep needling
- Prevention: direct needle slightly anteriorly or
  perpendicularly. Do NOT direct posteriorly toward the
  fibular neck.

**Anterior tibial recurrent artery:** small artery in the
region. Minor bleeding/hematoma risk.

**Comparison with GB30:** both are MODERATE risk points on the
GB channel involving the same nerve system (sciatic-peroneal).
GB30 risks sciatic nerve injury at depth; GB34 risks common
peroneal nerve injury laterally. GB30 is deeper (50-75 mm)
while GB34 is more superficial (25-40 mm), but the nerve is
closer to the surface at GB34.

**Pregnancy:** no specific contraindication (unlike GB21).

---

**Observation 18 — Cascading Pre-Branch Nerve Access
(GB30→GB34):**

GB30 and GB34 access the SAME nerve system
(sciatic→peroneal) at two successive hierarchical levels:

| Point | Nerve | Level | Pre-branch status | Access |
|-------|-------|-------|-------------------|--------|
| GB30 | Sciatic trunk | Hip | Before tibial/peroneal split | L4-S3, entire posterior+lateral lower limb |
| GB34 | Common peroneal | Knee | Before superficial/deep split | L4-S2, anterior+lateral leg + foot dorsum |

Sciatic trunk at GB30 (L4-S3):
— Tibial division (NOT re-accessed on GB channel)
  └→ calf muscles, plantar foot
— Common peroneal division
  └→ GB34 (L4-S2) — second-level access
     — Superficial peroneal → lateral leg + peroneals
     — Deep peroneal → anterior leg + foot dorsum

Between these two access points (GB31-GB33), the channel
traverses an entirely different nerve territory — the lateral
femoral cutaneous nerve (L2-L3). The spinal segment pattern
is non-monotonic: L4-S3 (GB30) → L2-L3 (GB31-GB33) → L4-S2
(GB34). The channel dips to a "higher" spinal level on the
thigh before returning to the sciatic system at the knee.

This cascading pattern explains why GB30-GB34 EA is a
standard sciatica pairing: it provides two entry points to
the same neural pathway at different hierarchical levels. The
proximal point (GB30) modulates the whole trunk; the distal
point (GB34) provides more specific access to the lateral
branch. Together they create a neural "pincer" — accessing
the common peroneal system from both above (trunk) and
directly at (branch) the division point.

This two-level pre-branch pattern is unique in the Atlas. No
other documented meridian accesses the same nerve system
twice at successively finer resolutions.

**Observation 19 — He-Sea / Lower He-Sea Overlap: Hand vs
Leg Channel Pattern:**

GB34 is both the He-Sea of the GB channel AND the Lower
He-Sea of the Gallbladder organ. The child plan characterized
this as "unique" — but ST36 also holds both designations.
The full pattern reveals a systematic hand/leg distinction:

| Channel type | Organ | He-Sea | Lower He-Sea | Same? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Yang LEG | ST | ST36 | ST36 | Yes |
| Yang LEG | GB | GB34 | GB34 | Yes |
| Yang LEG | BL | BL40 | BL39 | Debated |
| Yang HAND | LI | LI11 | ST37 | No |
| Yang HAND | SI | SI8 | ST39 | No |
| Yang HAND | TE | TE10 | BL39 | No |

All three Yang HAND channels have their Lower He-Sea on a
DIFFERENT channel — their own He-Sea is at the elbow, too
distant from the abdominal Fu organs. The Lingshu Ch.4
principle ("六腑皆出足三阳" — all six Fu organs emerge at
the three Yang leg channels) solves this by assigning leg-
channel "proxy" points for arm-channel organs: ST37 serves
as LI's Lower He-Sea on the ST channel, ST39 serves SI's,
BL39 serves TE's.

For the two Yang LEG channels (ST and GB), no proxy is
needed — their own He-Sea is already on the lower limb,
anatomically positioned to serve as both. This is not
"unique" to GB but reveals the architectural logic of the
Lower He-Sea system: it ensures every Fu organ has a
treatment point on the Yang leg meridians, regardless of
whether the organ's own channel runs through the arm or
the leg.

Updated neural zone table (14 zones through 34 points):

| Zone | Points | Primary Nerve | Segment |
|------|--------|---------------|---------|
| 1 | GB1 | Lacrimal/Zygomatic | CN V1/V2 |
| 2 | GB2-GB8 | Auriculotemporal | CN V3 |
| 3 | GB9-GB12 | Occipital nerves | C2-C3 |
| 4 | GB13-GB15 | Supraorbital | CN V1 |
| 5 | GB16-GB19 | V1→C2 transition | V1/C2 |
| 6 | GB20 | Deep suboccipital | C1-C3+XI |
| 7 | GB21 | Supraclavicular+XI | C3-C4+XI |
| 8 | GB22-GB23 | Intercostal | T4 |
| 9 | GB24 | Intercostal | T7 |
| 10 | GB25-GB26 | Subcostal/lower intercostal | T11-L1 |
| 11 | GB27-GB28 | Iliohypogastric/ilioinguinal | L1 |
| 12 | GB29, GB31-GB33 | Lateral femoral cutaneous | L2-L3 |
| 13 | GB30 | Sciatic (inf. cluneal cutaneous) | L4-S3 |
| 14 | GB34 | Common peroneal (lat. sural cutaneous) | L4-S2 |

14 zones in 34 points. Zone 12 extended to include GB31-GB33
(same LFCN nerve territory as GB29, despite GB30/Zone 13
intervening — confirming the non-monotonic spinal segment
pattern noted in Obs 18). Body zone density: 8 zones in 14
body points (GB21-GB34) = 0.57 zones/pt.

---

## GB35 — Dương Giao (阳交, Yáng Jiāo)

**Evidence tier: B (Standard Entry)**
"Yang Intersection" (阳交) — 阳 (Yáng, Yang) + 交 (Jiāo,
intersection/crossing). Named for this point's role as the
crossing point where the Foot Shaoyang (GB) regular channel
meets the Yang Wei Mai extraordinary vessel. The name encodes
the architectural identity: a junction between two distinct
channel systems.

GB35 is the **Xi-Cleft (郄穴) of Yang Wei Mai** — specifically
of the extraordinary vessel, NOT of the GB regular channel.
The GB channel's own Xi-Cleft is GB36 (Waiqiu), located at the
SAME transverse level but on the opposite border of the fibula
(anterior vs posterior). This dual Xi-Cleft arrangement at the
same height is unique in the 14-meridian system.

GB35 is the **FIRST extraordinary vessel Xi-Cleft** documented
in the Atlas. All 9 previous Xi-Clefts (LU6, LI7, SP8, LR6,
HT6, PC4, SI6, TE7, GB36) belong to regular channels. Four
extraordinary vessels have designated Xi-Cleft points: Yang Wei
Mai (GB35), Yin Wei Mai (KI9), Yang Qiao Mai (BL59), Yin Qiao
Mai (KI8). The other four (Chong, Ren, Du, Dai) do not have
standardized Xi-Clefts.

GB35 is a **Meeting Point of GB and Yang Wei Mai** (足少阳、
阳维脉之会). The Yang Wei Mai pathway (documented at AP-10
TE5) passes through GB35 on its ascending trajectory: BL63
(lateral ankle) → **GB35 (lateral leg)** → lateral thigh/hip →
posterior costal region → SI10 → TE15 → GB21 → neck → ST8 →
GB13–GB20 → GV16 → GV15. GB35 marks the vessel's second
point — the first LOWER LEG point on the Yang Wei Mai
trajectory.
→ Cross-ref: AP-10 TE5 (line 241)

### Vị trí giải phẫu

**Surface:** posterior-lateral leg, 7 cun above the tip of the
lateral malleolus, on the posterior border of the fibula.
Approximately at the junction between the upper 2/3 and lower
1/3 of the leg.

**Depth sequence:** skin → subcutaneous tissue → crural fascia
→ posterior intermuscular septum between peroneus longus
(lateral compartment) and soleus (posterior compartment) →
fibular periosteum at depth.

**Innervation:** superficial peroneal nerve (L4-S1) for
cutaneous supply. The lateral sural cutaneous nerve (branch of
common peroneal) contributes at the posterior border. Deep
needle reaches the lateral-posterior intermuscular septum with
mixed lateral and posterior compartment innervation.

**Vascular:** peroneal artery perforating branches at the
posterior fibular border. Small caliber — minimal hemorrhage
risk.

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

The lateral-posterior intermuscular septum separates the
peroneal muscles (lateral compartment) from the soleus and
deep posterior compartment muscles (flexor hallucis longus at
depth). This septum attaches to the posterior fibular border —
making GB35 a periosteal-fascial junction point.

Needle insertion crosses the crural fascia and enters this
intermuscular septum. The septum contains free nerve endings,
fibroblasts, and loose connective tissue — the same fascial
enrichment pattern documented at other intermuscular septum
points (GB31, TE5-TE6).

### Phân loại huyệt

**Xi-Cleft: YES — Xi-Cleft of Yang Wei Mai** (阳维脉之郄穴)

This is NOT the Xi-Cleft of the GB regular channel (that is
GB36 Waiqiu). GB35 serves as the acute-condition point
specifically for Yang Wei Mai pathology.

**Extraordinary vessel Xi-Cleft system (4 standardized):**
| Extraordinary vessel | Xi-Cleft | Location (channel) |
|---|---|---|
| **Yang Wei Mai** | **GB35 (Yangjiao)** | **7 cun above lat. malleolus (GB)** |
| Yin Wei Mai | KI9 (Zhubin) | 5 cun above medial malleolus (KI) |
| Yang Qiao Mai | BL59 (Fuyang) | 3 cun above lat. malleolus (BL) |
| Yin Qiao Mai | KI8 (Jiaoxin) | 2 cun above medial malleolus (KI) |

All 4 extraordinary vessel Xi-Clefts are located on the LOWER
LEG — between knee and ankle — despite the vessels themselves
traversing the entire body. A clustering parallel to the Lower
He-Sea system (Obs 19), where leg points serve as access for
systems with wider distribution.

Five Shu: No
Yuan-Source: No
Luo-Connecting: No
Back-Shu: No
Front-Mu: No
Eight Confluent: No
Hui-Meeting: No

Special designations: Xi-Cleft of Yang Wei Mai + Meeting of
GB and Yang Wei Mai.

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM acupoint:** GB35 Yangjiao. Meeting of GB and Yang Wei
Mai. Functions: benefits the Shaoyang (calms alternating
chills/fever), activates the channel (lateral leg pain), eases
the chest and hypochondrium (Shaoyang territory). Classical
combination: GB35+TE5 for Yang Wei Mai conditions (both on the
Yang Wei Mai pathway).

**2. Trigger point:** peroneus longus trigger point at the
proximal lateral leg is in the general region. PL TrP refers
pain to the lateral malleolus and lateral ankle — no match for
GB35's primary Yang Wei Mai indications (chills/fever). Weak
correspondence for the systemic indication. 🟡

**3. Ashi correspondence:** tender in lateral leg strain and
peroneal tendinopathy. Consistent with the local pain
indication but not with the systemic Yang Wei Mai function. 🟡

**4. Marma point:** no specific Marma at the mid-lateral leg
in classical Ayurvedic texts.

**5. Head zone:** L4-S1 dermatome. No relationship to the
alternating chills/fever indication (systemic, not segmental).

**6. Anatomy Trains:** on the **Lateral Line** (Myers) via the
peroneus longus. GB35 sits on this myofascial continuity from
the foot through the lateral leg to the hip and shoulder. 🟡

**7. Neurogenic spot:** no data at this specific location.

**8. Cupping point:** the lateral leg surface is adequate for
cupping application.

**Convergence score: 3/8** (TCM, TrP-partial, Anatomy
Trains). The Yang Wei Mai function — GB35's defining Xi-Cleft
indication — has no cross-tradition support. Local
musculoskeletal correspondences exist but do not explain the
systemic traditional function.

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Nguồn cổ:
- Zhenjiu Jiayi Jing (Huangfu Mi, ~282 CE): lateral leg pain,
  knee swelling, chest fullness with difficulty breathing.
- Zhenjiu Dacheng (Yang Jizhou, 1601): lateral leg pain, knee
  pain, alternating chills and fever (往来寒热 — the hallmark
  Shaoyang symptom), facial swelling.

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- **Yang Wei Mai conditions (PRIMARY Xi-Cleft function):**
  alternating chills and fever, exterior defense disorders
- Activates the channel: lateral leg pain, knee pain/swelling
- Chest and hypochondriac fullness (Shaoyang territory)
- Facial swelling, body heaviness (secondary)

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

🔴 Unvalidated for all indications. No RCT, SR, or case
series isolating GB35. No neuroimaging study exists.

As the first extraordinary vessel Xi-Cleft documented, GB35
has no comparative evidence base. The Yang Wei Mai's clinical
significance is established through its confluent point TE5
(AP-10), not through its Xi-Cleft.

### Dynamic acupoint properties

No sensitization data at GB35. The intermuscular septum
location might show altered tenderness in lateral compartment
pathology, but the Yang Wei Mai systemic function (alternating
chills/fever) has no known peripheral sensitization mechanism.
→ Cross-ref: C04 S4

### Cơ chế giải thích

**Local mechanism:** periosteal and intermuscular septum
afferents (L4-S1) → dorsal horn → segmental modulation for
lateral leg and knee pain. Standard somatic pathway for the
local indications.

**Yang Wei Mai systemic mechanism:** the alternating chills/
fever indication requires thermoregulatory modulation — a
supraspinal mechanism via brainstem → hypothalamic pathways.
No direct evidence exists for GB35 → thermoregulation.

The Yang Wei Mai vessel "links all Yang channels" (维络诸阳)
and governs exterior defense (Wei Qi). If the vessel functions
as a distributed Yang-channel coordination network, its
Xi-Cleft (GB35) would serve as an acute-access point for
exterior defense dysregulation — conceptually parallel to
TE5's Eight Confluent role for the same vessel, but
specifically for acute conditions.

**Gap:** no mechanism study isolates GB35 from GB34 (2 cun
proximal, same nerve territory) or from GB36 (same level,
anterior border).
→ Cross-ref: C04 S3.4

### Kỹ thuật kích thích

Châm: 15–25 mm perpendicular insertion. Needle enters the
posterior intermuscular septum. De Qi: distention and soreness
at the lateral leg, sometimes radiating toward the knee or
ankle along the superficial peroneal nerve distribution.

Classical combination: GB35+TE5 for Yang Wei Mai conditions
(both on the vessel's pathway). GB35+GB36 (posterior + anterior
at same level) for comprehensive lateral leg treatment.

Cứu: applicable. Moxa for Cold-type lateral leg Bi pain.

### An toàn

LOW risk. Lateral leg with adequate soft tissue.

Precautions:
- Peroneal artery perforating branches at the posterior fibular
  border — small vessels, but apply pressure after withdrawal
  in patients on anticoagulants
- No major nerve at risk (superficial peroneal nerve runs
  within the lateral compartment, not at the posterior border)
- No pregnancy contraindication

---

## GB36 — Ngoại Khâu (外丘, Wài Qiū)

**Evidence tier: B (Standard Entry)**
"Outer Mound" (外丘) — 外 (Wài, outer/external) + 丘 (Qiū,
mound/hill). Named for the lateral (outer) prominence of the
fibula at this level — the point sits on the palpable anterior
border of the fibular shaft, which forms a slight "mound"
beneath the skin.

GB36 is the **Xi-Cleft (郄穴) of the GB regular channel** —
the 4th Yang Xi-Cleft fully documented in the Atlas, after LI7
(AP-02), SI6 (AP-06), and TE7 (AP-10). Located at the SAME
transverse level as GB35 (7 cun above the lateral malleolus)
but on the ANTERIOR border of the fibula. Two Xi-Cleft points —
one for the extraordinary vessel (GB35), one for the regular
channel (GB36) — separated by the width of the fibula at the
same height.

### Vị trí giải phẫu

**Surface:** lateral leg, 7 cun above the tip of the lateral
malleolus, on the anterior border of the fibula. Same
transverse level as GB35 (posterior border).

**Depth sequence:** skin → subcutaneous tissue → crural fascia
→ anterior intermuscular septum between peroneus longus
(lateral compartment) and extensor digitorum longus (anterior
compartment) → fibular periosteum at depth.

**Innervation:** superficial peroneal nerve (L4-S1) is the
primary cutaneous nerve. The deep peroneal nerve (L4-S1) is
in the anterior compartment accessible at depth. GB36 is
slightly more anterior than GB35, placing it closer to the
anterior compartment nerve supply.

**Vascular:** anterior tibial artery branches at depth in the
anterior compartment. Minimal surface vessel risk.

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

The anterior intermuscular septum separates the lateral
compartment (peroneus longus and brevis) from the anterior
compartment (extensor digitorum longus, extensor hallucis
longus, tibialis anterior). This septum attaches to the
anterior fibular border.

Compared to GB35 (posterior septum), GB36 accesses a different
fascial plane with different muscular boundaries. Same bone,
same height, different connective tissue architecture — the
fibula serves as the dividing structure between the two
Xi-Cleft points' target tissues.

### Phân loại huyệt

**Xi-Cleft: YES — Xi-Cleft of GB** (足少阳之郄穴)

**Xi-Cleft across all documented channels (9th regular, 4th
Yang):**
| Channel | Xi-Cleft | Yin/Yang | Primary Xi-Cleft function |
|---------|---------|----------|--------------------------|
| LU | LU6 | Yin | Hemoptysis (blood) |
| LI | LI7 | Yang | Acute toothache, facial pain |
| SP | SP8 | Yin | Menstrual blood, acute pain |
| LR | LR6 | Yin | Uterine blood |
| HT | HT6 | Yin | Night sweating (cardiac) |
| PC | PC4 | Yin | Chest blood, cardiac pain |
| SI | SI6 | Yang | Acute MSK pain, vision |
| TE | TE7 | Yang | Sudden deafness (ear) |
| **GB** | **GB36** | **Yang** | **Lateral body acute pain, chest fullness** |

**Yang Xi-Cleft pattern — 4th confirmation of channel-domain
specificity (AP-10 Obs. 8):**
- LI7: acute dental/facial pain — LI domain (face/oral)
- SI6: acute MSK pain + vision — SI domain (scapula/eye)
- TE7: sudden deafness — TE domain (ear)
- **GB36: acute lateral body pain, chest fullness — GB domain
  (lateral body, Shaoyang territory)**

GB36 treats the GB channel's LATERAL BODY acute emergencies.
The pattern holds across 4 Yang Xi-Clefts: each treats the
SIGNATURE ACUTE EMERGENCY of its channel's primary functional
territory.

**Yin vs Yang Xi-Cleft asymmetry (now 6 Yin + 4 Yang):**
- Yin: UNIVERSAL blood pattern (6/6 regardless of channel)
- Yang: CHANNEL-SPECIFIC domain pattern (4/4 = own channel's
  acute emergency)
→ Cross-ref: AP-02 LI7, AP-06 SI6, AP-10 TE7 (Obs. 8)

Five Shu: No
Yuan-Source: No
Luo-Connecting: No (GB37)
Back-Shu: No
Front-Mu: No
Eight Confluent: No
Hui-Meeting: No

Special designations: Xi-Cleft of GB.

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM acupoint:** GB36 Waiqiu. Xi-Cleft of GB. Functions:
activates the channel for acute pain (lateral leg, lateral
body), eases the chest and hypochondrium, calms the spirit.
Classical "mad dog bite" (疯犬伤) indication — one of very few
acupoints with a specific acute traumatic emergency indication,
reflecting the Xi-Cleft "acute crisis" principle taken to its
most extreme.

**2. Trigger point:** peroneus longus TrP at the anterior
fibular border — same general area. PL TrP refers pain to the
lateral malleolus. Moderate anatomical correspondence for
lateral leg pain. 🟡

**3. Ashi correspondence:** tender in lateral leg strain,
peroneal tendinopathy, and lateral compartment overuse.
Consistent with local pain indication. 🟡

**4. Marma point:** no specific Marma at the anterior
mid-lateral leg.

**5. Head zone:** L4-S1 dermatome. Matches lower limb pain
indication only.

**6. Anatomy Trains:** Lateral Line (Myers) via peroneus
longus — same as GB35. 🟡

**7. Neurogenic spot:** no data.

**8. Cupping point:** adequate surface for cupping application.

**Convergence score: 3/8** (TCM, TrP-partial, Anatomy
Trains-partial). Same level as GB35 — the lateral leg corridor
shows consistent moderate cross-tradition support for local
pain but no corroboration for systemic traditional functions.

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Nguồn cổ:
- Zhenjiu Jiayi Jing (Huangfu Mi, ~282 CE): neck stiffness
  (颈项强), chest fullness (胸满), lateral costal pain.
- Zhenjiu Dacheng (Yang Jizhou, 1601): mad dog bite
  (疯犬伤), lateral leg pain, skin pain, epilepsy (痫证).
  The "mad dog bite" indication is distinctive — GB36 is one
  of very few acupoints with a specific acute traumatic
  emergency indication in the classical literature.

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- **Xi-Cleft acute function (PRIMARY):** acute lateral body
  pain, acute chest/hypochondriac fullness and pain
- Neck stiffness (项强) with lateral component
- Lower limb pain, lateral leg pain
- Calms the spirit: epilepsy, mania (secondary classical)

Kinh nghiệm lâm sàng:
- GB36 is clinically LESS used than GB34 (2 cun proximal) for
  most lateral leg conditions. Its primary clinical value is as
  the Xi-Cleft for ACUTE GB channel presentations — acute
  hypochondriac pain and acute lateral migraine attacks
- Combined with GB34 for acute sciatica and lateral knee pain
- The "mad dog bite" indication, while historically
  interesting, is not a modern clinical application

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

🔴 Unvalidated for all indications. No RCT, SR, or case
series isolating GB36.

Xi-Cleft evidence profile (now 9/12 documented): only SP8 has
ANY modern evidence among Xi-Cleft points (Chen 2015
sensitization). The evidence desert across 9 Xi-Cleft points
is the most consistent gap in the Atlas — a category with
strong theoretical importance but near-zero controlled data.

### Dynamic acupoint properties

No sensitization data at GB36. The Xi-Cleft should show
increased sensitivity during acute GB channel conditions
(acute lateral migraine, acute hypochondriac pain, acute
gallbladder colic). The SP8 precedent (Chen 2015:
sensitization during acute dysmenorrhea — the ONLY Xi-Cleft
with sensitization data) suggests this is testable.
→ Cross-ref: C04 S4, AP-04 SP8

### Cơ chế giải thích

**Local mechanism:** anterior intermuscular septum afferents
(L4-S1) → dorsal horn → segmental modulation. Same neural
territory as GB35, accessed through a different fascial plane.

**Xi-Cleft acute mechanism:** Xi-Cleft points are proposed to
accumulate blood and Qi, creating higher-density afferent
zones particularly responsive during acute conditions. The
fascial intermuscular septum at GB36 — with enriched nerve
endings and fibroblast population — may provide the anatomical
substrate for this "accumulation."

**GB36 vs GB35 differentiation:** both at 7 cun, same primary
nerve (superficial peroneal, L4-S1). The anterior (GB36) vs
posterior (GB35) border distinction may differentiate:
- GB36 (anterior): closer to anterior compartment (deep
  peroneal nerve territory) → motor system access
- GB35 (posterior): closer to posterior compartment (tibial
  nerve territory at depth) → sensory/autonomic access

This anatomical separation may underlie the functional split:
GB36 for channel-specific acute pain (motor-sensory), GB35 for
Yang Wei Mai systemic regulation (autonomic). Unverified.
→ Cross-ref: C04 S3.4

### Kỹ thuật kích thích

Châm: 15–25 mm perpendicular insertion. Needle enters the
anterior intermuscular septum. De Qi: localized soreness at
the anterior fibular border, occasionally radiating distally.

Classical combination: GB36+GB34 for acute lateral leg/knee
pain (Xi-Cleft + He-Sea). GB35+GB36 (posterior + anterior at
same level) for comprehensive lateral compartment treatment.

For acute GB channel conditions: GB36+GB24 (Xi-Cleft +
Front-Mu of GB) for acute gallbladder pain — the traditional
"acute disease treat Xi-Cleft" principle combined with
organ-diagnostic Front-Mu.

Cứu: applicable.

### An toàn

LOW risk. Lateral leg with adequate soft tissue depth.

Precautions:
- Anterior tibial vessels at depth in the anterior compartment
  — standard depth (15–25 mm) stays within the intermuscular
  septum, above the deep vessels
- No major nerve at risk at standard depth
- No pregnancy contraindication

---

## GB37 — Quang Minh (光明, Guāng Míng)

**Evidence tier: B (Standard Entry)**
"Bright Light" (光明) — 光 (Guāng, light/brightness) + 明
(Míng, brightness/clarity). The name EXPLICITLY encodes the
point's traditional function: treating eye disorders to restore
bright, clear vision. GB37 is the ONLY point on the GB channel
with a vision-related name, and one of few points in the entire
361-point system named after a sensory function rather than
anatomy or location.

GB37 is the **Luo-Connecting point (络穴)** of the GB channel,
linking the Foot Shaoyang (GB) to its paired channel, the Foot
Jueyin (LR). The Luo vessel branches from GB37 and connects
to the Liver channel. This creates the bidirectional Yuan-Luo
pairing:
- **LR as host:** LR3 (Yuan-Source) + GB37 (Luo of GB)
- **GB as host:** GB40 (Yuan-Source) + LR5 (Luo of LR)
→ Cross-ref: AP-12 LR3 (line 115), LR5 (line 1540)

The LR-GB Luo pairing reveals an asymmetric functional
partition of Liver-attributed functions:
- **LR5** (Luo of LR): genital disorders — Liver's genital
  domain (Luo pathway ascends to genitalia, Lingshu Ch.10)
- **GB37** (Luo of GB): vision disorders — Liver's visual
  domain (Liver "opens into the eyes," 肝开窍于目)

Both are classical Liver functions, distributed between the
Yin Luo (private/internal: genital) and the Yang Luo
(external/sensory: vision). The paired channel's Luo points
partition the organ's function space along the Yin-Yang axis.

### Vị trí giải phẫu

**Surface:** lateral leg, 5 cun above the tip of the lateral
malleolus, on the anterior border of the fibula. In the groove
between the fibula and the peroneus longus/brevis muscles.

**Depth sequence:** skin → subcutaneous tissue → crural fascia
→ between peroneus longus and peroneus brevis → anterior
border of fibula (periosteum at depth).

**Innervation:** superficial peroneal nerve (L4-S1) — cutaneous
and muscular branches to the peroneal compartment. Same nerve
territory as GB35-GB36, 2 cun distal.

**Vascular:** peroneal artery perforating branches. Small
caliber at this level.

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

At 5 cun above the lateral malleolus, the needle passes
through the peroneal compartment — between the peroneus longus
(superficial) and peroneus brevis (deep). Both muscles share
superficial peroneal nerve innervation. The compartment is
enclosed by the crural fascia laterally and the anterior/
posterior intermuscular septa medially. This muscular target
differs from GB35-GB36 (intermuscular septum targets) — GB37
enters MUSCLE BELLY rather than fascial plane.

### Phân loại huyệt

**Luo-Connecting: YES** — Luo-Connecting point (络穴) of the
GB channel.

**Luo-Connecting points across documented channels:**
GB37 is the 9th Luo point documented (LU7, LI6, SP4, HT5,
SI7, PC6, TE5, LR5, GB37). The Luo points show remarkable
functional diversity — from Four Command Points (LU7) to
Eight Confluent pairs (SP4, PC6, TE5) to speech (HT5) to
vision (GB37).

**GB Luo vessel pathology (Lingshu Ch.10):**
- Excess (实): lower leg reversal cold (足胫寒 — cold/
  reversal in the shin and foot)
- Deficiency (虚): atrophy and weakness of the lower limbs
  (痿躄 — inability to walk/sit/stand)

The Luo pathology describes LOWER LIMB conditions — leg cold
(excess) and leg weakness (deficiency). The VISION function
comes from a different classical framework: the point's name
(光明 "Bright Light") and the channel connection (GB→LR→eyes).
Two distinct traditional logics producing two distinct function
sets at the same point.

Five Shu: No
Yuan-Source: No (GB40)
Xi-Cleft: No (GB36)
Back-Shu: No
Front-Mu: No
Eight Confluent: No
Hui-Meeting: No

Special designations: Luo-Connecting of GB.

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM acupoint:** GB37 Guangming. Luo-Connecting of GB.
Functions: benefits the eyes (PRIMARY — name encodes vision),
activates the channel (lateral leg pain), spreads Liver Qi
(via Luo vessel connection to LR). Classical combination:
BL1 + GB37 for comprehensive vision treatment (local + distal,
Taiyang + Shaoyang) — one of the oldest vision prescriptions.
→ Cross-ref: AP-06 SI6 (line 2133)

**2. Trigger point:** peroneus brevis TrP is in the general
area (lateral leg, anterior fibular border). PB TrP refers
pain to the lateral malleolus and lateral heel — no visual
correspondence. 🟡

**3. Ashi correspondence:** tender in peroneal tendinopathy
and lateral ankle instability referral patterns. Consistent
with local pain indication only. 🟡

**4. Marma point:** no specific Marma at the mid-lateral leg
for vision in classical Ayurvedic texts. The Marma system does
not link lower limb points to vision.

**5. Head zone:** L4-S1 dermatome. ZERO relationship to the
visual system (optic nerve = CN II, visual cortex = occipital
lobe). The segmental gap between L4-S1 and vision is among the
LARGEST in the Atlas.

**6. Anatomy Trains:** Lateral Line (Myers) via peroneus
longus/brevis. No visual system connection in myofascial
continuity theory. 🟡

**7. Neurogenic spot:** no data.

**8. Cupping point:** adequate lateral leg surface.

**Convergence score: 3/8** (TCM, TrP-partial, Ashi-partial).
The VISION function — GB37's defining and name-encoded
indication — has ZERO cross-tradition support. All non-TCM
systems recognize only the local musculoskeletal function. This
mirrors the pattern at SI6 (vision with no non-TCM support,
AP-06) and TE7 (deafness with no non-TCM support, AP-10).

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Nguồn cổ:
- Lingshu Ch.10 (Jing Mai): Luo vessel pathology — excess:
  lower leg cold/reversal; deficiency: atrophy, unable to walk.
- Zhenjiu Jiayi Jing (Huangfu Mi, ~282 CE): eye pain, night
  blindness (雀目, "sparrow eyes" — poor vision in dim light),
  lateral leg pain.
- Zhenjiu Dacheng (Yang Jizhou, 1601): eye disorders (目疾),
  night blindness, blurred vision, knee and leg pain, lower
  limb atrophy.

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- **Benefits the eyes (PRIMARY):** night blindness, blurred
  vision, eye pain, optic atrophy, early-stage cataracts
- Activates the channel: lateral leg pain, knee pain
- Luo pathology: lower limb weakness, lower limb cold/
  numbness (if affected channel is GB → treat via Luo)

Kinh nghiệm lâm sàng:
- GB37 is a standard component of vision prescriptions:
  BL1 + GB37 (local + distal, documented in AP-06 SI6);
  GB37 + LR3 (Yuan-Luo pair of the Wood/eye organ system)
- In AMD systematic review (Li 2023, Complementary Therapies
  in Medicine), GB37 was among the most commonly used points
  alongside BL1 and local eye points (AP-06 SI6 line 2142)
- Distinguished from GB20 (vision via suboccipital/vertebral
  artery) and BL1 (vision via local periorbital) — GB37 is
  the DISTAL vision point, far from the eye
→ Cross-ref: AP-06 SI6 (BL1+GB37 combination)

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

🔴 Unvalidated for all indications. No RCT or SR isolating
GB37 as a single point.

GB37 appears in vision treatment protocols (particularly AMD
and age-related eye conditions) as a supporting point but has
never been studied independently. The AMD systematic review
(Li 2023) lists GB37 among commonly used points but reports
multipoint protocols only — GB37's individual contribution
cannot be assessed.

### Dynamic acupoint properties

No sensitization data at GB37. The Luo point might show
altered tenderness during acute Liver-Gallbladder conditions
(Luo pathway connection), but no study has examined this.

The vision function poses a theoretical sensitization question:
would GB37 show any measurable change during acute visual
pathology? Given the L4-S1 → visual system segmental gap,
classical viscerosomatic sensitization is implausible. Any
sensitization at GB37 during visual conditions would require a
supraspinal feedback pathway with no known anatomical basis.
→ Cross-ref: C04 S4

### Cơ chế giải thích

**Local mechanism:** superficial peroneal afferents (L4-S1) →
dorsal horn → segmental modulation for lateral leg and knee
pain. Standard somatic pathway for the musculoskeletal
indications.

**Vision mechanism — EXTREME non-segmental claim:**
GB37 at L4-S1 treating vision disorders represents the MOST
EXTREME non-segmental sensory claim in the Atlas. Compare:
- SI6 (C8-T1) → vision: flagged as "among the most extreme"
  (AP-06 line 2149)
- GB37 (L4-S1) → vision: even further from the visual system

The segmental gap: L4-S1 (lower leg) has no spinal pathway
to CN II (optic nerve), the visual cortex (occipital lobe), or
any visual processing structure. Not segmental, not trigeminal,
not autonomic via known pathways.

**Classical rationale (channel pathway, not neural):**
GB37 (Luo of GB) → Luo vessel connects to LR channel → Liver
"opens into the eyes" (肝开窍于目) → therefore GB37 treats eye
disorders. This is a CHANNEL THEORY explanation, not a neural
mechanism — the "connection" is through the Luo vessel network,
not through anatomical nerve pathways.
