**Possible supraspinal mechanism:** somatic afferents from
L4-S1 → dorsal horn → spinothalamic tract → thalamus →
cortical processing. Acupuncture at lower limb points can
modulate widespread cortical activity (demonstrated for ST36,
GB34, SP6 by fMRI). If GB37 stimulation modulates visual
cortex activity via diffuse cortical modulation, this could
provide a mechanism — but this is entirely speculative with
no GB37-specific neuroimaging data.

**Gap:** GB37 needs point-specific fMRI study examining visual
cortex activation. Without it, the vision mechanism remains
the least explained traditional function in the Atlas.
→ Cross-ref: C04 S3.4

### Kỹ thuật kích thích

Châm: 15–25 mm perpendicular insertion into the peroneal
compartment. De Qi: soreness and distention at the lateral
leg, sometimes radiating to the lateral malleolus along the
superficial peroneal nerve distribution.

**Yuan-Luo protocol:**
- LR as host: LR3 + GB37 — for Liver-related eye disorders,
  Liver Qi stagnation affecting GB territory
- GB as host: GB40 + LR5 — for GB conditions treated via
  Liver Luo vessel
→ Cross-ref: AP-12 LR3 (line 115), LR5 (line 1540)

**Vision protocol:**
- BL1 + GB37: local periorbital + distal lower limb
- GB37 + LR3: Yuan-Luo pair for Wood/eye organ system
- GB37 + GB20: distal + suboccipital (vertebral artery/
  visual cortex access via occipital stimulation)

Cứu: applicable for deficiency-type vision conditions
(classical: nourish the eyes with warming).

### An toàn

LOW risk. Lateral leg, adequate soft tissue.

Precautions:
- Superficial peroneal nerve runs within the peroneal
  compartment — direct nerve contact may cause sharp shooting
  pain toward the dorsum of the foot. Adjust depth if sharp
  pain occurs
- No major vessel at risk at standard depth
- No pregnancy contraindication

---

## GB38 — Dương Phụ (阳辅, Yáng Fǔ)

**Evidence tier: B (Standard Entry)**
"Yang Assistance" (阳辅) — 阳 (Yáng, Yang) + 辅 (Fǔ, assist/
support/supplement). The name describes the point's location on
the Yang (lateral) side of the leg near the fibula — 辅 (Fǔ)
also means "secondary bone" or "splint bone," an alternative
classical name for the fibula (contrasted with the tibia as
the primary bone). "Yang Assistance" encodes both anatomy
(lateral/fibular) and function (supporting Yang energy).

GB38 is the **Five Shu River/Fire point** (经穴/火穴) of the
GB channel. As a Yang channel of the Wood organ, the Five Shu
element assignment is: Well=Metal, Spring=Water, Stream=Wood,
**River=Fire**, He-Sea=Earth. The River point traditionally
treats body heaviness, joint pain, and voice changes.

As Fire (Son of Wood), GB38 is the **Sedation point** (泻穴)
of the GB channel — used to drain excess from Wood/Gallbladder.
When Gallbladder excess manifests as irritability, bitter
taste, temporal headache, or hypochondriac distension, GB38
draws off the excess via the Mother-Son draining principle.

The GB Sedation-Tonification pair:
- **Tonification** (母穴): GB43 (Spring/Water — Water
  generates Wood)
- **Sedation** (泻穴): GB38 (River/Fire — Wood generates Fire)
→ Cross-ref: plan.md §3 Five Shu table

### Vị trí giải phẫu

**Surface:** lateral leg, 4 cun above the tip of the lateral
malleolus, on the anterior border of the fibula. 1 cun distal
to GB37, 1 cun proximal to GB39.

**Depth sequence:** skin → subcutaneous tissue → crural fascia
→ between peroneus brevis and extensor digitorum longus →
anterior fibular border (periosteum at depth).

**Innervation:** superficial peroneal nerve (L4-S1). At this
level (4 cun above the malleolus), the superficial peroneal
nerve is transitioning from the lateral compartment toward the
subcutaneous layer — it pierces the crural fascia at
approximately this level to become superficial. Needle
insertion may encounter the nerve as it emerges through the
fascia.

**Vascular:** peroneal artery perforating branches. Anterior
tibial artery at depth in the anterior compartment.

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

At 4 cun above the lateral malleolus, the peroneus brevis is
transitioning from muscle belly to tendon. The needle enters
the boundary between the peroneal and anterior compartments at
a point where tissue is becoming more tendinous. The crural
fascia is thinning as the fibula becomes more superficial
distally.

The superficial peroneal nerve's fascial emergence point is
clinically significant: this is a known site of nerve
entrapment (superficial peroneal nerve entrapment syndrome),
where the nerve exits through a fascial tunnel. GB38's
location near this fascial exit may contribute to the
characteristic sharp de Qi that can radiate toward the dorsum
of the foot.

### Phân loại huyệt

**Five Shu: YES — River/Fire** (经穴/火穴)

**River point properties (经穴):** in the Five Shu system,
River points correspond to conditions where "the voice changes
and the body feels heavy" (音变身热 — Lingshu Ch.1). The River
point treats voice alteration, generalized body symptoms, and
joint involvement.

**Fire element properties:** Fire on a Wood channel = the Son
element (Wood generates Fire). This makes GB38 the Sedation
point — draining excess from Wood/GB by activating the Son
(Fire). Clinical application: Gallbladder excess (irritability,
bitter taste, temporal headache, hypochondriac distension).

**Sedation point in paired comparison:**
| Channel | Sedation point | Element | Five Shu type |
|---|---|---|---|
| LR (Yin Wood) | LR2 (Xingjian) | Fire | Spring |
| **GB (Yang Wood)** | **GB38 (Yangfu)** | **Fire** | **River** |

Same sedation element (Fire) on paired channels, different
Five Shu types — confirming the Yin-Yang Five Shu orthogonality
pattern (AP-01 Obs. 22).
→ Cross-ref: AP-12 LR2

Yuan-Source: No (GB40)
Luo-Connecting: No (GB37)
Xi-Cleft: No (GB36)
Back-Shu: No
Front-Mu: No
Eight Confluent: No
Hui-Meeting: No

Special designations: Five Shu River/Fire, Sedation point.

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM acupoint:** GB38 Yangfu. River/Fire, Sedation point.
Functions: clears Gallbladder Heat (Sedation → drains Wood
excess), benefits the lateral body (channel activation), treats
Shaoyang migraine (Fire point clearing Heat from the temporal
region).

**2. Trigger point:** peroneus brevis TrP — refers pain to the
lateral malleolus and posterior lateral heel. Moderate
anatomical correspondence for lateral ankle/leg pain. 🟡

**3. Ashi correspondence:** tender in peroneal tendinopathy and
lateral ankle instability. Local pain correspondence. 🟡

**4. Marma point:** no specific Marma at this location.

**5. Head zone:** L4-S1 dermatome. No relationship to Shaoyang
headache indication (temporal region = CN V3, not spinal).

**6. Anatomy Trains:** Lateral Line via peroneus brevis. 🟡

**7. Neurogenic spot:** the superficial peroneal nerve fascial
emergence point is in this area — a potential site of
heightened neural sensitivity. This is NOT the traditional
"neurogenic spot" concept but represents a distinct mechanism
(nerve entrapment site). 🟡

**8. Cupping point:** adequate surface, though becoming narrow
as the leg tapers distally.

**Convergence score: 3/8** (TCM, TrP-partial, Ashi-partial).
Consistent with the lateral leg corridor pattern: moderate
local musculoskeletal support, no corroboration for systemic
functions (migraine, Gallbladder Heat clearing).

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Nguồn cổ:
- Zhenjiu Jiayi Jing (Huangfu Mi, ~282 CE): Shaoyang headache
  (偏头痛, temporal migraine), bitter taste in mouth (口苦),
  hypochondriac pain, lateral knee pain, lower limb cold/
  numbness.
- Zhenjiu Dacheng (Yang Jizhou, 1601): temporal headache,
  dizziness, eye pain, lateral body pain, knee/ankle joint
  pain, fever with body heaviness.

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- **Clears Gallbladder Heat (PRIMARY — Sedation point):**
  migraine (Shaoyang type), bitter taste, yellow tongue
  coating, irritability
- **River point indications:** body heaviness, joint pain
  (especially knee and ankle), voice changes
- Activates the channel: lateral leg pain, lateral ankle pain

Kinh nghiệm lâm sàng:
- GB38 is used as a SUPPLEMENTARY point in Shaoyang migraine
  protocols (where GB20, GB8, TE5 are the primary triad). Its
  Sedation function adds a draining component when migraine
  presents with Gallbladder Heat signs (bitter taste,
  irritability, thirst)
- Combined with GB34 for lateral knee conditions (He-Sea +
  River = proximal + distal on same channel)
- The LEAST clinically prominent point in this 5-point
  corridor — often omitted in favor of GB34 or GB39

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

🔴 Unvalidated for all indications. No RCT, SR, or case
series isolating GB38.

### Dynamic acupoint properties

No sensitization data. The superficial peroneal nerve fascial
emergence point near GB38 is a recognized site of clinical
sensitivity — patients with superficial peroneal nerve
entrapment show spontaneous tenderness at this location. This
is a NERVE ENTRAPMENT phenomenon, distinct from the traditional
viscerosomatic sensitization model.
→ Cross-ref: C04 S4

### Cơ chế giải thích

**Local mechanism:** superficial peroneal afferents (L4-S1) →
dorsal horn → segmental modulation for lateral leg, ankle, and
knee pain.

**Sedation/Fire-clearing mechanism:** the concept of "draining
Wood excess via Fire" has no identified neural substrate. The
Sedation point theory predicts that GB38 stimulation should
produce calming/inhibitory effects on Gallbladder-related
symptoms — but the distinction between GB38 vs adjacent points
(GB37, GB39) in the same nerve territory is mechanistically
unclear.

**Migraine mechanism:** GB38 is used for Shaoyang migraine,
but the neural pathway from L4-S1 to the temporal headache
region (CN V3) requires the same supraspinal mechanism as all
lower limb → headache claims. No GB38-specific neuroimaging
data exists.

**Gap:** the Sedation point concept is testable — does GB38
produce different autonomic or cortical effects compared to
non-Sedation points in the same nerve territory (GB37, GB39)?
This comparison has never been studied.
→ Cross-ref: C04 S3.4

### Kỹ thuật kích thích

Châm: 15–20 mm perpendicular insertion. The fibula is
increasingly superficial at this level — periosteal contact
is more likely than at proximal points.

De Qi: soreness at the lateral leg, with possible sharp
radiation toward the dorsum of the foot if the superficial
peroneal nerve is contacted at its fascial emergence.

Classical combination: GB38 as Sedation point in Gallbladder
excess protocols. Combined with GB43 (Tonification point) for
balanced GB regulation.

EA: GB38 may be included in lateral leg EA protocols
(GB34-GB39) but is not a standard EA endpoint.

Cứu: applicable for Cold-type conditions. CAUTION: as the
Sedation point, moxa (warming/tonifying) may contradict the
sedation function in Heat-excess presentations — classical
texts prefer needling (reducing method) at the Sedation point.

### An toàn

LOW risk. Lateral leg, fibula increasingly superficial.

Precautions:
- Superficial peroneal nerve emergence: the nerve exits the
  crural fascia near this level. Direct contact produces sharp
  shooting pain — redirect the needle if encountered
- Periosteal contact (fibula): increasingly common distally.
  The fibula is close to the surface at 4 cun level
- No major vessel at risk
- No pregnancy contraindication

---

## GB39 — Huyền Chung (悬钟, Xuán Zhōng)

**Evidence tier: B (Standard Entry)**
"Suspended Bell" (悬钟) — 悬 (Xuán, suspended/hanging) + 钟
(Zhōng, bell). Named for the point's location near the lower
fibula, where the bone tapers to a slender form resembling a
bell hanging above the lateral malleolus.

**Alternative name: 绝骨 Juégu** ("Severed Bone" or
"Terminated Bone") — 绝 (Jué, severed/terminated) + 骨 (Gǔ,
bone). Describes the anatomical observation that the fibula
becomes so thin at this level that it seems to "disappear."
This alternative name explicitly connects the point to its
bone/marrow governance function — the name refers to bone, and
the point governs Marrow.

GB39 is the **Hui-Meeting of Marrow** (髓会) — one of the
Eight Hui-Meeting Points (八会穴), the system in which each
point governs one of 8 fundamental body structures. The full
system (first documented at LR13, AP-12; expanded at GB34):

| Hui-Meeting | Point | Governs |
|---|---|---|
| Zang organs | LR13 (Zhangmen) | 5 solid organs |
| Fu organs | CV12 (Zhongwan) | 6 hollow organs |
| Qi | CV17 (Shanzhong) | Respiratory/metabolic |
| Blood | BL17 (Geshu) | Blood |
| **Tendons** | **GB34 (Yanglingquan)** | **Sinews/tendons** |
| Vessels | LU9 (Taiyuan) | Blood vessels |
| Bones | BL11 (Dazhu) | Skeletal |
| **Marrow** | **GB39 (Xuanzhong)** | **Brain, spinal cord, bone marrow** |
→ Cross-ref: AP-12 LR13 (line 2258), GB34

The GB channel alone holds **2 of the 8 Hui-Meeting points**
(GB34 = Tendons, GB39 = Marrow) — the only channel with two
Hui-Meeting points. Combined with the paired LR channel's LR13
(Zang), the LR-GB pair holds 3 of 8 Hui-Meeting points.

GB39 has significant cross-references across the Atlas:
- **HT5+GB39 for post-stroke aphasia** (AP-05): EA with fMRI
  evidence of cortical language area activation
- **SP6 dysmenorrhea active control** (AP-04): GB39 used as
  "unrelated acupoint" control (Chen & Chien 2013, p=0.371)
- **GB34-GB39 lateral leg EA** (GB34 section): standard lower
  limb tendon/muscle protocol

### Vị trí giải phẫu

**Surface:** lateral leg, 3 cun above the tip of the lateral
malleolus, on the posterior border of the fibula. In the groove
between the posterior edge of the fibula and the tendons of the
peroneus longus and peroneus brevis.

At this level, the fibula is significantly thinner than at
GB35-GB36 (7 cun) — the 绝骨 "severed bone" name describes
this thinning. The fibula tapers rapidly in its distal third.

**Depth sequence:** skin → subcutaneous tissue → crural fascia
→ between peroneus brevis tendon and the fibula → fibular
periosteum at depth.

**Innervation:** superficial peroneal nerve (L4-S1) for
cutaneous supply. The sural nerve (L5-S2, from medial sural +
lateral sural cutaneous) contributes to the posterior-lateral
territory near the ankle. Deep needle reaches the periosteal
afferents of the fibula (Aδ fibers).

**Vascular:** peroneal artery perforating branches at the
posterior fibular border. The lateral malleolar arterial network
begins forming at this level — slightly more vascular than
proximal points.

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

The fibular periosteum at 3 cun above the malleolus is thin
and richly innervated — periosteal Aδ afferents produce the
characteristic deep bone-aching sensation at GB39. The peroneus
brevis tendon (inserting on the base of the 5th metatarsal)
passes immediately adjacent, with its tendon sheath containing
mechanoreceptors and nociceptors.

The point name 绝骨 highlights a clinical finding: the fibula
is so thin and close to the surface that periosteal contact
during needling is nearly unavoidable. This periosteal
stimulation activates different afferent populations than
muscular stimulation at proximal points (GB35-GB37) —
predominantly Aδ periosteal nociceptors vs Aβ/C muscular
mechanoreceptors.

### Phân loại huyệt

**Hui-Meeting: YES — Hui-Meeting of Marrow** (髓会)

This is the second Hui-Meeting point on the GB channel (after
GB34 = Tendons). GB is the only channel with two Hui-Meeting
points.

**What is "Marrow" (髓)?** Key Question #10 from the plan.

Classical textual definitions:
- 脑为髓之海 "Brain is the Sea of Marrow" (Lingshu Ch.33,
  Hailian)
- 诸髓者皆属于脑 "All Marrow pertains to the Brain" (Suwen
  Ch.10, Wuzang Shengcheng)
- 肾生骨髓 "Kidneys generate Bone Marrow" (Suwen Ch.5,
  Yinyang Yingxiang Dalun)

Classical 髓 encompasses THREE structures:
1. **脑 Brain** — "Sea of Marrow" (Lingshu Ch.33)
2. **脊髓 Spinal cord** — continuous with the brain as the
   "Marrow pathway"
3. **骨髓 Bone marrow** — generated by Kidneys, fills bones

This is much BROADER than modern "bone marrow" (hematopoietic
tissue). Classical Marrow = the entire central nervous system
(brain + spinal cord) PLUS bone marrow, unified by the concept
of a shared constitutional substance: 精 (Essence) → 髓
(Marrow) → 脑 (Brain). The developmental pathway Kidney-
Essence → Marrow → Brain parallels modern understanding of
embryological relationships but unifies structures with
different developmental origins under one classical concept.

Five Shu: No
Yuan-Source: No (GB40)
Luo-Connecting: No (GB37)
Xi-Cleft: No (GB36)
Back-Shu: No
Front-Mu: No
Eight Confluent: No

Special designations: Hui-Meeting of Marrow.

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM acupoint:** GB39 Xuanzhong (Juegu). Hui-Meeting of
Marrow. Functions: nourishes Marrow and benefits the brain
(益髓健脑), strengthens bones (强骨), benefits the neck and
nape (利颈项), activates the channel (通经活络). Classical
"hundred diseases of the brain" point — used for conditions
involving brain, spinal cord, or bone marrow.

**2. Trigger point:** peroneus brevis TrP at the distal lateral
leg. PB TrP refers pain to the lateral malleolus and posterior
lateral heel. Moderate anatomical correspondence for lateral
ankle pain. 🟡

**3. Ashi correspondence:** tender in peroneal tendinopathy,
lateral ankle instability, and lateral malleolar periostitis
(the thin fibula is vulnerable to direct trauma). Local pain
correspondence. 🟡

**4. Marma point:** Gulpha Marma (ankle Marma) is in the
general vicinity but centers on the ankle joint proper, not the
distal fibula. Partial anatomical overlap. 🟡

**5. Head zone:** L4-S1 dermatome. No relationship to Marrow
(CNS) governance. The segmental gap between L4-S1 and brain
function parallels the extreme non-segmental claims at GB37
(vision) and HT5 (speech at C8-T1).

**6. Anatomy Trains:** Lateral Line via peroneus brevis tendon
at this level. The LL connects the lateral ankle to the lateral
hip and beyond. 🟡

**7. Neurogenic spot:** no data.

**8. Cupping point:** not ideal — fibula is superficial and
the leg tapers. Small cup only.

**Convergence score: 4/8** (TCM, TrP-partial, Ashi, Marma-
partial). Slightly higher than adjacent points due to Gulpha
Marma partial overlap. The Marrow governance function — GB39's
defining indication — has no cross-tradition support,
consistent with the pattern that the most systemically
important traditional functions are TCM-unique.

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Nguồn cổ:
- Lingshu Ch.33 (Hailian): the Sea of Marrow (脑) when
  sufficient produces energy and vigor; when insufficient
  produces dizziness, tinnitus, leg weakness, visual
  impairment, and desire to lie down. GB39 as Hui-Meeting
  of Marrow addresses these Marrow-deficiency symptoms.
- Zhenjiu Jiayi Jing (Huangfu Mi, ~282 CE): neck stiffness
  (項強), knee/leg pain, bone-Bi syndrome.
- Zhenjiu Dacheng (Yang Jizhou, 1601): hemiplegia, lower limb
  paralysis, neck stiffness, bone/marrow disorders, throat
  soreness.

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- **Marrow governance (PRIMARY):** dizziness, tinnitus (Marrow
  insufficiency → brain symptoms), bone diseases, paralysis,
  myelopathy
- **Benefits the neck and nape:** neck stiffness (项强) — one
  of GB39's most commonly used clinical functions
- Activates the channel: lateral leg pain, lateral ankle pain
- Hemiplegia, stroke rehabilitation (brain = Sea of Marrow)

Kinh nghiệm lâm sàng:
- GB39 is a standard point in stroke rehabilitation protocols:
  GB39+ST36+LI11 for hemiplegia (三点处方 "three-point
  prescription")
- GB39+HT5 for post-stroke aphasia (EA 2Hz, AP-05)
- GB39 for neck stiffness is one of its most reliable clinical
  applications — especially stiff neck upon waking (落枕)
- Distinguished from GB34 (Tendons = musculoskeletal system) —
  GB39 addresses the CNS (Marrow = brain + spinal cord) while
  GB34 addresses peripheral structures (tendons and muscles)

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

**1. Post-stroke aphasia (HT5+GB39) — 🟡 Plausible
(mechanistic)**

fMRI evidence: EA at HT5+GB39 modulated static and dynamic
functional connectivity among brain networks in post-stroke
aphasia patients (Frontiers in Neurology, 2022). Changes in
language-related cortical areas were demonstrated. Earlier
fMRI study (Chinese Journal of Integrative Medicine, 2015)
showed functional activation in distributed cortical areas.
→ Cross-ref: AP-05 HT5 (lines 1488-1494)

Protocol: EA 2 Hz, 30 minutes, HT5+GB39 bilateral.
Multicenter RCT protocol registered (Trials 2022) but
outcome data not yet published.

The speech pairing logic: HT5 (Heart Luo → tongue root =
peripheral speech pathway) + GB39 (Marrow governance = central
language processing). One point for the speech apparatus, one
for the brain.

Confidence: 🟡 — neuroimaging evidence supports cortical
activation, but no published clinical outcome data. fMRI
findings are MECHANISTIC, supporting the Marrow=brain
interpretation but not clinical efficacy.

**2. Dysmenorrhea active control — notable methodological
finding**

GB39 was used as the active control acupoint in SP6
dysmenorrhea research (Chen & Chien 2013): SP6 acupuncture vs
GB39 acupuncture → p=0.371 (NOT significant). GB39 was chosen
as "functionally unrelated" to menstrual pain.
→ Cross-ref: AP-04 SP6 (lines 279, 405-413)

The selection logic: GB39 (L4-S1, lateral leg) has no
traditional menstrual indication and a different segmental
innervation from SP6 (L3-L4, medial leg → uterine segments).
This neural domain separation made it a credible active
control. The non-significant result raised the point-
specificity question (is the acupuncture effect point-specific
or non-specific?), but notably SP6 ACUPRESSURE was significant
(p=0.001) — suggesting modality matters more than point
selection for some conditions.

**3. Marrow governance — 🔴 Unvalidated**

No direct evidence for GB39's "Marrow governance" claim. The
HT5+GB39 fMRI data supports cortical modulation but does not
demonstrate that GB39 alone, or the "Marrow" aspect
specifically, is responsible.

**4. Neck stiffness — 🔴 Unvalidated**

Widely used but never studied as an isolated intervention.
GB39 appears in neck stiffness protocols alongside GB20, GV14,
and other cervical points.

### Dynamic acupoint properties

No sensitization data specific to GB39. The periosteal
location (thin fibula close to surface) might show altered
sensitivity during bone/marrow pathology, but no study has
examined this.

The "Marrow deficiency" symptom complex (dizziness, tinnitus,
leg weakness, visual impairment — Lingshu Ch.33) would be the
appropriate clinical context for sensitization testing. If GB39
shows increased tenderness during CNS conditions but not during
musculoskeletal conditions, this would support the Marrow-
specific functional assignment.
→ Cross-ref: C04 S4

### Cơ chế giải thích

**Local mechanism:** periosteal and superficial peroneal
afferents (L4-S1) → dorsal horn → segmental modulation for
lateral leg and ankle pain. The thin fibula periosteum
produces strong Aδ afferent input — periosteal stimulation is
among the most potent forms of somatic afferent activation.

**Marrow governance mechanism (supraspinal):**
The claim that a single lower-leg point "governs" the entire
Marrow system (brain + spinal cord + bone marrow) requires
wide-ranging supraspinal modulation. The HT5+GB39 fMRI data
demonstrates cortical modulation from this territory — but
the specific pathway (L4-S1 afferents → which brainstem relay
→ which ascending pathway → cortical language areas) remains
uncharacterized.

Possible pathway: periosteal Aδ afferents (potent brainstem
activators) → PAG → diffuse cortical modulation. Periosteal
stimulation produces stronger PAG activation compared to
muscular stimulation — the thin fibula at GB39 may provide
exceptionally strong periosteal input. This could explain why
a lower-leg point was designated for brain/CNS governance: the
point with the most intense periosteal stimulation potential
on the lateral leg was empirically associated with the
strongest central effects.

**HT5+GB39 speech mechanism:**
- HT5 (C8-T1): ulnar nerve afferents → brainstem →
  Heart-tongue pathway. fMRI shows language cortex activation.
- GB39 (L4-S1): periosteal/peroneal afferents → brainstem →
  Marrow governance (brain modulation). fMRI confirms
  cortical activation.
- Combined: two different spinal levels (cervical + lumbar)
  converging on cortical language networks via independent
  brainstem pathways. The dual-input approach may produce
  stronger cortical modulation than either alone — a testable
  hypothesis.

**Gap:** GB39 needs point-specific fMRI study (without HT5)
to determine its independent cortical modulation pattern. The
current evidence conflates HT5 and GB39 contributions.
→ Cross-ref: C04 S3.4

### Kỹ thuật kích thích

Châm: 15–25 mm perpendicular insertion. Fibula is superficial
at this level — periosteal contact is common and often
intentional (the 绝骨 name implies bone-level stimulation).
De Qi: deep bone-aching sensation at the lateral leg, with
distention radiating toward the malleolus.

**EA protocols:**
- **HT5+GB39:** post-stroke aphasia protocol. EA 2 Hz,
  30 minutes, bilateral.
  → Cross-ref: AP-05 HT5 (line 1476)
- **GB34-GB39:** lateral leg protocol for lower limb tendon
  and muscle disorders. Same-channel EA pair spanning 6 cun
  of the lateral leg.
- **GB39+ST36:** frequently combined in stroke rehabilitation
  (with LI11 as a third point)

Bấm huyệt: firm pressure over the posterior fibular border.
The bony landmark makes localization straightforward.

Cứu: applicable for Marrow deficiency conditions (dizziness,
bone weakness). Warming/nourishing function complements the
tonification approach for constitutional deficiency.

### An toàn

LOW risk, with specific precaution.

Precautions:
- **Periosteal contact:** nearly unavoidable due to thin
  fibula. Not dangerous but produces sharp bone-aching
  sensation — warn the patient
- Peroneal artery perforating branches: slightly more
  concentrated than at proximal points. Apply pressure after
  withdrawal
- Sural nerve: contributes to posterior-lateral skin near
  the ankle. Sharp radiation toward the lateral foot suggests
  sural nerve contact — adjust angle
- No pregnancy contraindication

---

**Observation 20 — Dual Xi-Cleft at Same Level: Regular
Channel vs Extraordinary Vessel:**

GB35 (Xi-Cleft of Yang Wei Mai) and GB36 (Xi-Cleft of GB) are
both located at 7 cun above the lateral malleolus — sharing
the same transverse measurement level. They occupy OPPOSITE
borders of the fibula: GB35 posterior, GB36 anterior. This is
the ONLY instance in the Atlas where two Xi-Cleft points share
the same height.

The fibula serves as an anatomical DIVIDING LINE between two
functional domains at the same level:

| | GB35 (posterior) | GB36 (anterior) |
|---|---|---|
| Xi-Cleft of | Yang Wei Mai (extraordinary) | GB channel (regular) |
| Fascial target | Lateral-posterior septum | Lateral-anterior septum |
| Treatment domain | Systemic Yang Wei Mai pathology | Channel-specific acute emergency |
| Primary indication | Alternating chills/fever | Lateral body acute pain |

The dual Xi-Cleft arrangement raises a functional architecture
question: does the extraordinary vessel Xi-Cleft follow the
same pattern as regular channel Xi-Clefts? The regular channel
pattern (AP-10 Obs. 8) shows Yin = blood (universal) and
Yang = channel-domain acute emergency (channel-specific). GB35
as the first extraordinary vessel Xi-Cleft may follow a THIRD
pattern: vessel-domain acute conditions.

Three Xi-Cleft pattern types emerging:
1. Yin regular channel: blood-specific (universal across all
   Yin channels)
2. Yang regular channel: domain-specific acute emergency
   (different for each channel)
3. Extraordinary vessel: vessel-specific pathology (to be
   confirmed with BL59, KI8, KI9)

**Observation 21 — Marrow (髓) as Classical Neuroscience:
Brain + Spinal Cord + Bone Marrow:**

GB39 (Hui-Meeting of Marrow) answers Key Question #10:
classical 髓 (Marrow) is NOT equivalent to modern "bone
marrow." The classical texts (Lingshu Ch.33, Suwen Ch.10)
explicitly define Marrow as encompassing brain, spinal cord,
AND bone marrow — a scope closer to "central nervous system +
hematopoietic tissue" than to "bone marrow" alone.

The HT5+GB39 speech/aphasia evidence provides an empirical
test: if GB39 governs "Marrow," and Marrow includes brain,
then GB39 stimulation should affect brain function. The fMRI
data (Frontiers in Neurology 2022) shows cortical language
area activation. The pairing logic is coherent: HT5 accesses
the peripheral speech apparatus (Heart → tongue via Luo
vessel), GB39 accesses the central language processor (Marrow
→ brain). One peripheral, one central.

Among the 8 Hui-Meeting points, Marrow governance may be the
BROADEST in modern anatomical scope:

| Hui-Meeting | Modern scope | System breadth |
|---|---|---|
| Tendons (GB34) | Musculoskeletal connective tissue | Peripheral |
| Vessels (LU9) | Vascular system | Peripheral |
| Bones (BL11) | Skeletal system | Structural |
| Blood (BL17) | Hematological | Systemic |
| **Marrow (GB39)** | **CNS + bone marrow** | **Central + hematopoietic** |

This also explains why GB39 was selected as active control for
SP6 dysmenorrhea (AP-04): the researchers correctly identified
GB39 as belonging to a different functional domain (CNS/Marrow,
L4-S1) from SP6 (reproductive/uterine, L3-L4). The neural
domain separation validates the classical functional
specificity — each Hui-Meeting point genuinely operates in a
distinct physiological domain.

Updated neural zone table (15 zones through 39 points):

| Zone | Points | Primary Nerve | Segment |
|------|--------|---------------|---------|
| 1 | GB1 | Lacrimal/Zygomatic | CN V1/V2 |
| 2 | GB2-GB8 | Auriculotemporal | CN V3 |
| 3 | GB9-GB12 | Occipital nerves | C2-C3 |
| 4 | GB13-GB15 | Supraorbital | CN V1 |
| 5 | GB16-GB19 | V1→C2 transition | V1/C2 |
| 6 | GB20 | Deep suboccipital | C1-C3+XI |
| 7 | GB21 | Supraclavicular+XI | C3-C4+XI |
| 8 | GB22-GB23 | Intercostal | T4 |
| 9 | GB24 | Intercostal | T7 |
| 10 | GB25-GB26 | Subcostal/lower intercostal | T11-L1 |
| 11 | GB27-GB28 | Iliohypogastric/ilioinguinal | L1 |
| 12 | GB29, GB31-GB33 | Lateral femoral cutaneous | L2-L3 |
| 13 | GB30 | Sciatic (inf. cluneal cutaneous) | L4-S3 |
| 14 | GB34 | Common peroneal (lat. sural cutaneous) | L4-S2 |
| 15 | GB35-GB39 | Superficial peroneal | L4-S1 |

15 zones in 39 points. Zone 15 extends the cascading
pre-branch nerve access pattern from Observation 18 to a
THIRD level of resolution: the GB channel now accesses the
sciatic-peroneal nerve system at three successive branching
points:
- Zone 13 (GB30): sciatic trunk — BEFORE tibial/peroneal split
- Zone 14 (GB34): common peroneal — AFTER sciatic split,
  BEFORE deep/superficial split
- Zone 15 (GB35-39): superficial peroneal — AFTER deep/
  superficial split

No other documented meridian traverses the same nerve system
at three branching resolutions. The 5-point superficial
peroneal corridor (GB35-GB39, spanning 4 cun) matches the
saphenous corridor (SP5-SP9, 5 points) as the longest same-
nerve sequences in the Atlas.

## GB40 — Khâu Khư (丘墟, Qiū Xū)

**Evidence tier: B (Standard Entry)**
"Mound Ruins" (丘墟) — 丘 (Qiū, mound/hill) + 墟 (Xū,
ruins/depression). Describes the palpable hollow anterior-
inferior to the lateral malleolus — a "ruined mound" where
the bony prominence gives way to a depression. Some texts
read 墟 as a variant of 虚 (void/market), but the "mound
depression" reading best matches the anatomy.

GB40 is the **Yuan-Source point** (原穴) of the Gallbladder
channel — the 9th Yuan-Source documented in the Atlas (after
LU9, LI4, HT7, SI4, PC7, TE4, LR3, SP3). For Yang channels,
the Yuan point is SEPARATE from the Five Shu system — an
extra transport point between Stream and River (Nan Jing
Ch.62-66). On Yin channels, Yuan coincides with the Stream
point. GB40 fits this pattern: located spatially between
GB41 (Stream/Wood, metatarsal base) and GB38 (River/Fire,
4 cun above malleolus).

The Yin/Yang Yuan-Source divergence is now confirmed at
9/12 — ALL 5 Yin channels overlap Yuan=Stream, ALL 4
documented Yang channels separate Yuan from Five Shu.
100% consistency across 9 data points.
→ Cross-ref: AP-01 LU9 (Yuan=Stream=Hui-Meeting triple),
AP-02 LI4 (Yuan separate, first Yang confirmation)

Yuan-Source diagnostic function: Yuan points reflect the
state of the organ's fundamental Qi. GB40 tenderness or
sensitivity suggests Gallbladder dysfunction. Combined
with the Back-Shu (BL19) and Front-Mu (GB24) for a three-
level diagnostic-treatment triad.

**Host-Guest (主客) Yuan-Luo pairing:**
- GB as Host: **GB40 (Yuan) + LR5 (Luo)** — treating GB
  conditions via the Liver Luo pathway
- LR as Host: **LR3 (Yuan) + GB37 (Luo)** — treating
  Liver conditions via the GB Luo pathway

GB40 completes the third vertex of the GB Yuan-Luo
triangle. The bidirectional system has been documented
from the LR side (AP-12 LR5, line 1540) and the GB Luo
side (GB37 in this file). With GB40, all three connection
points are now fully documented.
→ Cross-ref: AP-12 LR5 (Host-Guest technique, line 1539)

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): anterior and inferior
to the external malleolus, in the depression on the lateral
side of the tendon of m. extensor digitorum longus.

Anatomical landmarks: in the depression anterior-inferior
to the tip of the lateral malleolus. The depression is
formed between the lateral malleolus posteriorly, the
tendons of extensor digitorum longus and peroneus tertius
anteriorly, and the calcaneofibular ligament deeply. The
sinus tarsi (a canal between talus and calcaneus) is
accessible at depth.

Spatial relationships:
- GB39 (Xuanzhong) is 3 cun proximal — on the posterior
  border of the fibula at the lateral leg level
- GB41 (Zulinqi) is distal — between 4th-5th metatarsal
  bases on the dorsum of the foot
- BL60 (Kunlun) is posterior — behind the lateral
  malleolus in the Achilles tendon area
- ST41 (Jiexi) is anterior — at the front of the ankle
  joint, more medial than GB40

Needle depth: 15–25 mm perpendicular or slightly oblique
toward the sinus tarsi.

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Intermediate dorsal cutaneous nerve** (terminal branch
  of superficial peroneal nerve, L4-S1): primary cutaneous
  innervation of the anterolateral ankle. At this level,
  the superficial peroneal nerve has emerged from the deep
  fascia and divided into its terminal cutaneous branches
- **Sural nerve (S1-S2):** runs posterior to the lateral
  malleolus. GB40's anterior position places it primarily
  in superficial peroneal territory, but the sural nerve
  is nearby — the transition zone between two nerve
  territories
- **Deep peroneal nerve:** passes under the extensor
  retinaculum at a deeper level. At standard needling
  depth, not typically engaged
- Spinal segments: L4-S1 (transitional, with S1-S2 sural
  contribution)
→ Cross-ref: A01 S2.3

This represents a new neural zone — **Zone 16**. Transition
from superficial peroneal trunk (Zone 15, GB35-39 in the
lateral leg) to superficial peroneal terminal branches at
the ankle, with sural nerve overlap. The nerve has emerged
from the lateral compartment and divided into its cutaneous
end-branches.

Fascial plane: at the ankle, the crural fascia thickens
into the superior and inferior extensor retinacula. GB40
lies in the depression adjacent to these retinacular
structures. The sinus tarsi fat pad is the deep target.
→ Cross-ref: A03 S3.4

Vascular: anterior lateral malleolar artery (branch of
anterior tibial artery), lateral tarsal artery. Branches
of the peroneal artery's perforating branch may also
contribute. All are small caliber vessels.

Muscle layers (superficial → deep):
- Skin → subcutaneous tissue
- Inferior extensor retinaculum (fascial band)
- Fat pad overlying the sinus tarsi
- No major muscle belly — this is a tendinous/ligamentous
  area between extensor tendons and the malleolus

### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

**Yuan-Source: YES.** Yuan-Source point of the Gallbladder
channel. For Yang channels, the Yuan point is a SEPARATE
6th transport point — Yang channels receive extra Qi from
San Jiao (Triple Burner), granting them an additional
point between the Stream and River (Nan Jing Ch.62).

The Yin/Yang Yuan-Source pattern (9/12 confirmed):
- ALL Yin channels: Yuan = Stream (single point, dual role)
- ALL Yang channels: Yuan ≠ Five Shu (separate point)
This is now the most consistently confirmed structural
rule in the Atlas. The remaining 3 channels (ST, BL, KI)
are predicted to follow the same pattern.
→ Cross-ref: AP-01 LU9 (first Yin confirmation), AP-02
LI4 (first Yang confirmation)

Five Shu: **No.** GB40 is NOT a Five Shu point. This
confirms the Yang channel pattern — Yuan is an extra point.
(The Five Shu points are GB44/43/41/38/34.)

Xi-Cleft: No (GB36).
Luo-Connecting: No (GB37).
Back-Shu: No (BL19 at T10).
Front-Mu: No (GB24).
Eight Confluent: No.
Hui-Meeting: No.
Special designations: None.

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM acupoint:** GB40 Qiuxu (丘墟). Yuan-Source.
Primary functions: spreads Liver-Gallbladder Qi, clears
Damp-Heat from the lower Jiao, activates the channel
(ankle/foot pain), benefits the eyes (via LR-GB Wood
connection). Used for: lateral ankle pain/sprain (local),
hypochondriac pain and distension (channel), acid reflux
(GB Qi rebellion), bitter taste (GB Heat), eye redness
and swelling (LR-GB→eyes), timidity and frequent sighing
(善太息 — GB Qi deficiency sign, Su Wen Ch.8: GB governs
decision-making, 决断).

**2. Trigger point:** peroneus brevis at its fibular groove
near the malleolus has trigger points that refer pain to
the lateral ankle and dorsum of foot. Extensor digitorum
brevis origin at the calcaneus (just inferior to GB40)
also develops TrPs with local ankle pain. Moderate
correspondence — GB40 is between these two TrP zones. 🟡

**3. Ashi correspondence:** STRONGLY tender in lateral
ankle sprains. The anterior talofibular ligament (ATFL) —
the most commonly injured ankle ligament — is deep to
GB40. Chronic ankle instability produces persistent
tenderness at this depression. Strong Ashi
correspondence. 🟡

**4. Marma point:** **Gulpha Marma** (गुल्फ, ankle joint)
— one of the 107-point Ayurvedic Marma system. Located at
the ankle joint region. Both medial (KI3/KI6) and lateral
(GB40) surfaces are included. Moderate-strong
correspondence — Gulpha is a Sandhi Marma (joint type),
and GB40 sits directly over the ankle joint depression. 🟡
→ Cross-ref: A09 S3.2

**5. Head zone:** L5-S1 dermatome at the lateral ankle.
Gallbladder viscerotome = T7-T10. Segmental gap: ~9-12
segments — consistent with the foot Yuan-Source distance
pattern. Foot Yuan points have inherently larger segmental
gaps than hand Yuan points. Any organ-level effects require
supraspinal pathways.
→ Cross-ref: A07 S2.2

**6. Anatomy Trains:** **Lateral Line (LL)** — the peroneal
muscles are key stations on the LL, which descends through
the lateral leg to the lateral foot. GB40 at the peroneal
tendon area is on the distal LL pathway. The LL is primarily
a lateral/rotational stabilizer — the same functional
domain as the GB channel's lateral body territory. 🟡
→ Cross-ref: A03 S3.4

**7. Neurogenic inflammatory spot:** No specific data for
the lateral ankle.

**8. Cupping point:** Not a standard cupping site. The
ankle depression is too small for standard cups.

**Convergence score: 4/8** (TCM, Trigger Point moderate,
Ashi strong, Marma). The lateral ankle depression provides
good anatomical correspondence across multiple traditions
due to its clinical significance in ankle pathology.

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Nguồn cổ:
- Jiayi Jing (Huangfu Mi, 259 CE): lateral chest distension
  (胸胁满), acid reflux (善呕), lower limb atrophy and
  inability to walk, lateral ankle pain, cold malaria.
- Zhenjiu Dacheng (Yang Jizhou, 1601): hypochondriac pain,
  frequent sighing (善太息), timidity and indecisiveness
  (善惊恐), eye redness (目赤肿痛), bitter taste (口苦),
  lateral ankle sprain, night blindness (雀目).
- Deadman/WHO: hypochondriac pain and distension, frequent
  sighing, malaria, pain and swelling of the lateral
  malleolus, eye redness, night blindness, breast abscess.

The **timidity and frequent sighing** (善太息, 善惊恐)
indications are specifically GB-related: Gallbladder governs
decision-making (决断, Su Wen Ch.8). Yuan-Source access to
GB Qi directly addresses GB deficiency manifesting as
indecisiveness and timidity. This is the MOST specific
Yuan-Source → organ function correlation on the GB channel.

Kinh nghiệm lâm sàng:
- Lateral ankle sprain: GB40 is the primary local point.
  Combined with BL60 (posterior), ST41 (anterior), and
  Ashi points for comprehensive ankle treatment
- Hypochondriac pain protocol: GB40 (Yuan) + GB34 (He-Sea)
  + GB24 (Front-Mu) — three GB special points addressing
  the organ from different levels
- BL19 (Back-Shu) + GB24 (Front-Mu) + GB40 (Yuan) =
  the Shu-Mu-Yuan triad for comprehensive GB treatment.
  Three-level organ access: posterior (Shu), anterior (Mu),
  distal (Yuan)

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

🔴 Unvalidated for all indications. No RCT or SR isolating
GB40 as a single point.

GB40 appears in ankle rehabilitation protocols for lateral
ankle sprain as part of multipoint prescriptions (typically
with BL60, ST41, KI3, Ashi), but GB40 has never been tested
in isolation. The ankle sprain indication has the strongest
face validity — a local point directly over the ATFL — but
lacks controlled evidence.

### Dynamic acupoint properties

No sensitization data at GB40. Yuan-Source points
theoretically show enhanced sensitivity during organ
dysfunction. GB40 sensitivity during cholecystitis or
hepato-biliary conditions would be predicted by Yuan-Source
theory but has not been studied.

The ankle sprain context provides an alternative
sensitization pathway: ATFL injury → local inflammation →
GB40 becomes Ashi-positive. This represents MECHANICAL
sensitization (tissue injury) rather than viscerosomatic
sensitization (organ→point reflex). Both pathways converge
at the same point but through different mechanisms.
→ Cross-ref: C04 S4

### Cơ chế giải thích

**Local mechanism:** ankle joint afferents (L4-S1) →
dorsal horn → segmental modulation for ankle and foot
pain. The sinus tarsi and ATFL are deep targets —
periarticular needling modulates proprioceptive and
nociceptive input from the ankle joint capsule. This is
the most straightforward mechanism and explains the
ankle sprain indication.

**Yuan-Source → organ mechanism:** L4-S1 afferents at
GB40 have NO segmental relationship to the gallbladder
organ (T7-T10 viscerotome). The Yuan-Source diagnostic
claim (GB40 sensitivity reflects GB organ status) and
the therapeutic claim (stimulating GB40 benefits the
gallbladder) are BOTH non-segmental, requiring supraspinal
pathways. Same challenge as all distal Yuan points —
the mechanism for organ-level effects from extremity
points remains the central unsolved problem.
→ Cross-ref: C04 S3.4

**Eye/vision mechanism:** via the LR-GB Wood connection
(Liver "opens into the eyes"). Non-segmental (L4-S1 →
vision). Same category as GB37 (Luo) vision claim but
less extreme in classical emphasis — GB40 is a secondary
eye point, not a primary one.

### Kỹ thuật kích thích

Châm: 15–25 mm perpendicular into the sinus tarsi
depression. De Qi: distention and heaviness at the ankle,
sometimes radiating to the dorsum of the foot or along
the lateral malleolus.

**Yuan-Luo protocol:**
- GB as host: GB40 (Yuan) + LR5 (Luo) — for GB channel
  conditions treated via the Liver Luo vessel
- LR as host: LR3 (Yuan) + GB37 (Luo) — for Liver
  conditions treated via the GB Luo vessel
→ Cross-ref: AP-12 LR5 (line 1539), GB37 in this file

**Shu-Mu-Yuan triad:**
BL19 (Back-Shu) + GB24 (Front-Mu) + GB40 (Yuan) =
comprehensive three-level GB treatment. Posterior, anterior,
and distal organ access.

Bấm huyệt: firm pressure into the ankle depression.
The bony landmarks make localization straightforward.

Cứu: applicable for GB deficiency patterns (timidity,
indecisiveness, frequent sighing — cold/Qi deficiency
type). Warming moxa at GB40 supplements GB fundamental
Qi via the Yuan-Source mechanism.

### An toàn

LOW risk. Open depression with minimal vital structures
at standard depth.

Precautions:
- **Sinus tarsi:** deep insertion enters a confined
  articular space. May produce sharp joint-capsule
  sensation. Not dangerous but adjust depth if joint
  discomfort occurs
- **ATFL (anterior talofibular ligament):** deep to
  GB40. In acute ankle sprain, the ligament is inflamed
  — vigorous manipulation could aggravate injury. Use
  gentle technique in acute sprains
- Anterior lateral malleolar artery branches: small
  caliber. Apply pressure after withdrawal if bleeding
- No pregnancy contraindication

## GB41 — Túc Lâm Khấp (足临泣, Zú Lín Qì)

**Evidence tier: B (Standard Entry)**
"Foot Overlooking Tears" (足临泣) — 足 (Zú, foot) + 临
(Lín, looking down/approaching) + 泣 (Qì, weeping/tearing).
The 足 prefix distinguishes this point from its head
counterpart **GB15 (头临泣, Tóu Lín Qì, "Head Overlooking
Tears")**. Both share the 临泣 name — the SECOND head-foot
naming pair on the GB channel (the first being GB11/GB44,
窍阴 Qiaoyin). GB is the ONLY documented channel with TWO
head-foot naming pairs.

Both naming pairs encode VISION/ORIFICE functions:
- GB15/GB41 (临泣 Linqi): "Overlooking Tears" — lacrimation
- GB11/GB44 (窍阴 Qiaoyin): "Portal of Yin" — sensory
  orifices

This reflects GB's dual identity as the Yang pair of
Liver ("opens into the eyes," 肝开窍于目) and the channel
with the most cranial points (20/44).

GB41 is the **Five Shu Stream/Wood point** (输穴/木穴) AND
**Eight Confluent point opening Dai Mai** (带脉). Triple
category membership: Stream + Horary + Eight Confluent.

As Stream/Wood where the organ element is also Wood, GB41
is the **Horary point** (本穴) — strongest when used during
GB peak time (23:00–01:00, 子时 Zi hour).
→ Cross-ref: plan.md §3 Five Shu table

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): on the dorsum of the
foot, in the depression distal to the junction of the 4th
and 5th metatarsal bases.

Anatomical landmarks: on the lateral side of the tendon of
extensor digitorum longus (4th tendon), at the proximal end
of the 4th-5th intermetatarsal space. The depression is
palpable between the metatarsal bases where the bone widens
before the tarsal articulation.

Spatial relationships:
- GB40 (Qiuxu, Yuan) is proximal — at the ankle depression.
  GB40→GB41 crosses from the ankle to the dorsal foot
- GB42 (Diwuhui) is distal — further along the 4th-5th
  intermetatarsal space
- LR3 (Taichong, Yuan of LR) is in the 1st-2nd
  intermetatarsal space — the MEDIAL counterpart to GB41's
  lateral position. Interior-exterior paired channels,
  opposite sides of the dorsal foot

Needle depth: 10–15 mm perpendicular or oblique.

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **4th dorsal digital nerve** (from intermediate dorsal
  cutaneous nerve, terminal branch of superficial peroneal
  nerve, L5-S1): primary sensory nerve at the 4th-5th
  metatarsal interspace on the dorsum
- **Deep peroneal nerve lateral branch:** may contribute
  to the 4th intermetatarsal space in some individuals
  (variable anatomy)
- Spinal segments: L5-S1
→ Cross-ref: A01 S2.3

**Neural zone: Zone 17** (dorsal foot digital nerves,
L5-S1). Distinct from Zone 16 (GB40, ankle transition)
in that the nerve has fully divided into its terminal
digital branches and the sural contribution is minimal.
Zone 17 extends from GB41 through GB44.

Fascial plane: thin dorsal foot fascia over the
interosseous muscles. The 4th dorsal interosseous muscle
is the primary deep target.
→ Cross-ref: A03 S3.4

Vascular: 4th dorsal metatarsal artery (from the arcuate
artery, branch of dorsalis pedis). Small caliber.

Muscle layers (superficial → deep):
- Skin → thin subcutaneous tissue
- Dorsal digital expansion (extensor tendons)
- 4th dorsal interosseous muscle
- Plantar interosseous muscles (deep)

### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

**Five Shu: YES — Stream/Wood (输穴/木穴).** The Stream
point traditionally treats heaviness of the body and joint
pain (Nan Jing Ch.68: "输主体重节痛"). For Yang channels:
Well=Metal, Spring=Water, **Stream=Wood**, River=Fire,
He-Sea=Earth.

**Horary point (本穴): YES.** Wood organ (GB) + Wood Five
Shu (Stream) = SAME ELEMENT. GB41 is at peak potency during
the Gallbladder hour (23:00–01:00 子时). Horary point
selection is a time-based treatment strategy unique to when
organ-element and Five Shu-element align.

**Eight Confluent: YES — opens Dai Mai (带脉, Belt Vessel).**

Paired with **TE5 (Waiguan, opens Yang Wei Mai)**. The 4
Eight Confluent pairs:

| Upper limb | Lower limb | Extraordinary Vessels |
|---|---|---|
| SI3 (Houxi) | BL62 (Shenmai) | Du Mai + Yang Qiao Mai |
| **TE5 (Waiguan)** | **GB41 (Zulinqi)** | **Dai Mai + Yang Wei Mai** |
| LU7 (Lieque) | KI6 (Zhaohai) | Ren Mai + Yin Qiao Mai |
| PC6 (Neiguan) | SP4 (Gongsun) | Chong Mai + Yin Wei Mai |

**Same-family Shaoyang pairing:** Both TE5 (Hand Shaoyang)
and GB41 (Foot Shaoyang) belong to the SAME meridian
family. This is the second Yang same-family confirmation
(after SI3+BL62 Taiyang). Yang pairs = same-family; Yin
pairs = cross-family (LU7+KI6, PC6+SP4).
→ Cross-ref: AP-10 TE5 (line 227), AP-06 SI3 (Obs. 10)

**Dai Mai (带脉, Belt Vessel) — the ONLY horizontal vessel:**
The Dai Mai encircles the waist like a belt, "binding" (束)
all longitudinal channels. GB26-28 at the waist ARE the Dai
Mai territory points; GB41 at the foot OPENS the Dai Mai for
distal access. The opener is distal; the targets are local.
→ Cross-ref: GB26 (line 5271), GB27, GB28 in this file

Dai Mai pathology:
- 带下 (leukorrhea, literally "below the belt")
- Lumbar weakness and heaviness
- Abdominal distension
- Irregular menstruation
- Dai Mai dysfunction = loss of binding force — structures
  descend, fluids leak, the waist weakens

**Inner/Outer Gate extended system — NOW COMPLETE:**
All 4 points in the paired Eight Confluent system are now
documented across the Atlas:

| Role | Points | Vessels | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| Inner Gate (Yin) | PC6 + SP4 | Yin Wei Mai + Chong Mai | AP-09 + AP-04 |
| Outer Gate (Yang) | TE5 + GB41 | Yang Wei Mai + Dai Mai | AP-10 + **this entry** |

PC6+SP4 = Interior conditions (nausea, cardiac, anxiety)
TE5+GB41 = Exterior conditions (headache, lateral pain,
belt zone disorders)
→ Cross-ref: AP-10 TE5 (Inner/Outer Gate, line 263)

Yuan-Source: No (GB40).
Luo-Connecting: No (GB37).
Xi-Cleft: No (GB36 regular, GB35 Yang Wei Mai).
Back-Shu: No (BL19 at T10).
Front-Mu: No (GB24).
Hui-Meeting: No.

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM acupoint:** GB41 Zulinqi (足临泣). Triple function:
(a) Stream/Wood Horary — joint pain, body heaviness at GB
hour; (b) Eight Confluent — opens Dai Mai for leukorrhea,
irregular menstruation, lateral belt-zone disorders; (c)
distal vision point — "Overlooking Tears" for eye redness
and lacrimation. Combined with TE5 as the standard Eight
Confluent pair for temporal/lateral headache and Dai Mai
conditions. Combined with GB26 (local Dai Mai) for belt-
zone pathology.

**2. Trigger point:** 4th dorsal interosseous — referred
foot pain in the 4th metatarsal area. Very small muscle
with limited trigger point literature. Weak
correspondence. 🔴

**3. Ashi correspondence:** tender in 4th-5th metatarsal
stress, metatarsalgia, or march fracture of the 4th
metatarsal. Limited clinical significance as an Ashi
point. 🟡

**4. Marma point:** **Kshipra Marma** (क्षिप्र, between
metatarsals) — interosseous space on the dorsum of the
foot. The classical Kshipra is more specifically between
the big toe and 2nd toe (matching LR3), so GB41's
correspondence at the 4th-5th interspace is partial. 🟡

**5. Head zone:** L5-S1 dermatome at the dorsal foot.
Non-specific for organ correspondence.
→ Cross-ref: A07 S2.2

**6. Anatomy Trains:** **Lateral Line (LL)** terminates at
the lateral foot. GB41 is at the distal LL endpoint —
the same myofascial line that passes through GB26-28
(Dai Mai belt points) at the waist. The LL's trajectory
from lateral trunk to lateral foot parallels the GB
channel and is the closest myofascial analog to the Dai
Mai's "binding" function. 🟡
→ Cross-ref: A03 S3.4

**7. Neurogenic inflammatory spot:** No data.

**8. Cupping point:** Not applicable at the dorsal foot.

**Convergence score: 3/8** (TCM, Ashi limited, Anatomy
Trains). Lower convergence than GB40 — the distal dorsal
foot has fewer cross-tradition correspondences. The point's
significance is overwhelmingly CATEGORICAL (Stream, Horary,
Eight Confluent) rather than anatomically convergent.

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Nguồn cổ:
- Nan Jing Ch.29: Eight Confluent point (八脉交会穴).
  GB41 opens Dai Mai. Paired with TE5.
- Nan Jing Ch.68: Stream point (输主体重节痛) — "Stream
  treats body heaviness and joint pain."
- Ling Shu Ch.2 (本输 Ben Shu): identifies GB41 as the
  Stream point of Foot Shaoyang.
- Jiayi Jing: headache, dizziness, eye redness, tinnitus,
  breast abscess (乳痈), lateral costal pain, malaria,
  thigh/knee pain, dorsal foot pain.
- Zhenjiu Dacheng: lateral/temporal headache (偏头痛 —
  Shaoyang territory), irregular menstruation, leukorrhea
  (带下 — Dai Mai pathology), breast swelling and pain
  (乳胀痛), lacrimation in wind (迎风流泪 — the 临泣
  name connection).

The **breast indication** (乳痈, 乳胀痛) specifically
reflects Dai Mai function: the Belt Vessel encircles the
trunk at the level that includes the breast. GB41 as Dai
Mai opener accesses this circumferential territory. This
is analogous to SP4 (Chong Mai) treating abdominal
conditions — the opener point accesses the vessel's entire
domain from a distal position.

Kinh nghiệm lâm sàng:
- **TE5+GB41:** the standard Eight Confluent pair. Used for
  temporal/lateral headache (Shaoyang migraine protocol),
  irregular menstruation, leukorrhea, lateral trunk pain,
  ear conditions. Combined with local points (GB20, GB8,
  Taiyang EX-HN5) for the full Shaoyang headache
  prescription
  → Cross-ref: AP-10 TE5 (line 404)
- **GB41+GB26:** distal opener + local Dai Mai point for
  belt-zone pathology (leukorrhea, lumbar weakness)
- **GB41 Horary timing:** used at 23:00–01:00 (子时) for
  maximal potency during GB peak. Practical limitation:
  clinical treatment at midnight is rare outside inpatient
  settings

**Eight Confluent evidence landscape (updated with GB41):**

| Point | Vessel | Evidence tier | Key evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| PC6 | Yin Wei Mai | 🟢 Confirmed | Multiple Cochrane (PONV) |
| TE5 | Yang Wei Mai | 🟡 Plausible | JAMA migraine trial component |
| SI3 | Du Mai | 🟡 Plausible | Cervical protocols (indirect) |
| GB41 | **Dai Mai** | **🔴 Unvalidated** | **No point-specific data** |
| SP4 | Chong Mai | 🔴 | — |
| LU7 | Ren Mai | 🔴 | — |
| KI6 | Yin Qiao Mai | 🔴 | — |
| BL62 | Yang Qiao Mai | 🔴 | — |

5/8 Eight Confluent points remain at 🔴. Only PC6 has 🟢.

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

🔴 Unvalidated for all point-specific indications. No RCT
or SR isolating GB41 alone.

GB41 appears frequently in multipoint protocols for migraine
(as part of the TE5+GB41 pair) and menstrual disorders (as
part of Dai Mai treatments), but its individual contribution
has never been assessed.

**Methodological note:** the Eight Confluent system is
clinically used as PAIRS (TE5+GB41 together), not as
individual points. Testing GB41 alone would not reflect
clinical practice. The relevant evidence gap is for the
TE5+GB41 PAIR for Dai Mai-domain conditions (menstrual
disorders, leukorrhea, lateral trunk pain) — which also
has no dedicated study.

### Dynamic acupoint properties

No sensitization data at GB41. Would expect sensitization
during Dai Mai pathology (leukorrhea, irregular
menstruation, lateral girdle pain) — the extraordinary
vessel opener might show heightened reactivity when its
vessel's domain is disturbed. Unstudied.

The Horary point function adds a temporal sensitization
prediction: GB41 might show circadian variation in
sensitivity, with peak sensitivity during the 子时
(23:00–01:00) window. Acupoint impedance or thermal
measurements during the Horary window could test this
prediction — an untested classical claim with a testable
modern methodology.
→ Cross-ref: C04 S4

### Cơ chế giải thích

**Local mechanism:** dorsal foot afferents (L5-S1) →
dorsal horn → segmental modulation for foot and toe
conditions. Standard somatic pathway.

**Dai Mai opener mechanism:** L5-S1 afferents from GB41
have NO segmental relationship to the Dai Mai territory
(waist, T11-L1 where GB26-28 lie). The "opening"
mechanism — how a foot point accesses a waist-level
vessel — is non-segmental. Possible pathway: strong
afferent input from L5-S1 → brainstem → descending
modulation of the T11-L1 territory. The same type of
non-segmental supraspinal mechanism invoked for all
distal-to-proximal acupuncture effects.
→ Cross-ref: C04 S3.4

**Vision/lacrimation mechanism:** the "Overlooking Tears"
function (L5-S1 → lacrimation/vision) faces the same
non-segmental challenge as GB37's vision claim. The
classical rationale differs from GB37's Luo pathway:
GB41's vision connection comes from the channel
trajectory (GB channel starts at GB1, lateral canthus
→ eye territory). The CHANNEL carries the explanation,
not the nerve.

**Horary mechanism:** the time-dependent treatment
enhancement (strongest at 子时) has no known
neurophysiological correlate. Circadian variation in
autonomic tone and hormonal levels exists (cortisol,
melatonin) but has not been linked to point-specific
responsiveness.

### Kỹ thuật kích thích

Châm: 10–15 mm perpendicular or oblique toward the
sole. De Qi: distention at the dorsum of the foot,
sometimes radiating along the metatarsal space toward
the toes.

**TE5+GB41 Eight Confluent protocol:** the standard
pair for Dai Mai + Yang Wei Mai conditions. Typically
needled bilaterally: TE5 on the forearm (opens Yang
Wei Mai), GB41 on the foot (opens Dai Mai). The pair
accesses the temporal/lateral territory (Yang Wei Mai)
and the belt/girdle territory (Dai Mai) simultaneously.
Used for: temporal headache, lateral trunk pain,
irregular menstruation, leukorrhea.
→ Cross-ref: AP-10 TE5 (line 404)

**GB41+GB26 local protocol:** distal opener + waist-
level Dai Mai point. For focused Dai Mai conditions.

**Horary timing:** when possible, use GB41 at 23:00–
01:00 (子时) for maximal element-aligned potency.

Bấm huyệt: firm pressure between the 4th-5th metatarsal
bases. Moderate difficulty — tendons are close, less
muscle mass than leg points.

Cứu: applicable for Dai Mai cold/deficiency patterns
(cold leukorrhea, lumbar cold and weakness). Warming
moxa at GB41 supplements the Belt Vessel binding function.

### An toàn

LOW risk. Dorsum of foot, thin soft tissue.

Precautions:
- Tendons of extensor digitorum longus are superficial —
  angle the needle between tendons rather than directly
  into them
- 4th dorsal metatarsal artery: small caliber. Apply
  pressure if bleeding occurs
- Interosseous muscles are thin — periosteal contact with
  the metatarsal bone is possible at standard depth. Not
  dangerous but produces bone-aching sensation
- No pregnancy contraindication (GB41 is NOT on the
  classical pregnancy caution list despite its menstrual
  indications)
