## GB42 — Địa Ngũ Hội (地五会, Dì Wǔ Huì)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable)**
"Earth's Five Meetings" (地五会) — 地 (Dì, earth/ground) +
五 (Wǔ, five) + 会 (Huì, meeting/convergence). The
interpretation is debated: (a) meeting of five channel
influences at this ground-level point, (b) 地 as "foot on
the ground" with 五会 as "five meetings" of tendons on the
dorsum. Some classical commentators relate the name to the
convergence of five extensor tendons visible on the dorsal
foot surface.

GB42 is located between the 4th and 5th metatarsal bones,
distal to GB41 (Zulinqi). Its primary distinguishing feature
is the **classical moxa prohibition** — the 5th documented
in the Atlas.

**Moxa prohibition:** The Zhenjiu Jiayi Jing (针灸甲乙经,
Huangfu Mi, 259 CE) explicitly states: "地五会...禁不可灸，
灸之令人瘦" — "Moxibustion is prohibited at Diwuhui;
moxibustion causes the person to become emaciated/thin."

Updated moxa prohibition table (5 documented):

| Point | Classical source | Stated reason | Risk category |
|---|---|---|---|
| LU8 (Jingqu) | Jiayi Jing | 不可灸 | Vascular (radial artery) |
| LR10 (Wuli) | Multiple | 禁灸/禁针 | Vascular (femoral artery) |
| LR12 (Jimai) | Su Wen | Classical prohibition | Tissue (external genitalia) |
| TE23 (Sizhukong) | Multiple | 禁灸 | Thermal-ocular (orbital rim) |
| **GB42 (Diwuhui)** | **Jiayi Jing** | **令人瘦 (causes wasting)** | **Energetic/functional** |

GB42 is UNIQUE among the 5 prohibited points: the concern
is NOT local tissue damage (no major artery, no sensitive
organ) but a SYSTEMIC pathological effect. The previous 4
prohibitions all address immediate anatomical risks. GB42's
prohibition addresses a FUNCTIONAL outcome — metabolic
wasting. This constitutes a third category of moxa safety:

1. **Anatomical safety** (LU8, LR10): vascular damage risk
2. **Tissue sensitivity** (LR12, TE23): irreversibly
   vulnerable structures
3. **Functional contraindication** (GB42): systemic
   pathological effect with no obvious anatomical basis
→ Cross-ref: AP-10 TE23 (moxa prohibition table)

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): on the dorsum of the
foot, between the 4th and 5th metatarsal bones, proximal
to the 4th metatarsophalangeal joint, medial to the tendon
of extensor digiti minimi.

Anatomical landmarks: in the 4th-5th intermetatarsal space,
distal to GB41. While GB41 is at the metatarsal BASES
(proximal), GB42 is in the mid-shaft region of the
intermetatarsal space.

Needle depth: 5–10 mm perpendicular.

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **4th dorsal digital nerve** (intermediate dorsal
  cutaneous nerve branch, L5-S1): same territory as GB41
- Spinal segments: L5-S1
- Zone 17 (dorsal foot digital nerves)
→ Cross-ref: A01 S2.3

Vascular: 4th dorsal metatarsal artery branch.

Muscle layers: skin → thin subcutaneous tissue → dorsal
interosseous fascia → 4th dorsal interosseous muscle.
Very thin soft tissue over tendons and metatarsal bones.

### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

No special category. The moxa prohibition is a SAFETY
classification, not a point category membership.
→ Cross-ref: B01 S3.1

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM acupoint:** GB42 Diwuhui (地五会). Functions:
clears Heat, activates the channel, benefits the ears and
eyes. Indications: tinnitus, deafness, headache, eye
redness and swelling, breast pain (乳痛), lateral costal
pain, dorsal foot pain. Low clinical prominence — most
indications overlap with the neighboring GB41 (which is
far more clinically significant).

**2-8.** Minimal correspondence. The 4th intermetatarsal
mid-shaft has no significant trigger point, Marma, or
myofascial line relevance beyond what GB41 already covers.

**Convergence score: 1/8** (TCM only). Consistent with
low-prominence trajectory points on the distal foot.

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Nguồn cổ:
- Jiayi Jing: tinnitus, deafness, breast pain, inner eye
  redness, lateral costal pain. Moxa prohibition noted.
- Deadman/WHO: headache, eye redness and pain, tinnitus,
  breast abscess, dorsal foot pain and swelling.

Most indications replicate GB41 and GB43 — GB42 appears
to function primarily as a mid-space trajectory point
between the Stream (GB41) and Spring (GB43).

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

🔴 Unvalidated. No point-specific study.

### An toàn

LOW risk. Dorsal foot point over intermetatarsal space — no
major vessel or nerve at standard depth.

**MOXA PROHIBITION (禁灸):** Jiayi Jing explicitly prohibits
moxibustion ("灸之令人瘦"). See intro for full analysis and
updated prohibition table. Acupuncture is NOT prohibited.

No pregnancy contraindication.

---

## GB43 — Hiệp Khê (侠溪, Xiá Xī)

**Evidence tier: B (Standard Entry)**
"Narrow Ravine" (侠溪) — 侠 (Xiá, narrow/flanking) + 溪
(Xī, stream/ravine). Describes the narrow web space between
the 4th and 5th toes — a "ravine" between two toe
prominences. The 侠 character also carries the meaning
"knight-errant" (侠客), but the anatomical reading is
primary here.

GB43 is the **Five Shu Spring/Water point** (荥穴/水穴)
of the GB channel. Yang Five Shu element sequence:
Well=Metal, **Spring=Water**, Stream=Wood, River=Fire,
He-Sea=Earth. The Spring point traditionally treats Heat
in the body and changes in facial complexion — the Spring
is where Qi begins to "gush," stronger than the Well
(trickle) but not yet at full flow (Stream).

As Water (Mother of Wood in the Sheng cycle), GB43 is the
**Tonification point** (母穴/补穴) of the GB channel.
Water generates Wood — when Gallbladder function is
deficient (timidity, indecisiveness, pale complexion,
weak resolve), GB43 supplements the Mother to strengthen
the Son.

The GB Tonification-Sedation pair:
- **Tonification (母穴):** GB43 (Spring/Water — Water
  generates Wood)
- **Sedation (泻穴):** GB38 (River/Fire — Wood generates
  Fire)
→ Cross-ref: GB38 in this file (line 8308)

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): on the dorsum of the
foot, between the 4th and 5th toes, proximal to the web
margin, at the junction of the red and white skin.

Anatomical landmarks: in the web space between the 4th
and 5th toes, at the dorsal margin where thin dorsal skin
meets thick plantar skin. Same positional pattern as
other Spring points: LR2 (1st-2nd toe web), HT8 (4th-5th
finger web), SI2 (5th finger ulnar web).

Needle depth: 5–10 mm oblique proximally.

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **4th common dorsal digital nerve** → splits into proper
  dorsal digital nerves of the adjacent sides of 4th and
  5th toes. From intermediate dorsal cutaneous nerve
  (superficial peroneal terminal branch, L5-S1)
- The plantar aspect receives contribution from the lateral
  plantar nerve (S1-S2), but GB43 at the dorsal/web margin
  is primarily in superficial peroneal territory
- Spinal segments: L5-S1
- Zone 17 (dorsal foot digital nerves)
→ Cross-ref: A01 S2.3

Fascial plane: minimal muscle. Skin → thin subcutaneous
tissue → interdigital fold tissue. The web space contains
interdigital arteries and nerves but no significant muscle
mass.
→ Cross-ref: A03 S3.4

Vascular: dorsal digital arteries (from 4th dorsal
metatarsal artery). Small caliber.

### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

**Five Shu: YES — Spring/Water (荥穴/水穴).** The Spring
point is the second of the Five Shu, where Qi "gushes"
more strongly than at the Well. Traditional domain:
treats Heat in the body, facial color changes (Nan Jing
Ch.68: "荥主身热" — "Spring treats body Heat").

**Tonification point (母穴): YES.** Water is the Mother
of Wood in the Sheng (generating) cycle. GB43 = Mother
element → supplements deficient Gallbladder function.

Complementary with GB38 (Sedation/Fire/Son): GB43
SUPPLEMENTS what is deficient, GB38 DRAINS what is
excessive. Same domain (head/sensory symptoms), opposite
therapeutic strategies.
→ Cross-ref: B01 S3.1

Yuan-Source: No (GB40).
Luo-Connecting: No (GB37).
Xi-Cleft: No (GB36).
Eight Confluent: No.
Hui-Meeting: No.
Special designations: None.

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM acupoint:** GB43 Xiaxi (侠溪). Spring/Water,
Tonification. Primary functions: clears Gallbladder Fire,
resolves Damp-Heat in the lower Jiao, benefits the head
and ears, clears the eyes. Key indication: tinnitus,
deafness, dizziness, headache (Shaoyang territory).
Distinguished from GB38 (which DRAINS excess) — GB43
SUPPLEMENTS deficiency. The Tonification-Sedation pair
forms the most refined bi-directional regulatory system
on the channel.

**2. Trigger point:** no significant trigger point in the
toe web space. The interosseous muscles are too distal and
small for reliable trigger point mapping. 🔴

**3. Ashi correspondence:** tender in 4th-5th toe web
inflammation, interdigital maceration, or neuroma of the
4th interspace. Limited clinical significance. 🟡

**4. Marma point:** **Kshipra Marma** (क्षिप्र) — the
interdigital Marma. Classical Kshipra is between the big
toe and 2nd toe, so the 4th-5th toe correspondence is
partial and distal. 🟡

**5. Head zone:** L5-S1, S1-S2 dermatome at the toes.
Non-specific.

**6-8.** No significant correspondence.

**Convergence score: 2/8** (TCM, Ashi limited). Consistent
with other toe-web Spring points — functionally defined
rather than anatomically convergent.

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Nguồn cổ:
- Nan Jing Ch.68: Spring point (荥主身热) — "Spring treats
  body Heat." Applied to Gallbladder Fire conditions.
- Ling Shu Ch.2 (本输 Ben Shu): identifies GB43 as the
  Spring point of Foot Shaoyang.
- Jiayi Jing: headache, dizziness (头晕), tinnitus,
  deafness, eye redness and swelling, lateral costal pain,
  fever without sweating, dorsal foot swelling and pain.
- Zhenjiu Dacheng: lateral headache, dream-disturbed sleep
  (多梦), bitter taste (口苦), intercostal pain, cheek
  swelling, tinnitus.

The **dream disturbance** (多梦) indication is specifically
GB-related: Gallbladder governs decision-making (决断, Su
Wen Ch.8). GB dysfunction → poor decision-making in dream
state = nightmares and disturbed dreams. GB43 as
Tonification point strengthens this decision-making capacity
by supplementing the Mother (Water generates Wood).

Kinh nghiệm lâm sàng:
- **GB43 tonification protocol:** supplement technique
  (reinforcing manipulation) for GB deficiency — timidity,
  indecisiveness, easily startled, weak resolve
- **Tonification-Sedation pair:** GB43 (supplement, Mother)
  and GB38 (reduce, Son). Used together for mixed
  excess/deficiency patterns: drain excess GB Fire (bitter
  taste, irritability) while supplementing GB Qi (resolve,
  decision-making)
- Migraine acute treatment: GB43 (distal Spring, clears
  Heat) + GB20 (local) + GB8 (temporal)

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

🔴 Unvalidated for all indications. No RCT or SR isolating
GB43 as a single point.

The Tonification point concept (Mother supplementation) has
no dedicated evidence base. Among all Tonification points
documented (LU9, LR8, HT9, PC9, SP2, LI11, SI3, TE3,
GB43), NONE has been tested specifically for tonification
vs non-tonification technique comparison. The entire
Five Element treatment strategy remains empirically
unvalidated.

### Dynamic acupoint properties

No sensitization data at GB43. Spring points might show
sensitization during acute febrile conditions (the
traditional Spring point domain = Heat), but this has
not been studied for any Spring point.
→ Cross-ref: C04 S4

### Cơ chế giải thích

**Local mechanism:** digital nerve afferents (L5-S1) →
dorsal horn → segmental modulation for toe and foot
conditions. Standard somatic pathway.

**Shaoyang headache mechanism:** non-segmental. L5-S1 →
CN V3/temporal region requires supraspinal pathways.
Same challenge as all distal Shaoyang points (GB41, GB44,
TE5). The mechanism is no different from the general
distal-to-proximal problem.
→ Cross-ref: C04 S3.4

**Tonification mechanism:** the Mother-Son supplementation
concept (stimulating the Water point to strengthen Wood
function) has no known neurophysiological correlate. No
study has demonstrated that manipulating a specific Five
Shu element point produces element-specific physiological
changes.

### Kỹ thuật kích thích

Châm: 5–10 mm oblique proximally. The web space is thin —
limit depth to avoid puncturing through to the plantar
surface. De Qi: pricking or distention at the web,
sometimes radiating along the toe margins.

**Tonification-Sedation protocol:**
- Deficient GB: GB43 with supplementing technique
  (reinforcing needle manipulation — slow insertion,
  rapid withdrawal, following the channel direction)
- Excess GB: GB38 with reducing technique (rapid insertion,
  slow withdrawal, opposing the channel direction)
→ Cross-ref: GB38 in this file

Bấm huyệt: pinch the web space between thumb and index
finger. Easy self-treatment location.

Cứu: applicable for GB deficiency (cold type). Small
moxa cones or stick moxa appropriate for the toe-level
application. Warming the Spring point supplements the
Water element to nourish Wood.

### An toàn

LOW risk. Toe web, minimal depth.

Precautions:
- Dorsal digital arteries: small caliber. Apply pressure
  if bleeding occurs
- Thin skin at web space — use sharp, fine-gauge needles
  to minimize tissue trauma
- Avoid puncturing through to the plantar surface
  (painful and unnecessary)
- No pregnancy contraindication

---

## GB44 — Túc Khiếu Âm (足窍阴, Zú Qiào Yīn)

**Evidence tier: B (Standard Entry)**
"Foot's Portal of Yin" (足窍阴) — 足 (Zú, foot) + 窍
(Qiào, portal/orifice) + 阴 (Yīn, Yin). The 足 prefix
distinguishes this from **GB11 (头窍阴, Tóu Qiào Yīn,
"Head's Portal of Yin")** — the FIRST of GB's two head-foot
naming pairs (the second being GB15/GB41, 临泣 Linqi).

GB44 is the **Five Shu Well/Metal point** (井穴/金穴) of
the GB channel — the most distal point, at the lateral
nail corner of the 4th toe. Well points are where Qi
"wells up" from the extremities. The Well is a trickle —
the smallest flow, but the origin of all that follows.

As Metal (Ke Controller of Wood), GB44 provides the
strongest regulatory RESTRAINT on Wood/Gallbladder excess.
Metal controls (克) Wood in the Five Element restraining
cycle. When Gallbladder Fire blazes (bitter taste, red
eyes, irritability), GB44 applies the Metal control brake.

**Well point traditional functions:**
- Treats fullness below the Heart and mental restlessness
  (Nan Jing Ch.68: "井主心下满" — "Well treats fullness
  below the Heart")
- Emergency point for loss of consciousness (Jing-Well
  bleeding technique)
- Entry/exit of channel Qi — GB44 is the EXIT point of
  the Gallbladder channel. Qi flows GB44 → LR1 (entry
  point of Liver channel, big toe), completing the
  Shaoyang→Jueyin paired channel transition

GB44→LR1 channel transition: from the 4th toe (GB44,
lateral, Shaoyang) to the big toe (LR1, medial, Jueyin).
Qi crosses from lateral to medial on the dorsum of the
foot. Neurally: from superficial peroneal territory
(L5-S1) to deep peroneal territory (L4-L5). The neural
transition mirrors the channel transition.
→ Cross-ref: AP-12 LR1

**Complete Five Shu set — 6th documented set:**

| Five Shu | Element | Point | Role |
|----------|---------|-------|------|
| Well | Metal | **GB44** | **Ke Controller** |
| Spring | Water | GB43 | Tonification |
| Stream | Wood | GB41 | Horary |
| River | Fire | GB38 | Sedation |
| He-Sea | Earth | GB34 | Ke Subject |

All 5 Five Element roles present, matching the paired
channel LR (5th set). Yin-Yang orthogonality confirmed
for the 6th time: same ELEMENTS map to same ROLES, but
the Five Shu TYPE carrying each element reverses between
Yin and Yang channels (e.g., LR Ke Controller = LR4
River/Metal; GB Ke Controller = GB44 Well/Metal).
→ Cross-ref: plan.md §3, AP-01 LU (Obs. 22)

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): on the lateral side of
the 4th toe, 0.1 cun proximal to the corner of the nail.

Anatomical landmarks: at the lateral nail angle of the
4th toe — the standard Well point position. The nail fold
creates a natural landmark. Same positional pattern as all
other documented Well points: LR1 (big toe lateral), HT9
(little finger radial), PC9 (middle finger tip), LU11
(thumb radial), LI1 (index finger radial), SI1 (little
finger ulnar), TE1 (ring finger ulnar).

Needle depth: 1–3 mm superficial prick, or bloodletting
technique.

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Proper dorsal digital nerve of the lateral 4th toe**
  (from intermediate dorsal cutaneous nerve → 4th common
  dorsal digital nerve → proper digital branch). The MOST
  TERMINAL nerve access point on the GB channel — furthest
  from the spinal cord along the entire neural pathway
- Spinal segments: L5-S1
- Zone 17 (dorsal foot digital nerves)
→ Cross-ref: A01 S2.3

The nail bed has exceptionally high density of free nerve
endings (Aδ and C fibers) — the richest sensory innervation
per unit area on the foot. This density is the anatomical
basis for the Well point's potent sensory stimulus
(bloodletting at nail level produces intense nociceptive
input).

Fascial plane: skin → nail fold tissue → periosteum of
distal phalanx. Minimal soft tissue — the bone is
immediately beneath the nail at this level.

Vascular: proper dorsal digital artery (lateral 4th toe).
Tiny vessel, but bloodletting is the primary traditional
technique.

### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

**Five Shu: YES — Well/Metal (井穴/金穴).** The Well point
is the first of the Five Shu — the most distal, where Qi
originates. Yang channels: **Well=Metal**, Spring=Water,
Stream=Wood, River=Fire, He-Sea=Earth.

**Ke Controller (克穴): YES.** Metal controls (克) Wood
in the Five Element restraining cycle. GB44 provides the
STRONGEST regulatory restraint on Wood/Gallbladder excess.
When Gallbladder Fire blazes, the Ke Controller applies the
element-restraining brake.

Ke Controller ≠ Sedation: the Sedation point (GB38 River/
Fire = Son element) drains excess through the Sheng
(generating) outflow. The Ke Controller (GB44 Well/Metal)
restrains excess through the Ke (restraining) cross-cycle.
Two distinct excess-management strategies from different
Five Element relationships.
→ Cross-ref: B01 S3.1

Yuan-Source: No (GB40).
Luo-Connecting: No (GB37).
Xi-Cleft: No (GB36).
Eight Confluent: No.
Hui-Meeting: No.
Special designations: None.

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM acupoint:** GB44 Zuqiaoyin (足窍阴). Well/Metal,
Ke Controller. Primary functions: clears Heat (especially
Gallbladder Fire), benefits the head and sensory orifices,
regulates Qi, calms the spirit. Key indications: lateral
headache (偏头痛), tinnitus, deafness, eye pain, sore
throat, bitter taste (口苦 — the defining GB Fire
symptom, Su Wen Ch.35: 胆瘅), dream-disturbed sleep
(多梦), lateral costal pain.

The **bitter taste** (口苦) indication deserves emphasis:
it is the MOST SPECIFIC Gallbladder symptom in classical
diagnosis. Su Wen Ch.35 describes 胆瘅 (Gallbladder Heat
disease): "有病口苦...名曰胆瘅" — "disease with bitter
mouth, named Gallbladder Heat." GB44 as Ke Controller =
the point designed to restrain exactly this type of excess.

**Well point emergency (急救):** bloodletting at GB44 for
loss of consciousness, high fever, acute sore throat. The
Twelve Jing-Well bleeding technique (十二井穴刺血) uses all
12 Well points bilaterally for coma/stroke emergency — GB44
is one of the 12.

**2. Trigger point:** no significant trigger point at the
toe tip. 🔴

**3. Ashi correspondence:** tender in 4th toe injury,
deformity, or paronychia. Limited. 🟡

**4. Marma point:** no specific Marma at the toe nail. The
nearest Kshipra is between toes, not at the nail level. 🔴

**5-8.** No significant correspondence.

**Convergence score: 1/8** (TCM only). Consistent with
all documented Well points: PC9 (1/8), HT9 (1/8), LR1
(2/8). Well points are FUNCTIONALLY defined (Five Element
roles, emergency bloodletting) rather than anatomically
convergent with other traditions.

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Nguồn cổ:
- Nan Jing Ch.68: Well point (井主心下满) — "Well treats
  fullness below the Heart."
- Ling Shu Ch.2 (本输 Ben Shu): identifies GB44 as the
  Well point of Foot Shaoyang.
- Jiayi Jing: lateral headache (偏头痛), tinnitus,
  deafness, eye pain (目痛), sore throat, bitter taste
  (口苦), lateral costal pain, dream-disturbed sleep
  (多梦), insomnia, loss of consciousness (急救 — Well
  point emergency).
- Zhenjiu Dacheng: temporal headache, irritability and
  anger (善怒), bitter taste, tongue stiffness, tinnitus,
  insomnia with excessive dreaming.

The **irritability and anger** (善怒) indication represents
Gallbladder Fire Rising — the emotional counterpart to
bitter taste. GB44 as Ke Controller restrains both the
somatic (bitter taste) and emotional (irritability)
manifestations of Wood excess.

**Key differentiation from GB43:**
- GB43 (Tonification/Water): SUPPLEMENTS deficiency
- GB44 (Ke Controller/Metal): RESTRAINS excess
Same symptom domain (head, orifices, dreams), opposite
therapeutic strategies, different Five Element mechanisms
(Sheng supplementation vs Ke restraint).

Kinh nghiệm lâm sàng:
- **Jing-Well bleeding:** prick with three-edged needle
  (三棱针) or lancet, squeeze 3-5 drops of blood. For
  acute conditions: high fever, acute tonsillitis/sore
  throat, loss of consciousness, acute eye pain
- **GB44+LR1 paired channel bleeding:** bilateral
  bloodletting at GB44 (Shaoyang exit) + LR1 (Jueyin
  entry) addresses the entire Wood-element circuit. For
  acute Liver-Gallbladder Fire Rising (red eyes, bitter
  taste, irritability, temporal headache)
- **GB44+GB34:** Ke Controller (restraint) + He-Sea
  (descending) — dual approach to Gallbladder excess
  from different levels

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

🔴 Unvalidated for all point-specific indications. No RCT
or SR isolating GB44 alone.

Jing-Well bloodletting for emergency consciousness has some
general evidence (systematic reviews on Well-point bleeding
for acute stroke) but studies use all 12 Well points
bilaterally — GB44's individual contribution cannot be
isolated. The mechanism (intense nociceptive arousal) is
generic to all Well points, not GB44-specific.

### Dynamic acupoint properties

No sensitization data at GB44. Well points as the most
distal Qi access points might show altered sensitivity
during channel or organ disturbance, but no study has
examined this for any Well point.
→ Cross-ref: C04 S4

### Cơ chế giải thích

**Well point emergency mechanism:** intense peripheral
nociceptive input from nail-bed bloodletting → strong
Aδ and C-fiber activation → ascending via spinothalamic
and spinoreticular tracts → reticular activating system
(RAS) arousal → consciousness recovery. This is the MOST
PLAUSIBLE mechanism for Well-point bloodletting emergency
effects — a powerful noxious stimulus arousal response.
The high free nerve ending density at the nail bed
ensures maximal afferent activation per unit area of
stimulation.

**Ke Controller mechanism:** the Metal-controls-Wood
restraining relationship has no known neurophysiological
correlate. No study has demonstrated element-specific
physiological changes from Ke Controller point
stimulation.
→ Cross-ref: C04 S3.4

**Head/orifice mechanism:** L5-S1 → headache, tinnitus,
eye symptoms, bitter taste = non-segmental. The
bitter taste claim is particularly challenging: gustatory
function (CN VII, IX) has no known pathway from L5-S1
afferents. All head/orifice effects require supraspinal
pathways.

**Head-foot Qiaoyin naming pair (KEY QUESTION #11):**

GB11 (头窍阴) and GB44 (足窍阴) share 窍阴 — "Portal
of Yin."

| | GB11 (Head 窍阴) | GB44 (Foot 窍阴) |
|---|---|---|
| Location | Post-auricular, mastoid | Lateral 4th toe tip |
| Neural zone | Zone 3 (C2-C3) | Zone 17 (L5-S1) |
| Five Shu | No | Well/Metal (Ke Controller) |
| Tier | C | B |
| Common indications | Tinnitus, deafness, headache, eye pain | Tinnitus, deafness, headache, eye pain |

There is NO direct neural connection between these two
points (C2-C3 vs L5-S1). The naming does not encode a
neural relationship. Instead, the head-foot pair encodes
a CHANNEL-AXIS concept: both points mark locations where
the GB channel intersects Yin-associated sensory orifice
functions — GB11 near the ear (Kidney=Water→"opens into
the ears"), GB44 at the Shaoyang→Jueyin channel
transition (the point where Yang meets Yin).

The 窍 (Qiao, portal/orifice) concept relates to the
Seven Orifices (七窍): eyes (2), ears (2), nostrils (2),
mouth (1). Both points share nearly identical classical
indications (tinnitus, deafness, headache, eye pain) —
the same orifice-related symptoms treated from opposite
ends of the channel. This is the classical principle of
distal-to-proximal treatment encoded in nomenclature.

### Kỹ thuật kích thích

**Bloodletting (刺血):** primary technique at Well points.
Prick with a three-edged needle (三棱针) or sterile lancet,
squeeze 3-5 drops of blood. Used for acute conditions:
high fever, acute sore throat, loss of consciousness,
acute eye redness and pain.

Châm: 1–3 mm superficial. De Qi: sharp pricking sensation
at the nail edge. The minimal depth and high nerve density
make the stimulus brief but intense.

**GB44+LR1 paired channel bleeding:** bilateral bloodletting
at both exit (GB44) and entry (LR1) points of the
Wood-element circuit. Treats acute Liver-Gallbladder Fire
Rising (red eyes, bitter taste, irritability).

Bấm huyệt: firm nail-edge pressure. Self-applicable for
acute headache — pressure at GB44 can be performed
anywhere without instruments.

Cứu: small moxa cones directly on or adjacent to the
nail. Used for deficiency/cold conditions (warms the
channel extremity). Less commonly used than bloodletting
at this point.

### An toàn

VERY LOW risk (standard for all Well points).

Precautions:
- **Bloodletting:** use sterile lancet. Avoid deep
  puncture into the periosteum of the distal phalanx —
  superficial prick of the skin only
- **Infection control:** the nail fold is prone to
  paronychia if hygiene is compromised. Ensure sterile
  technique and clean the site before and after
- No major vessel or nerve at risk at standard depth
- No pregnancy contraindication

---

**Observation 22 — Four-Level Cascading Nerve Access:
Sciatic-Peroneal System Complete:**

Extending Observation 18 (two-level at GB30→GB34) and its
Zone 15 update (three-level at GB35-39), the ankle-foot
points (GB40-GB44) complete a FOUR-LEVEL cascading nerve
access pattern through the sciatic-peroneal system:

| Level | Zone | Points | Nerve structure | Resolution |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 13 | GB30 | Sciatic trunk (~2 cm ∅) | BEFORE tibial/peroneal split |
| 2 | 14 | GB34 | Common peroneal | AFTER sciatic, BEFORE deep/superficial |
| 3 | 15 | GB35-39 | Superficial peroneal trunk | AFTER deep/superficial split |
| 4 | 16-17 | GB40-44 | Terminal dorsal digital (<1 mm) | AFTER trunk→terminal split |

The GB channel traverses the SAME nerve system (sciatic →
peroneal → digital) at FOUR successive branching
resolutions — from the thickest trunk (sciatic, ~2 cm
diameter at GB30) to the finest terminals (proper digital
nerve, <1 mm at GB44). This is the most detailed nerve-
tracing architecture of any meridian in the Atlas.

Clinical implication: stimulation at different branching
levels accesses different fiber-type compositions. The
trunk (GB30) contains ALL fiber types (motor, sensory,
autonomic). Each successive branch carries fewer types.
Terminal digital nerves at the nail bed (GB44) are
predominantly cutaneous Aδ and C fibers. The cascading
pattern provides a natural experimental framework for
testing how nerve access level affects clinical outcomes.

No other documented meridian even reaches 3 branching
levels. LU's radial artery corridor (LU7-9) and SP's
saphenous corridor (SP5-9) are single-nerve corridors
without branching-resolution variation. The 4-level
cascade is unique to GB and reflects its extraordinary
length (44 points, second only to BL) traversing the
entire sciatic nerve system from hip to toe.

**Observation 23 — Two Head-Foot Naming Pairs: GB's Unique
Nomenclatural Architecture:**

GB is the ONLY documented channel with TWO head-foot
naming pairs:

| Pair | Head point | Foot point | Shared name | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | GB11 头窍阴 | GB44 足窍阴 | 窍阴 Qiaoyin | "Portal of Yin" — sensory orifices |
| 2 | GB15 头临泣 | GB41 足临泣 | 临泣 Linqi | "Overlooking Tears" — lacrimation/vision |

Both pairs encode VISION/ORIFICE functions. This naming
architecture is unique to GB because of two converging
factors:

1. **20 head points** (most of any channel) — providing
   multiple head-point candidates for naming pairs
2. **Yang pair of Liver** ("opens into the eyes," 肝开窍于
   目) — vision/orifice function is the channel's dominant
   sensory theme

The naming pairs create CHANNEL-AXIS correspondences: a
function named at the head (GB11 near the ear, GB15 near
the eye) is echoed at the foot (GB44 at the toe, GB41 at
the metatarsal), encoding the classical principle that
distal points treat proximal conditions.

Functional correspondence across the pairs:
- GB15 (头临泣): Yang Wei Mai meeting → head wind/tears →
  diagnosed AT the head
- GB41 (足临泣): Eight Confluent for Dai Mai → TREATS via
  distal access from the foot
- GB11 (头窍阴): post-auricular sensory orifice → local
  access to ear/mastoid
- GB44 (足窍阴): Well/Metal Ke Controller → RESTRAINS
  orifice-related excess from the foot

In both pairs, the HEAD point diagnoses/accesses locally,
while the FOOT point treats distally — the same division
of labor encoded in the same name at opposite ends of the
body. This answers **Key Question #11**.

Updated neural zone table (17 zones through 44 points):

| Zone | Points | Primary Nerve | Segment |
|------|--------|---------------|---------|
| 1 | GB1 | Lacrimal/Zygomatic | CN V1/V2 |
| 2 | GB2-GB8 | Auriculotemporal | CN V3 |
| 3 | GB9-GB12 | Occipital nerves | C2-C3 |
| 4 | GB13-GB15 | Supraorbital | CN V1 |
| 5 | GB16-GB19 | V1→C2 transition | V1/C2 |
| 6 | GB20 | Deep suboccipital | C1-C3+XI |
| 7 | GB21 | Supraclavicular+XI | C3-C4+XI |
| 8 | GB22-GB23 | Intercostal | T4 |
| 9 | GB24 | Intercostal | T7 |
| 10 | GB25-GB26 | Subcostal/lower intercostal | T11-L1 |
| 11 | GB27-GB28 | Iliohypogastric/ilioinguinal | L1 |
| 12 | GB29, GB31-GB33 | Lateral femoral cutaneous | L2-L3 |
| 13 | GB30 | Sciatic (inf. cluneal cutaneous) | L4-S3 |
| 14 | GB34 | Common peroneal (lat. sural cutaneous) | L4-S2 |
| 15 | GB35-GB39 | Superficial peroneal | L4-S1 |
| 16 | GB40 | Superficial peroneal terminal + sural | L4-S1/S1-S2 |
